# user_stories.md — Cartograph

**Schema (fixed):**
`ID · Title · Actor · Narrative(As a / I want / So that) · Priority(MoSCoW) · Status · Acceptance Criteria(AC-XXXX, Given/When/Then) · Security · Performance · Trace(Milestone, Crate, Flow?, Test IDs)`

**Status enum:** `Draft · Ready · In-Progress · In-Review · Blocked · Done · Deferred`

**Status rule:** a status describes `main`, not a plan. `Done` means every AC
of the story has a `rust`/`story` binding in [`test-map.md`](test-map.md) that
is not marked PARTIAL and runs in CI, or a recorded manual result, and no
manual procedure bound to its ACs is still UNEXECUTED, pending, not run or
without a recorded result. `In-Progress` means some
ACs have that evidence, or only PARTIAL evidence, and the rest do not. `Draft`
and `Ready` mean no AC has any evidence yet. Statuses were last set from `main` for #1759.

> Convention: Security and Performance facts are mapped onto the owning US/AC. Trace binds each story to a milestone, the owning crate(s), and test IDs (see `US-TM.md`).

---

### US-0001 — Add a repo set and define system topology
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** to add 1..N GitHub repos and declare which repos form one system **so that** the engine treats them as a single analyzable whole.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0001** Given valid GitHub App/PAT auth, when I add a repo URL, then it is cloned read-only and listed with commit SHA. The token comes from the auth ladder (ADR-0009): `GH_TOKEN`, then `GITHUB_TOKEN`, then `gh auth token`. `gh` is found on `PATH` or, for a desktop app launched from Finder or a launcher with a minimal `PATH`, at the standard Homebrew and Linuxbrew install paths. No token means anonymous, so only public repos can be cloned.
- **AC-0002** Given a multi-repo set, when I edit `cartograph.system.toml`, then declared layer hints and channel identities are applied at ingest. Guided new-manifest creation (#1671, in progress) preserves exact repository/configuration identities and explicit service/gateway declarations, previews validated TOML and saves only on request. Service base URL lines are trimmed and blank lines omitted for preview and save while the editable draft is retained. Duplicate configuration keys and invalid bindings are refused. Saving never overwrites an existing manifest or starts recovery; cancellation and failure retain the draft.
- **AC-0003** Given an unauthorized repo, when I add it, then I get a clear auth-failure with remediation and no partial clone. The remediation says whether a credential was found and refused (check its repo access) or none was found (sign in with `gh auth login` or set `GH_TOKEN`).
- **AC-0049** Given any completed ingest, when its summary is shown, then TypeScript and Terraform each report source-file, node, and edge counts including explicit zeros.
- **AC-0050** Given a non-empty graph, when I request a clear and confirm the warning, then all graph facts are removed while durable job history remains intact.
- **AC-0076** Given accumulated terminal jobs, when I clear finished jobs and confirm the warning, then done/failed/cancelled rows are removed while queued/running/resumable interrupted jobs and all graph facts remain intact (an ended investigation's interrupted row is cleared per AC-0202).
- **AC-0077** Given the production Jobs surface, when it renders, then it offers only lifecycle verbs on existing work (Cancel/Retry/Resume and Clear finished) — no job-creation control.
- **AC-0078** Given any recovery command (ingest, add-repo, add-system, or an ingest retry/resume), when it runs, then extraction executes on a blocking worker thread — the webview/main thread never blocks, the UI stays interactive, and job progress renders throughout.
- **AC-0085** Given one or more ingested targets, when the Workspace renders, then it states what the current system contains — each repo with its commit identity, derived from the graph's own facts (never from history logs, which survive a clear) — and that further ingests merge into the same system; the destructive action is labeled in system terms (Clear system) and its confirmation names exactly what is removed (every recovered fact) and what survives (job history and settings). The shell breadcrumb reflects evidence-backed system contents after startup or refresh even without an in-memory ingest summary; an empty, cleared, or evidence-less graph shows No system, regardless of raw node counts or retained job history.
- **AC-1013** Given a loaded system, when the user starts a new project (Workspace, **New project…**) and confirms, then Cartograph restarts with an empty project: the graph and its derived caches, registered sources and managed checkouts, retained source and receipts, findings, investigations and their chats, reviews and curations, decisions, staged proposals, section drafts, work packets, job history and metrics are set aside as the previous project (AC-1014), while settings, run limits, the local-model and investigation choices, the egress log, MCP client identities, installed adapters and registries, the debug log and provider keys stay with the application. The confirmation names the repos set aside and what is shared; the action is refused while a job is queued or running; the change runs at the next launch before any store opens, and a launch that stops part way finishes it on the next one (#1994).
- **AC-1014** Given more than one project, when the user opens Workspace **Projects**, then every project is listed with the loaded one first and marked, each named by its name or else its repos; switching to a parked project (confirmed, refused while a job is queued or running) sets the loaded project aside whole and restarts with the chosen one loaded as it was left, with no re-ingest, while settings stay with the application; any project can be renamed (one line of at most 120 characters, empty clears it); a parked project can be deleted once confirmed, the loaded one cannot; a launch that stops part way through a switch finishes it on the next one, and a project from before this change becomes the first listed project (#1994).
- **AC-0094** Given a running recovery job (ingest, add-repo, or add-system), when I navigate away from the Recovering screen or run it in background, then it stays reachable from the status bar ("Ingesting…") and from a "View live" action on its row in the Jobs surface; while it runs, both surfaces show a best-effort, non-persisted live detail of the file and layer (application/infra/language) currently being read, throttled so it never floods the event bridge.
- **AC-0195** Given an extraction in which a node id or a (source, destination, label) relation occurs more than once — one relation cited from several call or import sites, or distinct declarations sharing one id — when it is ingested (direct, add-repo, or add-system), then the graph keeps exactly one fact per node id and per relation, the summary's node and edge totals are the distinct facts the whole operation published — the union across every repo it loaded (Repo nodes included) and every later whole-graph stage (observed BACKS stitching, found-ADR relinking), less any fact those stages retracted — so a first ingest into an empty graph quotes the same totals as the Workspace graph counts, each per-layer row counts distinct facts, and the collapsed occurrences, including a globally keyed fact published by more than one repo, are reported explicitly as merged nodes, merged edges, and node identity collisions (one id carrying differing facts) — never silently.
- **AC-0196** Given an ingest summary, when Workspace shows the last ingest, then merged occurrences and node identity collisions are stated beside the totals whenever either is non-zero, and no merge line appears when both are zero.
- **AC-0197** Given a local preflight, when it scans a repository, then the scan runs on a blocking worker thread rather than the invoking webview/main thread, and the Preflight surface shows live, throttled progress naming the file being read and its position in the walk — and, while the tree is still being listed, the directory being entered and how many files have been found so far.
- **AC-0198** Given a running preflight, when I cancel it (or start another preflight, which supersedes it, or switch the source away from a local tree, or change the local path, which abandons it), then it stops before reading the next file (or, while the tree is still being listed, before entering the next directory), persists no findings (AC-0215), reports that it was cancelled rather than a raw error (an abandoned scan reports nothing), and a superseded or abandoned result never replaces the current one.
- **AC-0215** Given a preflight whose register write is already in progress, when it is cancelled, superseded or abandoned, then exactly one of the two wins, decided atomically at the last step before the write commits and without Cancel ever waiting on the write: if the cancel lands first, the write is rolled back inside its transaction, the register keeps the rows it held before the write, and the scan reports that it was cancelled; if the write has already claimed its commit, the cancel loses, the findings are committed, and the scan completes and reports its result, never a cancellation (an explicit Cancel then shows that result; a superseded or abandoned scan's result is still ignored by run id).
- **AC-0202** Given an investigation ended by owner death, whose Job row is `interrupted` and offers no Retry/Resume, when I clear finished jobs and confirm, then that row is removed — the confirmation counts it — while its task keeps its interrupted or outcome-unknown status and journal in investigation history; the row is never relabelled done or failed, and resumable interrupted jobs are kept.
- **AC-0203** Given an extraction that cites one (source, destination, label) relation from several sites, when it is ingested, then the stored edge keeps the props, tier and confidence of the last occurrence in deterministic extraction order and cites the evidence spans of every occurrence with that same tier, confidence and extractor — sorted, de-duplicated and bounded, with any spans beyond the bound counted on the edge rather than dropped silently — and whenever more than one occurrence is unioned its content hash is derived from their distinct fact hashes together with the canonical span list (spans beyond the bound and their count included), so adding, removing or moving any cited site changes the hash, a single-site relation keeps its extracted hash; a collapsed relation publishes without a primary-source receipt binding, since no single receipt attested it; and re-ingesting the same commit yields an identical edge and graph hash.
- **AC-0224** Given the Connect step, when it opens with a target kept from an earlier visit, then the target field is focused with its whole value selected, so typing replaces rather than appends to it; pressing Enter in the field submits the preflight exactly when the Preflight button is enabled (never for a blank target or without a backend); and when the preflight then ends without a report (an error such as a missing path, or a cancel), the Preflight step says it did not complete and demotes Run full recovery from the primary action — it stays available, since recovery scans the target itself and fails closed if it cannot read it.
- **AC-0705** Given a system manifest (`cartograph.system.toml`, or a directory holding one) on the Connect step, when I run Preflight, then each declared repository is listed in manifest order: every local member is resolved relative to the manifest's directory and shows its own detected languages, frameworks and adapter coverage (the same report a local preflight shows, eval lines pending recovery's AST proof, AC-0199), and every remote or managed entry (`https://`, `git@`, `file://`, or `owner/name` shorthand with nothing by that path beside the manifest) is shown as explicitly deferred to recovery and is never cloned or fetched by the preview (#1655).
- **AC-0706** Given a system-manifest preflight preview, when it completes, then it has written no source registration, no register finding and no graph fact, and states that it recorded nothing; a member that is already a registered source is read only through its registered-source access guard (as recovery reads it), never around it, and an unregistered member is read in place and stays unregistered. Recovery (`add_system`) still scans each repository itself and alone publishes the reconciled findings (AC-0209).
- **AC-0707** Given a running system-manifest preview, when it is cancelled, superseded by another preflight, abandoned by switching the source, or any member cannot be read (a missing directory, or a registered source its guard refuses), then the preview returns no members at all — never a partial list — the failure names the member it stopped at, a cancel reports that nothing was recorded, and a superseded preview's late result never replaces the current one.
- **Security:** Tokens stored in OS keychain; never logged; least-privilege App scopes.
- **Performance:** Shallow/sparse clone; 1 GB repo clones within bounded progress feedback.
- **Trace:** M0–M3 · `ingest`, `core-graph`, `app`, `ui` · — · T-0001..0003,T-0049..0050,T-0076..0078,T-0085,T-0094,T-0195..0198,T-0202..0203,T-0224,T-0705..0707

### US-0002 — Deterministic extraction of server-side facts (TS/JS/Python/Go/Java/Kotlin)
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** import/call graphs, endpoints, and data access extracted statically **so that** server facts are Confirmed without inference.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0004** Given a TS repo, when ingested, then endpoints (method/path/handler) are extracted via the framework adapter and marked Confirmed.
- **AC-0005** Given typed TS, when building call edges, then intra-procedural edges are complete and inter-procedural edges resolve where types permit.
- **AC-0006** Given any extracted fact, when inspected, then it carries provenance (file/span/commit, tier, extractor_id).
- **AC-0053** Given Python that import-proves FastAPI or Flask, when the repo is ingested, then Confirmed T0 File/Symbol/IMPORTS/CALLS facts plus literal Endpoint/HANDLES registrations are recovered with exact evidence, directory-proven imported calls are joined deterministically, lookalike framework calls are ignored, and the ingest summary reports Python separately.
- **AC-0054** Given Go that import-proves `net/http`, chi, or gin, when the repo is ingested, then Confirmed T0 File/Symbol/IMPORTS/CALLS facts plus literal Endpoint registrations are recovered with exact evidence, local-package calls and handlers are joined deterministically, unresolved handler expressions retain the Endpoint with an explicit HANDLES Gap, dynamic routes and lookalike registrations are ignored, files requiring an undeclared build target are excluded, application layer hints are honored (server, events or client; since AC-0434 Go also carries client calls), and the ingest summary reports Go separately.
- **AC-0079** Given a Java repository, when it is ingested, then classes/interfaces/enums/records and methods become Confirmed T0 Symbols with exact evidence spans, same-class and import-proven cross-file calls are joined deterministically repo-wide, a declared-package import whose target cannot be proven fails closed to an explicit Gap, calls on foreign-package imports assert nothing (the import itself closes over a proven external boundary, AC-0207), and the ingest summary reports Java separately.
- **AC-0080** Given Java that import-proves Spring Web annotations (named or wildcard `org.springframework.*` imports), when a `@RestController`/`@Controller` class is parsed, then `@{Get,Post,Put,Delete,Patch}Mapping` methods become Confirmed Endpoints with class-level `@RequestMapping` path composition and HANDLES edges to their handler methods; lookalike annotations without the proving import produce no endpoints.
- **AC-0095** Given a `.js`/`.jsx`/`.mjs`/`.cjs` repo, when ingested, then the same TypeScript-crate grammar recovers Confirmed T0 File/Symbol/IMPORTS/CALLS facts and Express/Next/React-Router endpoints and screens as it does for `.ts`/`.tsx` (no separate JS adapter, and Preflight/Settings report JavaScript as installed rather than a requestable planned adapter); an extensionless relative import resolves to the real file and symbol regardless of its actual source extension once the whole directory is known, rather than a phantom `.ts`-guessed placeholder.
- **AC-0098** Given a Kotlin repository (`.kt`/`.kts`), when it is ingested, then classes, interfaces, objects, data classes, enums, and functions (top-level, member, and extension) become Confirmed T0 Symbols with exact evidence spans; same-scope and import-proven cross-file calls (type/object receivers and imported top-level functions) are joined deterministically repo-wide, with a declared-package import whose target cannot be proven — or is declared ambiguously more than once — failing closed to an explicit Gap; an import of an extension function (overloads included), top-level property, type alias, annotation class (including the grammar's expression-shaped parse of an annotated one) or enum entry resolves to its declaring File when exactly one file declares it; a call constructing an imported class or data class that exactly one file declares, and that declares no `invoke`, is a constructor call, which (as for a same-file class) is not modeled and raises no Gap, while an ambiguous class, an object's or interface's `invoke` call (wherever the operator is declared), a class declaring `invoke`, or a member the source never declares (a generated `X.serializer()`), still gaps, and a same-named factory function still resolves; calls on foreign packages and unproven receivers asserting nothing (a foreign import itself closes over a proven external boundary, AC-0207); import-proven Spring Web annotations compose class-level `@RequestMapping` with `@{Get,Post,Put,Delete,Patch}Mapping` into Confirmed Endpoint/HANDLES facts (lookalikes without the proving import produce no endpoints, and a present-but-non-literal path — on the method or the class base — fails closed to an explicit route Gap instead of a Confirmed endpoint); Preflight/Settings report Kotlin as installed (`t0.adapter-kotlin`) rather than a requestable planned adapter; and the ingest summary reports Kotlin separately.
- **AC-0099** Given a TS/JS source with an `eval()` or `new Function()` whose code argument is compile-time-known — a string literal, a substitution-free template, or a same-file `const`/const-object member proven by binding (never a name coincidence; for `new Function` the last string argument is the body and earlier ones are parameter names), then the string content is parsed by the same extractor and its Symbols, CALLS, and event sites are emitted as Confirmed T0 facts carrying `via: "eval"` with evidence citing the argument's span at the eval site, the enclosing symbol gains a CALLS edge into the extracted code's entry symbol so flows cross the eval boundary, and recovery reconciles Preflight's `inline-eval` findings from that same extraction (adapter-supplied claims, so the textual scan and the AST proof never disagree; AC-0199/AC-0200): a fully proven site's finding closes, while a const-shaped-but-unproven argument downgrades to an explicit potential Gap and any other dynamic argument — including a shadowed local `eval`/`Function` binding, which is not the global and yields no facts — stays an Unsupported finding, never a guessed fact. Every eval-recovered fact's content hash is derived from its final namespaced form (a node's label and id, an edge's label and endpoints), so the same code at two eval sites never yields distinct facts sharing one hash, and re-ingest stays deterministic.
- **AC-0100** Given how Node/TS actually resolve modules, when the directory-wide reconciliation runs, then (a) an extensionless relative import matching no file resolves through the `index.<ext>` directory convention, (b) an explicit `.js`/`.jsx` relative specifier with no such file on disk resolves to its `.ts`/`.tsx` sibling (the NodeNext idiom), (c) a bare specifier matching a tsconfig/jsconfig `paths` alias or explicit `baseUrl` resolves to the real file with the deciding config cited as provenance evidence, and (d) a bare specifier naming a workspace package inside the ingested tree resolves through that package's `exports`/`module`/`main` map with its `package.json` cited; every resolution is a proof against files that exist — unmatched specifiers keep their explicit `mod:` node or placeholder, and external packages are never resolved into. The tsconfig parse is shared with the toolchain detector's stored facts (parse once, `adapters-fw::tsconfig`).
- **AC-1504** Given a relative import that names a barrel module (a directory `index.<ext>` or any file) which re-exports the imported name through `export * from`, `export { a as b } from`, or `export { a }` of an imported `a`, when the directory-wide reconciliation runs, then a call to that name (a function call, or a typed-member call on a class it names) links to the declaring file's symbol with `resolution: "re-export"`, `via` listing the barrels crossed in order, and each barrel's export statement cited in the edge's evidence, following nested barrels. An explicit named re-export shadows `export *`. When `export *` reaches different declarations, the name is never re-exported, or the statement is `export * as ns`, the explicit call Gap stays (#995 measurement).
- **AC-1514** Given a TypeScript typed member call whose receiver is proven to be a class `T` that declares no method `m` but inherits it through `extends` (a same-file class or a relatively imported one, followed through extension reconciliation and barrel re-exports, abstract or not), when directory-wide reconciliation runs, then the call's `CALLS` edge reaches the nearest base's instance method `B.m` as Confirmed T0 with resolution `inherited-member`, `inherits_via` listing the classes walked, and every `extends` clause relied on added to its evidence; a base imported from a package, a namespace member, a mixin call or a global binds nothing, a class declared twice in one file has no proven base, the walk stops on a cycle or after 16 bases, and any such call keeps its Gap, now also when the receiver is a same-file class (previously dropped without a Gap); methods of an `abstract class` are named `A.m` like any class's, so they can be reached at all (#258).
- **AC-1515** Given a TypeScript typed member call whose receiver is proven to be an interface `I` (an RPC `…Shape` proxy, an injected service), when directory-wide reconciliation runs, then the call gets one `CALLS` edge to `m` on each class that implements `I`, directly, through an interface that extends `I`, or as a subclass of an implementing class (the nearest declaration in its `extends` chain), with interface names bound by named or namespace imports of relative files or same-file declarations, and a receiver typed by a same-file top-level interface declared once queued the same way; each edge is InferredStrong T0 with resolution `interface-implementation`, naming `interface`, `implementation` and the number of `alternatives`, and cites the call and every `implements` and `extends` clause relied on; product code never reaches a class declared in test code, even one answering with a method it inherits from product code, test code reaches one only when nothing else implements `I`, and an interface with no implementation declaring `m` or more than 8 targets keeps its Gap (#258).
- **AC-1506** Given a repository whose `.cartograph/types.toml` lists exact package names (`read = ["@scope/name", "name"]`), when the directory-wide reconciliation runs, then a call `name()` on a named import, or `ns.name()` on a namespace import, of a listed package's root specifier that the importer's `package.json` declares is proven, and the proven calls of one caller into one package become one `CALLS` edge from the calling symbol to the package's external `mod:` boundary with `resolution: "dependency-types"`, the sorted `members`, their `declarations` (function, class, variable or namespace) and `declared_in` files in the same order, and the installed `package_version`, citing every call site and the manifest's dependency line once. Only the package's `package.json` and declaration files (`types`, `typings`, the `.` export's `types` condition, nested conditions included, else `index.d.ts`, else `@types/<name>`, following relative `export *` and `export { … } from`) are read, from `node_modules` between the manifest and the repository root, only inside the root after symlinks, and within 64 files and 8 MiB. Without the file nothing under `node_modules` is opened and the facts are unchanged. An unproven call (a default import, a subpath, `export =`, a type, a missing or ambiguous member, an unavailable package) stays omitted and no Gap is added. A malformed or symlinked list refuses the walk, and an untracked list keeps a commit snapshot `workdir` (#995, ADR-0078).
- **AC-0192** Given a proven Spring mapping in Java, when its path is a string literal or an array initializer of string literals (`@GetMapping({ "/vets" })`, `value = { "/a", "/b" }`), then each literal becomes its own Confirmed Endpoint composed with the class base, and an empty or absent path takes Spring's default; when the path on the method or the class-level `@RequestMapping` is present but not provably literal (a constant, a concatenation, any expression), then the mapping yields an explicit route Gap bound to its handler and no Endpoint — never a fabricated `/`.
- **AC-0199** Given a local preflight, when it scans TS/JS sources, then it performs no TS extraction (no AST parse, #243) and reports every textual `eval(`/`new Function(` line as an Unsupported `inline-eval` finding marked pending AST proof at recovery — none is claimed closed or downgraded, and none is dropped.
- **AC-0200** Given a completed local recovery, when its TS extraction has classified the eval sites, then the register's preflight findings for that repo are replaced by the textual scan of the captured bytes the claims were proven on (not the live tree), reconciled with those claims (AC-0099), so the register and the Preflight surface (which receives the reconciled report, potential-Gap lane included, with the recovery's summary) quote the post-recovery classification and no pending wording survives. The reconciliation scan runs only after the recovered facts are published, and its write goes through the per-repo fence only once the recovery's job has settled as completed, so a recovery that fails earlier, or is cancelled at any point before it completes (even during the reconciliation scan), leaves the pending findings in place; a preflight of that repo that began before the reconciliation cannot overwrite it afterwards, while a preflight started after recovery is newer and reports the lines as pending again until the next recovery (attested reuse of the recovery's proof is #438).
- **AC-0201** Given a TS/JS `eval()` or `new Function()` whose code argument is const-shaped (an identifier or member expression) but not provable to a literal, when the file is extracted, then the site becomes exactly one explicit `Gap` node — `attempted_tiers: ["T0"]`, a reason stating the argument could not be proven to a literal, and Gap-tier provenance citing the call site plus every unproven argument (inside proven eval code, the one outer-literal span instead, AC-0213) — owned through `DEPENDS_ON` by its enclosing symbol (or its File at top level), with an id keyed by repo, path and site offset so the same source yields the same Gap on every run and in the captured production lane; nothing about the site is asserted as Confirmed, and proven or dynamic sites emit no such Gap; a proven `eval()` whose code itself holds such a site is claimed const-unproven rather than covered, so Preflight keeps its potential-Gap finding, and a nested Gap's content hash derives from its final namespaced id.
- **AC-0209** Given a completed GitHub-backed (`add_repo`) or system-manifest (`add_system`) recovery, when its TS extraction has classified the eval sites, then each recovered repo's register preflight findings are replaced exactly as a local recovery's are (AC-0200): the textual scan of the captured bytes, reconciled with that repo's claims (AC-0099), scanned only after the recovered facts are published and written through the per-repo fence only once the recovery has completed. The recovery summary carries the reconciled report — a single report for a GitHub add, one per repo in manifest order for a system add. A recovery that fails or is cancelled, including a system recovery whose later repo fails after an earlier one recovered, writes no repo's findings.
- **AC-0214** Given a local, GitHub-backed or system-manifest recovery, when it publishes its job as completed, then its reconciliation's place in the per-repo fence (AC-0200, AC-0209) is already reserved and every source it wrote is ready and released, so a preflight of that repo started after the job shows done can read it and is always newer than the reconciliation: if that preflight persists first, the reconciliation writes nothing over it, and if it persists afterwards it is not refused. A preflight begun before the reservation still cannot overwrite the reconciled findings, and a recovery whose cancel wins the race still writes nothing.
- **AC-0216** Given preflight reports and recovery reports for the same repo that settle in any order — a preflight that overlapped a recovery, or a recovery re-run from Jobs → Retry — when they reach the Preflight surface, then the register order decides which is shown, not the order they arrive in: every register write carries its epoch; a preflight that a recovery reconciled past answers with the reconciled report the register holds instead of reporting a cancellation; a recovery's report replaces the shown report when no preflight began since the recovery started or when the shown report is the same repo's, and never when its reconciliation wrote nothing because a newer preflight's findings, or an overlapping recovery's newer reconciliation of that repo, were already there; the surface never shows an older report for a repo than the newest it has received. A retried recovery returns its reconciled report with the retried job row, applied under the same rule.
- **AC-0212** Given a proven TS/JS `eval()` or `new Function()` whose decoded code itself holds further eval sites, when the file is extracted, then the outer site's claim is the worst of its nested claims — dynamic over const-unproven over covered — so a nested dynamic site (for example `eval("eval(x + 1)")`) keeps the line's Unsupported `inline-eval` finding in Preflight even beside a const-unproven one, while the facts recovered from the proven outer code stay Confirmed T0.
- **AC-0220** Given a Vite or webpack config (`vite.config.*`, `webpack.config.*`) in the ingested tree, when bare JS/TS specifiers are resolved and classified, then the config is read as data and never executed: each literal `resolve.alias` key (object form, Vite's `{ find, replacement }` array form with a string `find`, and, in webpack configs only, the exact-match `key$` — a Vite key keeps a trailing `$` as spelled) whose replacement is provably a filesystem path — a relative string literal, `path.resolve`/`path.join` over `__dirname` and string literals, or `fileURLToPath(new URL('…', import.meta.url))` — governs files beneath the config's directory; a specifier it addresses (the key itself, or `key/…` unless exact) resolves to the real file only when the alias provably belongs to the exported config (the file's only `resolve.alias` object, inside the object given to `export default`, `module.exports =`, or `defineConfig(…)`), every same-directory config addressing it agrees on key and target, and the literal replacement proves a file — with the config's key span cited as provenance evidence — and otherwise stays an explicit Gap whose reason names the key and the config, never a Confirmed external package. Keys aliased to another package name, computed keys, regex `find`s, and non-literal replacements are skipped and keep their AC-0207 classification.
- **AC-0213** Given a fact recovered from proven `eval()` / `new Function()` code whose evidence cites several spans inside the decoded code (such as a nested const-unproven eval Gap and its `DEPENDS_ON` edge, which cite the inner call and each unproven argument), when the file is extracted, then — decoded offsets not being mapped back into the escaped literal — its provenance cites the outer string argument's span exactly once, never N identical copies, and the fact carries `unmapped_inner_spans` with the number of inner spans left unmapped; facts with a single evidence span carry no marker.
- **Security:** No code leaves device at T0.
- **Performance:** Incremental tree-sitter parse; re-parse only changed files by `content_hash`.

- **AC-0206** Given a proven Spring mapping in Java or Kotlin, when the class-level `@RequestMapping` base and the method mapping are composed, then segments meet at exactly one `/` (never `//`) and a trailing slash spelled in either literal is kept (a run of trailing slashes as one) — `"/api/"` with a bare `@GetMapping` is `/api/` and `"/api"` with `@GetMapping("/")` is `/api/` — so `/a` and `/a/` are distinct Confirmed Endpoints, matching Spring 6's default of no trailing-slash matching; a client fetch path that differs from a route only by a trailing slash never confirms against it.

- **AC-0557** Given a proven Spring controller in Kotlin, when a function carries a function-level `@RequestMapping` (#1605), then it becomes an Endpoint per proven verb and route. Its verbs are the union of the class-level and function-level `method =` restrictions, as Spring combines them (verb shorthands included). An empty union serves every method (`ALL`). Each proven `RequestMethod.X` (single, `[…]` or `arrayOf(…)`, qualified, imported or import-aliased) gives that verb, and a non-empty literal path array (`value = ["/a", "/b"]`) gives one route per element composed with the class base as AC-0206 does. An unprovable verb at either level (a constant, a spread, an imported enum entry, a lookalike `RequestMethod`) becomes a route Gap with method `?`, and a dynamic path becomes one route Gap naming its verbs, never a guessed method. A lookalike `RequestMapping` with no Spring import proves nothing.
- **Trace:** M1, M10 · `adapters-lang-kotlin` · — · T-0557

- **AC-0555** Given a proven Spring controller in Java, when a handler carries a method-level `@RequestMapping` (#1598), then it becomes an Endpoint per proven verb and route: its verbs are the union of the class-level and method-level `method =` restrictions (as Spring combines them, verb shorthands included), an empty union (`method` absent or `{}` at both levels) serves every method (`ALL`), each proven `RequestMethod.X` (single or array) gives that verb, and the path composes with the class base as AC-0206 does. An unprovable verb at either level (static import, constant, lookalike `RequestMethod`) or a dynamic path becomes one route Gap naming its verbs, never a guessed method, and a lookalike `RequestMapping` with no Spring import proves nothing. No proven handler is silently dropped.

- **AC-0207** Given any T0 adapter (TS/JS, Python, Go, Java, Kotlin, Terraform) whose edges name an endpoint no parsed declaration defines, when it closes over that endpoint, then the placeholder node is never left without provenance: it cites the edge that named it (evidence span, the producing extractor), states a human-readable `reason`, and records its `boundary` (ADR-0031). It is Confirmed only when the adapter proves the boundary — an **external** import the repository provably cannot provide (a JVM package that no Java or Kotlin source declares, lies beneath, or encloses, with every header readable — multiline file annotations included; in Go module mode a standard-library path or a module outside every repository module and local `replace`; a Python top-level name no repository directory or module shares; a bare JS/TS specifier matching no tsconfig alias or `baseUrl` path, literal bundler `resolve.alias` key (AC-0220), workspace package, `#` subpath import, or repository source directory, citing the `package.json` that declares it when one does; under a tsconfig that `extends` another, whose inherited aliases are not loaded, only a manifest-declared dependency) or an **internal** repository-declared package (a JVM package, a Go package directory — beneath a `go.mod` or a local `replace` target resolved against its declaring `go.mod`, whose complete directory and proving Go file resolve inside the repository root (never lexically or through a symlink outside it), and never beneath a module path some version-qualified `replace` names, since the selected version is unknown — a workspace package cited by its manifest). Every other endpoint — an in-system import with no unique declaration, an unresolved relative import, symbol, or resource, or any import when the adapter cannot decide — stays an explicit Gap carrying that reason, so external dependencies never count as system gaps. a placeholder named by several references is Confirmed only when every reference proves the same boundary (one unresolved reference keeps it a Gap), including across adapters in a polyglot ingest (one adapter's Gap is never overwritten by another adapter's Confirmed placeholder for the same id); a JVM header scan reads Unicode annotation names and arguments without panicking, and fails closed to unknown on anything it cannot parse; JVM imports whose complete target (a type, a top-level function, or a member whose Symbol the type defines) the repository declares exactly once resolve to the declaring File, and a prefix alone never does; a Python absolute import resolves to the recovered File it names from the repository root, the root that directory-proven calls already assume (`a.b` is `a/b.py` or the package `a/b/__init__.py`), only when exactly one of the two exists and no parent is a plain module (`a.py` without `a/__init__.py`) that Python would load instead; a relative JS/TS import of a non-source asset (stylesheet, image, JSON) names that file rather than a `.ts`-guessed phantom and is internal when the file exists and the import does not climb above the repository root; an observed (T1) state resolution supersedes a Terraform placeholder's Gap provenance; and the Gap register classes placeholder gaps by their reason rather than an internal edge label.
- **AC-0226** Given a Java or Kotlin import an adapter proves external (AC-0207), when its `IMPORTS` edge closes over a placeholder, then the edge and its `Module` node key on the imported package — found by the JVM capitalization convention (the first dotted segment that starts uppercase begins the imported type or member, everything before it is the package) — rather than the full imported type or static member, while the edge's `specifier` still names exactly what was imported; a package with no capitalized segment (a wildcard already stripped to its package) keeps its full path as its id; an internal or unresolved (Gap) import is unaffected and keeps its full path, since only a proven-external boundary is re-keyed; two imports proven external from the same package close over one shared `Module` node, and their edges collapse per the existing repeated-relation rule (AC-0195) rather than a new merge convention (ADR-0033).

- **AC-0230** Given a Java class or interface declaring more than one method (or constructor) under the same name, when it is ingested, then each overload's symbol id carries an erasure-level parameter signature suffix (`Owner.getPet(String)` vs. `Owner.getPet(int)`, ADR-0035) so every overload is a distinct Confirmed T0 Symbol instead of one collapsing onto the last extracted; a call site resolves to the one same-class or import-proven cross-file overload whose parameter count it matches (`min_arity`/variadic-aware), and resolves to an explicit Gap, never a guess, when no local overload accepts the call's argument count or more than one does; a static import naming a method with no parameter list keeps proving its boundary by name prefix rather than requiring an exact overload match.
- **AC-0284** Given Java that import-proves `jakarta.persistence`/`javax.persistence` annotations, when the repository is ingested, then each `@Entity` class becomes a Confirmed T0 DataEntity (`storage: jpa`) with its entity name, table (`@Table(name=)`, else the entity-name default), fields with their spelled types, the `@Id` field marked, and static/`@Transient` state left out, cited to the proving annotations; `@OneToMany`/`@ManyToOne`/`@OneToOne`/`@ManyToMany` fields become REFERENCES edges to the resolved target entity carrying cardinality, owning side, `mappedBy`, `@JoinColumn` and `@JoinTable` — several fields to the same target entity are one edge whose `relations` list holds every field's metadata sorted by field name and whose provenance cites every field's evidence; a Spring Data repository interface (`Repository`, `CrudRepository`, `PagingAndSortingRepository`, `JpaRepository` and the `List*` variants, import-proven) binds to its entity by a REFERENCES edge from the interface Symbol, and calls using the Spring Data naming vocabulary (`find*`/`get*`/`read*`/`query*`/`search*`/`stream*`/`count*`/`exists*`, `save*`/`delete*`/`remove*`) on a receiver typed as that repository become READS/WRITES edges from the calling method, the receiver being the declaration visible at the call site (a parameter of the enclosing method, constructor or lambda, a catch parameter, an enhanced-for variable, a try-with-resources resource, or a local declared before the call in an enclosing block — never a local declared later or in a sibling block) and every call site being its own cited occurrence; a computed entity or table name (its Gap connected to the class or entity it qualifies), an entity name declared by more than one declaration — two classes in different packages or the same class in two source roots, so one Gap replaces the merged entity, names every claiming FQN and makes links to or from it fail closed — a relation or repository entity that is not a recovered entity, a `this.x` receiver whose field the immediately enclosing type does not declare, and lookalike annotations without the JPA import fail closed to an explicit Gap or assert nothing, and data_model.md renders the entities, relations and access edges, listing every relation field of an aggregated relation.
- **AC-0847** Given TypeScript that import-proves `typeorm` decorators (never by name alone), when the repository is ingested, then each `@Entity` class becomes a Confirmed T0 DataEntity (`storage: typeorm`) with its table (the `@Entity` string argument or `name` option), its `extends` clause, and its own columns (`@Column`, `@PrimaryColumn`, `@PrimaryGeneratedColumn`, the date and version columns) with field name, spelled type, database column name, `nullable`, `unique` and primary key (from the decorator or `primary: true`); a `@ChildEntity` argument is its discriminator, never a table. Each relation decorator (`@ManyToOne`, `@OneToMany`, `@OneToOne`, `@ManyToMany`) is a relation field with cardinality and owning side, and the relations to one target entity are one `REFERENCES` edge citing every member; a target class declared in the same file or imported by a relative path resolves to that entity, and any other target (a path alias, a package, a non-literal) is a `REFERENCES` edge to an explicit Gap. The data model lists each entity's columns with their citations (#1443).
- **AC-0858** Given recovered `DataEntity` facts, when `data_model.md` is compiled, then its diagram draws only the entities and the `REFERENCES` / `MAPS_TO` relations between two entities (no `Symbol` reader or writer node), and an "Entities" section has one subsection per entity, under its name, with the table it is stored in and its citation, its columns (name, type, key, nullable), the entities it references and is referenced by with the citations of those relations, and the callables that write and read it by qualified name, grouped by the operations their relation records (the first eight per operation, then "and N more"); the node, column and relation tables with every id and citation follow unchanged (#1983).
- **AC-0298** Given Python routes declared on an import-proven Flask `Blueprint` or FastAPI `APIRouter`, when the repo is ingested, then each Endpoint's path composes the whole mount chain up to the application — the router's literal prefix with each literal mount prefix (`register_blueprint(url_prefix=…)` replaces a Blueprint's own prefix; `include_router(prefix=…)` composes with an APIRouter's), through nested routers and mounts in other files resolved through proven imports — and the evidence cites the decorator, the router, each mount and the application. A route is Confirmed only when every mount on its chain is made on a proven application or router of the same framework (module-level, imported, or assigned from the import-proven factory in the same function); a mount on a parameter annotated with the application type yields InferredStrong `mount_receiver_annotated`, any other receiver InferredStrong `mount_receiver_unproven` (dropped when another chain confirms the router), and a router with no observed mount InferredStrong `mount_not_observed`. A non-literal prefix, a `**options`/`*args` expansion that may carry it, or a cyclic or over-deep chain yields a Gap linked to the handler instead of a path; lookalike `route` decorators on unproven receivers assert nothing.
- **AC-0339** Given a Flask route handler in a file that import-proves `request` (`from flask import request`, or `flask.request` on an imported `flask`), when the repo is ingested, then its Endpoint's `request_inputs` lists the literal names the handler body reads: `query` from `request.args`, `form` from `request.form`, both from `request.values` (by `.get("k")`, `.getlist("k")`, `["k"]` or `"k" in …`), and `body` from `request.get_json(...)` / `request.json` read directly or through a local name bound to it (`data = request.get_json() or {}`) and read the same ways, with `scope: "handler_body"` and a Confirmed `prov` citing each read expression (at most 64 spans, else `evidence_truncated`). A key that is only assigned or deleted (`data["k"] = v`, `del data["k"]`) is not a read. A handler parameter or local that rebinds the imported name (`def view(request)`, `request = …`) means the handler is not reading Flask's `request`, and nothing is recorded for it. Nested functions, classes and lambdas are outside the handler, and helpers it calls are not followed. Any other read — a computed key, the body name passed, iterated or rebound, or `request` itself passed — adds a `partial` reason (`body_read_dynamically`, `body_name_rebound`, `query_read_dynamically`, `form_read_dynamically`, `request_passed`) instead of a guessed name; a handler that reads nothing, or a lookalike `request`, records none. `scripts/score-api-contract.mjs` scores those names against a contract document's `Input:` bullets (headers, including `Name: header …` bullets, and path parameters excluded) and reports recall, precision and per-endpoint differences (#721).
- **AC-0340** Given a route handler whose route resolves to Flask (directly or through its Blueprint mounts), when the repo is ingested, then its Endpoint's `responses` records what the handler's own `return` statements prove, each read as `body` or `(body, status[, headers])`. The response `kinds` are `json` for a call of Flask's `jsonify` or a dict literal, `html` for `render_template`, and `redirect` for `redirect`. The `statuses` come from a literal status, else 200 for `json` and `html`, and else a redirect's literal `code` (302 when absent). The top-level JSON `keys` are `jsonify` keyword names, or the literal string keys of one dict argument or dict literal. Each recorded `return` is cited under a Confirmed `prov` (at most 64 spans, else `evidence_truncated`), with `scope: "handler_body"`. A JSON body whose keys are built elsewhere (`jsonify(user_to_dict(u))`, `jsonify(**payload)`, a computed key) adds the `partial` reason `response_built_elsewhere`. Any other body adds `response_not_proven`, and a non-literal status adds `status_computed`. `abort(…)` and raised errors are not followed; repository decorators and dict-building helpers are followed per AC-0341. Nested scopes are skipped. A helper name the handler rebinds is not Flask's: a parameter, an assignment, a `from … import` or a nested `def` or `class`. A FastAPI route, whose tuples are not `(body, status)`, records no responses, even in a file that also imports Flask. Nested `prov` of both `responses` and `request_inputs` is retargeted to the current commit when a cached file is reused. `scripts/score-api-contract.mjs` scores top-level keys (recall and precision) and documented statuses (recall) against each documented endpoint's `Output:` section (#721).
- **AC-0341** Given a Flask route handler whose responses depend on repository functions, when the repo is ingested, then its Endpoint's `responses` follows them after every file is read, lists each followed function's Symbol under `followed`, sets `scope: "handler_body_and_followed"`, and cites each statement proven in the followed function. A bare `@decorator` on the handler, resolved to a same-file function or through a local import, is followed when it is a top-level function that takes one parameter (typed or not) and returns a wrapper it defines. Calling the wrapped function passes through, `return None` is not a response, and `x = check(…); … return x` (`x` bound once) adds `check`'s responses (an auth wrapper's 401/403/440), up to 4 follows deep; `x` bound any other way too (`except … as x`, `with … as x`, an import, a nested `def`) is not followed. When the check is unknown, `x = check(…)` adds `response_not_proven`. Decorators outside the repository, decorator calls (`@limiter.limit(…)`) and functions not shaped as a wrapper are not followed. A dict is built by a local bound once to a dict literal and extended only with literal-key subscript assignments (`ret["k"] = …`); any other binding or use of it (an alias, an argument, a method call, `del`, a closure) leaves it unproven. Its keys are recorded for `return ret` and `jsonify(ret)`. They are also recorded for `jsonify(helper(…))` and `jsonify(**helper(…))` when every return of the repository `helper` is such a dict or a dict literal; otherwise the call adds `response_built_elsewhere`. Keyword names beside such splats (`jsonify(alias=…, **helper(…))`, or `**{…}` with literal keys) are read with the helper's keys (#1998). The followed evidence is retargeted to the current commit when a cached file is reused (#940).
- **AC-0399** Given an AC-0340 Flask handler, when the repo is ingested, then a returned `str` (a string literal, f-string or implicit concatenation) is a `text` response, 200 unless a literal status is given, and a call of Flask's `make_response(body[, status[, headers]])` (imported and not rebound) is read as `(body, status)` with its first argument classified by the AC-0340 rules. The call may be returned directly or through a local bound once by a plain assignment and otherwise only returned (alone or as a tuple's first element) or given cookies or headers (`x.set_cookie(…)`, `x.delete_cookie(…)`, `x.headers[…] = …`). A returned tuple's own status overrides the one given to `make_response`. Keyword or splatted `make_response` arguments, a second binding, or any other use of the local (a `status_code` assignment, passing it to a call) stays `response_not_proven` (#1182).
- **AC-0410** Given a Flask application with import-proven Flask-Admin, when an `Admin` assigned once to a name is given an app (constructor or `init_app`) and views by inline constructor (`index_view=`, `add_view`, `add_views`), then each `@expose` method of a registered repository view class — its own or a base's, unless a class earlier in its C3 method resolution order redefines the name — is a Flask `Endpoint` (`mount: "flask_admin"`) named `<endpoint>.<method>` with the path Flask-Admin 1.5.7 composes (`endpoint=` else `admin`, the model name or the class name lower-cased; `url=` absolute, relative under `admin.url`, or `admin.url/<endpoint>`) and its methods. It is Confirmed on a recovered Flask application and InferredStrong on an annotated parameter or other expression; responses read the method as an AC-0340 handler; `auth` is `unknown` with `flask_admin_access_check:<class defining is_accessible>` or `flask_admin_default_allows_all`. Library-generated routes (model views, a default or non-overriding index view) are one Gap per view, and a non-literal `endpoint`/`url`/rule/methods, an unrecovered or unmodelled base, a hierarchy Python's C3 linearization refuses, a view or base name not bound once to the import or module-level class it names (or shadowed where it is used), a repository `__init__`/`_get_endpoint`/`_get_view_url`/`create_blueprint` override, an index view not passed as a constructor, a repeated `init_app`, an `Admin` without an app, or a view registered on a receiver not proven to be an `Admin` is a named Gap, never a guess. A rebound `expose` or `Admin` is not Flask-Admin's (#1193).
- **AC-0415** Given AC-0410's registered Flask-Admin views, when every exact `flask-admin` pin in the repository's root `uv.lock`, `poetry.lock`, `pdm.lock` or `requirements*.txt`/`requirements*.lock` names a release whose route tables were checked against its source (1.5.7, 1.5.8), then a model view gains `BaseModelView`'s routes (`index_view` `/`, `create_view` `/new/`, `edit_view` `/edit/`, `details_view` `/details/`, `delete_view` `/delete/`, `action_view` `/action/`, `export` `/export/<export_type>/`, `ajax_lookup` `/ajax/lookup/`, `ajax_update` `/ajax/update/`) and an index view that does not define `index` (including `Admin`'s default, with its `endpoint`) gains `index` at `/`, under the view's composed URL and named `<endpoint>.<method>`. Each is a Flask `Endpoint` (`mount: "flask_admin"`, `handler_origin: "library"`) whose handler is the library method, an external boundary, citing the view, the site and every pin; it is at most InferredStrong, since the table is release knowledge, not source. A method a repository class earlier in the view's C3 order than the library base routing it defines is that class's: its own `@expose` is routed, and without one there is no route; one defined only after that base (`class V(ModelView, LateMixin)`) leaves the library route. An exact pin may carry `--hash=` options and `\` continuations. Package tables are read whole, whatever their key order, indentation, comments or quoted keys; a non-plain `name` or `version` is inexact. No pin keeps AC-0410's Gap. An inexact requirement, an unchecked release, a lockfile whose escaped key or header, or inline `package` array, could hide a pin, or an eligible root lockfile that cannot be read (a symlink, too large, not UTF-8) is a named Gap (`flask_admin_version_not_exact:<file>`, `flask_admin_version_not_modelled:<version>`, `flask_admin_lockfile_syntax_not_modelled:<file>`, `flask_admin_lockfile_not_inspected:<file>`) (#1203).
- **AC-0421** Given AC-0410's and AC-0415's Flask-Admin endpoints, when in the view's C3 order the first class defining `is_accessible` is a repository view class whose method is a single `return` (docstring allowed) of import-proven, unrebound `flask_login.current_user.is_authenticated` (the file's only binding of the name a single module-level import) or of an `and` chain with it as an operand, the first class defining `inaccessible_callback`, if any, never returns `None` (no earlier statement returns or yields, and its last returns a string, an import-proven `flask` `redirect`/`abort`/`make_response`/`render_template` call, `x or` one of these, or a conditional whose branches both are), no repository class in the chain defines `_handle_view` or `_run_view`, every repository class defining one of these four hooks is itself a view class, every exact `flask-admin` pin is a checked release and every exact `flask-login` pin is 0.3 to 0.6, and no repository code replaces `flask_login.current_user` or a Flask response helper or writes an `anonymous_user` attribute or a view hook attribute (by an assignment, `for`, comprehension, `with … as` or `del` target) or names either in a string (as any `setattr`, `object.__setattr__` or `__dict__` key would), then each of the view's exposed and library routes has `auth: required` (`authenticated: true`, basis `flask_admin_is_accessible_requires_login`, its guard the `is_accessible` symbol, InferredStrong, citing the check, the callback's response and every pin, and recording the assumption that no computed or escaped name sets `anonymous_user`). A hook bound in any form (a loop or `with` target, an import, a `match` capture) belongs to that class and is proven only as its single plain method. Otherwise `auth` stays `unknown` with `flask_admin_access_check:<class>` followed by the first unproven condition (for example `flask_admin_is_accessible_not_login_check:<class>`, `flask_admin_inaccessible_callback_may_return_none:<class>`, `flask_admin_view_overrides:_handle_view:<class>`, `flask_admin_hook_outside_view_class:<class>`, `flask_login_version_not_pinned`, `flask_login_anonymous_user_replaced`, `flask_admin_view_hook_replaced`) (#1216). - **AC-0433** Given a Java call whose argument count matches more than one same-class or import-proven cross-file overload, when it is ingested, then each string, integer, long, boolean, character or `null` literal and each lambda or method-reference argument rules out the overloads whose parameter at that position it provably cannot bind to in any of Java's applicability phases (a string literal binds only to `String` or a supertype of it, an `int` literal never narrows to `short`/`byte`/`char`, a lambda never binds to a primitive or array, and is never excluded by a class name because a project type may shadow `java.lang` names, a type variable accepts anything, and a varargs tail is never narrowed); the call resolves to the one surviving overload with `overload_resolution: literal-argument-kinds` on its CALLS edge, and stays the existing ambiguous-overload Gap when zero or several survive, with no most-specific selection.
- **AC-0434** Given a Go file that imports `net/http` under any name, when its application layers (server, events or client) are ingested, then each call of `Get`/`Head`/`Post`/`PostForm` on the import or on its `DefaultClient`, and each `NewRequest(method, url, …)`/`NewRequestWithContext(ctx, method, url, …)`, becomes a fetch site owned by its enclosing function or method (else its file). The verb functions give their method (`PostForm` is POST). A request's method is an `http.MethodX` constant, a plain-word literal as written, GET for `""`, or otherwise `?`. A URL is a literal only as exact text (a raw string, or an interpreted string with no escape), and anything else is computed. The import name proves the package only when the top-level declaration around the call never uses it except as a package qualifier: a parameter, local, function-literal or type of that name yields nothing, and so do lookalike packages. A site stitches like any other fetch site, and a method-less (`ANY`) route matches every method, so a Go `HandleFunc("/x", …)` route can be fetched; a HEAD site also matches a Go `net/http` GET route, which `ServeMux` serves for HEAD (chi and gin GET routes do not); a method route on the same path makes the match ambiguous, which yields a cited Gap (#1279).
- **AC-0435** Given a Java product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each call of a `RestTemplate` verb method on a receiver proven to be `org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate` becomes a fetch site owned by its enclosing method or constructor. The verbs and their methods: `getForObject`/`getForEntity` GET, `headForHeaders` HEAD, `postForObject`/`postForEntity`/`postForLocation` POST, `put` PUT, `patchForObject` PATCH, `delete` DELETE and `optionsForAllow` OPTIONS. `exchange`/`execute` with at least four arguments take an `HttpMethod.X` constant proven by its single-type import or fully qualified spelling, whose first segment no visible variable, declared type or imported type obscures, else `?`. The receiver is proven when it is a non-anonymous `new RestTemplate(…)` or the local, parameter or field visible at the call declares the type: a bare or `this.` field of the immediately enclosing named type only, so anonymous class bodies, inherited and outer-class fields, pattern bindings and untyped lambda parameters prove nothing. The type is proven by its fully qualified spelling, or by its simple name under a single-type import in a file that declares no type or type parameter of that name; wildcard imports and lookalike packages prove nothing. The URL is the first argument, a literal only as an exact string literal (URI templates such as `/users/{id}` included), and anything else is computed. RestTemplate sites are never joined to a Retrofit base URL, and they stitch like any other fetch site (#1283).
- **AC-0437** Given a Java product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each abstract method of an interface whose `@FeignClient` annotation is proven, and which carries a proven Spring mapping annotation, becomes a fetch site owned by the method with the entry `Interface.method`. The annotation is proven by its fully qualified spelling, or by its simple name under a single-type import in a file that declares no type of that name; wildcard and lookalike imports, classes, default and static methods and unproven mappings prove nothing. A mapping annotation is proven as Spring endpoints prove it, and additionally only when no type the file declares shadows its simple name, or when it is spelled by its exact fully qualified name. A mapping annotation or `RequestMethod` proven only by a wildcard import is settled at the directory join: a product type of that name in the file's package obscures it, so the mapping yields no site and the method becomes `?`. `@Get/Post/Put/Delete/PatchMapping` give their method; `@RequestMapping` gives GET without a `method`, the method of one `RequestMethod.X` proven as Spring endpoints prove it, else `?`. The path is the client's `path` prefix (one leading `/`, no trailing `/`) joined to the mapping path with a leading `/`; a dynamic or multi-valued path, a non-literal prefix, or a class-level `@RequestMapping`, which OpenFeign rejects, makes it computed, unless that mapping is proven only by a wildcard import and a same-package type obscures it. The base joins by concatenation: a literal `url`, trailing `/` removed and `http://` prepended when it does not start with `http`; a `${…}` placeholder or non-literal `url` as computed; and without a `url` the load-balanced service name, computed. A computed base resolves only through a system-manifest declaration, else the Gap names the client entry (#1287).
- **AC-0438** Given a Java product source whose Spring endpoint annotation (`@RestController`/`@Controller`, the class `@RequestMapping` base or the method mapping) is proven only by a wildcard import of its exact Spring package, when the repository also declares a product type of that simple name in the file's package, then the route is an explicit Gap citing the shadowing (also when its path is dynamic), not a Confirmed Endpoint, because a same-package type obscures an on-demand import (JLS §6.4.1). Any one proven stereotype makes a controller, so a directly proven stereotype keeps the Endpoint and wildcard-only stereotypes fail closed only when every one is shadowed. A named import, the exact qualified spelling, a wildcard in another package and a test-source type keep the Endpoint, and a qualified spelling of any other type (`@com.acme.GetMapping`) proves nothing. Single-file extraction settles wildcard-proven Spring and JAX-RS endpoints against the file's own types instead of dropping them (#1288).
- **AC-0441** Given a Java product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each `WebClient` request chain `<client>.<verb>().uri(<url>, …)` whose client is proven to be `org.springframework.web.reactive.function.client.WebClient` becomes a fetch site. The client may be a local, parameter or own-type field typed by the fully qualified name or by its single-type import in a file declaring no type of that name, or an inline `WebClient.create(…)` or `WebClient.builder()…build()`. The verb is `get`/`head`/`post`/`put`/`patch`/`delete`/`options`, or `method(HttpMethod.X)` with an unobscured import-proven constant, else `?`. The URL is the first `uri` argument as an exact literal, else computed. The base is the inline `create` or last `baseUrl` literal; a computed argument, a field-held client, or a builder with a `uriBuilderFactory` (which overrides `baseUrl` in either order) or an `apply` callback is a runtime base the stitcher reports as undeclared; an inline client without a base, or a request URL that is an absolute literal, keeps the URL whole. A literal base joins its path by concatenation, as Spring appends the request path and collapses a doubled `/`: the base keeps a trailing `/` before a relative literal path (`/api/` + `orders` is `/api/orders`) and drops it before a rooted or computed path (`/api/` + `/orders` is `/api/orders`), while a base without the slash concatenates as written (`/api` + `orders` is `/apiorders`) (#1380). Wildcard imports, lookalike or file-local `WebClient` types, a variable obscuring `WebClient`, untyped receivers, wrong-arity verb calls and test sources yield nothing (#1297).
- **AC-0475** Given a Java product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each inline request builder chain `HttpRequest.newBuilder([uri])…build()` whose root is a static call on `java.net.http.HttpRequest` (fully qualified, or by its single-type import in a file declaring no type of that name, with no visible variable obscuring it) becomes a fetch site without a base. Every link must be an `HttpRequest.Builder` member called with its arity (`uri`, `header`, `headers`, `setHeader`, `timeout`, `version`, `expectContinue`, `GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `DELETE`, `HEAD`, `method`), else the chain proves nothing. The last method link gives the method, `GET` by default; `method("<NAME>", body)` gives its literal only as an RFC 9110 token without lowercase letters (such as `PATCH` or `M-SEARCH`), else `?`. The last `uri(…)` gives the URL, else the `newBuilder` argument; it is a literal only as `URI.create("<lit>")` or `new URI("<lit>")` on a proven `java.net.URI`, else computed. A two-argument `newBuilder`, a builder held in a variable and built later, a chain without a URI, a foreign or obscured `HttpRequest` and test sources yield nothing (#1395).
- **AC-0476** Given a Java product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each inline request builder chain `new Request.Builder()…build()` whose root constructs `okhttp3.Request.Builder` without a class body (fully qualified, through the single-type import of `okhttp3.Request`, or of `okhttp3.Request.Builder` as `new Builder()`, in a file declaring no type of that name) becomes a fetch site without a base. Every link must be a `Request.Builder` member called with its arity (`url`, `header`, `addHeader`, `removeHeader`, `headers`, `tag`, `cacheControl`, `get`, `head`, `delete`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `method`), else the chain proves nothing. The last method link gives the method, `GET` by default; `method("<NAME>", body)` gives its literal only as an RFC 9110 token without lowercase letters, else `?`. The last `url(…)` gives the URL; it is a literal only as a string literal argument, else computed. A builder held in a variable and built later, a chain without `url`, a constructor argument, a foreign, file-declared or unimported `Request`, a qualified spelling captured by a declared or imported `okhttp3` type and test sources yield nothing (#1405).
- **AC-0477** Given a Kotlin product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each inline request builder chain `Request.Builder()…build()` whose root calls the `okhttp3.Request.Builder` constructor with no arguments (fully qualified, or through a named or aliased import of `okhttp3.Request` or `okhttp3.Request.Builder` in a file declaring nothing of that name, with no visible binding of the first segment) becomes a fetch site without a base. Every link must be a `Request.Builder` member called positionally with its arity and no trailing lambda (`url`, `header`, `addHeader`, `removeHeader`, `headers`, `tag`, `cacheControl`, `get`, `head`, `delete`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `method`), whose name the file neither declares nor imports as a function and whose literal arguments the member can take, else the chain proves nothing. The last method link gives the method, `GET` by default; `method("<NAME>", body)` gives its literal only as an RFC 9110 token without lowercase letters, else `?`. The last `url(…)` gives the URL; it is a literal only as a pure string literal, else computed. A builder held in a variable and built later, a chain without `url`, a constructor argument, a foreign, file-declared or unimported `Request`, a qualified spelling captured by a file-level `okhttp3` declaration or import, any file with a wildcard import (whose extensions may be called) and test sources yield nothing (#1409).
- **AC-0478** Given a Java product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each `new HttpGet|HttpHead|HttpPost|HttpPut|HttpPatch|HttpDelete|HttpOptions|HttpTrace(<url>)` whose type is a request class of Apache HttpClient 4 (`org.apache.http.client.methods`) or 5 (`org.apache.hc.client5.http.classic.methods`), through its single-type import in a file declaring no type of that name or a fully qualified spelling that no declared or imported `org` type captures, called with exactly one argument and no class body, becomes a fetch site without a base whose method is the class's. The URL is a literal as a string literal or a proven `URI.create("<lit>")`/`new URI("<lit>")`, else computed. A no-argument constructor, an anonymous subclass, a wildcard-imported, foreign, file-declared or unimported class, another class of the package and test sources yield nothing (#1411).
- **AC-0471** Given a Kotlin product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each call `HttpGet|HttpHead|HttpPost|HttpPut|HttpPatch|HttpDelete|HttpOptions|HttpTrace(<url>)` whose called name is proven to be a request class of HttpClient 4 (`org.apache.http.client.methods`) or 5 (`org.apache.hc.client5.http.classic.methods`) becomes a fetch site with the class's method and no base: a named import or alias in a file declaring nothing of that name, or a fully qualified spelling (also spaced) in a file with no wildcard import and no file-level `org` declaration or import, where no visible binding obscures the first segment. The call takes exactly one positional argument and no trailing lambda; the URL is a literal only as a pure string literal, else computed. No-argument constructors, named arguments, an unimported or foreign class, a file-declared lookalike, a bound first segment and test sources yield nothing (#1420).
- **AC-0479** Given a Kotlin product source (not a JVM test source set), when its application layers are ingested, then each call `<client>.<verb>(<url>)`, optionally with a trailing request block, whose receiver is proven to be `io.ktor.client.HttpClient` (a named-imported or fully qualified typed or constructed local, parameter or property, or `this.x`) and whose function resolves to an `io.ktor.client.request` extension (a named import or an alias keeping the imported verb, when the file declares no function of that name; a wildcard import of the package proves nothing, since a same-package extension in another file wins over it) becomes a fetch site.
