The URL is the single positional argument, a literal only as a pure string literal. A URL that is not an absolute literal joins a runtime base named by the client expression (a `defaultRequest` the file does not show), which the stitcher reports as an undeclared runtime base, resolved as an RFC 3986 reference. Block-only calls, named arguments, more than one argument, an unproven or foreign receiver, another package's function of a verb's name, a wildcard-only import and test sources yield nothing; a site with a request block cites the whole call including the block (#1416).
- **AC-0495** Given a Kotlin product source whose RestTemplate, WebClient, OkHttp, Ktor or Apache HttpClient request lies outside any function, in a property or top-level initializer (not an accessor, `init` block, constructor parameter default, property delegate or enum entry), when its application layers are ingested, then the request becomes a fetch site owned by its file (no symbol and no entry), with the same proof, method and URL reading as inside a function; a request inside a function stays owned by its innermost function (#1426).
- **AC-0442** Given a Kotlin product source whose Spring endpoint annotation (`@RestController`/`@Controller`, the class `@RequestMapping` base or the function mapping) is proven only by a star import, when the repository's product sources declare a same-package top-level type (class, interface, object, enum or annotation class) or type alias of that name, in Kotlin or, in a mixed JVM tree, in Java (Kotlin resolves explicit imports, then the package, then star imports), then that route becomes a route Gap whose reason names the shadowing, also when its path is dynamic. A controller stays proven while any proven stereotype is unshadowed, and a directly proven one dominates. A named import or the fully qualified spelling `{package}.{name}` proves directly, and any other qualified spelling proves nothing. Test source sets never obscure a product import, a `private` top-level Kotlin declaration obscures only within its own file, and default-package declarations obscure default-package files. Single-file extraction settles against the file's own declarations, and cached files keep their pending routes retargeted to the current commit (#1288).
- **AC-0443** Given a Kotlin source that uses `dynamic` or `suspend` as a name (a declared function, property or parameter, possibly after a comment, or a reference or call such as an expression body ending in `suspend`), or `dynamic` anywhere as a word, when it is parsed, then the soft keyword that the grammar lexes as a keyword no longer drops the declaration or, under stacked class annotations, the whole class. Its Symbols and endpoints are recovered with exact spans and source text, and a real `suspend` modifier and a `dynamic` type still parse as before (#1302).
- **AC-0470** Given a Kotlin source whose import list ends with a wildcard import (`import io.ktor.client.request.*`) directly followed by a `suspend` declaration, when it is parsed, then the soft-keyword pass keeps `suspend` as a modifier, because a `*` right after `.` ends a star import and opens no expression, so the declaration keeps its Symbol and facts; a `*` operator before `suspend` still marks a respelled name (#1417).
- **AC-0496** Given a Python repository that import-proves `aiohttp` as AC-0424 proves `requests`/`httpx` (no repository module or package named `aiohttp`, no patch that may mock the call), when its server layers are ingested, then `aiohttp.request(method, url)` becomes a fetch site read like `requests.request`, and a name bound once to `aiohttp.ClientSession(…)` under AC-0425's rules has its verb methods and `request(method, url)` read as fetch sites. Its `base_url`, the first positional argument or the `base_url=` keyword, read exactly as httpx's, becomes the site's base, and a URL is resolved against it as `ClientSession._build_url` does with `URL.join`, an RFC 3986 reference resolution (`https://h/api/` + `users` → `https://h/api/users`; `/users` → `https://h/users`); a URL with scheme and host stands alone. aiohttp has no module verb functions, so `aiohttp.get(…)` yields nothing, nor do splatted, rebound, patched or shadowed sessions (#1432).
- **AC-0444** Given a Kotlin source with a block comment (nested or not, possibly containing `//`) on the line before a declaration, including after an expression-bodied function, when it is parsed, then the declaration and its class are kept with exact spans: block comments are blanked to spaces in the same-length parse copy (line breaks kept), while string literals that look like comments are untouched (#1306).
- **AC-0456** Given a Kotlin source, when its application layers are ingested, then each call of a `RestTemplate` verb method on a receiver proven to be `org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate` becomes a fetch site owned by its innermost enclosing function, with the same verbs and methods as AC-0435, Spring's reified Kotlin extensions of those names included: a call with type arguments only when the file imports that extension from `org.springframework.web.client` by name, or by wildcard when no named import binds that name, and no call of a verb's name in a file that declares a function of that name (a same-package extension in another file is not seen). The receiver is proven by a typed local, parameter, constructor or class property, or `this.x`, whose stated type (or `RestTemplate(…)` initializer) is the fully qualified name or a name its named import binds to that type in a file that declares nothing of that name, or by an inline `RestTemplate()`. Star imports, another `RestTemplate`, an untyped or shadowing binding, an enclosing type parameter of the type's name, and a visible binding of a qualified constructor's first segment prove nothing. No Retrofit base joins these sites. `exchange`/`execute(url, method, …)` read an import-proven `HttpMethod.X` second argument; with four or more arguments an unproven method is `?`, and with two or three no site is made. A call with named arguments or a trailing lambda is not read, nor a one-argument `getForObject<T>(url)`, which the grammar parses as comparisons. The URL is the first argument, a literal only as a pure string literal (URI templates included, `$` templates not); any other URL is Computed.
- **AC-0457** Given a Kotlin source, when its application layers are ingested, then each `WebClient` request chain `<client>.<verb>().uri(<url>, …)` whose client is proven to be `org.springframework.web.reactive.function.client.WebClient` becomes a fetch site owned by its innermost enclosing function, with the entry `Type.function`. The client is proven as for AC-0456 by a typed local, parameter, constructor or class property, or `this.x` (an initializer alone proves nothing, since `WebClient` is an interface), or by an inline `WebClient.create(…)` or `WebClient.builder()…build()` whose qualifier no visible binding obscures. The verb is `get()`/`head()`/`post()`/`put()`/`patch()`/`delete()`/`options()`, or `method(HttpMethod.X)` with an import-proven constant, else `?`. The URL is the first `uri` argument, a literal only as a pure string literal, else Computed. The base is an inline `create("<literal>")` argument or the last `baseUrl("<literal>")` of an inline builder; a held client, or a builder with `uriBuilderFactory` or a lambda-configured link (`apply { … }`), has a runtime base named by the client expression; an absolute literal URL takes no base. No Retrofit base joins these sites. A builder chain proves a client only when every link is a `WebClient.Builder` member called in one of its shapes (argument count, a trailing lambda only for a functional parameter, and no non-string literal for a `String` parameter) and not declared or imported as a function by the file. A `uri(<url>, <function>)` call has a runtime URL. Named `uri` arguments, star imports, another `WebClient` and type parameters of its name prove nothing.
- **AC-0918** Given Go that registers routes on a router it receives (a parameter or `var` typed `*http.ServeMux`, `chi.Router`/`*chi.Mux` or `*gin.Engine`/`*gin.RouterGroup`, import-proven), when the repo is ingested, then those routes are Endpoints, the receiver resolved lexically so a shadowing local or a same-named local elsewhere proves nothing; a route's handler resolves when it is a func literal (its own Symbol `Enclosing/func@byte`, defined in the file and owning the calls inside it, a bound closure's too), a local bound once to a func literal, a method value on the enclosing method's receiver (`s.list` to `Service.list` in the package), or a wrapper call (the handler it takes, kept as `via` on the edge, else the wrapper itself); a call `s.save()` on the receiver is a call to that method; any other handler, and a package handler no Symbol answers, is a Gap with the route kept, its facts carrying the current commit when a reused file is re-ingested (#2006, #2007).
- **AC-0924** Given a repository with a `vercel.json` at its root (a regular file the root `.gitignore` does not ignore; a symlinked or ignored one is not read) and Go files under its top-level `api/` directory, when it is ingested, then each such file (not a `_test.go`, `_`- or `.`-prefixed path) that exports exactly one plain function taking `(http.ResponseWriter, *http.Request)` from `net/http` is an `Endpoint` (method `ANY`, framework `vercel`) at the path Vercel serves it at (`api/auth/login/index.go` at `/api/auth/login`, `api/x.go` at `/api/x`) that `HANDLES` that function; each `vercel.json` rewrite whose destination is such a path is an `Endpoint` at its source handled by the same function and marked `rewrite_of` (`(a|b)` one path per alternative, `:name` as `{name}`, a final `(.*)` or `:name*` as `{path...}`), citing the rewrite in `vercel.json`; a rewrite onto `/api/…` that no function serves, or whose source uses other pattern syntax, is a Gap, and one onto a static file is not a route. These endpoints group into Capabilities by function package like other Go routes (#2010).
- **AC-0926** Given a Vercel function (AC-0924) whose paths hold other HTTP endpoints of the same repository (a `net/http` mux it serves, e.g. `GET /api/web/session` under the rewrite `/api/web/(.*)`), below at least one literal segment, with no more specific function path for them, and registered in the function's own directory or in a package of the module its file imports (the Go adapter records that package's directory as `package_dir` on the `IMPORTS` edge; an outside import whose path merely ends like a local directory does not count), when Capabilities are derived and D1 is exported, then that function's endpoints are a front door: they are grouped into no Capability (the routes behind them are the operations), and D1 §1.3 states how many endpoints are such front doors and how many routes they pass requests on to; a root catch-all (`/{path...}`) and a function whose file does not import the routes' package (a shell serving `/devices/{id}` as a page) front nothing (#2010).
- **AC-0927** Given a repository with a root `vercel.json` (read as in AC-0924) that is not a Next.js project (no `next` in the nearest manifest) and JavaScript or TypeScript files under its top-level `api/` directory (`.ts`/`.tsx`/`.js`/`.jsx`; not a declaration file and not under a `_`- or `.`-prefixed segment), when it is ingested, then each file's default export is an `Endpoint` (method `ALL`, framework `vercel`, with its `language` and `handler_sym`) and each exported `GET`/`POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`/`HEAD`/`OPTIONS` function one for that method, at the path Vercel serves the file at (`api/users/[id].ts` at `/api/users/:id`, `index` at its directory, `[...x]` a prefix), handled by that function; an export bound any other way is a cited Gap; each `vercel.json` rewrite onto such a function at a fixed path is an `Endpoint` at its source marked `rewrite_of` (`{path...}` as a `/*` prefix); and the Go adapter no longer reports a rewrite onto such a file as a missing function (#2010).
- **AC-1023** Given a Kotlin product source using the Ktor server routing DSL, when it is ingested, then each verb builder (`get`, `post`, `put`, `delete`, `patch`, `head`, `options`) with a trailing handler lambda inside a `routing { }` block, or in the body of a `Route` (or `Routing`) extension function, becomes a Confirmed Endpoint (framework `ktor`) whose path composes every enclosing `route("…") { }` prefix as AC-0206 composes Spring paths, and whose handler is a `route handler` Symbol for the lambda (`…#<owner>/<verb>@<offset>`) that owns the calls inside it. Each builder name and the `Route` receiver are proven by a named import from `io.ktor.server.routing` (or Ktor 1's `io.ktor.routing`) or by a star import of it; a file that declares a function of that name proves nothing, and a star proof whose name the repository declares in the file's own package becomes a route Gap naming the shadowing. A `Route` extension group takes the prefix of each zero-argument call of its name inside routing, across files at the directory join, transitively through other groups. A path argument that is not one pure string literal, a prefix that is computed or comes from an unproven `route`, a group no routing block mounts, and a group whose name two files declare become route Gaps, never a guessed path. Without the routing import, and in a test source set (AC-0431), nothing is asserted.
- **AC-1025** Given a Kotlin product source using Android Room, when it is ingested, then each `@Entity` class becomes a Confirmed `DataEntity` table (`storage: "sql"`, framework `room`, `data:<repo>@sql:<table>`), named by its literal `tableName` or else its class name (folded to lower case in the id), whose columns are its `val`/`var` constructor and body properties, each named by a literal `@ColumnInfo(name = …)` where there is one (`@Ignore`, `@Embedded` and `@Relation` properties are not columns). In a `@Dao` type, a function's `@Query` that is one string literal (raw `"""` strings included, no template) is read by the shared SQL reader, and each table it reads or writes is a `READS` or `WRITES` edge (InferredStrong, resolution `sql-literal`, with the operations). `@Insert`, `@Update`, `@Delete` and `@Upsert` are Confirmed `WRITES` edges (resolution `room-entity-parameter`) to the entity that their first parameter's type names, through `List`, `Set`, `Collection`, `Iterable`, `Array` or `vararg`. Tables and types resolve against the repository's entities at the directory join. Each Room annotation is proven by the fully qualified spelling, a named import, or a star import of `androidx.room` (or `android.arch.persistence.room`); a star proof obscured by a same-package declaration of that name proves nothing. A query that is not a literal, a query naming no table, a table or type no recovered entity declares, a write naming its entity by an `entity =` argument, and a computed `tableName` are Gaps. Lookalike annotations and test source sets assert nothing.
- **AC-1027** Given a Kotlin product source that imports JetBrains Exposed (`org.jetbrains.exposed.*`), when it is ingested, then each `object` extending an import-proven Exposed `Table` (or an id table such as `IntIdTable`, `LongIdTable` or `UUIDTable`) becomes a Confirmed `DataEntity` table (`storage: "sql"`, framework `exposed`, `data:<repo>@sql:<table>`), named by its literal name argument or else by the object name less a `Table` suffix, whose columns are the literal names of its column builder calls (plus `id` for an id table); and each DSL statement called directly on a table object inside a function (`select`, `selectAll`, `selectBatched` read; `insert`, `batchInsert`, `insertIgnore`, `replace`, `update`, `upsert`, `deleteWhere`, `deleteAll` and their variants write, with the statement and its operations in the edge's props) adds an InferredStrong `READS` or `WRITES` edge from that function to the table, joined across files. A computed table name, and a statement whose receiver is not a recovered table, are reported as Gaps.
- **AC-1028** Given a Kotlin product source with a class annotated by an import-proven `jakarta.persistence` or `javax.persistence` `@Entity`, when it is ingested, then the class becomes a Confirmed `DataEntity` in the Java JPA shape (`storage: "jpa"`, framework `jpa`, `data:<repo>@jpa:<name>`), named by a literal `@Entity(name = …)` or else its class name, with a literal `@Table(name = …)` (and `schema`) or else the entity name as its table, and its `val`/`var` constructor parameters and backed body properties as `fields` (spelled type, `@Id`/`@EmbeddedId`, a literal `@Column(name = …)`; JPA or Kotlin `@Transient` properties are not fields, Kotlin's default-imported one unless a same-package `Transient` obscures it); and each `@OneToOne`, `@ManyToOne`, `@OneToMany` or `@ManyToMany` property adds a `REFERENCES` edge to the target entity (the property type, a collection's element type for a to-many, or `targetEntity = X::class`) with its cardinality, `mappedBy`, `@JoinColumn` and `@JoinTable` names in a `relations` list (several relations to one target are one edge listing every field, citing each), resolved across files among JPA entities only. A computed entity or table name, an unprovable target, a target that is not a recovered JPA entity, and an entity name that more than one class declares (one Gap, resolving no relation to or from it) are reported as Gaps.
- **AC-1100** Given a Kotlin product source declaring an interface that extends an import-proven Spring Data repository base (`Repository`, `CrudRepository`, `ListCrudRepository`, `PagingAndSortingRepository` or `ListPagingAndSortingRepository` from `org.springframework.data.repository`, or `JpaRepository` from `org.springframework.data.jpa.repository`; proven by the qualified spelling, a named or aliased import, or a star import that no same-package declaration of that name obscures), when it is ingested, then the interface Symbol gets a Confirmed `REFERENCES` edge (relation `repository`, framework `spring-data`) to the JPA entity (AC-1028) its first type argument names, resolved across files; and each call of a Spring Data vocabulary method (`find`, `get`, `read`, `query`, `search`, `stream`, `count`, `exists` read; `save`, `delete`, `remove` write; the verb ends the name or is followed by an uppercase letter or digit) on a receiver whose declaration states that repository type (a parameter, property or local with a written type) adds a `READS` or `WRITES` edge (resolution `declared-type`, with the method) from the calling function to that entity. A type argument that is not a recovered JPA entity, or an obscured star proof, is a Gap; a generic base's type variable, lookalike bases, inferred receivers, other method names and test source sets assert nothing.
- **AC-1303** Given a Spring endpoint whose handler marks a parameter `@Valid` (`jakarta.validation` or `javax.validation`) or `@Validated` (`org.springframework.validation.annotation`), proven by a named import, a wildcard import or the qualified spelling, when the repository is ingested, then each built-in constraint (`jakarta.validation.constraints` or `javax.validation.constraints`: `@NotNull`, `@NotBlank`, `@NotEmpty`, `@Size`, `@Min`, `@Max`, `@Pattern`, `@Email`, `@Past`, `@Future` and the other built-ins) on a field or record component of the parameter's type, or of a superclass the repository declares, becomes a row of the endpoint's `input_constraints` with the parameter, declaring type, field, constraint, its attributes (literals, and string or integer constants the repository declares once, settled) and `message`, citing the annotation. A constraint written on a handler parameter counts only when the controller class is `@Validated`. A type no handler validates contributes no row. A constraint the repository's own `@Constraint` annotation type defines (`custom_constraint`), one with `groups` or a `@Validated(...)` naming groups (`validation_group`), a `@Valid` field (`cascaded_field`) and an attribute that does not settle (`constraint_value`) are Gaps on the endpoint (`Endpoint -DEPENDS_ON-> Gap`) naming the annotation, never a guessed row (#2162). On jhipster-sample-app this gives 14 endpoints with 66 constraint rows and no Gap.
- **Trace:** M1,M10 · `adapters-lang-java`, `adapters-lang-kotlin`, `adapters-lang-rust`, `core-graph`, `core-prov`, `ingest`, `spec`, `app` · — · T-0004..0006,T-0053..0054,T-0079..0080,T-0095,T-0098..0100,T-0192,T-0199..0201,T-0230,T-0284,T-0310,T-0338,T-0360,T-0456,T-0457,T-1023,T-1025,T-1027,T-1028,T-1100

### US-0003 — IaC resource graph + cloud capability resolution (Terraform/Pulumi/AWS)
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** a deterministic resource graph with cloud-capability edges **so that** infra/cloud topology is Confirmed.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0007** Given Terraform HCL, when parsed, then a resource DAG is built from interpolation references.
- **AC-0008** Given AWS resources, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then TRIGGERS/ROUTES/GRANTS edges are emitted deterministically.
- **AC-0009** Given Terraform state/plan JSON, when available, then T1 enrichment supersedes ambiguous T0 refs (observed provenance).
- **AC-0043** Given an API Gateway v1 integration with direct resource references, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then a Confirmed ROUTES edge links the REST API to its integration target.
- **AC-0044** Given a Lambda permission with direct resource references, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then a Confirmed TRIGGERS edge links its source ARN to the Lambda function.
- **AC-0045** Given Lambda@Edge associations nested under default or ordered CloudFront cache behaviors, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then Confirmed TRIGGERS edges link the distribution to every referenced Lambda function.
- **AC-0046** Given an EventBridge Pipe with direct resource references, when resolved against the Capability Registry, then a Confirmed TRIGGERS edge links its source to its target.
- **AC-0047** Given an IAM policy that references an `aws_iam_policy_document` defined in the same extraction, when its statement resources are resolvable, then Confirmed GRANTS edges target those resources with actions and evidence from the document; an absent or unresolved document target remains explicit.
- **AC-0048** Given a Terraform module with a literal local source confined to the ingest root, when extracted, then its resources and internal edges are instantiated under the `module.<name>.` address prefix recursively and deterministically; remote, escaping, symlink-escaping, or cyclic sources remain explicit leaf modules.
- **AC-0051** Given TypeScript that import-proves an AWS Pulumi constructor, when a resource with a literal logical name is parsed, then a Confirmed T0 Resource plus REFERENCES/DEPENDS_ON (including `parent`/`dependsOn`) and applicable shared Capability Registry edges are emitted; lookalike constructors without a Pulumi import produce no IaC facts.
- **AC-0052** Given a Pulumi stack export or preview JSON artifact declared as `pulumi_json`, when it is ingested, then matching T0 Pulumi resources gain separate Dynamic/Confirmed observed inputs, outputs, URN, and evidence while Pulumi secret wrappers are redacted and unmatched observations never fabricate T0 resources.
- **AC-0225** Given a Terraform module referenced by an explicit literal `source` (AC-0048) under a directory the ingest root's own `.gitignore` excludes, when it is extracted, then its files are still expanded (an explicit reference overrides `.gitignore`, ADR-0034) and the reported Terraform layer file count includes them — derived from the same incremental-cache file contexts extraction parsed or reused, so it matches file-for-file rather than undercounting from a separate walk; given a Go module root whose own `.gitignore` excludes `go.mod`, when the module path is resolved, then `go.mod` is treated as absent (an ambient direct read honors `.gitignore`, unlike an explicit reference) and internal-import proof that depends on it falls back to Gap exactly as it does with no `go.mod` at all.
- **AC-0316** Given TypeScript or JavaScript code that the repository has no IaC for, when it is ingested, then each proven client site becomes a `USES` edge from its enclosing symbol to an observed `Resource` (`props.observed = true`, `kind` = `browser-storage`, `sdk-client` or `auth-provider`). Proven sites are registry clients whose binding is import- or require-proven from the entry's module, and `indexedDB.open`, `caches.open` or Web Storage use in a file that does not bind that name. WebExtension storage (`<api>.storage.<area>` for the `local`, `sync`, `session` and `managed` areas) is one `webextension-storage` Resource per area when `<api>` is the unbound `chrome` or `browser` global or any name a default, namespace or `require` import of `webextension-polyfill` binds, and the file never writes `<api>.storage`; an area reached both ways keeps the polyfill `module`. There is one Resource per provider and instance identity. A literal identity names the instance, and a URL keeps only its scheme and authority (never credentials, paths or queries). An env reference records the variable name and never its value. A call without the declared identity names the provider alone. Bindings are proven lexically at each site: a parameter, local or block that rebinds the name, or a shadowed `window`/`self`/`globalThis` receiver, proves nothing. A runtime-computed identity becomes a Gap naming the site, with credentials and recognized secrets removed from its source text, and a literal that cannot be recorded safely is withheld whole. The client table is versioned registry data that extension files layer onto, and every edge it proves cites its registry file (#734).
- **AC-0317** Given TypeScript or JavaScript code that calls HTTP APIs, when it is ingested, then each call through the platform `fetch` (bare, or on an unbound `window`/`self`/`globalThis` that the file does not rewrite) or an import-proven registry HTTP client (axios, ky) whose URL starts with a proven absolute `http(s)` origin becomes a `USES` edge to one observed `Resource` per origin (`kind` = `http-api`), with the calling client on the edge. The URL head is the known leading text of a literal, literal `const`, template or `+` concatenation; text cut short by an unknown piece counts only once its authority is closed, so a host left open is never truncated to a wrong origin. A leading env reference records its name. Relative URLs name no other origin and make no resource. A computed registry-client URL is a Gap; a computed `fetch` URL is left to the client-call pass, which already records it (#734).
- **AC-0318** Given Python code that the repository has no IaC for, when it is ingested, then each proven call to a `pypi` registry client (boto3 `client`/`resource` for the service its literal first argument names, redis, the `limits.storage` Redis, Sentinel and Cluster rate-limit storages, smtplib, SQLAlchemy `create_engine`) becomes the same observed `Resource` and `USES` shape as AC-0316, with the same identity, credential and Gap rules; an identity declared as the first argument is also read from that parameter's keyword (`uri=`, `url=`, `host=`) when the registry entry names it. A client is proven only through a module-level import the file never rebinds; any other write of the bound name in the file (assignment, `def`/`class`, loop, `with`/`except` alias, parameter, walrus, `global`, a member write or `setattr` of the export the call reads, an import binding it to something else, or a wildcard import) proves nothing, and neither does a function-local or relative import alone. `os.environ[...]`, `os.environ.get(...)` and `os.getenv(...)` record the variable name, and a module-level name assigned once is followed one step (#734).
- **AC-0331** Given Python whose client identity names another repository module's constant (`config.X` through `from app import config`, `from app.config import X`, or `import app.config` then `app.config.X`), when it is ingested, then the constant is followed one step: the module must be exactly one repository file (`a/b.py` or `a/b/__init__.py`) that no package on the way rebinds, assigning the name once at module level to a literal or an env read and never otherwise writing, importing or star-importing it. No file may write that member or the reading module's binding through a proven binding or by dotted name (`setattr`, `monkeypatch.setattr`, `mock.patch`, `patch.object`, with positional or keyword operands; a splatted operand voids the follow), and no member write through any other root (an alias, a parameter) may use the constant's or the binding's name. A computed-name `setattr` through a root that may be any module (anything but a method's receiver or a name bound only by imports of one target, and anything beneath a member chain), or a computed-key write of `globals()`, `vars(x)` or `x.__dict__` reaching the module, voids the follow; a named member write through any chain voids it by name. When the repository makes a computed-name write on an undecorated or `@classmethod` method's receiver, the follow rests on that receiver convention: the resource and `USES` edge are InferredStrong and marked `identity_assumes_method_receivers`, never Confirmed. The `USES` edge then cites the defining assignment beside the call and records `identity_from`; a resource the follow creates cites it too, while one another site already proves keeps that proof unless it rested on the receiver assumption. Anything else stays the call site's Gap with its own expression (#734).
- **AC-0337** Given an AC-0318 call whose identity is a parameter of a module-level function (every parameter plain or typed, no default or splat, the parameter never rebound in the body), when it is ingested, then the parameter is followed to its one caller: the function's module must be one repository file that defines it once and names it nowhere else; across the repository, exactly one call through a proven import reaches it and no file reads it other than by calling it (a value, an attribute, a re-export), makes a relative, local or unproven import that could reach it, star-imports anything, or writes it as a member (named, computed, or through a namespace write). The call must have no splats and pass the parameter positionally or by keyword, not both. The argument is classified in the caller and a `config.X` argument is followed as AC-0331 does. Dynamic lookups are not seen, so the resource and `USES` edge are InferredStrong and marked `identity_assumes_static_callers` (also `identity_assumes_method_receivers` when the repository makes AC-0331's receiver writes), never Confirmed, citing the argument through `identity_from`. Anything else stays the call site's Gap (#734).
- **AC-0365** Given a JavaScript or TypeScript file that calls `self.addEventListener('install' | 'activate' | 'fetch', …)` with `self` the unshadowed global, or whose site-root path (`/sw.js`) is the literal argument of `navigator.serviceWorker.register(…)` in the same repository (resolved only when exactly one file fits; relative literals prove nothing), when it is ingested, then its File node and the Symbols defined in it carry `runtime: "service-worker"`, cite the proving call in their evidence and content hash, and the Atlas places them, and observed `browser-storage` Resources, in the Client layer instead of Server (the Server band, for browser storage too; browser storage also stays listed under the Infrastructure layer filter as observed infrastructure, #734). An anonymous function passed to `X.addEventListener('<event>', fn)` or to a method call `recv.method(fn)` keeps its `anon@<byte>` id and gains a `display_name` (`<file> · <event> listener`, `<file> · <callee> callback`) that the Atlas prefers; anything unproven stays as before (#1078).
- **AC-0376** Given an AC-0316 or AC-0318 client site that is not an HTTP API and whose instance identity is runtime-computed or withheld as unrecordable, when it is ingested, then the site keeps its Gap and its `USES` edge to that Gap, and it also gets a `USES` edge to the provider resource `observed:<provider>`. That edge is marked `instance_unresolved` and names the Gap. The resource is marked `instance_unresolved` unless an identity-less site also proves it, whatever the site order, and it keeps no raw identity text. An HTTP site with a computed URL still yields only its Gap (#1116).
- **AC-0689** Given Terraform where two different root-module directories (for example `envs/prod` and `envs/staging`) declare the same resource address, when it is extracted, then neither declaration is lost: since ADR-0077 the ids are scoped by root module (AC-1500), so no `gap:tf-address-collision:` Gap is emitted any more, and a repository without such a layout gets no new fact (#1844).
- **AC-1500** Given Terraform in a repository, when it is extracted, then each `Resource` id is `res:{repo}@{rootdir}/{address}` where `{rootdir}` is the repo-relative directory of the root module that declares or instantiates it, and the repository root has no prefix, so Terraform only at the root keeps `res:{repo}@{address}` ids and identical nodes; a local module's resources are instantiated under the calling root module's scope after `module.<name>.`; a directory that is the resolved source of a local module call and is also read directly is scoped by its own directory and carries `child_module` instead of `root_module`; every `DEPENDS_ON`, `REFERENCES`, `GRANTS` (including grants chased through a policy document), Capability Registry edge and placeholder takes the scope of the block that names it; `logical_id` stays the Terraform address and the topology label names the module directory beside it; cold, repeated and warm-cache runs are identical (#1844, ADR-0077).
- **AC-1501** Given a repository's `state_json` and root-module-scoped Terraform (AC-1500), when it is enriched, then the state matches only nodes with neither a `root_module` nor a `child_module` prop, as before scoping; when it matches none of those and the repository has exactly one root module outside its root (every directory holding Terraform that is not a local module's source counts, including one that declares no resource), it matches that module's nodes; when there are several such root modules, nothing is applied and a T1 Gap `gap:tf-state-root:{repo}@` names the root modules and cites the state file (#1844, ADR-0077).
- **Security:** IAM GRANTS feed the security view; secrets in state are redacted.
- **Performance:** Registry lookups O(1) per resource type.
- **Trace:** M2,M6 · `adapters-lang-ts`, `iac`, `adapters-lang-go`, `dynamic`, `spec`, `app` · — · T-0007..0009,T-0043..0048,T-0051..0052,T-0225,T-0316..0318,T-0331,T-0689,T-1500..1501

### US-0004 — Event graph with channel-identity stitching
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** producers and consumers linked by channel identity **so that** flows cross service and repo boundaries.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0010** Given a literal channel id on both sides, when matched, then a Confirmed PUBLISHES/SUBSCRIBES edge is created.
- **AC-0011** Given a channel id from a present config/env file under a key the author allowlists (AC-1018), when resolved, then the edge is Confirmed via the config resolver.
- **AC-0012** Given a runtime-computed channel id, when unresolved at T0, then the hop escalates and, if still unresolved, emits a Gap with reason.
- **AC-0256** Given an EventBridge `PutEvents` site whose detail type is unresolved at T0, when an OTel trace is applied, then the Gap is filled only by observations attributed to that site that establish bus and detail type (with literal static components) as exactly one distinct scoped identity; a bus-only, contradicting, unattributed, multi-identity or batched case keeps the Gap with a reason naming the shortfall, and an observed bus is preserved as its own fact.
- **AC-0990** Given env files (`.env`, `.env.*`) that hold a secret-shaped value (a recognized secret pattern, a token-shaped value, a URL with a password, or a value under a credential key name that is not a template placeholder), when the config resolver indexes them, then the ingest is refused with every file, line and key named, no value disclosed, and the way to proceed (remove the values or files; declare non-secret values in `cartograph.system.toml` `[env]`) (#933).
- **AC-1018** Given env files (`.env`, `.env.*`), when the config resolver indexes them, then it reads a value only for a key listed in `env_read` in `cartograph.system.toml` or in `read` in the repository's `.cartograph/env.toml` (exact key names; a glob or other shape refuses the configuration; both empty by default), a listed URL value loses any `user@` part, and a site whose identity or base URL names a key an env file sets but no list names stays unresolved with a Gap naming the key, the file and how to list it; the secret refusal of AC-0990 still covers every line, listed or not, and `[env]` values need no listing (#933, ADR-0075).
- **Security:** AC-0990: an env file holding a secret refuses the ingest; the resolver never keeps a secret-shaped value. AC-1018: no env-file value is read unless the author lists its key.
- **Performance:** Channel index keyed by identity for O(1) match.
- **Trace:** M3,M5,M6 · `events`, `dynamic`, `adapters-fw`, `adapters-lang-ts`, `flowtracer`, `ingest`, `app` · ADR-0075 · T-0010..0012, T-0256, T-0990, T-1018

### US-0005 — Client-side interaction graph (React first)
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** screens, components, and data-fetch call sites extracted **so that** flows are anchored at user actions.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0013** Given a React/Next repo, when ingested, then Screen/Component nodes and FETCHES edges to endpoints are created.
- **AC-0014** Given a data-fetch call site, when the endpoint is resolvable, then FETCHES is Confirmed; otherwise it escalates.
- **AC-0452** Given a fetch whose resolved path and method are known, when its repository's endpoints are stitched, then a method-less route serves every method, whether spelled `ANY` or Express-style `ALL` (Express `all`, an unguarded node-http route, a Next.js pages/api handler). A method route beside it on the same path keeps the match an ambiguity `Gap`. Prefix routes (`/x/*`) are not matched, because their match semantics differ by producer (#1327).
- **AC-0453** Given a prefix route whose producer declares its `prefix_kind` (#1329), when same-repo fetches or cross-repo binding joins are stitched, then it matches by that kind: `raw` (node-http `startsWith`) when the raw string before `*` prefixes the request path, `segments+` (Next `[...x]`) when the segments before `/*` match followed by one or more non-empty segments, and `segments*` (Next `[[...x]]`) for zero or more. A prefix route with a missing or unknown kind, or whose producers disagree on its kind (`conflict`, including a node-http exact route of the same id as a Next catch-all), matches nothing, even a request whose literal path ends in `*`. A request that matches an exact route and a prefix route, or several prefix routes, stays an ambiguity `Gap`, with no precedence assumed. The cross-repo pass also lets a method-less `ANY`/`ALL` route serve every method.
- **Security:** —
- **Performance:** —
- **Trace:** M4 · `adapters-lang-ts` (tsx), `adapters-fw`, `events` · — · T-0013..0014, T-0452..0453

### US-0006 — Deterministic end-to-end flow tracer
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** cross-layer flows traced T0-first with explicit gaps **so that** flows are trustworthy.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0015** Given a trigger, when traced, then each hop resolves at the lowest possible tier and records its tier.
- **AC-0016** Given any unresolved hop, when no tier resolves it, then a Gap node truncates that branch (no silent completion).
- **AC-0017** Given a completed trace, when scored, then flow_status ∈ {Verified, Partial, Inferred} per the scoring rule.
- **AC-0083** Given a recovered WebExtension graph, when flows are traced, then extension user-action anchors trigger their own flows — every ExtensionContext, and every manifest Command routed to its dispatching context (`_execute_*` to the toolbar action, all others to background, with an explicit Gap when that context is absent) — whose hops walk ENTRY into the entry file and DEFINED_IN in reverse from that file into the symbols it defines, deterministically under input reordering, while internally published channels participate as mid-flow hops rather than triggers.
- **AC-0219** Given a flow node that owns a Gap through `DEPENDS_ON`, when the flow is traced, then an execution Gap (code the owner runs that T0 could not recover, e.g. an unproven `eval()`) appears as a terminal Gap hop with its reason and attempted tiers and the flow is Partial, while a Gap flagged `rule_evidence_gap` (rule-evidence scope, including decoded eval code awaiting a span mapping) and any `DEPENDS_ON` between non-Gap facts leave the flow's status unchanged (ADR-0032).
- **AC-0257** Given a caller-admitted graph and explicit limits for flow count, cumulative hop count and compact UTF-8 JSON output bytes, when bounded tracing runs, then each owned flow and hop is admitted before construction; success is byte-identical to ordinary tracing with unchanged anchors, traversal and deduplication order, reversed-edge identity, depth status, provenance, producing methods and score summation, while a limit returns an explicit resource/maximum error without a partial result. The caller separately bounds graph admission and linear borrowed traversal workspace.
- **AC-0330** Given a repository that declares scheduled jobs in a yacron-format file (`jobs:` as a sequence of mappings with a `schedule`), when it is ingested, then each mapping that declares a `schedule` becomes a `Schedule` trigger carrying its name, verbatim schedule and command, and anchors its own flow through `ENTRY` into the file it runs. The entry is Confirmed only when the root `Dockerfile`'s final stage proves where the repository sits in the container (a single `COPY . <dir>`) and the script path, a relative one joined to that stage's final `WORKDIR`, lies under it, citing both files. A later `COPY`/`ADD` (`--from` included) or `RUN` in that stage that may reach the entry leaves it InferredStrong, marked `entry_may_be_replaced_in_image` and citing the instruction. Without the mapping, a relative path against the repository root or a unique repository suffix of an absolute path is InferredStrong and marked `entry_relative_to_repo_root` or `entry_path_suffix`. A shell-composed command, inline code (`node -e`, `python -c`), an interpreter option outside the modeled grammar, an unknown or ambiguous file, a command that runs no script, or a YAML alias, anchor, tag or merge key is a Gap naming the job. A GitHub Actions `jobs` mapping is not a schedule (#770).
- **AC-0332** Given a repository that declares scheduled jobs in a crontab(5) file (`crontab`, `*.crontab`, `*.cron`, or a file directly in a `cron.d` directory), when it is ingested, then each job line (five time fields, each within its bounds with names only in the month and weekday fields, or an `@` macro, then a user field in `cron.d` files, then the command) becomes a `Schedule` trigger with `format` `crontab`, its verbatim schedule and command, citing its own line, and resolves its `ENTRY` exactly as AC-0330 does. Comment, blank and environment (`NAME=value`, spaces around `=` allowed) lines are not jobs. In a file with at least one job, any other line, and a command using an unescaped `%`, is a Gap naming the line. A file with no parsable job declares nothing (#779).
- **AC-0333** Given a repository with a Kubernetes `CronJob` document (`apiVersion: batch/…`, in any `.yml`/`.yaml` file, multi-document streams included), when it is ingested, then each container of `spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec.containers` becomes a `Schedule` trigger with `format` `kubernetes`, the job's `spec.schedule`, and its literal `command` then `args` as the command, named after the job (and the container when there are several), and resolves its `ENTRY` as AC-0330 does, except that an entry proven only through the root `Dockerfile` is `InferredStrong`, marked `entry_image_assumed_from_dockerfile`, since the image is not proven to be that build. A container without `command` or with an empty one (image entrypoint), one setting `workingDir`, an empty argument or one containing whitespace, a non-literal command list, a templated manifest (`{{`) or a YAML alias, anchor or tag is a Gap naming the job. Other kinds, and `CronJob` kinds outside `batch/`, declare nothing (#779).
- **AC-0334** Given a scheduled job whose command runs a Python entry with one option and value (`-j v`, `--job v`, `--job=v`), when it is ingested, then its `ENTRY` enters the function that value selects, `InferredStrong` (a static reading of the branch) and marked `entry_argument_dispatch`, carrying `dispatch` (option, value, function, file) and citing the branch, only when, on the straight-line path of the entry's `if __name__ == "__main__":` block (entering `with` bodies only) and in this order, `<parser> = argparse.ArgumentParser(...)` is the module's only binding of `<parser>` (with a top-level `import argparse` before the block the only binding of `argparse`), one `<parser>.add_argument` declares that option with only value-preserving keywords (`help`, `metavar`, `required`, `default`, `dest`, `type=str` with `str` never bound in the module, `action="store"`, `choices` listing the value), `<args> = <parser>.parse_args()` binds it, and one `if`/`elif` chain tests `<args>.<dest>` where every clause before the first one comparing it with the value compares it with another literal; the block reads the parser and the parsed arguments only as attributes after their bindings; and that branch makes exactly one bare call to a name bound once in module scope, by an undecorated top-level `def` before the block that the Python adapter emitted. Otherwise, including a converting `type`, `nargs` or other actions, a chain under another condition or loop, a write to or other use of the parsed arguments, a replaced, unproven or passed-on parser, a wildcard import, or a rebound, imported, decorated, conditional or later-defined function, the `ENTRY` stays the file as in AC-0330 and names why in `dispatch_unresolved`; a command passing no option makes no dispatch claim (#779).
- **AC-0335** Given a GitHub Actions workflow (a YAML file directly in `.github/workflows`) that declares `on.schedule`, when it is ingested, then each `cron` entry becomes a `Schedule` trigger with `format` `github-actions`, the workflow's `name` and its verbatim cron, citing the entry, whose `ENTRY` is the workflow file itself (Confirmed); the workflow's steps are not traced through. A cron that is not five schedule fields within their crontab(5) bounds (plus the start-value step `20/15` that GitHub documents), a non-literal one, or a YAML alias, anchor or tag on the entry, the `schedule` sequence or the `on` mapping is a Gap naming the entry. A workflow without `on.schedule`, and a YAML file outside that directory, declares no workflow schedule (#779).
- **AC-0336** Given an AC-0334 dispatch whose selected branch calls a function imported by a top-level absolute `from <module> import <name>` before the `__main__` block (the name's only module binding, not aliased), when it is ingested, then the `ENTRY` enters that function in the one repository file the module resolves to from the script's directory (`<module>.py` or `<module>/__init__.py`, with no package on the way shadowed by a same-named `.py` file), when that file binds the name once by a top-level `def` whose body the call runs, recording `dispatch.module` and the defining `file` and citing the import beside the branch. A branch whose one call is `asyncio.run(<bare call>)`, with `import asyncio` proven as AC-0334 proves `argparse`, dispatches as if it made that call. A call runs a function's body only as a plain call of a plain `def` or `asyncio.run` of an `async def`; a bare call of an `async def`, `asyncio.run` of a plain one, or either of a generator (a `yield` in its own body) proves no dispatch. The import must be the statement's only item binding the name (in `from m import f, g as f` the alias wins), and the same holds for `import argparse` / `import asyncio`. A repository `argparse` module, or for `asyncio.run` an `asyncio` module (`<name>.py` or `<name>/__init__.py` beside the script), shadows the standard library and proves no dispatch. A relative or aliased import, a module that is no repository file or is ambiguous, or a function the module does not define that way keeps the file and names why in `dispatch_unresolved` (#779).
- **AC-0890** Given a Python module outside test directories, `setup.py`, `conftest.py` and `noxfile.py` that a schedule job does not already enter, when it is ingested, then a top-level `if __name__ == "__main__":` block, or, without one, module-level statements that do work (a call, a loop, a block, `parse_args()`) in a script behind a `python` shebang or under a `commands/`, `scripts/`, `bin/`, `tools/` or `management/commands/` directory, becomes one Confirmed `EntryPoint` (`entry:{repo}@{file}#main`, `extractor_id` `t0.entry-point`) with an `ENTRY` into its `File`, carrying `name`, `kind`, `started_by`, `how` (`__main__` or `script`) and the default of a `--port` argument it declares. `kind` is read from the module, first match: `smtp-listener` (an import of `aiosmtpd` or `smtpd`), `database-listener` (a SQL `LISTEN` string in the module or in a repository module it imports, cited and named in `kind_evidence`), `queue-consumer` (an import of `pika`, `kombu`, a Kafka, NATS or Pub/Sub client), `operator-shell` (IPython `embed()` or `code.interact()` in the started code), `command` (under an operator directory, however it loops), `polling-loop` (`while True:` in the started code), `http-server` (a Flask app's `.run(...)` in the started code), else `process`. A module or relative path resolved against the repository root (or `src/`) is InferredStrong and marked `entry_module_relative_to_repo_root` or `entry_relative_to_repo_root`; an absolute path matched by a unique repository suffix is marked `entry_path_suffix` (#1997).
- **AC-0892** Given an AC-0891 declaration this reader cannot resolve to one repository file (a shell-composed command, inline code, a server naming no `module:object`, a missing or ambiguous module, script or path, a program no reader interprets), when it is ingested, then the `EntryPoint` still exists and its `ENTRY` is a Gap (`gap:entry-point:{repo}@{file}#{key}`) whose reason names why and which carries the command, so the gap register names the entry point; none is dropped (R-INT-4, #1997).

- **AC-0921** Given a `.js`/`.mjs`/`.cjs` module outside test directories whose first line is a `node` shebang, when it is ingested, then it is a process start entering its file: one `command` `EntryPoint` (how `subcommand`) per lowercase word it compares with `===`/`==` or a `switch` `case` against a value read from `process.argv` (directly, through a variable, an assignment or a destructuring, or through a same-file function's parameter or return value, names and functions resolved to their declaring function), else one start (`command` under an operator directory, else `process`); `--flag` options, `!==` and a same-named value not read from the command line are not commands; a script's subcommands are one operator-command capability of that script (#2008).
- **AC-0961** Given a Go file outside test, example and end-to-end trees (`e2e`, `testdata`, `examples`, `example`, `test`, `tests`) and not excluded by a `//go:build ignore` line, whose package is `main` and which declares a top-level `func main()`, when it is ingested, then it is an EntryPoint (how `func main`) citing that line: an `http-server` when the file serves HTTP (`ListenAndServe(`, `ListenAndServeTLS(`, `http.Serve(`), a `command` under an operator directory or `cmd/`, else a `process`; it enters the `main` Symbol when the Go adapter emitted it, else the file. - **AC-0966** Given a Java source outside test sources (`src/test/`, `src/it/`) and test, example and end-to-end trees (`test`, `tests`, `e2e`, `testdata`, `example`, `examples`; an `it` package is read), when it is ingested, then each of these is an `EntryPoint` keyed `{Class}.{method}` (followed by its parameter types when the class has more than one such method of that name), citing the method's declaration, that enters the method Symbol the Java adapter emitted with that signature, else its only one of that name, else the file: a `public static void main` with one `String[]`, `String...` or `String args[]` parameter (how `public static void main`), an `http-server` when its body runs Spring Boot (`SpringApplication.run(…)`, `new SpringApplication(…)` or `new SpringApplicationBuilder(…)`, the class proven by import or qualified spelling) and a repository file imports `org.springframework.web.bind.annotation` (that import is its `kind_evidence`), else a `command` under an operator directory, else a `process`; a method annotated `@KafkaListener`, `@RabbitListener`, `@JmsListener`, `@SqsListener` or `@StreamListener`, proven by import, is a `queue-consumer` carrying the literal `topics`, `queues`, `destination` or `value` it names as `sources`; a method annotated `@Scheduled` is a `scheduled-job` carrying the annotation's arguments as written as `schedule`; the `run(String...)` of a class implementing `CommandLineRunner` or `run(ApplicationArguments)` of one implementing `ApplicationRunner` (not another overload), and a `@Bean` method returning one, is a `startup-task`. Each records its method as `handler`. In `knowledge.capability.entry-point@2` a Java class's `@Scheduled` methods are one `Run scheduled jobs` capability, a startup task is `Run <class in words> at startup`, and a Java file's stem reads its CamelCase as words (#2020).
- **AC-1009** Given a Rust source outside test, example, bench, fuzz and end-to-end trees and not `build.rs`, when it is ingested, then a top-level `fn main()` in a binary root (`main.rs`, or a file directly under `bin/`) is a Confirmed `EntryPoint` whose kind is `desktop-app` when it builds a `tauri::Builder`, `grpc-server` when it builds a tonic `Server::builder()`, `http-server` when it serves axum, actix-web, hyper or warp, `command` under `bin/` or an operator directory, and `process` otherwise; each function annotated `#[tauri::command]` (or `#[command]` with `tauri::command` imported) is an `ipc-command` entry point with its `command_name`; and each method of an `impl Trait for Type` in a file naming `tonic` whose return type is `Result<Response<…>, Status>` is a `grpc-method` entry point with its `service`. Each enters the function's `Symbol` when the Rust adapter emitted it, else the file; comments and string literals state nothing (#2041).
- **AC-1104** Given a Kotlin source outside test sources (`src/test/`, `src/it/`) and test, example and end-to-end trees, when the repository is ingested, then a top-level `fun main()` or `fun main(args: Array<String>)` (or `vararg args: String`), and a `@JvmStatic` one of that parameter shape in an `object` or companion object, is a process `EntryPoint` (`how: fun main`): an `http-server` when its body calls Ktor's start (`embeddedServer(…)` imported from `io.ktor.server.engine`, or an engine's `EngineMain.main(…)`), citing that call, or calls Spring Boot's (`runApplication<…>(…)`, `SpringApplication.run(…)` or `SpringApplicationBuilder(…)`, imported) while a Kotlin or Java source's parsed imports name `org.springframework.web.bind.annotation` or a member of it, citing that import; a `command` under an operator directory; otherwise a `process`. A function annotated with an import-proven `@KafkaListener`, `@RabbitListener`, `@JmsListener`, `@SqsListener` or `@StreamListener` is a `queue-consumer` with the literal topics, queues or destinations it names; one annotated `@Scheduled` is a `scheduled-job` with its schedule as written; the `run(vararg String)` / `run(ApplicationArguments)` of a class implementing `CommandLineRunner` / `ApplicationRunner`, and a `@Bean` function declared to return one, is a `startup-task`. Each enters the function Symbol the Kotlin adapter emitted (`Class.function`, companion objects transparent), else its file. A class member `main`, an extension `main`, a `main` of another parameter shape, lookalike annotations and runner overloads start nothing, and a type or callable reference to a server start, a commented-out import or a longer package name is no server evidence.
- **AC-1110** Given an Android `AndroidManifest.xml` outside test, `androidTest`, build output and example trees, when the repository is ingested, then each `<activity>`, `<service>`, `<receiver>` and `<provider>` directly under `<application>` (not one marked `tools:node="remove"`, nor one inside a comment) is an `EntryPoint` of kind `android-activity`, `android-service`, `android-receiver` or `android-provider` (`how: <activity>` …), keyed by its class name resolved against the manifest's `package`, else the module's Gradle `namespace` (`namespace = "…"` or `namespace '…'` in the module's `build.gradle(.kts)`), a leading `.` or bare name being package-relative; it records the class (`component`), `android:exported` as stated, the actions its intent filters name, and `launcher` when one states the `MAIN` action with the `LAUNCHER` category; it enters the class's lifecycle function (`onCreate`; for a service `onStartCommand`, `onBind`, `onHandleIntent`, then `onCreate`; for a receiver `onReceive`) when the Kotlin or Java adapter emitted it, else the source file declaring the class, and a class no repository source declares, or a relative name with no package to resolve against, is an `ENTRY` Gap naming it; the `doWork` of a Kotlin class extending WorkManager's `Worker` or `CoroutineWorker`, the `createWork` of an `RxWorker` and the `startWork` of a `ListenableWorker` (proven by import from `androidx.work`) is a `background-job`; each such entry point is a capability titled by its class in words ("Open the main activity screen", "Receive broadcasts in boot receiver", "Run sync worker in the background"); and in `architecture.md` they run inside the repository's client application, which counts them ("2 Android components, 1 background job", technology Android), never as processes of their own (#2021).
