- **AC-0446** Given a Flask route decorated with a repository decorator that is not a guard, or with a `@factory(…)` call whose arguments are literals and whose factory returns an instance of a class in its own file, when authentication is derived, then the decorator is listed in `non_auth_decorators` (the route `none` when nothing else blocks, recording `non_repository_calls_in_repository_decorators_do_not_abort` and `werkzeug_errors_keep_their_status`) only when its request-time wrapper (the inner function a bare decorator, or the class's `__call__(self, f)`, returns, decorated with nothing but `functools.wraps(f)`) with the view parameter and wrapper name not rebound, returns only the wrapped call or `None`, raises only werkzeug HTTP errors other than 401/403 with literal arguments, never aborts (under any alias), calls an authentication library or a denying name such as `unauthorized` or `verify_jwt_in_request`, yields or awaits, and calls only methods of its own undecorated, base-less class (no dunder but `__init__`/`__call__`, attributes assigned only in `__init__` from literals or its literal-defaulted parameters, followed at most four deep) and repository functions proven not to abort, raise other errors, call an authentication library or be decorated; `from package import module` is read as a submodule only when the package binds no such name; a decorator, factory or class name written elsewhere, a replaced `werkzeug.exceptions` or `functools.wraps`, or any unproven step keeps the factory `unclassified_decorator` and the bare decorator `decorator_not_classified` (#1173).
- **AC-0448** Given a Flask `before_request` / `before_app_request` hook whose every value-return is a two-element `(body, status)` pair with a literal 429 or 5xx status other than 511 (a maintenance or rate response), when authentication is derived, then the hook does not block: undecorated routes it reaches stay `none`, with the assumption `request_hook_statuses_do_not_authenticate` recorded. A 401, 403, 407, 511 (Network Authentication Required), 404, 3xx or 2xx status, a three-element pair (headers), a non-literal status, or any other value-return still makes the hook blocking (#1317).
- **AC-0599** Given a spec export, then `endpoint-permissions.md` lists every recovered endpoint in a framework for which permission sites are recovered, in one of three places: with its recovered permission sites; under "none recovered", only when the adapter's reading was complete and the handler path was read to its end; or as partial, when the path meets an unresolved hop, has no handler, reaches a walk bound or the guard state is unknown. Endpoints in a framework with no permission-site recovery are listed under a coverage note, in neither table, and the document names the frameworks it covers. The document never states that an endpoint is unauthenticated or unprotected. Each row carries tier, confidence and span for the endpoint and each site; `verified-only` omits a row resting on an InferredWeak site and counts it, `best-effort` annotates it; every assertion resolves in the fact index; the same graph yields the same bytes.
- **AC-0964** Given a Java Spring handler whose method, or else its class, carries a method-security annotation proven by a named import, a wildcard import of its package or its exact qualified spelling (`@PreAuthorize`/`@PostAuthorize`, `@Secured`, Jakarta or javax `@RolesAllowed`, `@PermitAll`, `@DenyAll`), when the Java adapter recovers the repository, then the endpoint's `auth` is a Confirmed reading cited to the annotations: `required` with its guards, roles (`hasRole`/`hasAnyRole`, `@Secured` `ROLE_` attributes, `@RolesAllowed`), authorities (`hasAuthority`/`hasAnyAuthority`), the SpEL expression and the caller it admits in words (checks joined by `and` or negated are stated as the expression itself), or `none` when it admits every caller (`@PermitAll`, `permitAll()`); a value built from literals (escapes decoded), `+` concatenation and `static final String` constants resolves those constants across the repository's files, and a value that is not one, or names a constant the repository does not declare exactly once, is `unknown` naming it; and the reading is combined with the first `HttpSecurity` `authorizeHttpRequests`/`authorizeRequests` rule (`requestMatchers`/`antMatchers`/`mvcMatchers` with an optional `HttpMethod`, or `anyRequest()`, and `permitAll`, `authenticated`, `hasRole`/`hasAuthority` and their `Any` forms, `denyAll`, `access(…)`) whose path pattern (`**`, `*`, `{var}`) covers the endpoint, InferredStrong and cited to the rule: both must admit the caller, so a handler with no annotation takes the URL reading; a rule covering only some of the endpoint's requests, a conditional rule (under an `if`, `switch` or loop), an unread matcher, several filter chains or a `securityMatcher` leave it `unknown` even when the handler is annotated (its annotation kept as a site, its caller not stated), as does a handler with neither; rules apply in source order and a rule for another HTTP method is skipped whatever its matcher (servlet filters and interceptors are not read). `endpoint-permissions.md` covers Spring endpoints through this reading (a recovered site, none recovered, or partial), and D1 §1.2 lists each annotation's caller (#2020).
- **AC-1304** Given a Go `net/http`, chi or gin route, when it is ingested, then its `Endpoint` has `props.auth`: `required` with the guards, their basis and cited sites when middleware applied to the registration is a guard (applied by wrapping the handler, by chi `With`, or by `Use` on the same chi router or the same gin engine, for gin only as a statement earlier in the function that registers the route, another gin `Use` on the engine being an `unordered_middleware` reason; a repository function wrapping the handler counts only when it takes a handler, so a handler factory is not middleware; a guard is gin `BasicAuth` / `BasicAuthForRealm`, chi middleware `BasicAuth` or jwtauth `Authenticator` by import, Confirmed, or a repository function whose body answers the request with 401 or 403, or with a package-level error value built with one, InferredStrong, citing the answering call and the error value's declaration; a call answers the request when it is `http.Error`, `WriteHeader` on an `http.ResponseWriter` parameter, a response call on a `*gin.Context` parameter, or any call handed one of those parameters, so a status that is only logged does not count, and a test file's registrations and middleware are not read), else `unknown` with sorted reasons and never `none`. A middleware expression that resolves to no library entry or repository function is a Gap the endpoint `DEPENDS_ON`, and `endpoint-permissions.md` lists a Go guard as the endpoint's permission site (#2163).
- **AC-1305** Given an axum or actix-web route, when it is ingested, then its `Endpoint` has `props.auth`: `required` with the guards, their basis and cited sites when the handler takes an auth extractor (axum-extra `TypedHeader<Authorization<Bearer>>` / `<Basic>` or actix-web-httpauth `BearerAuth` / `BasicAuth` bound by `use`, Confirmed; or a repository type whose `FromRequestParts` / `FromRequest` impl rejects with 401 or 403, InferredStrong, matched in the handler's crate first and otherwise only when unique) or, for axum, when a `layer` / `route_layer` added after the route, on its router or one it is nested or merged into, is a guard (tower-http `ValidateRequestHeaderLayer` / `RequireAuthorizationLayer` `bearer` or `basic`, Confirmed; or `middleware::from_fn` / `from_fn_with_state` of a repository function that rejects with 401 or 403, InferredStrong; `ServiceBuilder` layers are read one by one), else `unknown` with sorted reasons and never `none`. A parameter wrapped in `Option` is not a guard, and a status that is only compared or matched against is not a rejection. Known non-auth layers are listed in `non_auth_middleware`, a layer expression this pass cannot read is a Gap the endpoint `DEPENDS_ON`, actix `wrap` middleware is not read, and `endpoint-permissions.md` lists a Rust guard as the endpoint's permission site and names its caller (#2163).
- **AC-0656** Given an endpoint whose auth fact is negative (a Flask route with guard state `none`), when the spec is exported, then `security.md` titles the finding "No authentication check recovered: {endpoint}" with Type `no_auth_check_recovered`, states once that a check outside the read source is not visible to the finding, and never contains the word "unauthenticated"; `endpoint-permissions.md` lists the same route under "none recovered"; the finding's stored category, identity hash, tier, confidence and provenance are unchanged.
- **AC-0660** Given a spec export, then `external.md` lists, by one boundary predicate (ADR-0063, SPEC-13): as outside, each literal HTTP origin that an outbound call or observed HTTP client names and no binding citation in the graph covers (a call the binding pass kept records whether a declared binding names its origin, and an observed HTTP client of a declared origin cites its bindings, and what the pass records is part of the fact's content hash so the graph hash follows it, #1843; a call kept before that outcome was recorded is unclassified), with its sites, spans, the flows that reach it and the data entities read or written on those flows; as unclassified with a reason, each target whose host is not known from source, each SDK or auth-provider client, and each published channel with neither a recovered subscriber nor a backing resource; and it counts, without listing as targets, calls that binding evidence places in a registered repository. A call resolved into another registered repository, a same-origin call, an in-process channel, browser storage and an imported package are not listed. A flow that breaks before the outbound call is not listed as reaching the target and stays partial (R-INT-4). A row carries the tier and confidence of the fact holding its literal and never a higher one; `verified-only` omits InferredWeak rows with a count (R-INT-5); the same graph gives the same bytes.
- **Security:** This story *is* the security projection.
- **Performance:** —
- **Trace:** M9 · `iac`, `spec`, `ui`, `adapters-lang-python`, `adapters-lang-java` · — · T-0041..0042,T-0380,T-0599,T-0964,T-0656,T-0660

### US-0016 — Recover WebExtension systems end to end
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** Cartograph to recover a browser extension's entry points, message topology, persisted data, permissions, capabilities, and flows **so that** a real TypeScript WebExtension such as Image Trail compiles into a useful official specification instead of a file-only graph.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- _(Issue #99 drafted these as AC-0055..0059 before that range was assigned; renumbered here.)_
- **AC-0071** Given a Manifest V2/V3 browser extension, when it is ingested, then manifest-declared service workers/background scripts, content scripts, extension pages, toolbar actions, commands, permissions, host permissions, and externally connectable boundaries become deterministic provenance-tagged topology facts (Extension/ExtensionContext/Command nodes with DECLARES/ENTRY edges) and exact-scope GRANTS security facts; declared entry files bind to their `.ts` sources when the built `.js` is absent, a missing entry is an explicit Gap, and the ingest summary reports the WebExtension layer separately.
- **AC-0072** Given import-proven or global Chrome runtime messaging plus explicit message definitions and handler registrations, when the repository is ingested, then deterministic channels and PUBLISHES/SUBSCRIBES edges connect extension contexts; unresolved or dynamic message identities remain explicit Gaps.
- **AC-0073** Given explicit IndexedDB schema/store declarations and repository operations, when ingested, then DataEntity nodes and READS/WRITES relations produce a cited data model.
- **AC-0074** Given recovered WebExtension entry points, message flows, data entities, found ADRs, and permission facts, when the official spec is compiled, then the artifact set contains useful cited assertions and the verified-only output is deterministic across repeat ingest of the same commit.
- **AC-0075** Given an ingest completes with partial or unsupported coverage, when the engineer reviews the result, then the recovery workspace presents artifact readiness, coverage diagnostics, explicit unsupported-pattern guidance, Atlas/Flow/Spec navigation, and evidence context instead of presenting a file graph as a complete analysis.
- **Security:** All new facts are T0 Deterministic/Confirmed or explicit Gap; target code stays read-only; manifest host and API permissions retain their exact scopes.
- **Performance:** Re-ingest uses the existing content-hash cache and determinism guarantees.
- **Trace:** post-M10 dogfood · `adapters-lang-ts`, `adapters-fw`, `events`, `spec`, `app`, UI · WebExtension flows · T-0071..0075

### US-0017 — Runtime-loadable adapter plugins
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** to install a gated adapter plugin while Cartograph is running **so that** an unsupported language or event system becomes a recovered layer instead of a permanent finding.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0068** Given a WASM adapter artifact in a discovery directory (project-local `.cartograph/adapters/` beating user-level on id conflict), when Cartograph loads it, then it activates only after a durable conformance-gate job passes SPI contract tests, the generator-supplied golden corpus (expected facts with exact evidence spans), and a double-run determinism check; a failed or ungated adapter stays in the proposed state under the standard propose/accept curation semantics.
- **AC-0069** Given facts emitted by a plugin adapter, when inspected, then their provenance carries `extractor_id@version` plus the plugin artifact's content hash, and re-ingesting the same commit with the same adapter set yields an identical whole-graph content hash (the US-0014 determinism invariant extends to plugin facts).
- **AC-0070** Given a plugin at extraction time, when it attempts network access, a filesystem write, or exceeds its fuel/memory bounds, then the call fails closed with an explicit finding and no partial facts join the graph (amended for #1810, ADR-0062: an exceeded fuel, memory or table bound refuses that file per AC-0658; the other cases fail the run); source access is read-only and host-mediated, and no LLM is ever invoked inside a T0 plugin.
- **AC-0093** Given a Preflight unsupported finding for an uncovered language, when the engineer follows its request-adapter action and a generated artifact lands in a discovery directory, passes the conformance gate, and is enabled for the project, then the next scan reports the language covered by the plugin (the originating unsupported finding closes), extraction routes the files the plugin's golden corpus claims through the plugin with host-pinned facts, and compiled-in adapters always win a contested extension.
- **AC-0594** Given a plugin adapter returns facts, then the host builds every fact's provenance itself: T0 Deterministic and Confirmed, the pinned extractor id, and a content hash computed by the host. A fact's own `prov` contributes evidence spans only, and only when it states Deterministic and Confirmed, writes no other provenance field, and every span names the mediated repository, commit and file with a byte range inside that file. Any other fact fails the file's extraction with a malformed-fact error naming it, and no fact from that file joins the graph (R-INT-2).
- **AC-0658** Given a gated plugin whose extraction of one claimed file fails with a malformed fact, a guest error or wasm trap, or an exceeded fuel, memory or table bound, then that file is refused for that plugin, no fact from that call joins the graph, the ingest continues, and the same tree gives the same graph and refusals on every run; the plugin's coverage row records exact counts by class (`malformed_fact`, `guest_error`, `bound_exceeded`), the refused paths sorted and bounded with an omitted count, and no guest error text; refused files are outside the plugin's file count and the inventory is limited; `coverage.md`, `coverage.json` and the Workspace coverage view list them as not covered by that plugin; the wall-clock deadline, a stack overflow, instantiation and a failure without a trap code still fail the ingest; an inventory with no refusal keeps its bytes and older stored inventories still read (ADR-0062).
- **AC-1803** Given a project's saved plugin sandbox bounds (`plugin_max_fuel_billions` 1 to 100, default 10; `plugin_max_memory_mib` 16 to 1,024, default 64; `plugin_deadline_seconds` 1 to 60, default 5; all advanced operator limits whose descriptions warn to raise them only for trusted plugins), when a gated plugin extracts, then each call runs under those bounds, a call past the fuel or memory bound is refused for that file as AC-0658 describes, and with no saved bounds plugins run exactly under the AC-0070 defaults. The table bound stays fixed, the conformance gate always runs under the defaults so a verdict never depends on a project's settings, and non-default bounds enter the coverage basis so inventories recorded under other bounds are stale (#1653).
- **Security:** AI-authored code runs sandboxed (no network, no writes, bounded resources) per ADR-0017; fail closed.
- **Performance:** Plugin extraction may trail native adapters; first-class languages stay compiled in.
- **Trace:** post-M10 · `adapters-*`, `ingest`, `app`, UI · — · T-0068..0070, T-0093, T-0594, T-0658

### US-0018 — In-app Help, single-sourced with the wiki
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** help inside the app — a native Help menu, an in-app Help view, and contextual per-surface topics — **so that** guidance is reachable without leaving the work, and never drifts from the published wiki.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0090** Given the native app menu, when it renders, then a Help submenu offers Cartograph Help (opens the in-app Help view), User guide — wiki (opens the wiki in the system browser, never the webview), Report an issue (the tracker's new-issue page, system browser), and About Cartograph with the app version.
- **AC-0091** Given the in-app Help view, when opened — from the menu, the command palette, or the `?`/F1 shortcut — then it renders a keyboard-navigable topic TOC (concepts plus every surface) and the selected topic's content entirely from markdown bundled at build time, offline, with no network dependency.
- **AC-0092** Given any surface, when its contextual help entry (the header's Help action) is used, then the Help view opens on that surface's topic; topics are authored once under `docs/help/`; the PR gate validates that source without reading the wiki, and a post-merge workflow mirrors main's topics to the wiki so the two cannot drift.
- **AC-0222** Given the desktop app on macOS, Linux, or Windows, when it opens an external link (the Help submenu's wiki and new-issue pages), then the complete URL — every query parameter after an `&` included — reaches the system browser through the platform's URL handler (`open`, `xdg-open`, or the Windows URL protocol handler, never `explorer`), and on an OS with no known launcher nothing is spawned and the refusal is logged; and on Windows neither that launcher nor the auth ladder's `gh auth token` step (AC-0001, ADR-0009) flashes a console window.
- **AC-0730** Given a new user with a downloaded release build on macOS, when they open the **First run** Help topic (`docs/help/first-run.md`, listed right after Concepts and linked from the README), then it walks them from installing and first opening the build (the `unsigned-dev` **Open Anyway** case included), through what works with no provider (T0 recovery), setting up the local lane (Ollama, the required models and the commands that fetch them) and saving a cloud provider key and what consent means, to a first ingest and where to look afterwards; and the page names the app version against which every step was performed on a clean macOS account (#1796).
- **Security:** External links open via the system browser; help content ships in the bundle (no runtime fetch).
- **Performance:** Topics render from pre-bundled strings; no I/O on open.
- **Trace:** post-M10 · `app`, `ingest`, UI, `scripts` · — · T-0090..0092, T-0222, T-0730

### US-0019 — Toolchain recovered as cited facts
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** the system's toolchain — frameworks, build tools, package managers, compiler configs, CI, containers — recovered from its config files as cited graph facts **so that** the spec states what the system is built *with*, not just what it does.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0096** Given a tree containing recognized config files (`package.json`, `tsconfig.json`/`jsconfig.json`, lockfiles, `Dockerfile`/`docker-compose.*`, `.github/workflows/*`, language manifests such as `Cargo.toml`/`pyproject.toml`/`go.mod`/`pom.xml`/`build.gradle`), when it is ingested, then each detected tool becomes a Confirmed T0 `Tool` node (`tool:{repo}@{name}`) whose allowlisted resolved settings are props, with `DEFINED_IN` edges to the config `File` node(s) that prove it and provenance citing the exact declaring span; Preflight's framework chips quote the same detection registry as the graph (single source, never disagreeing), and the spec export gains a toolchain artifact fed by the same nodes.
- **AC-0097** Given env files (`.env`, `.env.*`, `*.env` and `env.example`-style templates) or secret-shaped strings in any config, when toolchain facts are extracted, then env files contribute key names only (values are never read into facts, except under AC-1021), any stored string that looks secret-shaped is dropped rather than stored, and configs authored in code (`vite.config.ts`, `webpack.config.js`, …) are detected by presence only — never evaluated — with an explicit `config-behind-code` Unsupported finding naming the limitation. An observed resource identified by an env key is linked to each env file declaring that key, and the topology map draws that link to the file named by its path, citing no span. Redaction fails closed.
- **AC-0598** Given a tree with GitHub Actions workflows or Dockerfiles, when it is ingested, then each workflow yields its triggers, its jobs with their declared position, `needs` relations and dependency depth, and each job's steps in declared order with their `uses` reference or `run` command; each Dockerfile yields its stages with base image, the relations between stages, its ordered instructions and its final stage. Every fact is T0 Confirmed with the exact byte span it states, and the same tree yields the same facts. Matrices, expressions and reusable workflows are recorded as declared and never evaluated. A secret reference is stored as its name only, `with:` values and `env:` maps are not stored, and command text passes redaction. A file that cannot be read exactly yields no facts and is listed in coverage as present and not read, as are GitLab CI, Jenkins, CircleCI and Azure Pipelines files; a `needs` naming a missing job is an explicit Gap.
- **AC-0650** Given a graph with recovered pipeline facts, when a spec is exported, then `pipelines.md` lists each pipeline with its triggers, its jobs in dependency order and each job's steps in declared order, each row with tier, confidence and source span and resolving in the fact index; a job whose needs do not resolve or form a cycle is stated as not ordered and never placed; `verified-only` leaves out InferredWeak rows and states how many, `best-effort` includes them marked; files present and not read are listed with their recorded reason; the reader's remaining limits are stated in every export; the same graph yields the same bytes. `Containerfile` and `*.dockerfile` names are read, and `RUN --mount=…,from=<stage>` is a stage relation.
- **AC-0661** Given a repository with Compose files, when it is ingested, then each file that parses and has a `services` mapping yields one `Environment` identified by its path, named only where the file states a top-level `name`, and one `Workload` per service with its image, ports, environment key names (never values), env files, dependencies and profiles, each with a span (ADR-0070, SPEC-14); a construct the reader does not evaluate, an unparseable file, and a dependency on an undeclared service yield Gaps; an environment file whose environment is carried only by its name yields a Gap and no `Environment`; Kubernetes manifests, kustomize and Helm files are recorded in coverage as present and not read. Given a spec export, then `environments.md` lists the environments, their workloads, each workload name declared in more than one environment with the properties that differ and both spans, and the Gaps; no path is shown as an environment name; the same commit gives the same facts and bytes.
- **AC-0662** Given a repository with Kubernetes manifests and kustomization files, when it is ingested, then each `Deployment`, `StatefulSet`, `DaemonSet` and `Job` document yields a `Workload` with its containers (image, ports, environment key names, referenced ConfigMap and Secret names; never a value or any `data`), each kustomization file yields an `Environment` named only where it states a `namespace`, with `DECLARES` to every such workload its `resources` chain reaches inside the repository and `PATCHES` for each patch-file document that matches one, stating only what the patch writes (ADR-0070, SPEC-14); remote or missing entries, unmatched and inline patches, JSON patches, cycles and unevaluated transformers yield Gaps naming them; other manifest kinds and Helm files are recorded in coverage as present and not read. Given a spec export, then `environments.md` shows a workload reached by several environments once per environment with that environment's patch and image overrides, lists workloads no environment declares, and merges nothing; the same commit gives the same facts and bytes.
- **AC-1502** Given a repository with Helm charts and helmfiles, when it is ingested, then each `Chart.yaml` yields a `Workload` of kind `helm-chart` with its name, version, appVersion, type, dependency names and the sorted key paths of its default values file (never a value; at most 8 levels and 512 paths, the rest counted), each release of a `helmfile.yaml` yields an `Environment` `env:{repo}@{helmfile}#{release}` named only by a namespace it states, with `DECLARES` to the local chart it installs listing each values file the release names and that file's key paths; a templated helmfile, `bases`, `helmfiles`, a remote or missing chart, a missing values file, inline values, `set`, `setString`, `secrets` and `valuesTemplate` yield Gaps naming them, and a chart values file no release names whose file name names an environment is a name-only Gap; templates are never rendered (ADR-0070 decision 9, SPEC-14 increment 3, #2167). Given a spec export, then `environments.md` lists each chart once per environment that installs it, or with "no environment", with its values keys and each release's values files; the same commit gives the same facts and bytes.
- **AC-1503** Given a repository with Spring Boot configuration files and settings files, when it is ingested, then each document of a base `application.{yml,yaml,properties}` activated for a plain profile `P` yields an `Environment` `env:{repo}@{file}#P` named `P` and cited at the activation, and each `application-P.*` whose profile a base file of the same directory declares (`spring.profiles.active`, `.include`, a group entry or an activated document) yields an `Environment` named `P` cited at that declaration and never in itself; each lists the key paths it sets and no value; a profile expression, a placeholder declaration and an activation in a profile-specific file are Gaps; an undeclared profile file that is not a template, and a `settings/*.py`, `config/*` or `config/environments/*` file named by an environment word, are name-only Gaps (ADR-0070 decision 10, SPEC-14 increment 3, #2167). Given a spec export, then `environments.md` lists each profile environment with its settings keys; the same commit gives the same facts and bytes.
- **AC-1021** Given env files and an env-read allowlist (AC-1018), when toolchain facts are extracted, then each env file's `Tool` carries `settings.values` for the allowlisted keys it assigns (first assignment wins; a multiline value, a secret by AC-0990's test or a token-shaped value is never kept; a URL loses any `user@`) and cites exactly the line of each kept value, while its `File` node, its `DEFINED_IN` edge and every env file without an allowlisted value stay span-free; a template file (`.env.example`, `env.sample`, `.env.dist`, an `example`/`sample`/`template`/`dist` name segment) is marked `settings.template = true`, and the config resolver reads every real env file before any template so a real value always wins; the evidence reader shows an env-file span alone, never a window around it, and refuses an env-file span over a line break; Preflight's detection never carries a value (#933, ADR-0075).
- **Security:** AC-0097 is the security surface: fail-closed redaction (AC-0009 standard); no config value is stored unless allowlisted and non-secret-shaped. AC-1021: an env-file value is stored only for an author-listed key, and its citation opens that line alone.
- **Performance:** Only name-matched config files are read; one sorted deterministic walk shared with Preflight's skip set.
- **Trace:** post-M10 · `ingest`, `core-graph`, `core-redact`, `events`, `spec`, `app`, UI · ADR-0058, ADR-0060, ADR-0070, ADR-0075 · T-0096..0097,T-0598,T-0650,T-0661,T-0662,T-1021,T-1502..1503

### US-0020 — Query one evidence-bound context through shared transports
- **Actor:** Human developer or development agent
- **As a** context consumer **I want** bounded, revision-bound reads of recovered facts **so that** the app and agent integrations can cite the same project evidence.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0101** Given a recovered graph, when a snapshot is built or paged, then reordered facts yield the same snapshot identity, any property change invalidates it, duplicate identities fail explicitly, and stale or mismatched cursors are rejected.
- **AC-0102** Given item and serialized-response byte budgets, when a query returns a page, then the entire response fits both budgets and exposes continuation explicitly; invalid budgets or a next fact that cannot fit fail rather than silently skipping it.
- **AC-0103** Given valid, absent, malformed, or ceiling-violating provenance, when a fact is returned, then only validated provenance is authoritative, invalid provenance is explicitly Gap, raw provenance is removed from properties, and inferred facts retain their confidence ceiling.
- **AC-0104** Given a label or neighborhood selection, when queried, then exact labels and the bounded undirected neighborhood select deterministically; absent domain facts return no invented concepts and unknown anchors fail explicitly.
- **AC-0105** Given the app context-query command, when invoked, then graph copying and query work run off the calling thread, read nodes and edges in one database snapshot even across multiple app processes, return the recovered-graph view, and preserve graph contents without model or network calls.
- **AC-0137** Given multiple database connections, when full or label-filtered graph snapshots are read through GraphStore, then nodes and edges belong to one SQLite revision; selections/order and malformed-unselected-property behavior are preserved, and paired counts are coherent without materializing facts.
- **AC-0138** Given legacy Atlas, spec, flow/topology, findings, metrics or semantic-preview graph reads, when another process commits between collection reads, then the application uses the shared coherent contract and performs downstream computations after the read transaction ends, preserving existing output/filter policy.
- **AC-0139** Given a node or edge property read failure, when a graph projection fails and its source is repaired, then the same reader can observe a later valid revision; tests exercise real spec/Atlas adapters without timing races, and no claim is made of atomic ingest or cross-store publication.
- **AC-0232** Given a coherent snapshot and a query by exact identifier, name or text under a declared, versioned matching policy, when deterministic discovery runs, then it returns bounded candidate handles with label, source identity, match reason, counts, coverage and omissions; same-name hits in different repos stay separate candidates and no candidate becomes a binding or a relationship.
- **AC-0233** Given a discovery continuation cursor, when the snapshot, normalized query or matching policy version differs from the one the cursor was issued for, then the continuation is rejected and no page mixes identities; an object that cannot fit the response budget fails explicitly instead of being skipped.
- **AC-0302** Given a graph store with the exact supported private generation schema, every committed insert/update/delete on nodes, edges, source-node/source-edge bindings or coverage inventories advances its database-local revision atomically through guarded row triggers, including writes from an already-open older connection. Rollback restores revision and publication state together; signed-counter exhaustion is a typed refusal without partial changes. An instance/revision token identifies storage mutation order, not content identity, recovery completion, or a transaction spanning other stores (#744, ADR-0047).
- **AC-0303** Given a legacy compatible store, additive initialization preserves facts, associations, coverage and instance identity; partial, future, foreign or oversized private schema metadata is refused before repair/body copies. Version/reset decisions occur under the write transaction; destructive reset including an empty reset advances revision atomically, and failure preserves prior state. A revisioned bounded read observes its revision and rows in one read transaction; oversized/malformed instance metadata is refused before String allocation (#744, ADR-0047).
- **AC-0460** Given an exact recovered node ID, when the context query selects `node { id }`, then it returns only that node, never neighbors or edges including self-loops; an unknown ID fails explicitly. Existing kind/label filters and response budgets apply, reference membership agrees with paged results, and cursors cannot cross node IDs or other scopes. Qualified queries preserve coverage identity. Existing neighborhood bounds and historical specialist schemas remain unchanged (#1350).
- **Security:** Read-only core API; no SQL, arbitrary filesystem paths, model execution, or transport-specific privilege in requests.
- **Performance:** At most 500 facts, 1 MiB serialized response and three hops; large-graph snapshot caching/latency is a later measured gate, not guaranteed by output bounds.
- **Trace:** H1 (SPEC-01) · `context-hub`, `core-graph`, `app` · — · T-0101..0105,T-0137..0139,T-0232..0233,T-0302..0303,T-0460 · ADR-0047

### US-0021 — Inspect and curate a persistent business-domain context
- **Actor:** Human developer
- **As a** developer **I want** domains linking features, rules, designs, decisions, tests and gaps **so that** I can understand behavior and intended architecture in one project context.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0106** Given the pinned external benchmark and declared configuration, when recovered, then features and business rules cite source evidence and report measured coverage against an independently reviewed expected set, with unsupported paths explicit.
- **AC-0107** Given documented intent, inferred interpretations and future designs, when viewed beside implementation facts, then their meanings and producing tiers remain distinct and a missing document is described as unknown intent.
- **AC-0108** Given an accepted proposal, when context, UI and exports are read, then the same reviewed projection includes it without upgrading its tier; source changes mark affected proposals stale until reconciled.
- **AC-0652** Given the base graph, the reviewed staged proposals with their basis assessments and the Workbench assertion decisions, when the curated projection is computed, then every accepted proposal gets exactly one state, the first that applies of `unverified`, `already-covered`, `unresolvable`, `stale`, `conflicted` and `eligible`, with a reason; only `eligible` records are curated items and every other accepted record is listed; pending and rejected proposals are neither; tier and confidence are carried as produced; an assertion decision that matches no current assertion is listed as stale; the curated revision is the same for the same inputs in any order, changes when a decision, a state, a reason, the graph hash or the policy version changes, and does not change with a review note, a timestamp or a review counter. No surface reads the projection yet.
- **AC-0653** Given reviewed staged proposals, when a spec is exported, then the bundle carries the curated revision and counts beside the graph basis and includes `curated.md`, which lists each eligible accepted proposal with its record identity, proposed edge, Gap, tier and confidence as produced, review status, source support and state, and every other accepted proposal with its state and reason; `verified-only` contains no item and states how many it left out, `best-effort` includes them marked InferredWeak; an item cites its Gap and endpoints through the fact index where the export admits them; accepting a proposal changes only `curated.md` and the curated basis, never the graph basis or another artifact; a projection that cannot be computed leaves the export intact and is stated with its reason; the artifact read alone equals the bundle's; work packets refuse the document.
- **AC-0654** Given a complete MCP read session, when the curated documents are read, then `curated` serves the same bytes and content identity as the export for the same inputs and mode and `curated-json` serves the same projection as data with the DR-4 fields; the read basis carries the curated revision or the reason it was not computed, and the basis identity changes with it; `h3_curated_available` is true only when the projection was computed for that session; acquisition is stale when a review or a Workbench rejection changes while it runs; no review is gathered before the session's bounded graph read succeeds; an agent read never compiles every document, so a Workbench decision that matches no stored fact leaves the projection not computed with that reason and fails no other read; a `verified-only` session gets no item, only the count; notes, timestamps, and pending or rejected records are not served; a scoped session reports the read as unavailable.
- **AC-0655** Given the Spec Workbench, when the documents are loaded, then a curated section shows the view, the curated revision of the loaded bundle, the counts, each curated item with its record identity, proposed edge, Gap, tier and confidence as produced and marked InferredWeak, review status, source support, basis and state, every other accepted record grouped by state with its reason, and the Workbench decisions that match nothing; the section and the bundle come from one computation, so they carry one revision; `verified-only` shows no item and the count left out; a projection that was not computed is stated with its reason; the section reloads after a review and after a change of export mode without polling; proposal history and the review dialog state an accepted record's state and reason.
- **AC-0659** Given a spec export or a single-artifact read from the desktop, then its work runs off the UI thread and holds no graph or decision lock after the snapshot read; the basis assessments of one export share one graph copy and identity, each still checked against that copy's store revision (a moved store is read again, and an assessment at another revision is stale); a Workbench decision that no stored fact carries costs one extra compile per store revision, graph content hash and set of such decisions, settled in memory only and never for an MCP read; the curated revision, counts and `curated.md` are the same as without sharing or settling.
- **AC-0843** Given saved investigation findings with their curation reviews and the current digest of each fact they cite, when the curated projection is computed, then every finding accepted as curated gets exactly one state, the first that applies of `unresolvable` (it cites no fact, or a cited fact is not in the graph), `stale` (a cited fact has another digest than the producing run recorded) and `eligible`, with a reason and the changed and missing ids; it keeps Agentic / InferredWeak and its statement, kind, title, limitations, role and source support as stored; a finding with no curation review is not listed and does not move the revision, and a rejected one is not listed and does; two accepted findings never conflict; the result is the same in any input order and two findings with one identity fail it; and one curated revision, `curated-revision-v3`, covers findings, drafts and proposals. A follow-up review (AC-0293) is not a curation review (ADR-0074, #1746).
- **AC-0844** Given a saved investigation finding, when a curation review of it is requested, then the caller names the finding by its review subject, an expected revision and `accepted` or `rejected` and nothing else; the host resolves the finding, its producing specialist and its citations from the immutable saved result, refuses a subject it would not compute for that finding, and writes nothing to the investigation store or its follow-up reviews; the first review stores the finding in a table of its own in the section draft store's file, with one entry per cited fact (its citation id, the digest the producing run recorded, and whether it rests on captured primary-source evidence), and that content can never be changed or deleted; every review is a compare-and-set on the revision the reviewer read, and rejecting an accepted finding withdraws it; a stored row that fails its integrity check is neither served nor reviewed; reads are bounded and never pass a partial read for a complete one; and the curated projection read by the export and an MCP session takes the reviewed findings from that store under the lock that reads the drafts, gives an accepted finding the state its cited facts' current digests decide (so accepting again never makes a stale finding eligible), and a session notices a curation review made after it gathered (ADR-0074 decisions 1 to 5 and 10, #1746). The Investigations surface offers the review (AC-0846).
- **AC-0845** Given findings accepted as curated, when `curated.md`, the Workbench curated read or the MCP `curated` and `curated-json` reads are produced, then all of them list the findings of one projection under its one revision; a `best-effort` read shows each eligible finding with its statement, title, claim kind, producing role, limitations, source support, identity and citation ids, marked T3 / InferredWeak and human-reviewed, and `curated.md` cites each fact a printed finding names once, so every citation id it prints has a row in the fact index; an eligible finding that cites a fact the export does not admit is named and not printed; a `verified-only` read shows no finding and states how many it left out; every accepted finding that is not eligible is listed in both modes with its state, its reason and the cited ids that changed or are missing, and its statement and title appear in no read; a project with no accepted finding gets no findings section in `curated.md`; and an MCP session serves the export's `curated.md` bytes (ADR-0074 decision 11, #1746). The Workbench draws the list (AC-0846).
- **AC-1020** Given an accepted staged proposal, when a person opens its review dialog, then beside its curated state it offers "Resolve gap again on current source", which opens the record's Gap in the Resolution Strategy dialog to stage a new proposal from the current capture, and "Withdraw acceptance", which rejects the record at its current review revision and reloads the Workbench so the record leaves the projection at the next curated revision; neither changes the saved record or offers to accept it again, and a rejected or pending record offers neither (#1746, MT-H3-01 step 4).
- **AC-1038** Given an accepted proposal whose basis graph or associations changed, when the curated projection is computed and every file its captured evidence cites still resolves at the current associations to a range with the same path, digest and byte length (and the same fact digest), then it is `stale` with reason `cited_files_unchanged` instead of `graph_changed` / `association_changed`, so a reviewer can re-confirm it as it is; a proposal with any changed or unreadable cited file keeps its earlier reason, and a basis recorded before this comparison existed reads as unverified for it and keeps its earlier reason too (#2251).
- **AC-0846** Given a saved investigation with findings, when a person reviews one for curation in the Investigations surface, then each finding shows its curation state (no curation review, accepted as curated or rejected as curated) with the revision and time, beside and separate from its follow-up review, and offers Accept as curated and Reject as curated; a decision is sent with the finding's review subject and the revision on screen, the screen shows only a write the host confirmed, and a conflict, a busy store or an unconfirmed reply blocks further decisions until the reviews are read again, with no automatic retry; a stale read never replaces a newer decision or another selection; the T3 / InferredWeak marking of every finding is unchanged by either review; and the Spec Workbench curated section lists the findings of the curated read it was given: counts, each eligible finding of a `best-effort` read with its statement, marks, role, source support, limitations and citation ids, the number left out of a `verified-only` read, and every other accepted finding with its state, reason and changed or missing ids and without its statement; a curation review recorded while a bundle is on screen reads the bundle again (ADR-0074 decisions 1 and 11, #1746).
- **AC-0109** Given multiple named project contexts, when the app restarts or changes project, then each domain's evidence, review history and task references remain scoped to its project and revision.
- **Security:** Agents propose only; human acceptance cannot mint confirmed facts.
- **Performance:** Domain queries expose scope and uncovered portions rather than claiming a complete inventory from partial extraction.
- **Trace:** H2–H3 (SPEC-01, SPEC-12) · `context-hub`, `agents`, `spec`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0058, ADR-0061 · T-0106..0109,T-0652..0655,T-0659

### US-0022 — Request durable specialist work through MCP and ACP
- **Actor:** Human developer or external development agent
- **As a** context consumer **I want** app or MCP requests to run bounded specialist investigations, including ACP runtimes **so that** agents can use and improve shared project context.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Ready
- **AC-0110** Given a permitted investigation from the app or MCP, when started, then one shared coordinator returns a durable task id with project/revision, named agent version, scope, tier ceiling, origin and budget.
- **AC-0111** Given an ACP runtime, when a task runs, then negotiated capabilities, environment permissions and per-tier egress constrain its tools; results are cited proposals rather than writes to confirmed facts or ingested target code.
- **AC-0112** Given a running task, when a caller cancels, disconnects or the app restarts, then progress and terminal outcomes remain queryable, cancellation is propagated and uncertain runtime outcomes require reconciliation before retries.
- **AC-0113** Given nested MCP-to-ACP delegation, when a worker requests further work, then ancestry, deduplication, depth and shared budgets bound recursion and prevent self-delegation loops.
- **AC-0114** Given a real external MCP caller and compatible ACP runtime, when the interoperability acceptance procedure runs, then it reads context, starts an investigation, observes progress, cancels/reconnects and retrieves a cited result using negotiated Tasks support or explicit lifecycle tools.
- **Security:** ACP permissions alone are not a sandbox; repository text never authorizes tools, filesystem access, or egress.
- **Performance:** Long work returns task identity promptly; bounded progress and result retrieval survive transport lifetime.
- **Trace:** H4–H5 (SPEC-01) · `context-hub`, `agents`, `app`, `ui` · — · T-0110..0114

### US-0023 — Evaluate architecture and validate a modernization workflow
- **Actor:** Human developer
- **As a** developer **I want** feature-specific architecture findings and an evidence-backed improvement workflow **so that** I can prioritize justified changes and preserve business behavior.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Ready
- **AC-0115** Given a feature scope, when evaluated, then deterministic structural measurements cite revision-bound evidence and report missing dependency/history coverage separately from confidence.
- **AC-0116** Given a large file, when a specialist assesses it, then responsibility, cohesion, coupling and documented constraints inform the finding; size alone does not establish a god file and inferred judgments remain labeled.
- **AC-0117** Given approved future design and acceptance criteria, when an external agent changes a separately authorized checkout, then re-ingestion compares the result to that design and observed tests without overwriting original evidence or silently declaring equivalence.
- **AC-0118** Given a new pilot user, when they independently ingest and investigate a repository, then a recorded pilot measures setup completion, question accuracy/citations, coverage, investigation time/cost, and one reviewed improvement before market readiness is claimed.
- **Security:** No target-code writes from analysis; implementation authorization is separate and scoped.
- **Performance:** Evaluation is feature/domain scoped with explicit runtime and token budgets.
- **Trace:** H6–H7 (SPEC-01) · `context-hub`, `agents`, `app`, `ui` · — · T-0115..0118

### US-0024 — Recover source-backed rule evidence with real lexical owners
- **Actor:** Developer investigating business behavior
- **As a** developer **I want** cited callable owners and guarded-exit evidence **so that** business rules can be recovered without guessed dispatch or stronger predicates than the code establishes.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0119** Given TypeScript object methods and property-bound callbacks, when extracted, then each has exact T0 provenance, a real scope-qualified symbol and DEFINED_IN edge; same-named members of different objects and objects nested inside classes remain distinct.
- **AC-0120** Given nested or shadowed callable declarations, when their calls are extracted, then the nearest actual callable owns the calls and direct-call targets require lexical binding proof; unrelated file-wide name matches and unknown member dispatch do not become confirmed links; nested Nest decorators require their actual unshadowed imported bindings.
- **AC-0121** Given unchanged source and existing unambiguous top-level/class callables, when re-extracted, then identities and content hashes are deterministic and existing identities remain stable; anonymous route symbol and endpoint ownership continue to agree, and nested Nest controllers bind HANDLES to the actual qualified decorated-method symbol.
- **AC-0122** Given supported guarded returns/throws, when recovered, then source conditions, branch polarity, owner, local effect and exact evidence are retained without treating return strings as rejection or an ancestor-condition list as a complete execution predicate.
- **AC-0123** Given unresolved calls, mutation, loops or exception control flow, when rule evidence is recovered, then unsupported behavioral interpretation and dependencies remain explicit gaps rather than omitted prerequisites or invented semantics; parser-recovered exits or controlling branches yield an explicit unsupported_rule_syntax omission Gap rather than Confirmed rule evidence or a silently dropped throw.
- **AC-0124** Given rule evidence with secret-shaped source literals, when stored or exported, then a shared deterministic redaction boundary prevents their disclosure while preserving source references and an explicit redaction indication.
- **AC-0125** Given recovered rule evidence, when the inventory, shared context and exports are inspected, then owner, conditions, local effect, dependencies, confidence and incomplete interpretation are consistent; computed callable keys disclose omitted source names and unresolved runtime keys; the pinned benchmark records only fully supported matches.
- **AC-1044** Given a rule-evidence string literal that is a dotted lowercase message key (`message.cannot-transition-to-payment-when-order-is-empty`: at least two `.`-separated segments of `-`-separated words, at least four words, each word lowercase letters with a vowel and at most 20 long or at most four digits), when it is recovered in any language adapter, then it is stored as written instead of being withheld as token-shaped, and D1 reads a refusal message key as its words (“cannot transition to payment when order is empty”), including the key passed to `ctx.translate(…)`. Any other token-shaped literal, including an AWS, GitHub, JWT or high-entropy value after a dotted prefix, is still withheld, and the toolchain's token-shape policy (AC-0097) is unchanged (#590).
- **AC-1048** Given a file whose rule recovery an adapter ends early (the Gap whose scope is the rule or the rest of the file, at a capture limit such as 256 rules or 1 MiB of rule payload per file, or at unsupported syntax), when its evidence is recovered and D1 is compiled, then the TypeScript Gap records `omitted_rule_sites`, the guarded exits and effects from the stop on that would each have been considered for a rule; §1.5's lead names each such file (at most ten, then a count) with why it stopped and what was not recorded, and the heading of the file's area carries the same note, so a reader knows that area's rule tables are incomplete; this holds when the file kept no rule, and when the graph has a stop and no rule at all. A file with no stop says nothing (#590).
- **AC-1050** Given a TypeScript callable that starts a `const` list empty (`const found = []`), fills it with `found.push(v)` under a guard (for example inside a `for…of` loop) and later rejects when the list is not empty, when its evidence is recovered and D1 is compiled, then each such push is a guarded change; a list that starts with values, a `let`, a parameter or a list declared after the push is not. D1 states the change as “the line's product variant is added to the found”, a bare `found.length` test as “the found are not empty”, and the rejecting guard adds “Here the found start empty, and when …, the line's product variant is added to the found.” (at most three pushes, each onto the tested list's own binding), so a reader sees what the list holds (#590 R05).
- **AC-1052** Given a TypeScript guard whose tested local holds a method's value (`const availableStock = await this.productVariantService.getSaleableStockLevel(ctx, line.productVariant)`, including in a `for…of` body and in a push that fills a collected list, AC-1050), when its evidence is recovered and D1 is compiled, then: a `const` declared and used in one loop body is a recorded definition (a loop around both is allowed; one between them still is not, AC-0165); a push onto a collected list keeps the definitions its conditions test; and an unguarded arithmetic `return a - b - c` that is a statement of the callable's own body after an `if` returning a value is a rule. D1 follows the value, as it follows a tested predicate, into the one repository method of that name (state-machine hook guards included): “The available stock is the “saleable stock level” value, as follows”, then its guarded value returns and its otherwise value, for example “When the variant's track inventory is “FALSE” or …, the product variant's saleable stock level is the largest safe integer (9007199254740991).” and “Otherwise, … is the stock on hand minus the stock allocated minus the effective out of stock threshold. Here the effective out of stock threshold is the out of stock threshold when the variant's use global out of stock threshold is set, and otherwise the variant's out of stock threshold.” A bare local tested whose definition is itself a test reads as that test; `Type.MEMBER` reads as the member; `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` as the largest safe integer. An otherwise value is not a row of its own, and a compared difference stays outside the rendering rules (#590 R06, R07).
- **AC-1054** Given a TypeScript service that calls a strategy through configuration (`const { stockLocationStrategy } = this.configService.catalogOptions; stockLocationStrategy.getAvailableStock(…)`), a default configuration that sets that key to a class instance (`catalogOptions: { stockLocationStrategy: new MultiChannelStockLocationStrategy() }`, where the class `extends` a base rather than `implements` the interface), and a strategy method that adds to totals it starts at a number and returns (`let stockOnHand = 0; … if (applies) { stockOnHand += stockLevel.stockOnHand } … return { stockOnHand, stockAllocated }`), when its evidence is recovered and D1 is compiled, then: the call resolves to that class's method as an inferred strategy implementation only when exactly one class of that name is declared; each guarded `+=` or `-=` on such a total is a guarded change (a total not returned, not started at a number, another operator, or one changed in a nested callable is not); D1 follows it from the guard that compares the value two call hops away and reads it as "the stock level's stock on hand is added to the stock on hand" (or "taken from"); and a tested local set from a predicate method of one of its arguments (`const applies = await this.stockLevelAppliesToActiveChannel(ctx, stockLevel)`) reads as that test of the argument, "the stock level applies to active channel", with the stored condition kept as its source.
- **AC-1056** Given a TypeScript guard that compares a count with a tested local set to the distinct values of a list (`const variantIds = unique(order.lines.map(l => l.productVariant.id))`), when D1 is compiled, then the guard adds "Here the variant ids are the distinct product variant IDs of the order's lines.", so a reader does not take a repeated value for a mismatch. Only `unique` or `uniq` of one `.map` whose arrow reads a member path of its own parameter is read this way; any other initializer is said as before (#590 VEN-READY-01).
- **AC-1058** Given an investigation by an unqualified specialist whose scope is the whole system (`All`), when it is prepared, then the host pins one store revision, streams the complete-graph identity at it and retains no facts; each query then reads one page from the store at that revision (the selected count by SQL, then only that page's rows, nodes by id and edges by source, label and destination), and its response, cursor and refusals are exactly those the complete in-memory snapshot gives. A task keeps only the stored copies of the facts its pages returned, so memory tracks the question, not the estate, and a whole-system task prepares under retained limits the whole graph exceeds. A store write that leaves the complete graph identical (a coverage record, say) moves later reads to the new revision, just as a held graph never noticed it; a changed graph refuses with InputChanged. A qualified whole-system task pages too (AC-1060) (#1602 option A).
- **AC-1060** Given an investigation by a coverage-qualified specialist whose scope is the whole system, when it is prepared, then the host reads the coverage records with their store revision, streams the complete-graph identity at that revision and retains no facts; its qualification and `context_id` are exactly those a held complete snapshot gives, and each query pages from the store under that qualification, response for response. A store write that leaves the complete graph identical (a replaced coverage inventory) moves later reads to the new revision once the identity streams equal again, and the task keeps its frozen qualification (AC-0239); a changed graph refuses with InputChanged. A qualified whole-system task prepares under retained limits the whole graph exceeds (#1602).
- **Security:** No model or source execution; no new raw predicate/return text stored before the redaction contract is implemented.
- **Performance:** Ownership and rule passes share the parsed tree; unsupported complexity remains bounded and explicit.
- **Trace:** H2 (SPEC-01/SPEC-02) · `adapters-lang-ts`, `core-graph`, `core-prov`, `core-redact`, `spec`, `context-hub`, `app` · — · T-0119..0125, T-1044, T-1048, T-1050, T-1052, T-1054, T-1056, T-1058, T-1060

### US-0025 — Resume review of immutable agent proposals
- **Actor:** Human developer
- **As a** developer **I want** agent results staged durably and reviewed by identity **so that** I can resume after restart and know the exact evidence and interpretation I accepted.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0126** Given a bounded host task and broker result, when staged, then complete proposal content, provenance, task/run identity, a graph snapshot read coherently under AC-0105 and a versioned basis manifest determine an immutable identity; validation rejects unsupported candidate/citation changes, repeated identical staging is idempotent, task source fields persist only as fingerprints, and bounded pre-write annotation validation rejects complete nonempty whitespace-normalized supplied text or contiguous 48-scalar excerpts without echoing source in diagnostics or changing accepted content.
- **AC-0127** Given a staged proposal, when reviewed by ID and expected review revision, then the host atomically records the decision without changing proposal content; unknown IDs and stale revisions fail, and both pending and reviewed records survive restart.
- **AC-0128** Given single or class escalation, when a result completes, then it is durably staged before reported success; failed persistence is a failed result and cancellation preserves already completed staged instances.
- **AC-0129** Given staged history after restart, when the review surface opens, then pending and reviewed results can be retrieved with bounded explicit pagination and reviewed by ID; accepted status states the record's curated state and its reason where the projection gives one (amended for #1746, ADR-0061; it formerly stated that shared-context reconciliation is pending).
- **AC-0130** Given current working-tree evidence assembly, when its task is staged or accepted, then fingerprints bind the exact supplied text while the record explicitly retains unverified source binding and pending reconciliation; a commit-shaped citation, unchanged snapshot or human acceptance cannot certify freshness.
- **AC-0131** Given staging and human review, when recovered graph/context/export state and legacy decisions are compared, then recovered facts and producing tiers remain unchanged and legacy caller-body decisions are not activated as staged proposals; full H3 projection remains an explicit later gate.
- **Security:** No raw task-source archive, caller-body review authority, source writes or model execution from staging/review.
- **Performance:** Staged records have a 128 KiB payload cap; history reads have bounded pages and explicit continuation.
- **Trace:** H3 prerequisite (SPEC-01/SPEC-03) · `agents`, `app`, `ui` · — · T-0126..0131

### US-0026 — Retain exact source captures for recovery evidence
- **Actor:** Recovery engine and developer inspecting evidence
- **As a** developer **I want** bounded immutable source captures **so that** later evidence can refer to the bytes actually retained without trusting a mutable checkout or a commit-shaped label.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0132** Given selected raw source buffers and a host-owned repository identity, when a capture is built, then a versioned canonical manifest binds kind, membership, paths, lengths and digests; reordered selection is identical while changed bytes, repository or kind are distinct, without serializing raw bytes in metadata or diagnostics.
- **AC-0133** Given selected local paths, when working-tree capture runs, then rooted regular-file reads reject symlinks, escaping paths, special files and exceeded limits; the acquired immutable buffer survives later file mutation without claiming an atomic filesystem or Git snapshot.
- **AC-0134** Given a full local Git commit and selected paths, when Git-object capture runs, then exact tree/blob bytes are retained independently of checkout conversion and HEAD movement; unsupported entries and missing objects fail without hooks, filters, shell commands or network fallback.
- **AC-0135** Given a capture and a bounded local store, when persisted and reopened, then manifests and byte objects publish atomically, identical persistence is idempotent, corrupted or mismatched identity fails closed, capacity errors preserve prior data, and strict nonempty bounded span reads validate the complete file reference before slicing the same buffer; restart retains the effective caller span cap, duplicate persistence can only tighten it without changing content identity, and missing or invalid stored policy fails closed.
- **AC-0136** Given a retained captured buffer, when the real TS parser consumes it and its span is read after source mutation and store restart, then the parser and reader see the captured bytes while legacy EvidenceRef and staged identities remain unchanged; this fixture does not certify production ingestion or complete H3.
- **AC-0821** Given another connection holding the capture store's write lock, when the store is opened or written, then it waits up to 5 s for that lock before failing busy, the same busy timeout as every other Cartograph SQLite store (#1653); before, it waited 1 s.
- **Security:** Local selected-file acquisition only; no target writes, repository execution, model calls or network; private raw-byte retention is separate from graph/proposal/export payloads and does not authorize egress.
- **Performance:** Hard acquisition, path, manifest, span and logical-store limits from SPEC-04; failures return explicitly without silently incomplete capture.
- **Trace:** H3 (SPEC-01), #387 prerequisite of #385 · `source-capture`, `spec` · — · T-0132..0136,T-0821


### US-0027 — Keep registered sources distinct and resolve evidence on the host
- **Actor:** Engineer and context consumer
- **As a** engineer **I want** lasting source identities and exact host-owned source lookup **so that** similarly named projects never share recovered ownership or display another project's code.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0140** Given separate local roots or linked worktrees, when registered and reopened through independent connections, then the same canonical root retains its opaque identity and separate roots remain distinct even with equal basenames or Git metadata; missing/moved/replaced roots follow the explicit logical-registration semantics in SPEC-05 without a physical-continuity claim.
- **AC-0141** Given normalized managed origins including same-basename local mirrors, when a clone is added or replaced, then the host reserves separate owned destinations before cloning, cleans only its own failed attempt, rejects unowned or substituted destinations, and preserves the existing authentication/no-partial-clone contract.
- **AC-0142** Given registered sources, when preflight/gate re-scan, direct ingest, manifest-local or clone intake runs, then adapters, caches, findings, reconciliation and metrics use one registered repository key; re-ingesting one source preserves another's facts and root-specific plugin permissions do not transfer.
- **AC-0143** Given a selected evidence reference, when the UI or agent task reader requests source, then the host resolves the exact registered repository and preserves bounded rooted reads; unknown, unavailable or legacy-unmapped references return unavailable without a first-repository or caller-root fallback and without claiming revision freshness.
- **AC-0144** Given a registered repository and revision label, when Repo facts and system display are produced, then canonical fact properties/hash omit operational roots and mutable display metadata, while an operational DTO supplies human labels and equal names remain disambiguated by exact repository identity; repeated recovery for a retained registration is stable without claiming cross-installation graph equality.
- **AC-0145** Given a new source-bound ingest job or a legacy path-only job, when retry is requested, then the new job resolves its registered source and the legacy job requires explicit fresh ingestion before any retry mutation; original job kind/history remains unchanged.
- **AC-0146** Given legacy graph/findings and durable history, when identity migration or later graph/job cleanup runs, then disposable legacy graph ownership is rebuilt and generated findings retire once, while registered sources and historical jobs, metrics, decisions and staged proposal JSON/IDs/reviews remain unchanged.
- **AC-0147** Given concurrent managed-source operations or an owning process that exits, when guards are acquired, then shared readers exclude replacement, exclusive guards last through parser/enrichment use and publication, contention fails without lock cycles, and OS handle closure releases ownership without making an interrupted source ready; guards are acquired before graph/cache locks and nested work reuses them. ADR snapshot validation and its fact patch share one SQLite write transaction, rejecting stale input without writes and rolling back failed patches.
- **Security:** Host lookup never accepts webview overwrite roots; source locks and registry identities grant no egress, target-code edits, source-verification flags or tier upgrades. Existing path/artifact consent stays root-specific.
- **Performance:** Bounded identities/origins and short SQLite registration transactions; try-only source locking never holds a database transaction across clone or extraction. Whole-graph/ADR snapshot costs remain explicit.
- **Trace:** H3 prerequisite #392 · `app`, `ingest`, `core-graph`, `ui` · — · T-0140..0147


### US-0028 — Inspect the retained primary source that produced a rule
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** source observations bound to the bytes their parser consumed **so that** later edits cannot silently change the evidence I inspect.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0148** Given registered TS/JS sources, when the participating production lane runs, then bounded exact enumeration that never follows, opens or resolves a symlink and captured buffers feed the real parser without participating cache reuse or live-read fallback, preserving explicit incomplete input coverage. A non-ignored, non-excluded symlink is skipped as the ordinary walk skips it (ADR-0030), so for UTF-8 trees both lanes select identical files; recovery completes, and coverage names the skipped symlinks as one `symlink-not-followed` limitation (count and first path) instead of refusing the whole tree. A non-UTF-8 name still refuses the tree, and a refusal names its fixed reason (#1229).
- **AC-0149** Given direct lexical rules, their owners and GOVERNS edges, when parsed from a CapturedFile, then versioned immutable metadata-only receipts bind the complete emitted fact, producer/grammar, source/capture and all original ranges; eval, placeholders and enrichment cannot acquire this binding by matching spans.
- **AC-0150** Given current facts and proposed receipts, when repository reconciliation publishes, then facts and exact current receipt associations commit atomically, mismatched final facts lose participation, ordinary mutations invalidate associations, and identical facts from different captures retain distinct receipt identities.
- **AC-0151** Given direct, retry, clone or manifest recovery, when participating facts are published, then captures and receipts are already retained under the exact registered source, same-named roots stay isolated and a persistence/capacity failure publishes no participating binding.
- **AC-1517** Given a captured TS/JS file already parsed by this process's captured lane for the same repository, when it is recovered again, then the attested parse is reused, with its evidence moved to the current commit, only when its retained bytes (digest and length), path, registered source, repository key, effective registry set and producer contract all equal the cached attestation; any difference parses the retained buffer again. Every receipt is minted afresh over the current capture, so the facts, receipts and receipt omissions equal a cold run's. An unchanged capture parses no file, a one-byte change parses exactly that file, files gone from the capture are counted as deleted, and a failed walk leaves no cached parse (ADR-0087, #438).
- **AC-0152** Given a typed fact, expected receipt and stored range index, when captured inspection runs after source edits/restart, then coherent host checks return the original strict UTF-8 bytes or explicit stale/unavailable status without arbitrary offsets, caller roots or working-tree fallback.
- **AC-0153** Given retained source, when capacity or forgetting is handled, then private bounded stores and source guards preserve immutable receipts/reviews, a refreshed inventory preview authorizes deletion of only that source's unshared bytes, and graph/job cleanup never deletes retained historical source. A receipt row whose stored source column disagrees with its header or payload fails closed on every read of a normalized row, and for a legacy row on any read of that row and on the full inventory of the source its column names, never on another source's inventory (AC-0837). On Unix, revalidation walks the retained canonical application path without following replaced symlink components or producing a new resolved-path string; original directory identity must still match.
- **AC-0154** Given the evidence and retention UI, when the user changes selection, inspects captured/current source or confirms forgetting, then the UI preserves exact fact/receipt/source identity, labels captured primary source with incomplete input coverage, rejects stale async results and previews affected references before deletion.
- **AC-0155** Given legacy graph/proposals and unrelated producers, when primary-source recovery and inspection run, then legacy EvidenceRef/staging bytes, producing tier/confidence and review state remain unchanged, raw source stays absent from graph/context/export/proposal payloads and H2/H3 verification is not inferred.
- **AC-1019** Given a captured TS/JS directory, when it is recovered, then each unresolved-call `Gap`, each `File` node and each `TestCase` the parser emits receives a provenance-only primary receipt for its exact span that the directory lane keeps only when the complete fact survives directory completion (a call completion resolves leaves no `Gap` receipt; the single-file lane mints none), admitted after every rule and declaration receipt; and a gap task cites them as `captured_primary_source` evidence, omitting a captured span past the per-span or combined evidence cap as `captured_span_over_limit` instead of failing, so a task on Vendure `packages/core/src/config` carries no working-tree evidence (#385).
- **AC-1022** Given a managed clone (direct clone or a remote manifest entry), when its TS/JS directory is captured, then the selected files are read from the exact Git blob objects of the cloned commit, not the checkout: the capture is a Git capture naming that commit and tree, each file carries its blob id and mode, and parser receipts cite those blob bytes. A selected file that is not a blob of that commit fails the recovery closed with no working-tree fallback. The chrome messaging and IndexedDB passes read the same capture, and a managed clone is captured whenever the TS pass runs, including for Pulumi infrastructure alone. Local folders keep a working-tree capture.
- **AC-1802** Given a project's saved source capture file limit (`source_capture_max_files`, AC-1800) and TS/JS selection walk bound (`ts_selection_max_visited_entries` 10,000 to 1,000,000, default 100,000, an advanced operator limit), when a source is recovered, then the TS/JS selection walk selects up to that many files and examines up to that many directory entries, including ignored ones, and fails whole one past either, so a raised capture file limit reaches the selection and not only the capture. The adapter refuses a walk bound above 1,000,000 entries or a file bound above the capture crate's 65,536-file ceiling before walking; directory depth stays fixed at 64, the walk retains at most 100,000 unfollowed symlink paths whatever the entry bound and fails whole one past that, and with no saved limits the walk behaves as before (#1653).
- **AC-1510** Given a TS/JS selection of up to 20,480 files and 320 MiB, such as a full VSCode checkout (16,180 files, about 234 MiB), when it is captured, then the capture admits it whole instead of failing on the selected-file or capture-byte maximum, and one file or byte past a maximum still fails the whole capture with no partial result. The maxima are sized from a measured whole-VSCode ingest (12.3 GB peak RSS) so a 24 GB host still fits the projected peak (#258).
- **AC-1800** Given a project's saved source capture limits (`source_capture_max_files` 1,024 to 65,536, default 20,480; `source_capture_max_mib` 16 to 1,024, default 320; both advanced operator limits, AC-1418), when a source is recovered, then its capture admits up to that many selected files and MiB and fails whole one past either, and with no saved limits it behaves exactly as AC-1510. The capture crate refuses any bound above its hard ceilings of 65,536 files and 1 GiB (half the 2 GiB store), the per-file, span and 8 MiB manifest bounds that the MCP read reservations charge stay fixed, and a capture made under raised limits still persists and reloads after the limits are lowered, because the store checks what it holds against the hard ceilings (#1653).
- **AC-1801** Given a project's saved capture store quota (`capture_store_max_mib` 256 to 2,048, default 2,048; `capture_store_max_captures` 100 to 10,000, default 10,000; both advanced operator limits that can only lower the store's hard maxima), when it is saved or the app starts, then the retained-source store admits new captures only within it: captures already retained stay readable, a capture past the quota is refused as store capacity until space is freed or the quota raised, a value above the hard maxima is refused without changing the quota, and with no saved quota the store behaves as before (#1653).
