- **AC-1024** Given a captured source with `.py` files, when it is recovered, then those files join the same capture (when they cannot, Python keeps its uncaptured lane and the TS/JS capture stands alone), the Python pass parses their retained bytes instead of the checkout, and each fact a captured file emits whose primary provenance lies wholly in that file receives a provenance-only `py-primary-v1` receipt (grammar `python`, no rule ranges) kept only when the fact survives directory resolution unchanged and admitted after every TS/JS receipt within the extraction budget. Rule-bearing facts and facts directory resolution derives (routes, Alembic, templates) stay uncaptured; on SimpleLogin 713/758 File, 12,736/12,868 Gap, 3,489/3,498 Symbol and 1,245/1,245 TestCase nodes are captured (#385).
- **AC-1026** Given a captured source with `.go` files, when it is recovered, then those files join the same capture as the Python lane's (when the combined set cannot be retained, Go and Python keep their uncaptured lanes and the TS/JS capture stands alone), the Go pass parses their retained bytes instead of the checkout, and each fact a captured file emits whose primary provenance lies wholly in that file receives a provenance-only `go-primary-v1` receipt (grammar `go`, no rule ranges) kept only when the fact survives directory resolution unchanged and admitted after every receipt already held within the extraction budget. Rule-bearing facts and facts directory resolution derives stay uncaptured; on Miniflux 441/441 File, 2,749/2,749 Symbol, 1,236/1,236 TestCase, 175/178 Endpoint and 4,354/4,444 Gap nodes are captured (#385).
- **AC-1029** Given a TS/JS primary-source receipt is minted, when its producer pins are recorded, then `parser_package` names the tree-sitter version the adapter actually links (`tree-sitter@0.27.2`, pinned exactly in the TS, Python and Go adapter manifests), and v2 and v3 receipts already persisted with the earlier recorded `tree-sitter@0.27.0` keep validating under their own contracts through the legacy pin list; the Python and Go lanes, which never recorded 0.27.0, do not accept it, and a test keeps each receipt contract's grammar and parser pins equal to its adapter manifest (#385).
- **AC-1031** Given a captured source with `.java` files, when it is recovered, then those files join the same capture as the Python and Go lanes' (when the combined set cannot be retained, those lanes keep their uncaptured reads and the TS/JS capture stands alone), the Java pass parses their retained bytes instead of the checkout, and each fact a captured file emits whose primary provenance lies wholly in that file receives a provenance-only `java-primary-v1` receipt (grammar `java`, `tree-sitter-java@0.23.5` pinned exactly, no rule ranges) kept only when the fact survives directory resolution unchanged and admitted after every receipt already held within the extraction budget. Rule-bearing facts and facts directory resolution derives (Spring endpoints, modules) stay uncaptured; on the JHipster sample app 136/136 File, 940/940 Symbol, 215/215 TestCase, 6/6 DataEntity and 312/343 Gap nodes are captured (#385).
- **AC-1312** Given a registered source whose only code is Java, when it is recovered, then its capture holds exactly its `.java` files, the Java pass parses the captured bytes rather than the checkout, its facts are published with `java-primary-v1` receipts, and a recovered Java symbol is described and each of its ranges read back verbatim from the retained capture after the checkout is gone, so an analyst can cite Java code with no TS/JS beside it (#1727).
- **AC-1033** Given a captured source with `.rs` files, when it is recovered, then those files join the same capture as the other non-TS lanes' (when the combined set cannot be retained, those lanes keep their uncaptured reads and the TS/JS capture stands alone), the Rust pass parses their retained bytes instead of the checkout (including for `#[path]` wiring and `#[cfg(test)]` module discovery), and each fact a captured file emits whose primary provenance lies wholly in that file receives a provenance-only `rs-primary-v1` receipt (grammar `rust`, `tree-sitter-rust@0.24.2` pinned exactly, no rule ranges) kept only when the fact survives directory resolution unchanged and admitted after every receipt already held within the extraction budget. Rule-bearing facts and facts directory resolution derives stay uncaptured; on three of Cartograph's own crates (32 `.rs` files) 32/32 File, 841/841 Symbol, 216/216 TestCase, 1,237/1,237 Gap and 16/19 DataEntity nodes are captured (#385).
- **AC-1035** Given a captured source with `.kt` or `.kts` files, when it is recovered, then those files join the same capture as the other non-TS lanes' (when the combined set cannot be retained, those lanes keep their uncaptured reads and the TS/JS capture stands alone), the Kotlin pass parses their retained bytes instead of the checkout, and each fact a captured file emits whose primary provenance lies wholly in that file receives a provenance-only `kt-primary-v1` receipt (grammar `kotlin`, `tree-sitter-kotlin-ng@1.1.0` pinned exactly, no rule ranges) kept only when the fact survives directory resolution unchanged and admitted after every receipt already held within the extraction budget. Rule-bearing facts and facts the directory join derives (unresolved-call gaps, modules, navigation screens) stay uncaptured; on SimpleLogin Android (175 `.kt` files) 175/175 File and 816/816 Symbol nodes are captured (#385).
- **AC-1037** Given a captured source whose Python, Go, Java, Rust or Kotlin lane builds facts during directory resolution, when it is recovered, then each lane fact whose every primary evidence entry cites one captured lane file receives a provenance-only receipt under that lane's contract for its final form (minted per captured file in capture order within the extraction budget, and kept only when publication materializes the fact as minted), so facts the directory join composes from one file, such as Spring endpoints from class and method mappings, and modules, are cited from captured source; a fact citing more than one file, a file outside the capture, or a rule stays uncaptured. On JHipster all 38 Spring endpoints and 191 modules are now captured, on Miniflux all 178 endpoints (#385).
- **AC-1039** Given a captured source whose Python, Go, Java, Rust or Kotlin lane builds a fact whose primary evidence cites several captured files of that lane, when it is recovered, then the fact receives one provenance-only receipt that names its first cited file and lists the others (at most 8, sorted, each cited), with every range validated and read back against its own captured file, and it is bound at publication like a single-file receipt; a fact citing a file outside the capture or a rule stays uncaptured. On SimpleLogin captured endpoints rise from 12 to 276 of 430, as Flask routes are cited together with their Blueprint mounts (#385).
- **AC-1041** Given a system manifest that declares `state_json`, `pulumi_json` or `otel_jsonl` inputs for a repository, when the system is recovered, then the declared inputs inside the manifest folder are captured under their own identity `input/<source id>` (never the repository's capture or commit), the T1 parsers read the captured bytes, and observed evidence cites that identity and the declared relative path; a declared input outside the manifest folder or past the capture limits is still read but keeps uncaptured evidence and is named in its producer's coverage reason as not captured, so unverified; captured inputs are never listed as source registrations of the system (#385, ADR-0080).
- **AC-1043** Given a recovered system whose manifest observation inputs were captured, when the recovery is published, then each repository's input capture is retained beside that repository's own captures under its input identity, its captures, files and bytes are counted in that source's retained-source preview and bound by its fingerprint, and forgetting the source removes the input captures too; a capture that cannot be retained fails the recovery like the source's own (#385, ADR-0080).
- **AC-1045** Given a recovered system whose manifest observation inputs were captured, when the recovery is published, then each fact whose observation evidence cites only one captured input file gets a provenance-only `obs-input-v1` receipt for the exact spans that evidence records, owned by the input identity `input/<source id>` (its source id and repo key), minted from the published view of the facts (a node's last occurrence, an edge's merged occurrences), admitted within the owning source's receipt share less what the source's own receipts of that recovery took (one that does not fit is a named store-capacity omission, recorded per fact only when the fact has no retained receipt of its own), counted in that source's retained-source preview and bound by its fingerprint, and reclaimed when the source is forgotten; a fact whose observation cites anything else, or an uncaptured input, gets none (#385, ADR-0079).
- **AC-1047** Given a recovered system whose observed receipts were retained, when the recovery is published, then each retained observed receipt is published as its fact's observed binding (fact, owning repo key, receipt id, complete fact digest) in `source_node_observations` or `source_edge_observations`, in the same transaction as the primary bindings and beside them, so a fact can hold both; each role is replaced per repo, checked against the final fact and never taken from another repo, and a fact write drops it as it drops a primary binding; an exact v2 association store migrates to v3 with empty observation tables; the retained-source preview counts observed bindings as current references and binds them in its fingerprint, and a later recovery reclaims every observed receipt that neither binds nor is pinned, also for a source with no captured code of its own (#385, ADR-0079).
- **AC-1049** Given a fact with a current observed binding, when it is inspected in the app, then `describe_observed_source` returns the observed receipt (its input identity `input/<source id>`, the registered source, the owning repo and each cited range) and `read_observed_source` reads a range back from the retained input capture, both checked against the current observed binding and the selected fact's complete digest; an observed read accepts only the source's input identity and a primary read never accepts an observed receipt; a superseded observed receipt can no longer be read; the captured-source panel lists the ranges under "Observed in", labelled as an observation and not a declaration, and shows only text of the current observed receipt (#385, ADR-0079).
- **AC-1051** Given a scoped MCP session whose scope covers a fact with a current observed binding, when `cartograph_evidence` is called with kind `retained_observed_input`, then the describe resolves the observed binding at the session's revision and returns a target that names the source's input identity `input/<source id>` and an `obs-input-v1` receipt with scope `observed_input_only`, and the read returns that captured span with redaction applied; kind `retained_primary_source` still resolves only the primary binding, a read whose kind does not match its target's role is refused, and a target relabelled as the source's own is refused (#385, ADR-0079).
- **AC-1053** Given a captured source whose recovery wants infrastructure, when it is recovered, then its `.tf` files (the Terraform adapter's own walk) join the same capture as the other lanes' (when the combined set cannot be retained, Terraform keeps its uncaptured read and the TS/JS capture stands alone), the Terraform pass parses their retained bytes instead of the checkout, and each final fact (after local module expansion) whose primary provenance lies wholly in one captured `.tf` file receives a provenance-only `tf-primary-v1` receipt (grammar `terraform`, `hcl-edit@0.9.8` pinned exactly as grammar and parser, no rule ranges), kept only when publication materializes the fact as minted and admitted after every receipt already held within the extraction budget. Local module directories are still listed in the checkout, a module file outside the capture is read there and carries no receipt, and agents and scoped MCP evidence accept the new receipt prefix (#385).
- **AC-1055** Given a captured source whose capture holds its Python selection, when it is recovered, then the Python observed-infrastructure pass (registry clients, HTTP fetch and event sites, configuration reads) reads every file and every followed module from the capture's retained bytes instead of the checkout, follows an identity only into a module among the captured paths, and a checkout edit after capture changes none of its facts; these facts carry no receipt and stay uncited (#385).
- **AC-0280** Given a captured TS/JS file, when it is parsed, then every directly emitted callable declaration also receives a provenance-only primary receipt for its exact span, so code that owns no rule is citable; rule receipts are admitted first (their own budget behavior is AC-0292) and declarations only use the remaining extraction budget, minted under a per-file bound and admitted greedily in manifest order, with each omitted declaration counted under its file path and never silently dropped (#691).
- **AC-0292** Given a captured TS/JS repository whose rule receipts exceed the extraction receipt budget, or a retained receipt store without room for a recovery's receipts, when it is recovered, then the recovery completes: receipts are admitted greedily in deterministic manifest order, every rule fact without a receipt stays in the graph and is counted under its file path, store omissions are counted the same way, and coverage names each kind under its own limiting capability (`ts-rule-receipts`, `receipt-store-capacity`) as a tool limitation. Integrity failures still reject the recovery, already-stored receipts are never removed, and bindings cite only receipts the store actually retained (#722).
- **AC-0282** Given a captured TS/JS file, when it is parsed, then each top-level non-callable variable, each first-level property of its object-literal initializer, each method decorator and the header of each callable larger than the 8 KiB inspection range also gets its own fact and a provenance-only primary receipt for its exact span; existing Symbol spans and identities are unchanged, quoted keys are never persisted, a header that alone exceeds the range is flagged as a named gap, and a section or header too large for one range is flagged as a named gap with oversized sections described one level deeper, and the coverage inventory names declarations left without a receipt as a limitation (#693, ADR-0039).
- **AC-0283** Given a captured TS/JS callable larger than the 8 KiB inspection range, when it is parsed, then each non-callable `const`/`let`/`var` declarator directly in its body also gets a citable Variable fact scoped to that callable (with its object-literal sections, bounded as in AC-0282); nested blocks, nested callables and callables within the range are unchanged, and these locals are minted after the file's own declarations so a tight budget omits only locals, which are counted (#699, ADR-0039).
- **AC-0299** Given a newly recovered TypeScript fact, when its primary-source receipt is minted, then it uses contract v3 (`ts-primary-v3:`). Each range is stored only as role, index and byte span, and its evidence and captured-file binding are derived from the receipt's single file, repo key and commit. Decoding expands v3 to the same ranges as the equivalent v2 receipt, so resolver, inspection, MCP and history behave unchanged. Exactly one wire encoding is valid per receipt: a range form that doesn't match its version, an unknown or duplicated field, an explicit `null` commit, a range count over the limit, or an expansion over the per-receipt byte bound are all rejected. v1 and v2 receipts keep decoding with unchanged ids (#725, ADR-0046).
- **AC-0301** Given a retained source the user confirms forgetting, when its capture bytes are removed, then in one transaction its receipts pinned by a saved investigation or a staged proposal keep their metadata, marked capture-forgotten and bound to the tombstone of the forget that first marked them, and every other receipt of that source is reclaimed. A tombstone records the source, the forgotten captures, the time and the reclaimed and pinned counts, and a reclaim ledger maps each reclaimed receipt id to it. An exact receipt id then resolves as present, capture-forgotten, reclaimed or unknown, never by source or path. The ledger is bounded by the existing receipt ceiling: the oldest entries are dropped first and then resolve as unknown. Tombstones are kept only while a ledger row or a marked receipt references them, and their recorded capture ids share one store-wide byte pool bounded by the receipt store's byte ceiling; when it is full a forget is still recorded, with its capture ids marked truncated. Recapturing identical bytes makes a capture-forgotten receipt present again. If the reclaim fails after capture deletion, the next confirmed forget completes it. The receipt store migrates from v1 to v2 in one transaction, a failure leaves v1 intact, and fresh and migrated stores have identical schemas (#725, ADR-0046 decision 3). Saved work packets also pin the receipts they cite: a packet that cites a source cannot be saved while that source is being forgotten, and the store refuses any packet body saved without its receipt index, so an older app instance still holding the store open cannot write unpinned packets (#749).
- **AC-0314** Given a recovered fact whose primary receipt was omitted because a declaration, rule or store-capacity receipt bound was reached, when the repository is published, then the graph store records a positive per-fact omission record bound to the complete fact digest the omitted receipt was emitted for (kept only while it equals the published fact's digest, and never for a fact that has a retained receipt binding; a later binding from any repository also takes precedence over an existing record when the fact is selected) alongside a per-repository omission index, migrating exact v1 association schemas to v2 in one transaction and invalidating omission rows whenever a fact is rewritten or deleted. Each fact in a bounded source selection carries its omission outcome, read in the same transaction and against the same copied graph revision as its association state, with the observed store revision returned and an expected-revision variant that refuses a changed basis (omission row events advance the revision even when no fact changes): `Omitted` with its repository key and omission kind when its digest in that revision matches a recorded omission row, `NotOmitted` only when every covered repository has a complete omission index and no row matches, and `Unrecorded` otherwise; the repository index is recorded as `incomplete` when omissions exceed the 262,144-record bound or when a producer only has bare per-file counts without fact keys, and `NotOmitted` never means a receipt exists, which only a current source binding establishes (#725).
- **AC-0319** Given a recovery of a source that completes and publishes its graph, when no other recovery of that source holds the source's retention guard, then every receipt of that source not pinned (by a saved investigation, a staged proposal, a saved work packet or a prepared task) and not bound by the current graph is reclaimed into the ledger under a tombstone with cause `superseded`, and resolves as superseded (unavailable, never forgotten). Pinned and capture-forgotten rows are untouched and capture bytes are kept. A busy source is skipped and settles on its next publish. A task prepared before or during the reclaim keeps its receipts. Existing tombstones migrate with cause `forgotten` (#725, ADR-0046 decision 5).
- **AC-0373** Given receipts persisted to the receipt store, when a v3 receipt is stored, then its per-file header is stored once per distinct header and source and the receipt as a compact body, every read rebuilds byte-identical canonical receipt bytes and id or fails closed (missing, foreign or contradictory header or body), the store budget charges each retained header once plus every body, forget and supersession remove a header only with its last referencing row, legacy rows stay readable unchanged, and an exact v3 store migrates to v4 in one transaction (#725, ADR-0046 decision 7).
- **AC-0497** Given a receipt store shared by several retained sources, when one source's recovery persists its receipts, then each receipt that would take the recovery's retained rows past that source's share, a ceiling proportional to its largest retained capture's bytes among all sources' (never below a sixteenth of the store, never above it, by count and by bytes with each header charged once), becomes a named omission instead of failing; already-stored identical rows stay retained and count against the share, and the store-wide bound still applies (#725).
- **AC-0503** Given a TypeScript directory recovery, when its receipts are held before persistence, then each file's identity is kept once and shared by every receipt minted for that file, each range keeps only its role, index and byte span, and the full receipt is rebuilt on demand with byte-identical canonical JSON and id. The extraction and per-file declaration budgets measure this retained memory, charging each shared header once, only when the first receipt under it is admitted; a refused receipt charges nothing. The same budget therefore holds more receipts, and omissions are still named by fact. By the owner's 2026-11-18 decision the ceilings are sized to cite all of REA, measured: one extraction and the host receipt store each admit up to 200,000 receipts and 128 MiB (previously 100,000 and 64 MiB), and one source binds up to 200,000 facts. Anything over a ceiling is still omitted and named, never dropped silently. The host checks, persists and binds the compact form directly, serializing one receipt at a time instead of holding the whole batch's JSON (#725, ADR-0046 decision 7).
- **AC-0822** Given another connection holding the receipt store's write lock, when the store is opened or written, then it waits up to 5 s for that lock before failing busy, the same busy timeout as every other Cartograph SQLite store (#1653); before, it waited 1 s.
- **AC-0836** Given a receipt store shared by several retained sources, when one source's supersession reads its receipt inventory, then only rows whose stored source is that source are rebuilt and decoded, so the decode work and decoded receipts held follow that source's rows, not the whole store; the returned ids, their order, the receipt bytes and the supersession result equal the store-wide read they replace (#1233, ADR-0046 decision 5).
- **AC-0837** Given a corrupt or foreign-owned receipt row of another source, when one source's supersession inventory is read, then that row does not fail it; the row still fails closed, without echoing its contents, on a read of that row and on the inventory of the source its stored column names, and the bounded store-wide SQL checks of ADR-0046 decision 7 still run on every read (#1233).
- **AC-0838** Given a receipt store whose capacity a recovery's receipts did not fit, so they became store-capacity omissions, when the source holding the store is forgotten, or a later publish of the same source supersedes the receipts its graph no longer binds, then the reclaimed rows free their capacity and the next recovery keeps every receipt that now fits, with no store-capacity omission (#725).
- **AC-0839** Given a recovery with store-capacity receipt omissions, when its coverage inventory names them under `receipt-store-capacity`, then the reason points at forgetting a retained source, or recovering it after it changed (supersession reclaim), as the way to free the space of receipts that nothing pins and the current graph no longer binds, says that a recovery of an unchanged source frees nothing and that neither frees room under a source's own share limit, and no longer says the store cannot reclaim space (#725).
- **Security:** Private local retention only; no target writes, implicit network, model disclosure, arbitrary-root reads or confidence upgrades. Deletion is exact-source and preview-bound.
- **Performance:** SPEC-04 capture limits, bounded enumeration and receipts, selected-object span reads and try-only locks; participating TS cache bypass is explicit.
- **Trace:** H3 prerequisite #395 · `adapters-lang-ts`, `source-capture`, `core-graph`, `ingest`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0039, ADR-0046 · T-0148..0155,T-0280,T-0282,T-0283,T-0299,T-0301,T-0314,T-0319,T-0373,T-0497,T-0836,T-0837
- **Trace:** H3 prerequisite #395 · `adapters-lang-ts`, `source-capture`, `core-graph`, `ingest`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0039, ADR-0046 · T-0148..0155,T-0280,T-0282,T-0283,T-0299,T-0301,T-0314,T-0319,T-0373,T-0497,T-0822


### US-0029 — Keep job ownership accurate across concurrent processes and retries
- **Actor:** Engineer running recovery and agent investigations
- **As a** engineer **I want** each live job attempt to keep exclusive execution ownership **so that** another startup or retry cannot steal it or accept stale worker updates.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0156** Given a new or legacy job store, when execution metadata initializes and rows are returned, then a private versioned namespace and recorded new-job attempts coexist with unchanged historical fields, while ownerless legacy rows remain explicitly unknown and require fresh operations rather than same-ID adoption or retry.
- **AC-0157** Given participating job workers, when execution is claimed or cloned into blocking work, then a host-owned persistent try-only OS guard excludes another claim until the last worker holder exits, without a database mutex spanning lock acquisition or a substituted lock granting ownership; before SQL claim, its file and rooted directory entries are synced, Windows handles must report NTFS and permit real write-access directory flushes, and unsupported/query/sync failures release ownership with the job unchanged.
- **AC-0158** Given an execution handle, when progress, checks, finish or fail occur, then the exact store/job/attempt/owner and allowed status fence every update; same-attempt cancellation remains unchanged and missing, interrupted or superseded ownership stops work distinctly.
- **AC-0159** Given another startup or confirmed owner exit, when recovery runs, then busy live attempts remain untouched and only the exact observed still-running recorded candidates whose locks were acquired become interrupted, without overwriting concurrent terminal transitions or reporting unchanged rows.
- **AC-0160** Given cancellation, cleanup or supported retry, when transitions race, then guarded transactions preserve the winning status, live cancelled workers exclude retry, source/kind admission precedes mutation and one retry atomically claims the next attempt without stale writes or an unowned queued interval.
- **AC-0161** Given plugin, ingest, add and single/class escalation workers, when progress, publication, cancellation or async waiter loss occurs, then all production lifecycle writes use the retained execution, blocking closures preserve ownership, and completed proposals remain staged and reviewable after cancellation under AC-0128, including an already-started model result that completes after job cleanup; missing history still stops subsequent work and lifecycle writes.
- **AC-0162** Given the Jobs surface, when legacy-unknown work or a busy/rejected retry is shown, then historical state is not presented as proven live or retryable, fresh recovery guidance is explicit and action failures remain visible without inventing a successful transition.
- **AC-0163** Given history and independent source/evidence contracts, when ownership migration, retry, recovery or job cleanup runs, then existing job kinds/fields and staged proposal IDs/content/reviews remain intact, tracking grants no tier/source freshness upgrade and source guards retain their separate scope.
- **AC-0823** Given another connection holding a write lock on the job store, the source registry or the work-packet store, when that store is opened or written, then it waits up to 5 s for the lock before failing busy; this is the one busy timeout every Cartograph SQLite store uses, pinned by a test for each store (#1653).
- **Security:** Private host-generated ownership only; no caller tokens, force takeover, target writes, added egress or implicit model replay.
- **Performance:** Short SQLite transitions, owned plans and try-only OS reservations; no mutex spans long work or blocking lock acquisition.
- **Trace:** Durable runtime prerequisite #393 (SPEC-07) · `app`, `work-packets`, `ui` · — · T-0156..0163,T-0823

### US-0030 — Inspect cited local definitions behind rule conditions
- **Actor:** Engineer or agent investigating business-rule evidence
- **As a** engineer **I want** rule conditions linked to supported local initializer structure and its gaps **so that** I can inspect source reasoning without mistaking it for established runtime or business meaning.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0164** Given stored rule evidence, when v1 or v2 is read or newly produced, then v1 retains its original wire meaning and bytes, v2 requires bounded local-definition evidence, and null, malformed or unsupported version combinations fail explicitly without rewriting historical content.
- **AC-0165** Given a condition dependency, when a local definition is recovered, then the declaration is a scope-proven directly initialized simple const in the same callable with structured before-use proof; shadowing, TDZ, mutation, unsupported scope crossings and non-admitted bindings retain explicit gaps.
- **AC-0166** Given an admitted initializer, when its expression is captured, then a cited flat arena preserves known operator/operand order, grouping, short-circuit and member syntax without substitution or evaluation, while runtime values and unsupported forms remain explicitly unestablished.
- **AC-0167** Given source expressions or excessive analysis, when local definitions are emitted, then every stored display/literal uses the source-aware sanitizer and all definition/node/dependency/depth/byte limits fail visibly without leaking withheld text or claiming truncated expressions complete.
- **AC-0168** Given nested definition evidence and revision changes, when source traversal or ordinary cache retargeting runs, then every declaration, use, initializer, expression, dependency and redaction reference is visited in the declared order and the complete fact hash reflects the actual revision.
- **AC-0169** Given captured parsing and historical receipts, when v2 definitions are produced or current facts change, then new versioned receipts bind the complete fact and every original range, v1 identities remain unchanged and readable, and oversized, stale, missing or mismatched inventories cannot acquire a partial or substituted current association.
- **AC-0170** Given shared context and the source rule inventory, when definitions are shown, then both surfaces retain the same structured evidence and citations, safely render stored expressions, disclose v1 evidence absence and unresolved value/business meaning, and preserve provenance filtering and the original condition.
- **AC-0171** Given the frozen Vendure oracle/input, when the local-definition increment is assessed, then repeated source-only runs and an independent source audit record separate revision/hash/score artifacts, preserve the eight-rule denominator and 41-case obligations, and distinguish definition improvement from complete rules or producer input closure.
- **Security:** T0 is model-free; no target writes, raw-source rereads in shared projections, secret-bearing fallbacks, tier upgrades or historical proposal rewrites.
- **Performance:** Bounded flat arenas and traversal; 16 definitions, 64 expression nodes and 128 initializer dependencies per rule, depth 16 (8 before AC-1046) and definition-chain depth 4, with existing payload/receipt caps retained.
- **Trace:** H2 prerequisite #398 (SPEC-08) · adapters-lang-ts, core-graph, spec, context-hub, app, ui · — · T-0164..0171

### US-0031 — Retain the exact source basis of agent investigations
- **Actor:** Engineer reviewing agent evidence
- **As a** engineer **I want** agent tasks and proposals to retain their actual source selections **so that** I can distinguish captured parser input, unverified evidence and later changes without altering what was reviewed.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0172** Given a supported gap task, when evidence is planned and acquired, then typed supporting facts and original citation occurrences follow deterministic candidate order under separate lookahead, attempted-read, supplied-item and captured-validation-byte limits, with explicit omissions and unread tails; unreadable legacy candidates do not consume supplied slots, and unused-tail failures cannot abort a completed prefix.
- **AC-0173** Given graph facts and current source associations, when a task is prepared, then one coherent read selects the bounded window, sorted per-source/access-kind guards are acquired outside store mutexes and one authoritative recheck rejects a changed visited basis; equal facts with different receipts remain distinct, and missing, invalid and absent bindings never collapse into one state.
- **AC-0174** Given a participating citation, when its evidence is acquired, then exact source/fact/digest/receipt/inventory/range checks return original retained strict UTF-8 bytes after checkout mutation or disappearance; corrupt, forgotten, unsupported or over-budget captured reads fail without live fallback, while valid absence remains explicitly working-tree unverified. A working-tree span longer than the per-span cap, or one that would pass the combined evidence cap, is omitted as unreadable rather than failing the whole task (#1635).
- **AC-0175** Given a prepared task and result, when staged, then immutable v2 identity binds graph and selected associations, every supplied evidence origin/text fingerprint and all candidate memberships/summary fingerprints; literal v1 bytes/IDs/reviews remain unchanged, malformed version/shape/bounds fail and raw task text remains transient under the existing replay policy.
- **AC-0176** Given preview or single/class execution, when evidence origins or receipts change, then the same prepared path changes the exact redacted payload and consent identity even for equal span text, preserving per-tier consent, broker limits and one-instance cloud grants without provider or source-write authority from retention.
- **AC-0177** Given a selected staged proposal, when current-basis assessment is requested, then separate graph, association, retained-availability and unverified dimensions report the observed state or operational failure without rewriting history, persisting a blanket fresh flag or certifying legacy references; source retention previews count actual v2 receipt references.
- **AC-0178** Given reingest, forgetting, cancellation or cleanup during a model call, when a result completes, then valid retained execution stages the original copied task basis, releases acquisition guards before the call and preserves completed results; later assessment reports changes without altering graph, provenance, exports or legacy history.
- **AC-0179** Given preview and staged-history UI, when mixed evidence, selection limits or a changed/unavailable basis is shown, then the user can distinguish each origin and assessment dimension, late responses cannot replace another proposal selection and an accepted result is never shown as fresh or as a curated item on the strength of review alone: its curated state comes from the projection (amended for #1746, ADR-0061).
- **Security:** Source acquisition is host-qualified and local; no arbitrary roots, target writes, captured-to-live fallback, extra egress, raw task archive or confidence upgrade.
- **Performance:** 64 request lookahead/attempt cap plus one slot key, 8 candidates/12 supplied spans, 8 KiB per span/48 KiB combined, 128 MiB captured validation work, 64 KiB source basis and 128 KiB staged record; graph/manifest costs remain separate.
- **Trace:** H3 prerequisite #401 (SPEC-09) · agents, core-graph, app, ui · — · T-0172..0179


### US-0032 — Investigate recovered context with durable specialists
- **Actor:** Developer
- **As a** developer **I want** named specialists to query recovered context, inspect original evidence and retain cited findings **so that** I can investigate business behavior, design evidence and gaps from the app and revisit the exact basis of an answer.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0180** Given a scoped question and a selected specialist, when an investigation starts, then the host binds the exact versioned Domain analyst or Evidence auditor definition, provider, normalized scope and limit profile to a distinct investigation contract; all findings remain T3/InferredWeak and no synthetic edge, target write or confirmed fact is created. New @3 defaults use compact recovered context and explain that empty initial input requires discovery through host-executed query/read actions; original @1/@2 definitions retain their prompts and cumulative-input behavior without changing saved task meaning.
- **AC-0181** Given concurrent identical starts or a lost start acknowledgment, when the same origin/request nonce is submitted, then one transaction returns the same durable task and initial Job; changed material conflicts, execution attachment is atomic, and history survives job cleanup. Disposable regression fixtures expose only static start-stage and numeric SQLite-code diagnostics on failure, with scoped per-thread capture and unchanged production errors, timeout and retry behavior.
- **AC-0182** Given a prepared frozen graph and cumulative selected-fact ledger, when a specialist queries or acquires additional evidence, then bounded queries use the original authorized snapshot and one coherent union observation rejects graph or previously selected association changes without rebinding inputs or substituting a current revision. Page budgets include evidence inventories; a fitting ordered prefix preserves its continuation cursor, only returned facts enter the ledger, and an individually oversized fact fails without being skipped.
- **AC-0183** Given an admitted typed fact and original receipt role/index, when evidence is read, then retained strict original bytes and nested v2 occurrences remain available after checkout deletion under exact receipt validation; missing, forgotten or invalid participating evidence never falls back, legacy absence remains unverified, and repeated attempts/full-file validation consume cumulative limits.
- **AC-0184** Given a provider action, when the coordinator processes it, then only one complete strict query_context, read_evidence or finish value is admitted, tool actions are journaled before execution, invented references and raw-source replay from any supplied item fail, and invalid output causes no hidden repair or additional model call except the bounded source-replay correction below. Ordinary analyst/auditor finish refusals retain a fixed, content-free validation class in the terminal error and event; an ordinary read of an unadmitted fact has its own fixed diagnostic. For one ordinary source-replay refusal only, the rejected finish is discarded and a fixed generic correction prompt may authorize one new finish-only invocation within existing limits; the refusal and subsequent invocation are durably ordered, and cancellation, replay, metadata, prior-finding and other refusal protections remain unchanged. Legacy terminal strings remain readable and staged completion refusal/retry rules are unchanged (#937).
- **AC-0185** Given a local or cloud investigation call, when its bounded provider API executes, then request/response entity-body acquisition, generation reservations, actual stop conditions, verified TLS, no redirects/retries and the remaining task deadline are enforced before action decoding; measured usage and unknown outcomes remain distinct from reservations or estimates and legacy completion remains compatible. Bounded transport @2 requests Ollama JSON actions with optional thinking disabled, binds that version into consent and new provider records, and leaves older unknown transport identities unchanged. An inactive optional response-schema field is bound before firewall preview/consent, admitted for bounded Ollama (extended by AC-0342, AC-0567 and AC-0568 to OpenAI-compatible, hosted OpenAI and Anthropic transports), counted within existing input/request bounds and emitted as object-valued format. Absent-schema bytes remain unchanged; malformed schema text, schemas containing JSON escapes or text changed by secret redaction, and unsupported transports are refused without changing schema semantics. No investigation selects a schema (#1309). A provider stop at the reserved output-token limit (Anthropic `max_tokens`, OpenAI-compatible or Ollama `length`) fails as the distinct text-free code `output_limit_reached`; it keeps the Incomplete terminal classification, and every other non-success stop remains `incomplete_response` (AC-0570).
- **AC-0186** Given successive cloud steps, when consent is previewed or approved, then each exact provider/model/payload/step/limit binding requires its own eligible grant consumed with invocation start; stale or reused approval, cancellation, local-only policy and declined consent cause no new call, and hiding the dialog or declining does not authorize fallback.
- **AC-0187** Given cancellation, disconnect, cleanup or process death, when investigation lifecycle is observed, then live execution ownership remains authoritative, a pending invocation is durably distinguishable from pre-call interruption, unknown outcomes are never automatically replayed, and a valid late finish is retained against its original input without executing any late tool action.
- **AC-0188** Given admitted context and findings, when journal/history/result records are stored or loaded, then strict versioned bounded SQL decoding (including typed round-trip serialization before comparison allocation), global admission limits, redacted history, source-free events and transactionally indexed actual receipt references preserve privacy and identity; capacity or storage failures never acknowledge a successful answer or silently evict history. An explicit effective time policy preserves the registered non-time fields exactly, retains unchanged defaults and historical static lookup, and reconstructs the frozen active-time allowance from saved limits without consulting current settings (#1633). Desktop task creation freezes active time and request timeout together; nonce retries keep that original pair, subsequent calls and replay validate it, and MCP retains its default-only contract.
- **AC-0189** Given an old investigation citation, when it is inspected after reingest or forgetting, then the host reads only the saved receipt occurrence and reports retained availability separately from historical meaning; graph-only and legacy citations disclose their limitations and cannot silently open current source.
- **AC-0190** Given the app investigation surface, when a developer asks, navigates, approves a step, cancels or reconnects, then Workspace, palette and Jobs reach the same durable task, ordered real activity and cited findings; stale responses cannot replace another selection, missing event pages recover explicitly, existing shortcuts remain stable and unknown outcomes have no generic Retry/Resume. Follow-ups preserve the saved specialist selection; if it or a selected composer version is unavailable in the loaded catalog, starting requires an explicit available replacement, with the missing version explained and historical identity preserved across catalog refresh or recovery.
- **AC-0191** Given pinned captured TypeScript inputs and an independent expected answer, when the implementation is verified, then production-module scripted and controlled real-provider investigations record actual query/read/finish steps, citations, role/model/input identities and limits without supplying the oracle; this evidence preserves the full unexecuted H4/H5 cross-ingress obligations and never establishes complete confirmed Vendure rules from T3 output.
- **AC-0204** Given independent reads of an investigation's detail and saved result, when they disagree about whether a result exists (a read that straddles the result commit), then the observation is rejected as inconsistent: the task is never recorded as completed without findings or as unfinished with findings, previously saved findings and activity stay visible, and the next refresh reads a consistent pair.
- **AC-0234** Given a model step, when its input is prepared, then a versioned per-invocation disclosure manifest names exactly which fact, artifact and source representations were supplied, is validated against the full host acquisition ledger before the call, and is bound to the durable step; the host ledger keeps complete acquisition identity under the existing transient-text retention rules. Compact @3 input shares only identical complete fact/inventory representations, preserves differing representations and query continuations, and seals hashes against the actual firewall-redacted payload before consent or dispatch; schema-2 results bind the saved finishing step and disclosure.
- **AC-0235** Given a finding that cites evidence acquired by the host but not disclosed to that invocation, or only an opaque evidence handle, when the finding is admitted, then that source-support claim is rejected; a handle proves identity, not that content was read. A host-selected source working set retains omitted fact/role/index handles and reasons, while source eligibility is checked against that invocation, including on historical result reads.
- **AC-0236** Given a compact projection and the current cumulative envelope on the same pinned acquisition, when both are measured, then bytes, reported input and output tokens, calls and latency are recorded separately, unavailable measurements stay unknown, and compaction records each omission with a stable handle for reacquisition.
- **AC-0260** Given an explicitly selected @4 specialist and an admitted investigation ledger, when the next model input is prepared, then a new immutable input version describes initial or subsequent turn progress from durable ordered action/input evidence, including the last completed result, newly discovered facts, exact continuation and unchanged repeated queries. It exposes cumulative raw-query bytes consumed and remaining using the same 96 KiB constant as host admission, without conflating compact payload bytes with acquired-response bytes. Invalid progress bindings fail before dispatch; reopening preserves the same basis, follow-ups start their own progress and retain the saved specialist selection. The @1–@3 bytes and prompts remain unchanged. The model still selects every action; the host does not paginate, read, retry or fabricate a finish for it, and existing source-citation and no-replay gates remain required. An explicitly offered `replay_query` whose selector authenticates against the saved ledger executes normally and remains distinct from an accidental repeated raw query. For the active @18 analyst, a canonical metadata read already present in the same frozen input is answered by a bounded runtime notice rather than a second acquisition: it consumes an action attempt but adds no query result, inventory entry, citation, or raw acquisition bytes. The next admitted call uses the existing reserved finish opportunity and asks for supported partial knowledge; a model that still proposes a read terminates explicitly without a fabricated result. The refusal and next-call mode must be reconstructed from the authenticated saved journal on reopen. After an unknown-anchor or unknown-node refusal, the next exploratory query schema offers a fresh all-scope page within the already admitted context instead of another guessed identifier or stale cursor. Generic historical refusal feedback preserves its original schema. Other specialist versions retain their historical behavior (#1634, #1332).
- **AC-0261** Given caller-selected exact captured source targets, when a new versioned investigation runs, then immutable requirements bind fact, original role/index, snapshot and receipt identity; stale selection fails closed and the host reports acquired, supplied and cited evidence separately. Only exact source actually supplied to the finishing invocation meets a requirement; unmet requirements preserve valid partial results without forced tools or retries, legacy tasks remain not_assessed, and reopen/history retain the assessment.
- **AC-0262** Given a captured source range used by an inspection requirement, when structured locations are presented, then byte and computed line/column units remain explicit and are validated against the exact retained source version; receipt inventory ordinals are never mistaken for original role indices, and opaque IDs or current working-tree text never establish source locations.
- **Security:** Per-tier and per-step cloud consent, host-owned scope/execution, closed read tools, all-input replay admission, transient raw payloads and bounded durable history; no target writes or delegation.
- **Performance:** SPEC-10 limits model/tool steps, cumulative context/source/file validation, body acquisition, time, events and total admission; start returns durable identity before expensive work on a worker.
- **AC-0267** Given a frozen V7 synthetic control and independently resolved source/inventory obligations per role, when the explicitly selected acceptance stages run, then immutable bounded receipts separate actual runtime/mechanical outcomes from an authenticated independent semantic review; the analyst stage exits before auditor dispatch, every continuation stays within the ordinary V7 budgets, and no prior control failure or remaining Vendure/native acceptance requirement is reclassified. Deterministic classification, receipt, preparation and review-binding tests never invoke a provider. Future-run usage checks preserve an authenticated trailing unadmitted invocation as unknown total telemetry, reject malformed response journals, and never turn a failed/no-result run into a passing control.

The non-bounded `complete` admits a response through the same envelope check (Refused as `ProviderError::Refused`, anything else not admitted as `InvalidResponse`), with a 1 MiB read cap and no retry. The response id, model and `prompt_tokens`/`completion_tokens` and optional `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` usage are recorded, and the transport workspace accounts for the choice envelope. `GET {base}v1/models` lists served model ids with a bounded read; no weight digest is claimed, and embeddings are unsupported. Earlier prerequisite activation-closed statements in AC-0480/0484/0485/0487/0488/0489/0553 describe their original slices; this explicit @18 integration supersedes those availability restrictions only. Sizing representations remain non-authoritative and acceptance-run authorization remains separate. UI preparation remains separately traced by AC-0553 (#1385; parent #1382).
- **Trace:** H4 · `app`, `ui` · — · T-0552

- **AC-0553** Given an exact saved @18 request7-family DTO or a test-supplied prospective compatible catalog, UI type/family/policy validation recognizes @18, requires explicit advertised selection to construct request7, and rejects absent/incompatible policy or a mismatched summary schema. Existing selection/order, the @3 default, historical @17 recognition and numeric limits remain unchanged. Fake advertised-catalog, mocked IPC and saved-DTO tests establish preparation only: the live backend catalog and public/store/run/resume gates remain unchanged and @18 dispatch stays unavailable. This does not authorize provider, captured-profile or acceptance execution (#1586; preparation for #1385).

Omitted selection remains @10 and historical definition/receipt bytes remain unchanged. Receipt assembly labels @18 only when the frozen analyst request and authenticated saved specialist both match @18 and the role is analyst; mismatched @18 identities are refused. Existing analyst versions and shared evidence-auditor@11 collection retain their historical receipt family. Ordinary synthetic preparation and identity tests establish tooling support only, not captured-profile execution, provider authorization or #598 acceptance (#1589).

- **AC-0556** Given a desktop-started investigation over a real estate (more than 50,000 combined graph rows), when it prepares, reads coverage or inspects evidence, then the frozen graph is read under the investigation ceiling (4 GiB, 10,000,000 rows, depth 32, 500,000,000 values) instead of failing with a limit error; MCP-started investigation tasks keep the fixed default limits for acquisition and the corresponding worker reservation, so their admission is unchanged; separately configured MCP read-session graph limits do not change investigation task acquisition (ADR-0052, #1602).

- **AC-0735** Given a stored recovered graph, when its complete-context identity is computed by streaming (`ContextSnapshot::complete_id_bounded`, or `complete_id_in` inside a scoped reader's pinned transaction), then the id is exactly the `context-v1` id `ContextSnapshot::new` assigns to the materialized graph, so `graph_snapshot_id` in ledgers and drafting staging comparisons are unchanged. The stream admits exactly the graphs `read_snapshot_bounded` admits at every row, raw byte, canonical byte, depth and value limit, delivers nodes by id then edges by (src, label, dst) by walking indexes (edges through the additive `idx_edges_src_label_dst`, which an existing store gains on open with its facts and identity unchanged) rather than sorting, and retains one row at a time instead of the whole graph. Out-of-order or duplicate input is refused, never hashed differently. This is the identity primitive for scope-aware preparation; investigation preparation does not use it yet and its limits and behaviour are unchanged (#1602).
- **Trace:** H4 · `core-graph`, `context-hub` · ADR-0052 · T-0735

- **AC-0775** Given scoped preparation computing the complete-graph identity by streaming, when it uses the preparation caps (`IdentityPassLimits::for_retained` with the task's retained limits), then the stream is admitted in aggregate under the ADR-0052 ceilings (10,000,000 rows, 4 GiB raw and canonical bytes, depth 32, 500,000,000 values) whatever the retained limits are, so a graph past an MCP-started task's 50,000-row default still gets exactly the `context-v1` id `ContextSnapshot::new` assigns, also inside a scoped reader held to the retained limits. The one row held at a time stays within the retained limits: a row whose raw stored bytes exceed the retained `max_bytes` is refused before its body is copied (`SingleRowByteLimit`), and one whose property values exceed the retained `max_values` (`SingleRowValueLimit`) or depth exceeds the retained `max_depth` is refused while it decodes, never hashed. Aggregate refusals keep their own errors, invalid row limits are refused, and uniform limits (a plain `SnapshotReadLimits`) keep exactly the `read_snapshot_bounded` admission of AC-0735. Investigation preparation is not wired to it yet, so task limits and behaviour are unchanged (ADR-0052 amendment 2026-11-18, #1602).
- **Trace:** H4 · `core-graph`, `context-hub` · ADR-0052 · T-0775

- **AC-0776** Given a scoped-preparation graph snapshot read refused at a size limit, when the investigation stops, then its saved error names the phase and the limit: `Investigation limit reached: host_limit; phase=<graph_identity|graph_scope>; limit=<snapshot_rows|snapshot_bytes|snapshot_values|snapshot_depth|snapshot_row_bytes|snapshot_row_values>.`, with raw and canonical byte refusals both reported as `snapshot_bytes`. It is classified as a limit stop and recognized as a `host_limit` message, and carries no fact, path or count. Invalid limits, invalid rows or JSON, schema and coverage failures are not snapshot size limits and keep their existing classification. Until preparation is wired (#1602), today's frozen-graph refusal keeps the plain `host_limit` message (ADR-0052 amendment 2026-11-18).

- **AC-1301** Given an investigation whose scope is a neighborhood (1 to 3 hops) or an area, when it is prepared, then the host pins one store revision (the coverage revision for a qualified task), streams the complete-graph identity inside a scoped reader at that revision (`complete_id_in` under `IdentityPassLimits::for_retained`) and retains only the scope's facts (the anchor's neighborhood, or the area's files, their DEFINED_IN members and the edges among them), never the whole graph. `graph_snapshot_id` and `scope_snapshot_id` are exactly the ids a full read would give, so ledgers, qualification and drafting staging are unchanged, and a scope prepares under retained limits the whole graph exceeds. A store that moves past the pinned revision refuses with InputChanged; a neighborhood whose anchor is missing, or whose hops are outside 1..=3, is invalid input. An `All` scope pages (AC-1058, AC-1060) (#1602, ADR-0052 amendment 2026-11-18).

- **AC-1302** Given a context prepared from a scope, when the task selects facts, then the selection re-reads only the selected nodes and edges at the pinned revision (`read_selected_facts_at_revision` under the task's limits) instead of the whole graph, and a selected fact that differs from the prepared one, or a store that moved past the pinned revision, refuses with InputChanged (#1602).

- **AC-0559** Given the desktop Investigations composer in its untouched initial state (no explicit budget, the initial specialist and no captured parent), when the host catalog advertises domain-analyst@19 with a valid Expanded (`investigation-v2`) request7 policy, then the composer preselects @19 under Expanded. @19 is a schema-backed analyst, so a local model receives a structured action schema. When @19 or its policy is not advertised, the composer keeps @3 under Standard. An explicit budget or specialist choice, a captured parent, an in-flight start or a pending start is never overridden, including on later catalog refreshes. Older specialists stay selectable, and catalog order, numeric limits and saved requests are unchanged. The original @18 default was approved on #1618 after the v0.59.0 pilot step 8 failure; #1647 advances the advertised desktop default to @19. For new composer state this supersedes the "@3 default" statements in AC-0552 and AC-0553. Quick dispatch keeps its own Standard-only list. Unit tests establish the selection; the pilot rerun establishes the actual-model behaviour (#1618).

- **AC-0560** Given the desktop app, when any model call is made, then a `SENT` entry with the request is appended to the local plain-text log `logs/model-debug.log` in the app profile as the request leaves, and a matching `RECEIVED` entry with the duration and the response or error when it finishes, so an in-flight call is visible as a `SENT` without a `RECEIVED`. Bounded investigation calls record the exact wire request and response bodies, single-shot completions record the redacted payload and the completion text, and embedding calls record the input texts and a vector-count summary instead of the vector values. The `RECEIVED` entry states the outcome the caller sees, including envelope refusals after a successful HTTP response and empty completions. Request headers, including API keys, are never recorded. Recording never blocks or delays a model call. Every recorded body and queued entry is charged to one fixed 8 MiB process pool before anything is copied: a fixed per-entry charge plus at most 1 MiB of decoded text per body and a short truncation note, whatever the input bytes. An entry the pool cannot cover is dropped without copying, and the count is written with the next entry. One writer thread formats a single entry at a time into at most 4 MiB, clipping with a stated note, and decodes message and output text by streaming without building a JSON tree; its scratch is that cap plus one decoded string no longer than the bounded body. Small call metadata (endpoint or provider id, evidence span headers, error text) is held outside the pool for the duration of the call. Cancellation and the deadline are checked after the request is recorded, so a call cancelled at that moment sends nothing. The log rotates at 25 MB to a single `model-debug.previous.log`, and nothing is sent off the machine. **Help → Debug Viewer** opens the log in the plain-text editor of the OS (TextEdit on macOS, Notepad on Windows, the default handler on Linux). This is an owner request before v1.0.0 (#1624).
- **Trace:** H4 · `llm`, `app` · — · T-0560

- **AC-0561** Given a selected investigation that is queued, preparing, running or awaiting consent, then a live status line above its details states the current step in plain words (for example "Waiting for the model to reply", "Running: searching the code graph", "The model's proposal was refused (not executed)"), how long the current step and the whole task have taken, ticking every second, the model calls and actions charged so far against the admitted limits, and separately the number of actions actually executed (a refused proposal can be charged without running). It also points to Help → Debug Viewer for each request and reply. Cancellation and pending consent take precedence over the last event. The line is derived only from recorded events and usage, with no simulated progress or model reasoning, and disappears once the task finishes. Each activity row is labelled with its stage: Waiting for model, Model replied, Proposed by model · accepted, Running, Executed or Not executed. This makes clear whether a model-proposed action was only proposed, is running, or actually ran. This is an owner request before v1.0.0, made while watching the v0.60.0 pilot (#1631).
- **Trace:** H4 · `ui` · — · T-0561

- **AC-0565** Given analyst@19 submits a supported finish citing known but unacquired source targets from its authenticated disclosure, then one durable corrective opportunity names those targets and permits only ordinary explicit acquisition within the remaining limits. Unknown, foreign, mixed-invalid, unavailable or already-attempted targets do not grant correction authority. The host never invents citations or performs implicit reads. Cancellation, stale/duplicate admission, and the once-only count survive reopen; @18 bytes and behavior stay unchanged. A second invalid finish is terminal (#1647).
- **Trace:** H4 · `agents`, `app` · — · T-0565

- **AC-0566** New semantic audit contracts are advisory: valid non-pass verdicts and typed not-completed outcomes retain the deterministically admitted candidate, without retry or extra invocation. Cancellation, task deadlines, privacy and ownership remain blocking. Historical contracts retain their blocking semantics. Saved detail and findings views label model feedback advisory, never verified. Analyst@20 requires cited support for qualifiers in findings and limitations; values are described by how cited code obtains them rather than by unestablished states. Historical terminal analyst@20 records accept only explicitly pinned previous prompt definitions with unchanged identity, operations and trust metadata; live tasks still require the current definition. Analyst@20 serializes stable instructions and disclosure before growing coverage/context/citations, with volatile fields last; this changes ordering and the role fingerprint only, preserving all JSON values, source bytes, limits and historical role wires. Analyst@20 instructs the model to paraphrase cited source rather than copy complete excerpts into authored prose. Its versioned evidence-boundary guidance requires limitations to name the searched scope and missing evidence, negative implementation assertions to have exact scoped support, and workflow answers to explain the acquired steps rather than only exception handling. An authenticated analyst@20 source-replaying finish spends the shared once-only correction allowance and receives fixed paraphrase feedback. With the allowance spent, replay is terminal with the content-free `source_replay` reason. Both paths retain measured response usage; no rejected prose or findings are persisted, and reopen preserves the result. Historical roles and privacy thresholds are unchanged. Given a new analyst@20 choice invocation, the prompt permits acquisition and qualifies terminal-wrapper syntax only when finishing. While pending obligations have offered acquisition actions, the response schema offers those actions without premature finish branches. Once every obligation has authenticated acquisition retained in the current ledger, only finish branches are offered; attempted but unavailable obligations retain exploration. Once every obligation has a completed attempt, insufficient-evidence finishing is offered; supported finishing additionally requires an acquired citation and restricts citation IDs to the acquired set. Empty-action blocked/deferred states retain the explicit historical fallback without satisfying obligations, and FinishOnly behavior remains unchanged. The same exact-role selector is used during input construction and validation, with citation-enum bytes included in existing reservation and workspace bounds. Typed-feedback reservation uses a contract-specific encoded bound covering every source action and unmet obligation, capped by the unchanged policy ceiling; nested escaping and projection scratch remain accounted. When an eligible finish cites known unacquired target IDs or receipt IDs whose matching source targets are all pending and offered, the next model-visible correction maps each mistaken target to its obligation and exact offered acquisition action, survives durable reopen, and is bound to the authenticated refusal and current choices. Query admission and exact source acquisition remain distinct; the mapping grants neither evidence nor citation authority. Historical @18/@19 prompt and record bytes remain unchanged. Foreign or mixed IDs, forged mappings, stale invocation cutoffs and duplicate publication fail closed; cancellation and the existing shared once-only correction allowance remain unchanged (#1650). Historical blocking semantic-audit contract (preserved on reopen): a structurally admitted candidate remains unpublished pending a model audit against the exact acquired spans. Every finding, overall limitation and finding-local limitation is addressed by a typed verdict bound to the candidate and ledger. Contradictions require an acquired witness; unsupported scope does not manufacture one. Honest disclosed partial answers may pass; indeterminate and rejection are terminal non-pass. Each new semantic audit reserves 16,384 output tokens from the original task budget before dispatch. The auditor retains the task request timeout. Each review emits the subject before its assessment, and each witness-bearing assessment emits the verdict before the citation ID. A separately configured reasoning auditor can enable thinking. Only answer content is admitted, and the response byte cap covers the entire body. Confirmed owner loss after audit dispatch terminates with unknown outcome, retains the single audit charge and unpublished candidate, and never replays the invocation; terminal reopen preserves that state. Audit settlement binds the exact saved invocation and candidate, stores a typed immutable receipt, and publishes only an uncancelled pass before the original deadline. Rejected, indeterminate, cancelled and late responses retain measured usage without publishing; duplicate settlement is refused and reopen rederives the decision. Provider failures and unadmitted replies terminate without a verdict receipt or result, retain the reserved audit charge and terminal clock sample, and leave unreported token totals unknown. An analyst invocation stopped without an admitted response retains all debits and terminal elapsed time, marks aggregate reported tokens unknown, and reopens terminal even after earlier measured calls. Deadline expiry between invocation commit and dispatch records a fixed timeout without dispatch. Before an analyst@20 finish can become an audit candidate, deterministic authored-field checks refuse schema placeholders, bare single-token colon labels and punctuation-only fields in titles, statements and limitations through fixed schema-v3 prose feedback. The original text is neither dropped nor echoed. This uses the existing shared once-only correction allowance; exhausted correction terminates without audit or publication. Terminal prose refusal reports incomplete or placeholder text without echoing it, rather than claiming missing source obligations. Ordinary prose mentioning code identifiers or balanced JSON remains allowed; historical roles and numeric ceilings are unchanged. A separately configured auditor uses its own local bounded provider, with no silent fallback to the analyst. Its provider profile and optional reported model digest are bound into the immutable audit invocation and the receipt's invocation hash; reopen validates this identity independently of the analyst. A configured auditor requesting the same model is refused before audit debit even on a different endpoint. When both reported model digests are available, equal digests are also refused. The analyst reported digest is retained with the auditor identity; dispatch, begin and durable replay enforce the same separation rule. A reported digest is metadata, not proof of executed weights. The single audit debit, original deadline and prepared request timeout remain unchanged; auditor transport failure and timeout retain the charge and reopen terminal. Historical same-provider audit records remain readable without granting a new dispatch. Production dispatch remains disabled. No audit repair is enabled until Phase B. A limitation directly denying an identifiable acquired path or symbol uses the shared correction allowance and names the conflicting evidence IDs. Candidate-evidence-auditor@3 retains @2 evidence scope: it supplies parent citation IDs per finding and finding limitation, labels overall scope, and judges local evidence boundaries separately from investigation-wide acquisition claims. Unproven material qualifiers receive indeterminate. The single audit invocation retains the existing bounds and typed reply format. The opt-in `domain-analyst@26` inherits @25 and additionally treats whole trimmed authored titles, statements and limitations matching `title`, `statement`, `findings`, `type` or `finish` (ASCII case-insensitive) as schema placeholders through the existing correction allowance. `Limitations`, `limitation` and sentences mentioning those words remain prose. Historical @20–@25 admission and saved-result reopen bytes remain unchanged, including an @25 limitation equal to `statement`; adopting @26 into frozen acceptance or a release requires a separate decision.
- **Security:** Refused finding prose is not retained as correction feedback. Typed mapping is validated against the saved contract and choices; source-target IDs never become admitted citation handles.
- **Performance:** Feedback and every escaped enclosing payload must fit existing policy bounds; no budget or numeric limit is raised. Failure to prove capacity refuses publication.
- **Trace:** H4 · `agents`, `app` · — · T-0566

- **AC-0563** Given the desktop Investigations composer, when the user explicitly chooses a budget or specialist, then that pair is saved in the app profile's settings database and restored after the app restarts, as long as the current catalog still offers it; otherwise the first-run default of AC-0559 applies. A fresh profile, or one that never made a choice, still starts with domain-analyst@19 under Expanded. A choice made in the current session is never replaced by a stored one. Only the identifiers are stored. Found by the v0.60.0 pilot step 8f (#1636).
- **Trace:** H4 · `app`, `ui` · — · T-0563

- **AC-0562** Given the desktop app, when the user chooses **Configure → Run Limits…** in the menubar, then a separate window shows the task time limit and the per-model-call timeout in minutes, with their shipped defaults (10 and 3 minutes) and maxima (60 and 10 minutes). Saving stores both values in the app profile; a value below the default or above the maximum is refused in the window and by the host, and nothing is written. **Restore defaults** clears both. The values persist across restarts and apply only to desktop investigations started afterwards by next-prompt specialist versions (the desktop defaults), including the run clock's active deadline; explicitly selected older specialist versions and MCP starts keep the shipped defaults, and the window says so. No other limit is editable, and the app Settings page is unchanged. Owner decisions on #1633 after the v0.60.0 pilot step 8 `deadline_exceeded`; ADR-0053. Using the stored values when a task starts is part of the same issue.

- **AC-0710** Given a started investigation, when its run clock decides how long the run may last, then the active-time deadline, the wall-clock cap and the per-wait consent budget all come from the limits saved on that task (`active_seconds`, `wall_seconds`, `consent_wait_seconds`), not from separate constants in the runtime. A consent wait never outlasts the wall-clock cap. The registered defaults stay 600 s active, 3,600 s wall and 900 s consent wait, so every existing run ends exactly when it did before. Only the active-time budget is user-configurable (AC-0562); the wall-clock and consent-wait budgets stay fixed registry values (#1652).

- **AC-0820** Given a bounded model call with a declared per-call timeout (`request_timeout_ms`, at most the ADR-0053 maximum of 600,000 ms), when the call is sent, then only that declared timeout, cut to the run's remaining deadline, decides when the call ends. The reusable transport client carries no shorter cap of its own: its fallback timeout equals the 600,000 ms maximum, so a per-call timeout raised in **Configure → Run Limits…** (AC-0562) is never cut off at 180 s. The default stays 180,000 ms and the wire, consent and protocol identity are unchanged. #1653.

- **AC-0564** Given the desktop app, when the user opens **Configure → Run Limits…**, then a **Local model** section lists the models installed in Ollama on this Mac (with their digests) and shows the saved completion model, default `qwen3:8b`. Choosing an installed model and **Use this model** saves it in the app profile; new desktop investigations on the local provider request that model, and the run records it in its provider descriptor. A model that is not installed is refused on save with an instruction to `ollama pull` it; a saved model that is no longer installed is shown as not installed, never silently replaced; if Ollama is unreachable the saved model still shows with the reason. **Use default model** returns to `qwen3:8b`. MCP starts and running investigations keep their model; Cartograph never downloads a model. The composer footer shows the saved task limit for specialists that use it. Owner decision on #1643 after the v0.61.0 pilot step 8c.

- **AC-1406** Given the desktop app, when the user opens **Configure → Run Limits…**, then a **Context window** section offers **Ollama default** or one of 8K, 16K, 32K and 64K tokens, and saves the choice in the app profile; any other value is refused by the host and nothing is written. New desktop investigations on the local provider then send that window as Ollama's `num_ctx` on every bounded call, and the provider id they record (and that consent and provenance bind) ends with `:num-ctx-<tokens>`. With **Ollama default**, no `num_ctx` is sent and the request bytes and provider id are unchanged. MCP starts, the cloud provider and running investigations keep what they had (ADR-0081, #1653).
