- **AC-1409** Given an acquired read representation bound for a provider, when the egress firewall evaluates it under `egress-read-deny/v1`, then it is refused exactly when the provider is Cloud and the repo-relative path matches a deny rule: the defaults for environment files (`.env`, `.env.*`, `*.env`), key material (`*.pem`, `*.key`, `*.p12`, `*.pfx`, `id_rsa*`, `id_ed25519*`, `*.keystore`, `*.jks`) and credential files (`.npmrc`, `.pypirc`, `.netrc`, `credentials.json`, `*.tfvars`, `.aws/credentials`, `.docker/config.json`), which cannot be removed, plus up to 64 validated project rules. A local provider and a non-matching path are allowed. A refusal carries a witness that only the firewall can build, naming the recipe, the rule, the exact read (repo, path, commit, byte range), the BLAKE3 hash of the refused bytes and the acquired attempt id, never the withheld text (ADR-0083, #679).
- **Trace:** H4 · `llm` · ADR-0083 · T-1409
- **AC-1410** Given a running next-prompt investigation on a cloud provider whose latest step acquired one or more source reads, when the host commits the firewall's `egress-read-deny/v1` witnesses for them, then each refused acquisition gets one immutable `FirewallRefused` attempt bound to it and one observation row holding only the recipe, rule, exact read, representation hash and acquired attempt id, never the withheld text, with one `read_firewall_refused` event, in one transaction. The acquisition, its citation and the ledger revision stay unchanged. The commit is refused with nothing written when a witness names other bytes or another attempt, when witnesses are duplicated, missing or empty, or when the acquisition was already refused, or when the task does not use a cloud provider. Readback reloads each observation, re-derives its hash and rejects a refusal whose observation was altered or removed, or no longer matches the acquired attempt and its saved citation (ADR-0083, #679).
- **Trace:** H4 · `app` · ADR-0083 · T-1410
- **AC-1413** Given a next-prompt investigation input whose ledger holds an acquired source read that the host firewall refused, when the host prepares the next v9 prompt with that citation withheld, then the payload carries no span or text for it, the citation leaves the citable `citations` list and appears once in `withheld_sources` as its handle with the closed outcome `firewall_refused`, and the sealed disclosure records that source as `firewall_refused`, distinct from omitted and redacted, with no disclosed text hash. The read stays acquired: later reads take the next evidence ordinal and count it toward the evidence limits. Preparation is refused when a withheld id is not an acquired source citation, also has supplied text, or when acquired text is neither supplied nor withheld. An input with nothing withheld keeps its prompt unchanged (ADR-0083, #679).
- **Trace:** H4 · `agents` · ADR-0083 · T-1413
- **AC-1414** Given a running next-prompt investigation on a cloud provider, when it reads a captured source whose path matches an `egress-read-deny/v1` rule, then the read is acquired as usual and settles in one transaction with its refusal: the acquisition attempt, then one `FirewallRefused` attempt per acquisition bound to its real attempt id, one `read_firewall_refused` event after the completion event, and one observation row without the text. The next prompt withholds the read (AC-1413), so no later provider call carries its span or text, it leaves every citation schema and the semantic audit, and it still counts as an acquired evidence item. Settlement is refused with nothing written when the candidate withholds a read that the firewall allowed, supplies one it refused, or claims a refusal on a task that is not cloud. On a cloud task, source admission reserves one refusal attempt per acquisition and one refusal event, so a read admitted at the last slots can still settle with its refusal. An allowed read, and every local run, settles exactly as before (ADR-0083, #679).
- **Trace:** H4 · `app` · ADR-0083 · T-1414
- **AC-1415** Given a cloud investigation whose source read the firewall refused (AC-1414), when the model finishes, then the refused read counts as an acquired own-role attempt but never as exactly supplied: a supported finish that leaves the source obligation unmet is refused with closed feedback, and the refused read neither resets nor increments the finish-refusal count, so a second unmet finish fails atomically with no result and its usage kept. With an allowed inventory obligation beside it, an insufficient-evidence finish is refused until every obligation has a completed own-role attempt, then completes. No call after the read carries its span, and reopen reproduces the task exactly (ADR-0083, #679).

- **AC-1417** Given a project, when its own `egress-read-deny/v1` rules are saved (`set_read_deny_rules`), then every investigation firewall applies them on top of the defaults, which cannot be removed: up to 64 validated relative patterns, kept in order across reopen with duplicates collapsed, replaced whole, and nothing written when any rule is invalid. `get_read_deny_rules` returns them. A stored rule that no longer validates fails closed, so no firewall is built and the run stops. The rules belong to the project, not the app (ADR-0083, #679).

- **AC-1407** Given the desktop app, when the user opens **Configure → Run Limits…**, then an **Ollama port** section shows the loopback port (default 11434) and saves a whole port from 1 to 65535 in the app profile; port 0 is refused by the host and nothing is written, and saving 11434 is the same as the default. Only the port is configurable: the host is always `127.0.0.1`. With the default port, the provider is exactly the one used before. MCP starts, the cloud provider and running investigations keep what they had (ADR-0081, #1653).

- **AC-1416** Given a saved loopback Ollama port (AC-1407), when the app reaches the local model for anything other than an investigation start (gated and local section composition, local area-expert asks, local gap escalation and its payload preview, the semantic preview, the knowledge-phase embeddings and the local lane status), then it reaches the Ollama on that port, not the default one. - **AC-1418** Given a project, when an operator limit from the closed registry is saved (`set_operator_limit`), then the value persists in the project's settings and applies to its consumer; a key outside the registry or a value outside the key's range is refused with nothing written, a cleared limit returns to its default, defaults stay unchanged until a value is saved, and a stored value that no longer validates stops the consumer instead of reverting silently. `get_operator_limits` lists every key with its value, default, range and whether it is advanced. - **AC-1408** Given the agent roster, when the host loads it, then each specialist role is one named agent defined in checked-in data (`crates/agents/data/roster-v1.json`): Domain Analyst `agt-analyst`, Evidence Auditor `agt-auditor`, Spec Writer `agt-spec`, Gap Triager `agt-gap` and Section Writer `agt-section`, each with a role line, a glyph and a tier ceiling. The agent version is its role's current version in the capability table (for example `agt-analyst@28` runs `domain-analyst@28`), so the specialist id every saved run records names the agent version that produced it, and a run of any older version names its role's agent. The ceiling is the highest confidence the agent's output may carry: a T3 ceiling admits Gap and InferredWeak only, a T2 ceiling never admits Confirmed, and a definition that is malformed, names an unknown role, repeats an id or a role, leaves a role out or binds a specialist whose output exceeds its ceiling is refused. The `list_agents` command returns the roster with each agent running or idle and its in-flight investigations per local and cloud provider, read from the saved active selection; dispatch stays the existing investigation start (#256).

None is dispatchable. A definition that is malformed, repeats an id or a pass, leaves a pass out, reuses a specialist agent's id or claims another ceiling is refused. The `list_pipeline_agents` command returns them with each running or idle: the IaC Grapher counts the repository recoveries running in the job store, and the Flow Tracer and Contract Matcher count the semantic previews running in this process, idle again when a preview ends; nothing about it is persisted (#256).

- **AC-0567** Given a hosted OpenAI investigation call, when `OpenAiProvider` runs on the bounded path, then it is a declared Cloud provider. The egress firewall requires consent bound to the exact payload, and release builds can reach only the pinned `https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions` endpoint.
  - **Request:** the consented system and user messages, `max_completion_tokens` from the reserved output limit, `store: false` and `stream: false`. The API key travels as a sensitive bearer header and is never logged. No tools or thinking template are sent.
  - **Schema:** a bounded response schema travels as `json_schema` with `strict: true`. It uses the AC-0568 projection plus these checks:
    - every object is closed and lists every property as required;
    - every `const` and `enum` schema carries its explicit scalar `type`, and a lone object or array `const` travels as a closed, fully required schema of typed literals with the array's exact length (strict mode cannot pin array positions, so each item admits any listed element). A structured literal with sibling keywords is refused. The live API rejects untyped and structured literals with HTTP 400, as #598 development showed;
    - `allOf`, `$ref` siblings and non-root definitions are refused;
    - the published limits (10 nesting levels, 5000 properties, 1000 enum values, 120000 schema string bytes) are enforced in one memoized pass before send, and each definition is counted once.
  - **Root wrapper:** a root that is not a plain object travels wrapped as the single required property `response`, with definitions kept at the root. Only the model's exact inner bytes reach host validation against the complete original schema. A wrapped reply in any other shape is Invalid.
  - **Responses:** envelope admission, refusal, stop and usage handling follow AC-0342 under the AC-0185 transport rules.
  - **Refusals:** a schema that strict mode cannot represent is refused as Unsupported before connecting. The unbounded completion path is unsupported.
  - **Model:** the model id is the caller's reviewed choice and is bound into provider identity (#1709).
- **Trace:** H4 · `llm` · — · T-0567
- **AC-0568** Given a hosted Anthropic investigation call with a bounded response schema, when the Messages request is built, then the schema travels as `output_config.format` (`json_schema`) in a wire-only projection. The host still validates the reply against the complete original schema.
  - **Dropped from the wire copy only:** constraints the API rejects, namely string and numeric bounds, `multipleOf`, `maxItems`, `uniqueItems`, and `minItems` above 1.
  - **Rewritten:** `oneOf` becomes `anyOf`, and structured enum members become exact branches. The API compiles only scalar `const`, so a structured literal is sent as the closed object schema that admits exactly it: `additionalProperties: false`, every key required, and scalar leaves as `const`. An array cannot be length-bounded on the wire, so an empty one admits only `null` items and a non-empty one admits items drawn from its own elements; the host enforces the exact array. Same-shaped structured choices in one `anyOf` merge into a single closed schema whose varying scalar leaves become an `enum`, which keeps large menus within the API grammar limit. Any combination this admits beyond the original choices is rejected by host validation. An array schema with `maxItems: 0` travels with `null` items, which keeps its unreachable item grammar out of the compile; the complete schema is projected and reference-checked first, so this never hides an unsupported or missing reference, and the OpenAI strict projection keeps such items unchanged.
  - **Constrained:** `additionalProperties` may only be `false`. Every `$ref` must name an existing root `$defs` or `definitions` entry, and reference cycles are refused.
  - **Inlined:** the API rejects `$defs` alongside `anyOf`, so the wire copy carries no definitions. Each local reference is replaced by its projected definition, and unreferenced definitions are not sent. The expansion is bounded by a fixed node budget. The OpenAI strict projection keeps root definitions and references.
  - **Refused as Unsupported before sending:** any other keyword, an external, missing or recursive reference, and a conflicting merge. Also refused: a `$ref` with sibling keywords, definitions below the root, and an inlined copy that exceeds the node budget.
  - **Unchanged:** absent-schema request bytes.
  - **Model pin:** the Opus lane is pinned to `claude-opus-5-5` (#1709).
- **Trace:** H4 · `llm` · — · T-0568

Both are checked before the one-attempt marker is consumed.

  - **Consent log:** an exclusive append-only `analyst-consent-log.jsonl` durably records the grant, each approval (step, revision, payload hash, payload bytes, redaction count, provider id), the cap decline and the revert, each before it takes effect.
  - **Revert:** on completion, error or a partial grant, T3 reverts to local, which revokes the consent, and its prior enabled flag is restored.

Reopen must match both saved values; omission preserves historical definition and manifest bytes. Preparation and execute=false reopen remain provider-free. Product defaults are unchanged.
  - **Unchanged:** Local definitions and their behavior. Unpinned, unavailable or mismatched hosted identities are refused (#1709, owner decision 2026-11-07).

- **AC-0571** Given an explicitly selected local `local-finish-4096-v1` policy for analyst @26, the host derives the generation mode from frozen saved acquisition state before each call: once all frozen acquisition attempts are complete, the first and every correction finish use FinishOnly, a schema without tool actions, and a 4,096-token allowance. Before that boundary Choice remains capped at 2,048, including an early finish emitted from Choice; this is a known limitation, and model output never grants the larger allowance.

Text-free invocation metadata persists `disclosure.admission.mode` and `generated_tokens`, including correction finishes; terminal receipts include these invocation records.

- **AC-0572** Given analyst @27 has an authenticated first finish refusal with complete finding-local authority, its single correction response is a finding patch, not a replacement whole answer. Only indices flagged by that saved refusal may be replaced with a full finding or explicitly withdrawn; unknown, duplicate, unflagged or ambiguous operations are rejected. Omitted indices retain their original finding. Replacements occupy the original position and withdrawals remove that position; surviving originals retain their order.
  - **Whole-answer fallback:** a first refusal without complete finding-local authority (including whole-answer entries, citation corrections, unmet obligations or omitted feedback entries) retains the existing @26 whole-answer correction path and its single shared allowance. It holds no volatile candidate and emits no patch schema. An authenticated text-free correction-mode marker distinguishes WholeAnswer from Patch and from a missing/lost marker. Reopen accepts legitimate fallback state and rejects missing, forged or swapped modes; it never treats a lost Patch as fallback.
  - **Volatile ownership:** the bounded first-finish candidate may remain in host memory for exactly one correction turn, bound to the persisted finish hash and refusal hash. Any binding mismatch refuses the merge. Candidate text is never written to disk, database, logs, events, receipts or crash/error output. Cancellation, interruption, restart or reopen releases it; a reopened pending patch fails deterministically and never asks the model to resend it. A text-free pending-patch marker is permitted. Scoped Drop releases the holder as soon as merge or failure resolves: **released, not securely erased**. No secure-erasure claim or new dependency is made.
  - **Bounds:** the existing 32 KiB action cap bounds the held candidate and the patch/merged action. Existing logical workspace accounting includes the held candidate simultaneously with the correction request, patch decoding and merged admission. No numeric ceiling is enlarged; insufficient existing capacity fails closed.
  - **Authentication and admission:** provider-authored response/action metadata remains authenticated independently of the host-merged finish fingerprint. Ordinary successful patches, as well as withheld projections, bind the merged result to the saved patch invocation and authenticated prior refusal through a distinct host-only hash domain. Provider action hashes are never replaced with merged hashes. Durable result and semantic-audit reopen revalidate this binding; telemetry is observation only and cannot supply admission authority. The whole merged answer passes the full privacy, citation, replay, configuration and @27 enumeration checks before any result text persists. Unflagged findings receive no exemption. The existing findings-withheld-after-correction projection operates on the merged candidate, and no empty/all-withdrawn result is admitted. Nonfinding fields remain subject to normal admission; finding patches cannot silently repair or discard them.
  - **Generation:** explicitly extend the local-finish-4096-v1 lane to @27. Its finding-patch correction is FinishOnly with a tool-free patch schema and up to 4,096 output tokens, derived from authenticated saved refusal state. Whole-answer fallback retains the existing host-derived Choice/FinishOnly boundary and full-finish schema. First pre-boundary Choice calls remain capped at 2,048. The 131,072 total and all other V2 limits remain unchanged; reservation and settlement rederive and authenticate the mode and policy.
  - **Text-free evidence:** each finish records the finding count, distinct flagged-index count, patched/withdrawn/kept counts and projection rejection code. Correction evidence distinguishes proposed patch entries from merged findings; an unavailable count is not fabricated. No retained field contains candidate or patch prose.
  - **Compatibility and verification:** @19–@26 behavior and serialized history are unchanged, including r5/r6/r7/r8 byte-identical receipt reopening on disposable copies. Tests cover merge ordering, strict indices, withdrawal, full re-admission of an invalid unflagged finding, all-withdrawn failure, hash mismatch, cancellation and restart mid-correction, memory accounting, and scans of database/files for synthetic candidate text after patched runs. The separately versioned @27 grammar remains limited to terminal-empty and unique-subject predicates; exact-key-list expansion is not included.

### US-0033 — Drop an application or repo set and see what was and was not covered
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** to drop one repo, a set of repos or an application folder into Cartograph and see what every deterministic producer covered before any interpretation **so that** I know the limits of what it recovered.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0237** Given one or more folders dropped on the Connect surface, when they are accepted, then they ingest through the same path as a typed path or system manifest, each repo is listed with its commit identity, and each folder reports an explicit accepted, rejected or failed outcome; completed sources and unresolved intake stay visible and the set is never reported as fully ingested while any folder is unresolved.
- **AC-0238** Given a completed ingest, when its coverage inventory is shown, then each repo reports which deterministic and observational producers ran, which inputs were missing and which capabilities are unsupported, with a scoped stopping reason for each, before any interpretation is offered. Each producer row counts only the facts it produced: channel and client-call stitching over every layer's sites is its own `t0.events` row, so a producer that ran on no files reports no facts (#874).
- **AC-0239** Given a scope with missing producers, when an interpretation or graph read is requested, then it proceeds explicitly qualified by the recorded coverage limitations and never claims exhaustive coverage. Explicit @6 investigations retain the full preparation-time qualification through every invocation, finishing disclosure and saved history; bounded detail reads use only the exact retained inventory, and optional source-inspection obligations remain independently assessed. Earlier investigation versions retain their historical contracts.
- **AC-0264** Given explicitly caller-selected source and inventory completion obligations, when internal staged generation-4 request primitives are admitted, then a single normalized envelope preserves exact source conflicts and context/repo/inventory identity, rejects conflicting authorities and present-null, and binds its own schema/hash domains under raw 1–12 obligations and the unchanged complete 8 KiB request ceiling before partition clones. Literal request generations 1–3, V1–V6 specialist fingerprints and existing input/disclosure/result identities remain frozen. V7 is absent from the production catalog and direct start remains refused until all completion-contract slices are implemented.
- **AC-0265** Given a staged completion request and real finishing invocation, when private finish admission and the refusal journal run, then security-invalid evidence fails before recoverable classification; supported candidates require every exact finishing representation, and empty insufficient candidates require completed durable attempts for every obligation. Bounded deterministic feedback and response usage join a caller-owned transaction with immutable task/step/ledger/disclosure binding; fresh-connection read-back rejects altered identity or order. The first refusal returns Continue and the second TerminalRequired without a third continuation or committed second-refusal Ready state. V7 remains unavailable until the final AC-0266 slice supplies public terminal publication, task recovery and UI integration.
- **AC-0266** Given an explicit V7 mixed completion request, when the production worker acquires evidence and evaluates a wrapped finish, then the actual request, retained context, ordered completed attempts, protected feedback and exact finishing disclosure determine supported or insufficient-evidence admission. The first unmet finish atomically records its response and returns bounded feedback; the second atomically terminates obligations-unmet without a result or a third call. Cancellation and unknown outcomes retain their execution meaning. Generation-2 stored tasks and generation-4 history reopen with the same assessment; legacy task bytes and generations remain unchanged. The UI exposes explicit host-resolved selections and distinguishes acquired, omitted, redacted and unavailable evidence without equating execution completion with evidentiary support. A syntactically admitted query refused by the frozen context for an unknown selector or invalid cursor returns fixed, bounded feedback through an immutable refusal record bound to the actual task, step, action and unchanged input ledger (#1617). The attempted tool remains charged; no successful page, citation or obligation attempt is fabricated. Host consistency, storage and budget failures remain terminal. Replay and continuation must validate the saved refusal before showing feedback.
- **AC-0268** Given a repo containing Vue single-file components, when it is ingested, then the `<script>` and `<script setup>` blocks of each `.vue` file are parsed by the JavaScript/TypeScript extractor with every span naming its place in the `.vue` file, template, style and custom-block text is never read as code, an explicit `.vue` import resolves to the component, re-extraction of unchanged files is byte-identical, and Preflight reports `.vue` as covered for script content while stating that templates are not parsed, with its risky-construct findings raised from script blocks only at the component's own lines.
- **AC-1306** Given an ingest of a repository holding sources of a language a built-in adapter reads (TypeScript, JavaScript, Python, Go, Rust, Java or Kotlin) and that adapter ran, when its coverage inventory is recorded, then each extractor family (target tests, persistence, rule evidence, endpoint auth) that no installed producer recovers for that language is a not-covered capability `extractor-not-run:<family>:<language>`, counting the language's source files, naming the adapter that read them and saying none is stated for them, and the inventory reads as limited; `coverage.md` and `coverage.json` carry it with the inventory's other not-covered entries. A language with every family, such as Python, adds nothing. Which languages have each family is one table, kept in step by a test with the adapters' extractors and the endpoint permission recoveries (#2164).
- **AC-0700** Given coverage inventories recorded by earlier ingests, when the app is relaunched, then Workspace restores each repo's recorded inventory from the store's bounded coverage read alone, without running an ingest or materializing graph facts, qualified by the registration and plugin basis that held across that read: a current inventory is shown as recorded, with its source inputs described as recorded rather than newly validated, and a stale one is named as stale with what was compared and nothing read from it. Clearing the graph removes the cards permanently even though source registrations and job history remain. A failed read (a corrupt or over-limit inventory included) is stated and shows no card, and a read superseded by a newer read, or requested before a clear, an ingest or a plugin change began, never restores older cards; a plugin change or gate run requalifies the recorded coverage (#1656).
- **Security:** Read-only intake of local folders the user chose; no target writes, no network fetch beyond existing GitHub intake.
- **Performance:** Existing ingest limits apply per repo; the inventory is built from facts already recorded during ingest.
- **Trace:** P1 (ROADMAP W1) · `ingest`, `agents`, `app`, `ui`, `adapters-lang-ts` · — · T-0237..0239, T-0264..0266, T-0268, T-0700

### US-0034 — Stitch calls across repos only on binding evidence
- **Actor:** Engineer
- **As a** engineer **I want** a client in one repo calling a service in another to be joined to that service's endpoint or channel when evidence proves the binding **so that** cross-repo flows are traced without guessing.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0240** Given a client call in one repo and an endpoint or channel in another, when stitching runs after every repo is ingested, then they are joined only with binding evidence such as a declared service in the system manifest, base-URL configuration or a gateway route, and the edge cites that evidence.
- **AC-0241** Given a cross-repo call without binding evidence, or with several equally supported targets, when stitching completes, then the call remains a Gap or labeled candidate with its reason and no new confidence tier is introduced.
- **AC-0315** Given a client that declares its API as data rather than as a literal URL at the call site, when the repository is recovered, then each declared call becomes a client call fact with its HTTP method, literal path template, the client entry it comes from and evidence spans for both the call and the base URL it joins. The two declaration styles are Retrofit `@GET`/`@POST`/`@PUT`/`@PATCH`/`@DELETE`/`@HEAD`/`@OPTIONS`/`@HTTP` annotations on an import-proven Kotlin or Java interface, joined to the repository's one `Retrofit.Builder().baseUrl(...)` by RFC 3986 resolution; and a literal `{ method, path }` entry of a const route table, joined by string concatenation to a base URL at a binding-proven call site: a wrapper whose first parameter is the route, or a direct `fetch(base + Table.ENTRY.path)`. A base read from runtime state resolves only through a system-manifest `env` declaration of its source text, never an env file. A non-literal path, an unresolved or ambiguous base, or an undeclared runtime base stays a Gap whose reason names the entry; a joined absolute URL binds across repos only on AC-0240 evidence (#737).
- **Security:** Uses only recovered facts and declared configuration; no live network probing.
- **Performance:** Stitching runs once per system after ingest and scales with the number of call sites, not repos squared.
- **Trace:** P1 (ROADMAP W2) · `events`, `ingest`, `app`, `adapters-lang-ts`, `adapters-lang-kotlin`, `adapters-lang-java` · — · T-0240..0241, T-0315

- **AC-0245** Given a test discovered in a target repo, when it is linked to a requirement, then discovered test, subject dependency, observed execution or coverage, assertion support and proposed verification are distinct qualified links, and "validates requirement" appears only with its own qualification.

- **Performance:** Artifact compilation stays within the existing spec compile path; content checks run on demand.

Selecting a flow beyond the overview cap fetches that exact flow before unchanged diagram bounds; stale identities and superseded selections cannot replace the current view (#1669).
