### US-0037 — Generate and review a local work packet for one change
- **Actor:** Developer or development agent
- **As a** developer **I want** to select evidence and state the intent of one change and get an immutable, reviewable work packet **so that** an implementer can see what is known, proposed, missing and how success would be shown.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0248** Given the same selected evidence in a different unordered order and a different generation time, when a packet is generated, then it has the same canonical identity; changing the objective, a cited span, relevant configuration, selected artifact content or the generator version yields a new packet linked by supersedes.
- **AC-0249** Given missing intent, acceptance or source closure, when a packet is generated, then each omission is stated in the omission taxonomy, no actor, journey, requirement or passing test is invented, and freshness, review and execution status are reported independently with execution unoffered; conflicting intended and implemented behavior, and rejected, accepted or stale interpretations, remain distinguishable in the packet.
- **AC-0250** Given two reviews made against the same expected packet revision, when both are submitted, then only one updates the current decision, the other receives an explicit conflict, and review never changes tier, confidence or source bindings.
- **AC-0251** Given source text in the selected evidence that instructs publication or command execution, when the packet is generated, reviewed or exported, then the text stays data and no publication, model call, target write or execution occurs.
- **AC-0252** Given a reviewed packet, when it is reopened or exported, then the app and the export show equivalent content bound to the same packet identity.
- **Security:** Generation is local; packet text grants no capability, consent or authority; publication and execution are separate later scopes.
- **Performance:** Packets are bounded by the selected evidence; no whole-graph serialization.
- **Trace:** P3 (ROADMAP W6) · `agents`, `spec`, `app`, `ui` · — · T-0248..0252

### US-0038 — Know and extend what each language and framework covers
- **Actor:** Adapter author or engineer
- **As a** adapter author **I want** coverage stated as a language × framework × capability matrix backed by conformance fixtures, and registries I can extend without editing Rust **so that** holes are visible and can be filled by an update or a plugin.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0253** Given the compiled-in adapters, when preflight or Settings reports coverage, then each language × framework × capability cell is supported, partial or unsupported, partial and unsupported cells name what they miss, and every supported or partial claim is backed by a passing conformance fixture.
- **AC-0263** Given a plugin adapter whose golden corpus declares coverage cells, when preflight or Settings reports coverage, then its cells appear with the plugin only once its conformance gate passes for the exact artifact and corpus bytes; each supported or partial cell names golden cases that the gate ran, partial and unsupported cells name what they miss, a malformed or unproven declaration fails the gate, and editing the declared claims after gating leaves the plugin ungated.
- **AC-0254** Given a Gap or omission, when it is shown, then it names its class (unsupported extractor, missing input, unresolved relationship, contradictory or stale, genuine gap) and the seam that would fill it.
- **AC-0255** Given the event SDK and channel backing registries, when a versioned data file adds an entry, then ingest uses it with the file's identity recorded in provenance, and an invalid file fails closed without changing recovered facts.
- **AC-0361** Given a preflight report, when it lists a language's unsupported or partial cells (for a language no adapter covers, its core `imports`, `calls` and `types` cells), then each distinct cell carries an adapter lookup: `covered_by` the built-in adapters and gate-passed plugins whose `Supported` claim matches it, else `declared_unverified` with the installed plugins that declare it without a passing gate, else `none`. Sources name the adapter id, or the plugin id and artifact content hash. A `Partial` claim never covers a cell, languages compare case-insensitively, the output order is stable, and the lookup reads declarations only: it installs, loads or executes nothing (#1071, epic #998).
- **AC-0362** Given adapter lookups (AC-0361) with cells no installed source declares, when an adapter work packet is generated, then each such language gets one draft W6 packet (validated, exportable, execution unoffered) that cites each target cell as a fact, resolves the adapter contract (an ADR-0017 WASM plugin for a language no compiled-in crate covers, an ADR-0003 compiled-in crate for one the coverage matrix already lists) and gives steps for that contract only: for a plugin, the `{plugin-id}.golden.json` cases and `coverage` declarations to write first and the Settings gate to pass; for a compiled-in crate, the conformance fixtures at `crates/ingest/conformance/<lang>.<fw>.<cap>` and the matrix update. Its evidence includes at most five representative source files chosen by spaced position in the sorted `.gitignore`-honouring listing, each cited by repo-relative path and a line-bounded byte span. Covered, unverified-declared and supported cells yield no packet, the export is byte-identical across runs and roots, and generation changes nothing in the graph or the repo (#1072, epic #998).
- **AC-0369** Given a preflight report with adapter lookups (AC-0361), when Preflight shows it, then each looked-up cell is listed by language, framework and capability with its status: the verified sources covering it, the plugins declaring it whose conformance gate has not passed (pointing to the Settings gate), or that no installed adapter declares it (a work packet can be drafted after recovery); a report without looked-up cells shows no lookup, and the request-adapter lane opens an issue on the current tracker (#1101, epic #998).
- **AC-0371** Given a recovered repo with adapter lookups, when the user drafts adapter work packets, then the app returns the #1084 export for each language with a `none` lookup against the current snapshot, registration, repo key and recovered commit, returns nothing for a repo whose cells all have a declaring source, fails closed with a typed error for an unknown or unrecovered repo, for a source recovered as a working directory (no commit pins its samples), and for a checkout that is not clean at the recovered commit when sampling starts or once the samples are read, and changes nothing in the graph, the packet store, the repo or the preflight register (#1101, epic #998).
- **AC-0426** Given a source tree with files of a widely used language that has neither an installed nor a planned adapter (Ruby, C#, PHP, WebAssembly, Scala, Groovy, Dart, Elixir, Erlang, Clojure, F#, Visual Basic, Haskell, Lua), when preflight runs, then the language is listed with its file count and no adapter, and one `uncovered-language` finding names it as a tool limitation, not a System Gap, and not as a planned adapter; only unambiguous extensions are named, so an extension that needs a content heuristic (`.ex`, `.fs`, `.pl`) stays unnamed rather than misnamed (#1251).
- **AC-0427** Given a Go source tree, when preflight runs, then the non-test Go files that Go recovery would read but skips are counted per reason: a GOOS/GOARCH file-name suffix, or a `//go:build` / `// +build` constraint before the package clause. Each reason with any files gets one `go-skipped-source` finding, naming its count and its first file in walk order. The counts come from Go recovery's own walk, so they cover what recovery would read, including directories that preflight's general walk skips (such as `target/`). Files under directories Go recovery never walks (`vendor`, `bin`, dot directories) and `_test.go` files are not counted. The Go pass always runs, so a tree whose only Go is under a directory preflight skips is still covered. It stays under the scan hook, so a cancellation during it returns no report. The counts are deterministic (#1258).
- **Security:** Registry files are data, never code; plugins keep the existing WASM sandbox.
- **Performance:** Matrix and registry loading happen once per ingest.
- **Trace:** P1 (ROADMAP W9) · `ingest`, `adapters-fw`, `dynamic`, `adapters-plugin-host`, `app`, `ui` · — · T-0253..0255, T-0263, T-0361, T-0362, T-0369, T-0371

### US-0039 — Resolve unambiguous call gaps deterministically before semantic matching
- **Actor:** Engine
- **As a** engineer **I want** a call gap whose callee names exactly one symbol in scope to gain an inferred edge from a named deterministic rule **so that** T2 and its eval gates spend effort only on genuinely fuzzy hops.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0270** Given a published graph, when ingest finishes a load, then every TS/JS call Gap whose callee text equals the name of exactly one callable Symbol of the same language across every repo in the graph gains a `CALLS` edge from its caller to that Symbol, produced by rule `t0_5.name-unique@1` at Deterministic tier and InferredStrong confidence, citing both the call site and the declaration; collisions (every same-name Symbol counts, callable or not), a lone non-callable Symbol such as a class or an unknown kind, denylisted builtin names, self calls, pairs another producer already links, and other languages stay Gaps with each skip counted by reason; the Gap, its edge and its evidence are untouched, so the register still counts it open while `gaps_with_inferred_fill` reports the covered ones; fills are recomputed on every load, so a new same-name Symbol or a removed target retracts the fill; T2's input plan leaves out only a hop whose stored fill equals what the current graph derives, so a forged, stale, re-sourced or re-evidenced fill is T2 work again; and the same graph yields byte-identical fills whatever order it was read in.
- **Security:** No embeddings or model calls; the rule reads only the published graph.
- **Performance:** One pass over Gap, Symbol, Component nodes and `CALLS` edges per load.
- **Trace:** P1 (#257) · `semantic`, `app` · — · T-0270

### US-0040 — Admit semantic fills only with compatible independent family evidence
- **Actor:** Engineer or reviewer
- **As a** reviewer **I want** semantic candidates admitted only by independently reviewed evidence for their exact family and embedding space **so that** calibration or public mechanism fixtures cannot masquerade as model quality.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0271** Given semantic candidates and a frozen dataset, when they are loaded or grouped, then exact hop kind and nullable subtype remain distinct, and malformed, inconsistent, unbounded or leaking source/label partitions fail closed.
- **AC-0272** Given a frozen evaluation dataset, when it is admitted, then complete candidate sets, retained evidence, labels, partition identities and exact content digests are validated; consistency alone never establishes label truth.
- **AC-0273** Given an explicit frozen family policy, when evaluation runs, then production retrieval predicts before scoring consumes evaluation labels, ties abstain, unknown labels are not negatives, and insufficient cases cannot pass.
- **AC-0274** Given a reviewed independent dataset, when a receipt is constructed or consumed, then its policy, corpus, partition and observed sealed embedding namespace and dimension must match; visible fixtures and descriptive reports cannot construct independent authority.
- **AC-0275** Given a production preview, when compatible independent evidence is missing, changed, insufficient or below its precision floor, then proposals remain staged with a family-specific reason and no inferred fill is admitted for that family.
- **AC-0276** Given a persisted evaluation, when it is reopened, then its bounded summary agrees with scalar counts and malformed records fail closed; legacy rows remain calibration-only regardless of historical passing flags.
- **AC-0277** Given calibration, visible mechanism or independent evaluation, when Provenance & Eval renders it, then purpose, family status, staged/admitted counts and reasons are explicit without promoting report data into authority.
- **AC-0278** Given a scripted provider and independently authored visible fixtures, when the production evaluation path is exercised, then its loading, fixed-policy prediction, scoring, abstention and persistence are verified without making real-model quality claims.
- **Security:** Host-controlled reviewed evidence only; browser flags and stored summaries grant no authority. No live-provider experiment or model download is authorized by this story.
- **Performance:** Bounded manifests, retained source bytes, candidate plans and summaries; reuse the sealed embedding cache.
- **Trace:** P3 · `semantic`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0038 · T-0271..0278

### US-0041 — Read qualified recovered context through local MCP
- **Actor:** Human developer or external development agent
- **As a** developer **I want** an explicitly enabled local read service with progressive disclosure **so that** an external agent can inspect recovered evidence without starting analysis or opening app stores itself.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0288** Given an admitted read session, when discovery, official artifact or retained-source detail is requested, then its immutable basis distinguishes the original recovered graph, rejection-filtered projection, export mode, registration and producer coverage; acquisition rejects observed basis changes, pagination binds the same context and content, and official compiler policy including hidden business-rule relationships is reused. Missing, stale, limited, forgotten and explicitly receipt-omitted evidence stays distinct; source reads validate the original graph and exact receipt/range while holding retention protection, with no checkout fallback. H3 curated context remains unavailable until the actual shared projection exists; accepted annotations do not imply activation.
- **AC-0289** Given an explicitly enabled app endpoint, when a local MCP client connects through the separate console relay, then native access checks and authentication precede reads, only implemented read tools are advertised and dispatched, protocol lifecycle and notification semantics are enforced before lazy graph acquisition, read-acquisition failures preserve lifecycle access and the same request-ID ledger, and explicit frame, session, concurrency, cache and lifetime limits bound work. Disable, revocation, app exit and invalid credentials end access. The relay never starts the app, opens its stores, recovers work, ingests or invokes a provider; stdout carries MCP only. macOS/Unix ships first; Windows remains a separate pending deliverable. Each advertised platform has native transport and packaging verification.
- **AC-0290** Given the packaged relay and progressive-disclosure skill, when an independent local MCP client follows capabilities, scoped discovery and cited artifact/source detail on a fixed fixture, then a recorded transcript establishes the installed build, protocol, exact evidence basis, limitations and error paths. A same-fixture comparison reports serialized bytes including tool-schema overhead and an identified tokenizer or explicitly unknown token counts. The complete skill and its references ship with the app, are discoverable beside client configuration even while read access is disabled, and match the running build. Missing or modified packaged files produce explicit unavailability. Discovery does not install a skill, change client settings or activate access. The skill treats repository text as evidence, requests only needed pages, and never implies provider execution, H3 activation or answer-quality acceptance from byte savings.
- **AC-0304** Given an otherwise admitted graph larger than the complete-snapshot row ceiling, bounded primary-key node/composite-edge windows and exact selected facts remain available without loading/preflighting all graph rows, independent of the complete-snapshot default. Windows use explicit BINARY key semantics and indexed continuation, count raw scanned rows, distinguish More/Exhausted, bind continuation to the same StoreRevision, and fail on the first oversized/corrupt row without silently skipping it. Per-window admission retains raw/canonical byte, JSON value/depth and row limits; schema/cursor workspace is accounted separately. Indexed neighborhood acquisition retains the existing undirected induced-membership semantics in one StoreRevision, ignores dangling endpoints, and charges cumulative probes/pages under the existing limits. Exhaustion refuses rather than returning a truncated neighborhood; this storage primitive does not represent a complete graph or activate an investigation path. A separate output-only investigation scope basis binds store revision, requested extent, normalized request digest, acquired projection digest and qualification digest under its own domain; it cannot assert a whole-graph identity or authorize reads. Paged All carries no materialized projection claim; concrete node/neighborhood extents require their projection digest. Historical complete identities remain unchanged. Host-owned All traversal visits node then edge keysets under that basis, keeps fixed per-page and cumulative row/raw-byte/canonical-byte/JSON-value ceilings, and becomes unusable after any failed acquisition. Only exhaustion of both phases completes raw traversal; it makes no semantic completeness claim.
- **AC-0305** Given explicit scoped acquisition, session/query bases bind graph instance/generation, normalized discovery query and exact labels, export mode, registration/configuration, rejection decisions and coverage qualification in a namespace incompatible with complete graph identities. Lean discovery returns handles, source/provenance summary and match reason; selected properties require detail. Exact compiler projection policy is reused, including endpoint closure for hidden BusinessRule relationships. Zero-match windows can continue; output-limited candidates are not skipped. Caller-selected `after_id` positions a raw tail, and Exhausted asserts only that tail: no global absence, complete-system claim, inferred degree or complete graph ID is emitted.
- **AC-0306** Given a visible selected fact in a current scoped session, retained evidence validates its exact complete fact digest, source binding, registered source, original version-aware receipt and range under retention protection. The selected binding and source registration are rechecked around the guard and host qualifications are rechecked before receipt access. Text reads reconstruct and compare the entire issued target; tampering, stale generation/qualifications, malformed/missing associations and unavailable retained bytes fail closed with no checkout fallback. Descriptor/target ingress and response limits precede allocation or file reads where applicable; one descriptor ordinal is returned at a time. Scoped targets cannot stand in for complete-context/investigation targets.
- **AC-0307** Given an activation policy, mode-specific checked worker and transport reservations are validated before admission. Scoped accounting includes fixed bases, admitted schema metadata, scope/coverage/rejection observations, keys/query normalization, current window/selected closure, digest/binding work, candidate/response roles and retained receipt/file/span workspace. Partition accounting additionally includes cumulative indexed acquisition, retained closure maps, frontier and typed rule-reference sets before lookup, projection/native renderer workspace, qualified content/assertions and protocol serialization while the frozen session remains live; arithmetic overflow refuses work. Numeric ceilings are unchanged. Cancellation of an async waiter does not free capacity owned by a running worker, and validation-read charges are nonrefundable. These are named logical payload units, not process RSS guarantees.
- **AC-0308** Given explicit scoped mode and a synthetic graph above the complete row ceiling, the real app worker and authenticated native Unix socket route initialization/list/capabilities/discovery/detail through scoped acquisition; a subsequent mutation makes continuation stale. Explicit complete mode still refuses the same over-limit graph and does not fall back. Mutations and complete artifact compilation are unavailable through the scoped tool catalog. This automated native fixture does not establish installed packaging, independent-client/provider operation, Windows support, or latency targets.
- **AC-0309** Given explicit activation policy, the template retains the complete default and the UI explains scoped partial reads, whole connected data-entity or single-rule partition scope, possible whole-component limit refusal, and unavailable flows/complete artifacts; the UI story does not establish native or provider acceptance.
- **AC-0311** Given a DataEntity component entry or an explicit BusinessRule observation at an expected StoreRevision, only a bounded storage reader pinned to one transaction constructs the private producer closure. Data partitions exhaust both indexed incident directions for the native data-model relation labels, include the connected DataEntity component and required context endpoints, and rule partitions include the selected observation's owner, graph dependency targets and gap references, including local definitions; context observations remain lookup-only. Missing required endpoints/references, an unavailable entry, corruption or exhausted acquisition/output limits return no prefix artifact. Native projection and rendering are reused, including hidden BusinessRule relationship policy. Sorted qualified closure and semantic roots produce distinct partition schema/basis/content identities; two visible entry roots in the same data component have identical content and identities regardless of acquisition order. These are closed stored-fact partitions with complete_system false, not complete graph or behavioral-completeness proofs.
- **AC-0312** Given a current scoped MCP session, cartograph_read_artifact accepts only typed data-model, rule-evidence or rule-evidence-index partition requests and returns the requested entry separately from the canonical component identity. The whole response obeys its byte limit, preserves scoped session/revision qualifications and partial-coverage flags, and refuses stale expected revisions or changed external qualifications without minting a replacement basis. A concurrent graph commit cannot alter the request's pinned snapshot; the next request at the old revision fails. The catalog distinguishes supported bounded partitions from complete-only and unavailable flow artifacts; no discovery-page union or caller graph vector becomes a closure proof. Partition work is admitted by the checked worker floor while complete acquisition, complete compiler output and all prior transport/evidence gates remain unchanged.
- **AC-0581** Given a retained evidence read through MCP, when the span text is returned, then it has passed the host's free-text redaction first, the response names the redaction policy version and the number of replacements, and a non-zero count tells the client the text is not byte-identical to the captured span. The response-size limit is checked on the redacted text. No MCP read returns the raw span (#1754).
- **AC-1809** Given a complete MCP read session, when a client calls `cartograph_query` with an exact node's neighborhood (one to three undirected hops), one exact node or all facts, optionally narrowed to nodes or edges and to exact labels, then it receives one bounded, deterministic page of that session's visible projection, the same facts the in-app agent's `query_context` selects: the nodes in range and the edges among them, each with its tier, confidence and evidence references, under the session's coverage qualification. A continuation is accepted only with the cursor and context that issued it; a forged session or basis is stale; an unknown field, a radius past three or an anchor the session does not show is refused without naming hidden facts. Facts a verified-only session or a rejection hides are never selected. The capabilities name `cartograph_query` as the relations route, the worker reservation charges its workspace, and scoped sessions do not offer it (#250).
- **AC-1810** Given a scoped MCP read session, when a client calls `cartograph_query` with an exact anchor and one to three hops, optionally narrowed to nodes or edges and to exact labels, then the host acquires that node's undirected induced neighborhood through the bounded revision-pinned reader, re-selects it over the session's visible projection so a node the session hides never connects two visible ones, and returns one bounded, deterministic page with `complete_system` and `absence_claim` false. Exhausting the window limits returns a limit, never a partial neighborhood. A continuation is accepted only with a cursor from the same selection at the same revision; a forged session basis is stale; an unknown field, a radius past three or an anchor the session does not show is refused without naming it. Capabilities list `cartograph_query` with the neighborhood scope, and the scoped worker reservation charges its workspace (#250).
- **Security:** Read-only allowlist; same-account credential trust is explicit; no provider or target-write authority is granted by a session or shortcut.
- **Performance:** Explicit bounded acquisition and disclosure; retained graphs, indexes and artifact caches require aggregate admission independently of frame limits. Raw graph bytes are not a resident-memory measurement.
- **Trace:** W8a (#718) · `spec`, `context-hub`, `mcp-wire`, `mcp-local`, `mcp-relay`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0044 · T-0288..0290,T-0304..0309,T-0311..0312,T-0581,T-1809,T-1810

### US-0042 — Record and reopen a saved finding's human follow-up decision
- **Actor:** Human investigation reviewer
- **As a** reviewer **I want** to record whether to use an exact saved investigation finding for follow-up and reopen that decision **so that** later work can identify my historical selection without mistaking it for evidence validation or execution permission.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0293** Given a validated saved investigation result, when I review one finding and reopen it after restart or finished-job cleanup, then only that exact finding's decision survives, bound to the whole immutable result, original task/definition/configured-provider/scope/limits identity and result-local finding ID; mixed-run subjects or corrupt saved evidence fail closed, while current runtime/catalog changes do not redefine historical identity.
- **AC-0294** Given a supported version-1 investigation store, when it opens, then one IMMEDIATE transaction migrates and validates the exact version-2 review schema without rewriting historical bodies/hashes or inserting pending rows; malformed or unsupported layouts fail closed and failed migration restores the original layout. Given concurrent decisions with one expected review revision, when both attempt to commit, then exactly one advances that revision and the other receives the durable winner; stale retries, storage/capacity/admission failures never report success or change durable state.
- **AC-0295** Given a readable historical result, when its review metadata loads, changes, conflicts or becomes unavailable, then the existing Investigations UI preserves the result/citations and T3/InferredWeak badge, labels only Pending review, Accepted for follow-up or Rejected for follow-up, and offers Use for follow-up / Do not use for follow-up with revision/time and deliberate conflict recovery; stale responses cannot replace a newer selection/decision, lost acknowledgments cause refresh rather than automatic resubmission, and the unchanged result API/readability is independent of the new metadata-only APIs.
- **AC-0296** Given either follow-up decision or a review read, when it completes, then it makes zero provider or current-source acquisition calls and changes no graph, Gap, context/export, immutable result/ledger/receipt reference, job/task lifecycle revision or model/tool counter; structurally valid historical unavailable/forgotten source observations do not rewrite the decision, and corrupt saved bindings disable review without a current-checkout fallback or a claim of verified evidence, curated activation, execution authority or #598 acceptance.
- **AC-0733** Given a follow-up decision whose connection cannot take the store's write lock because another connection holds it, when the job store's 5-second lock wait ends, then the command returns the typed `busy` outcome instead of a storage failure, nothing of that decision is written, and the UI states that the decision was not saved and asks for a refresh and a deliberate new choice without retrying by itself. A writer that gets the lock within the wait still receives the committed result or the durable winner (AC-0294). The 5-second wait is the product choice for every job-store connection (#1246): long enough for a competing review, task or migration write on a loaded host, short enough that a stuck writer surfaces as `busy` rather than a hung action.
- **Security:** Host resolves an ID-only subject from validated saved records; caller cannot supply findings, citations, provider identity, namespace or paths. Review is a human association, separate from graph-proposal curation.
- **Performance:** Existing raw action/metadata response bounds and task/global storage ceilings; checked UTF-8/scalar/index accounting, off-thread IPC and bounded current rows only. No audit stream or new execution budgets.
- **Trace:** W8b / H4 dependency (#730) · `agents`, `app`, `ui` · SPEC-03/SPEC-10 · T-0293..0296. Host and UI automation are mapped; native manual procedures remain UNEXECUTED.



### US-0043 — Request durable specialist investigations through MCP
- **Actor:** Authorized external agent
- **As an** authorized external agent **I want** to request and follow a durable specialist investigation through MCP **so that** reconnects and retries preserve the same host-owned work and cited outcome.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0320** Read-only credentials reject start, status, events, result and cancel. Only an owner app action grants task access; client JSON cannot choose principal or grant. Task argument envelopes accept only the fields of their closed operation; start request contents remain untrusted until host domain validation. The runtime grant primitive defaults read-only and revokes all shared sessions on revoke/drop; host authentication and an explicit owner-command grant are required. Settings separates client creation/selection from an explicit task grant, shows observed authority, and clears stale authority on lifecycle changes or failed refresh. The owner activation validates the registered principal and task reservation before opening an endpoint; owner status reports the live grant, and rotation requires renewed authorization. The authenticated Service factory passes its activation-owned session to the worker only after the transport handshake; default and rotated activations continue to deny task operations.
- **AC-0321** The same host-assigned principal survives reconnect/rotation; old credentials fail, rotated credentials lack a task grant until reconfirmed. Restart restores identity without execution authority. Host activation validates the selected durable principal before endpoint creation; rotation revokes old sessions and a cancelled rotation or concurrent disable/shutdown cannot restore access. Persisted client IDs contain no credentials or grants; bounded owner creation/listing preserves existing identities when creation capacity is full.
- **AC-0322** Same principal/nonce/normalized intent returns one task under concurrent requests and after graph/provider/capacity changes. Different intent refuses; another principal cannot collide with that namespace. Durable readers reject origin/intent/namespace disagreement before exposing task data, and namespace bytes count toward stored capacity. Host storage start admission and early retry lookup derive principal and normalized caller intent while holding the live grant through their transactions; new admission also derives identity capacity. Early MCP lookup reads only that principal’s nonce namespace before mutable context/provider preparation. The existing host start coordinator carries a live session through early retry and durable admission; App callers retain their existing namespace and detached worker path.
- **AC-0323** Migration preserves every old App task, body hash and nonce interpretation. Invalid layout or interrupted migration leaves the old store intact; textual app/MCP nonce collisions cannot alias tasks.
- **AC-0324** Status/events/result/cancel and follow-up history refuse foreign principals and App-created subjects without revealing which exists. Owner app inspection remains available. Host status/events/result/cancel hold the live runtime grant through their storage operation; revoked or read-only sessions refuse before opening a transaction. MCP cancellation chooses its family from the persisted task, not caller input.
- **AC-0325** Disconnect/disable does not cancel admitted work; explicit cancellation and restart recovery use the existing coordinator and never duplicate unknown provider execution. The completion coordinator limits detached owners of a supplied admitted reservation to the existing two-task ceiling and retains each slot inside the actual blocking worker until exit; a saved nonce replays before checking new reservation activity. Settings exposes owner-requested credential rotation only for a running service, clears old configuration while the operation is pending, and requires a fresh configuration request afterward; a failed request cannot leave stale running state or configuration displayed.
- **AC-0326** MCP sees pending-consent status but cannot approve it. App approval remains bound to exact task/step/revision/payload; cloud egress remains subject to existing policy.
- **AC-0327** Internal task-enabled connection admission requires the existing connection floor plus two independently live task reservations before acquiring a permit. Each task reserves the greater of its inclusive qualified-preparation peak and its retained initial frozen context, accepted read growth and the largest modeled execution/replay peak; overflow or a reservation below that floor refuses admission. The checked preparation component covers bounded graph/scope/coverage projections, archive/ledger validation and retained context; overflow refuses. Host query preparation uses those full graph/coverage bounds and the retained canonical app-path length, never the client page cap; query-retained metadata and guards stay charged across modeled execution. Accepted read growth reserves the bounded current ledger, shared query/inventory bytes and item slots, and evidence metadata/text/items throughout execution; transient acquisition remains separate. Caller-input construction, two retained current/candidate inputs, sealing and metadata/source preview certificates contribute checked logical bounds, including growth before final wire rejection. Original provider payloads and disclosure copies stay charged during continuation; public dispatch remains disabled. Retained source leases, receipt/selection metadata, base/candidate ledgers, acknowledged reservation/validation details and exact acquired text remain charged outside the execution-phase maximum. This retained-owner contribution excludes transient acquisition and settlement work; it does not complete task admission. Inventory preparation additionally reserves its DTO peak and host wrapper, retaining its two ledger copies and raw response across durable publication/replay. Later store/read/provider execution and allocator/native/RSS remain separate, and public task activation stays disabled. Request bytes are bounded before parse; event pages and result envelopes are bounded before allocation. Full nonce capacity refuses new starts while preserving existing retries and reads. Saved-record decoding exposes a checked logical scratch component for strict preflight and canonical comparison, including rejected keys and both comparison Values. Caller input/typed DTOs, custom serde behavior, collection slack and native/RSS remain separate; numeric decoder limits and task availability do not change. Bounded HTTP completions expose checked logical payload ownership for retained completion output and request/response peaks (`completion_bytes`, `request_peak_bytes`, `response_peak_bytes`), refusing arithmetic overflow. These bounds account for span/user-message overlap, capped wire bodies and receive chunk, actual response DTO/string/vector/JSON owners before semantic rejection, decoder scratch and concatenated output. Request serializers continue to borrow payload text. Borrowed authorization/provider configuration, profile validation, HTTP headers/client/TLS internals, unmodeled stacks and allocator/RSS are excluded and require separate accounting; this component alone neither admits a worker nor enables task transport. Core queries expose checked logical response and peak workspace components from separately admitted projection and request-wire bounds; the response component covers fact clones before byte rejection, and the peak includes normalized selection, neighborhood sets, identity construction and cursor replacement. Arithmetic overflow refuses the component. Borrowed snapshot, coverage envelope, framing and allocator/native/RSS ownership remain separate; this component does not enable task transport or establish complete worker admission. Coverage archive decoding bounds canonical encoding before allocating comparison trees, using its existing separate archive ceiling; nested field validation and accepted archive contents remain unchanged. This decoder safeguard alone does not establish complete worker admission. Bounded firewall preparation exposes checked logical ownership for retained prepared/authorized DTOs and preparation/authorization peaks (`prepared_bytes`, `authorized_bytes`, `prepare_peak_bytes`, `authorize_peak_bytes`). Authorization while retaining preparation composes `prepared_bytes + authorize_peak_bytes`; checked arithmetic refuses overflow. The component accounts for capped profile/payload strings, span/ID-set slots, redaction expansion before refusal and overlapping fingerprint buffers, using the same caps as enforcement. Caller actions, HTTP payloads, provider configuration, regex/native internals and allocator/RSS are excluded and require separate accounting; this component alone neither admits a worker nor enables task transport. Source-discovery cap refusals return `limit_exceeded`, while stale selections return `refresh_required`. Host-supplied source-discovery limits bound both completion-selection observations before new durable admission and survive completion-worker detachment and bound qualified registration/plugin/event discovery during context preparation; desktop preparation retains its existing path. Coverage proof batch validation exposes checked temporary logical workspace for identity canonicalization and sequential retained-inventory parse/derive, including rejected inputs. Callers separately bound typed proof serialization, each inventory body and repo count, and retain responsibility for decoded input ownership; single-record unmatched-key cloning is outside this batch model. Saved-ledger validation composes proof workspace before manifest admission, selection/receipt/citation indexes during bounded serialization, and subsequent canonical/domain-hash buffers. Archive semantic validation composes its separate bounded encoding and sequential proof/inventory phases. These helpers exclude retained inputs and decoding and do not establish complete worker admission. Inventory preparation separately composes archive-read detail/encoding, raw response and original/candidate ledger owners, with manifest escaping/revision growth and candidate validation before final admission. The host wrapper, frozen context/request, durable publication and later prompt/provider execution are separately owned. #866 composes qualified query coverage clones and outer request ownership with the existing core model; host evidence, retained old pages and full worker admission remain separate. Shared host receipt workspace accounts for retained decode and peak wire-variant/template/conversion/validation owners, including compact v3 preflight refusals, and is consumed by existing evidence reservation. Other SQL cells, caller lifetime, allocator/native memory and full task admission remain separate; receipt semantics and numeric policy ceilings are unchanged. Receipt fact matching exposes checked logical workspace against the fact's compact wire: key clone, digest canonicalization and encoding, and the provenance, range and rule decode/validation owners, including inventories rejected at the range cap. Rule validation charges typed owners from its wire and validator scratch from its existing collection caps. The caller retains the receipt and fact; host composition and worker admission remain separate. Callers holding an admitted value count may pass it in place of the one-value-per-byte assumption; it must count every value, including malformed members, and never loosens the wire-only model. Detached MCP execution retains the admitted source file-validation and span caps: retained metadata refuses excess before reservation/charge, and the final captured-text read enforces the same limits. Desktop source policy is unchanged. Task execution admission separately includes shared capture manifest decoding/canonicalization and bounded selected-object/span read workspace; receipt/guard owners remain retained outside the phase maximum. Source preparation/revalidation additionally composes complete selection observation, guard and receipt acquisition, receipt fact matching, target construction before refusal and capture metadata validation while preliminary guards remain held. Source candidate-ledger ownership and validation use the base ledger plus the actual citation envelope and validated target wire, including append/revision growth before manifest refusal; Durable source snapshot admission includes projected task/observation growth before row refusal, nested authority/attempt replay and returned owners retained across subsequent input construction; publication transaction accounting remains separate. Source capacity replay also retains the bounded per-task accounting map and pending-task set through nested immutable reservation validation; this component does not establish complete writer admission. Durable source settlement additionally retains its projected task, authority, attempts, candidate preparation and disclosure across validation, retained-source callback, capacity/journal replay, persistence and acknowledgment; terminal observations are bounded before final encoding.
- **AC-0328** Shared host adapters preserve specialist version, scope, policy, citations, T3 ceiling and retention lifetimes. App and MCP observe the same durable outcome; no direct graph mutation or relay database access occurs. An admitted snapshot can expose all/neighborhood fact references without cloning property payloads or constructing response pages; membership matches ordinary query pages, including induced edges and unknown-anchor refusal. Checked reference-set and peak workspace components fail on overflow; snapshot and consumed scope ownership remain separately admitted, and this helper alone does not establish worker admission or transport execution.
- **AC-0329** A real relay/socket fixture with deterministic provider observes start, ordered events, reconnect, cancel and result with clean shutdown. Evidence identifies exact build/profile and does not claim real-provider H4 or ACP H5.
- **AC-0840** The principal identity cap (`maximum_identities`) limits that principal's live (non-terminal) tasks; it is a local setting sized to the machine, not a lifetime quota. Terminal tasks the principal owns stay stored and readable but do not count toward the cap. "Live" is the store's existing terminal-state flag, the same one the global live-task ceiling counts (#1761, ADR-0073 question 4).
- **AC-0841** A new MCP start whose principal already has as many live tasks as its cap refuses with Capacity before any write, even while the global live-task ceiling has room; once one of those tasks becomes terminal the principal can start new work again.
- **AC-0842** A retry with the same principal, nonce and normalized intent returns the existing task without creating one, whether that task is live or terminal and whether or not the principal is at its live cap; a different intent on that nonce still refuses with Conflict.
- **Security:** Owner-only task grant, host-derived principal, own-task visibility and owner-only consent; relay has no database or provider access.
- **Performance:** Explicit bounded transport and storage admission; no silent nonce eviction or numeric investigation ceiling changes.
- **Trace:** H4 (#766) · agents, app, llm, mcp-wire, mcp-local, mcp-relay, ui · ADR-0028, ADR-0044 · T-0320..0329, T-0840..0842. Storage migration (#777), client registry (#783), origin consistency (#785), App nonce isolation (#788), host-internal principal-scoped reads/cancellation (#792), and principal/nonce storage admission (#795) cover prerequisites. Authenticated runtime authorization, MCP task creation, follow-up history and relay acceptance remain planned in #766.

### US-0044 — Recover the target tests that reference recovered behavior
- **Actor:** Reviewer or developer
- **As a** reviewer **I want** the repository's own tests linked to the endpoints they request, with what each asserts **so that** I can see which recovered behavior has a static test reference, which has none recovered, and where tests and the recovered contract disagree.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0343** Given pytest tests (`test*` functions at module level, or `test*` methods of module-level `Test*` classes, in `test_*.py` or `*_test.py` files), when the repo is ingested, then each becomes a Confirmed `TestCase` citing its definition. A call `c.get/post/put/patch/delete/head/options(…)` in a test is read only when `c` is a proven Flask test client: a test parameter naming an import-proven `@pytest.fixture` (or `@pytest.fixture(name=…)`) that returns or yields `….test_client(…)`, directly or through a local bound once, found as the nearest fixture of that name in the same file (the last definition) or the nearest `conftest.py` of its directory or an ancestor — a nearer fixture that is not proven a client shadows it — and not rebound in the test; or a local bound once in the test to `….test_client(…)`. A string-literal target (query and fragment dropped) is matched against recovered route templates of the call's method, preferring the most static segments. An import-proven `url_for("<name>")` that no parameter or local of the test shadows (neither `url_for` nor the `flask` module name) builds the URL of the Endpoint whose `endpoint_name` is that name, and the call's method then selects the Endpoint served at that URL. Flask Endpoints carry `endpoint_name`: the dotted Blueprint names along the mount chain (a mount's `name=` replaces the Blueprint's), then the route's `endpoint=` or the view function's name. Each test's calls to one Endpoint are one InferredStrong `REQUESTS` edge marked `client_app_assumed`, listing `via` (`url_for` or `path`), the statuses asserted with `==` on the response's `status_code` and the literal keys read from its `json` or `get_json()` between the call and the response name's next binding, and citing each call and the proving fixture or local. A call with `follow_redirects=` other than a literal `False`, or with `**mapping` unless it is a literal dict with only literal keys whose `follow_redirects`, if present, is a literal `False`, records no expectations and marks the edge `follows_redirects`. A computed target, an endpoint name or path no recovered Endpoint serves, a method not served at a name's URL, a name that builds more than one URL, or a path matching more than one Endpoint equally is a Gap linked from the test by `REQUESTS`, naming the request and the reason. Calls in helper functions are not read. These are static references and declared expectations: they do not prove that a test ran or which branches it reached.
- **AC-0344** Given recovered `TestCase` facts and their `REQUESTS` edges, when the spec bundle is compiled, then `test_arrangements.md` states that its references are static and not evidence that a test runs or passes, and counts the recovered tests, the test → endpoint references, the unresolved test requests (which `gap_register.md` lists) and the recovered Endpoints referenced by at least one test out of all recovered Endpoints. It lists each referenced Endpoint with the tests that reference it (by path and qualified name), the union of their asserted statuses and the union of the response keys they read, then each recovered Endpoint with no recovered test reference. Every reference and every unreferenced Endpoint is an inline assertion keeping its own confidence; a rejected inferred reference is suppressed, never upgraded.
- **AC-0345** Given a recovered Endpoint with `responses` and at least one resolved `REQUESTS` edge from a `TestCase`, when its repository is ingested, then the statuses its tests assert and the response keys they read are compared with its recovered `statuses` and `keys`. When the responses are complete and no referencing call follows redirects, a status or key that tests expect but the responses never return is one InferredStrong `asserted-not-returned` `Drift` (kind `test-drift`), and a returned status that no resolved reference asserts is one InferredWeak `returned-not-asserted` `Drift` that says unresolved test requests and helper calls are not read. Each cites the test calls and the response evidence and links `SERVED_AS` to the Endpoint and `ASSERTED_BY` to each test. When the responses are `partial`, or a referencing call follows redirects, the Endpoint is instead a Gap (kind `test-drift-gap`) naming that reason, not drift; so is an Endpoint whose own provenance, or whose `responses` provenance, is missing, invalid, Gap or cites no evidence. A test request whose provenance cites no evidence contributes nothing. A finding is never stronger than the Endpoint, the weakest referencing test or the response it rests on: those tiers cap the InferredStrong and InferredWeak defaults, and its edges carry the same tier. Unresolved test requests contribute nothing. `drift_register.md` lists both directions and the incomplete endpoints under `## Test and contract drift` once the repository has tests; verified-only export keeps the strong findings and drops the weak ones.
- **AC-0346** Given recovered `TestCase` facts, when a repository is ingested, then each test is linked by `IMPLEMENTED_BY` to the `Symbol` of its own function; and when the spec bundle is compiled, then each flow in `flow_dossiers.md` gains a **Test references (static)** table listing every participant with the tests that reference it directly — a resolved `REQUESTS` to an Endpoint, or a non-Gap `CALLS` from a function a non-Gap `IMPLEMENTED_BY` associates with the test — and the participants with no recovered test reference. A flow with no resolved hops lists its trigger as its one participant. It counts the referenced participants, states that a reference is not an execution and that reach through further calls is not counted, shows at most five tests per participant and counts the rest, and cites each edge proving a reference (the request, or the association and the call) once as an inline assertion keeping its own confidence. Unresolved requests and calls from non-test functions contribute nothing, and a repository without recovered tests renders its dossiers unchanged.
- **AC-0347** Given a TypeScript class decorated `@Resolver()` whose methods are decorated `@Query()` or `@Mutation()`, all import-proven from `@nestjs/graphql`, when the repository is ingested, then each such method is one `Endpoint` with `protocol: "graphql"`, `operation` `query` or `mutation`, a `field`, and an `api` scope, and the Endpoint `HANDLES` the method. The field is the method name unless the decorator names it with a literal first argument or a literal `name` option; a type thunk names nothing, and a computed name is a Gap (kind `graphql-field-computed`), not a guessed field. The API is the directory below the nearest `resolvers/` segment (`scope_basis: "resolver_directory"`), otherwise `default` (`scope_basis: "none"`). The Endpoint is InferredStrong because that mounting is assumed, and operations with the same API, kind and field are one Endpoint handling each method. `@Allow(Permission.X, …)` on the method is recorded as `permissions` only when every argument is a `Permission.<Ident>` member. `@ResolveField` methods and a look-alike `Resolver` not imported from `@nestjs/graphql` emit no Endpoint. A resolver class that is also an import-proven `@Controller()` keeps its HTTP routes. Namespace-qualified decorators (`@Gql.Query()`) are not read, as for HTTP controllers. GraphQL Endpoints are not matched against HTTP API docs.
- **AC-0348** Given Vitest, Jest or Bun tests (`describe`/`it`/`test` and their `.only`/`.skip`/`.concurrent`/`.sequential`/`.todo`/`.fails` forms, import-proven from `vitest`, `@jest/globals` or `bun:test`, or unshadowed globals) in `*.test.*`, `*.spec.*` or `*.e2e-spec.*` files, when the repository is ingested, then each test with a literal name under literally named `describe` blocks is a Confirmed `TestCase` named by its `describe > … > it` chain (a repeated chain is suffixed ` [n]`) and citing its call; a computed name (including `it.each`) is a Gap (kind `test-name-computed`). A call `c.query(doc)` anywhere in the test's arguments after its name is read only when `c` is a proven test client: destructured, under its registered name, from a `const` call to a registered client factory import-proven from its package or from the factory's own source file (`createTestEnvironment` of `@vendure/testing` yields `adminClient` → `admin` and `shopClient` → `shop`), and not shadowed at the call. The document is an import-proven `graphql(…)` or `gql` literal at the call, a `const` bound once to one in scope, or an exported `const` of the file a relative import resolves to (an explicit extension names exactly that file, or for `.js`/`.jsx` the TypeScript source NodeNext emits it from; an extensionless one names the first candidate path that exists; another candidate's same-named export is never used); its single operation's root fields (aliases resolved, `__typename` ignored) select the recovered GraphQL Endpoint of the client's API, kind and field, else the `default` API's. Each test's requests to one Endpoint are one `REQUESTS` edge with `via: ["graphql"]` citing each call, the client binding and the document: InferredStrong marked `client_api_assumed`, or InferredWeak marked `client_api_default_fallback` when any field fell back to `default`. A document that is not recovered, holds no or several operations, selects a fragment or interpolation at its root, or a root field no recovered operation serves is a Gap linked from the test by `REQUESTS`, naming the reason (and the field). Calls outside a test's arguments (hooks such as `beforeAll`, helper functions) are not read, nor are the bodies of functions the test only declares (a function declaration, method or class, or a function bound to a name or property, through any parentheses or type wrappers); a function passed as an argument, such as a wrapper's callback or a `.map` callback, is read. Each test is linked by `IMPLEMENTED_BY` to the Symbol of its callback, as AC-0346 requires. These are static references: they do not prove that a test ran or which operations it reached.
- **AC-0920** Given a JavaScript/TypeScript test file that imports Node's built-in runner (`node:test`), when it is ingested, then its `test`/`it` calls (named, or `test` as the default import) and `describe`/`suite` groups are TestCases as for Vitest/Jest, with framework `node-test`; a lookalike module proves nothing (#2008).
- **AC-0960** Given Go test files (`*_test.go`), when the repository is ingested, then each top-level `func TestXxx(t *testing.T)` (the `testing` import proven, the name after `Test` not starting lower-case) is a Confirmed `TestCase` (framework `go test`) citing its definition, `DEFINED_IN` its file and `IMPLEMENTED_BY` the Symbol of its function. A test file's functions are Symbols with the calls they make, resolved within the package (a test file's function answers only calls from test files and never shadows the package's own), and nothing else in it (routes, client calls, config reads, resources, rules) is a fact. An import-proven `httptest.NewRequest`, `http.NewRequest` or `http.NewRequestWithContext` in the test's own body whose method is a string literal or `http.MethodXxx` and whose target is a literal path or absolute URL (origin, query and fragment dropped) is matched, once the package's endpoints are known, to the Endpoint serving the method (`ANY` serves every method) whose route matches the path (`{name}`/`:name` one segment, a final `{name...}`/`*` the rest, a final `{$}` nothing more), the most literal segments winning; each test's requests to one Endpoint are one InferredStrong `REQUESTS` edge marked `handler_assumed`, citing each request and listing the statuses the test compares a response's `Code` or `StatusCode` with (`==`/`!=`, an integer or `http.StatusXxx`). A computed method or target, a path and method no Endpoint serves, or two equally specific matches is a Gap linked from the test by `REQUESTS`, naming the request and the reason. A call in one file of a package to a function another file of the package defines is a `CALLS` edge. These are static references: they do not prove that a test ran (#2019).
- **AC-1016** Given Rust source, when the Rust adapter recovers the workspace, then each `fn` annotated `#[test]`, `#[rstest]` or `#[X::test]` for a test runtime (`tokio`, `actix_web`, `actix_rt`, `async_std`, `sqlx`, `test_log`) is a Confirmed `TestCase` (framework `cargo test`, with the attribute) citing its definition, `DEFINED_IN` its file and `IMPLEMENTED_BY` the Symbol of its function. A request chain in the test's own body that starts at `Request::builder()` or `Request::get(uri)` (and the other verbs) of a `Request` the file's `use`s (a test module's `use super::*` included) or a qualified path prove to be `http`, `axum` or `hyper`'s, or at actix-web's `TestRequest::get()` (and the other verbs), `TestRequest::default()` or `TestRequest::with_uri(uri)`, whose method is the verb, a `.method(…)` string literal or `Method::X` (else GET) and whose target is a literal path or absolute URL (origin, query and fragment dropped) in the constructor or `.uri(…)`, is matched once every file is read to the Endpoint serving the method (`ANY` serves every method) whose route matches the path (`:name` and `{name}` one segment, a final `*name`, `{*name}` or `{name:.*}` the rest), the most literal segments winning; each test's requests to one Endpoint are one InferredStrong `REQUESTS` edge marked `handler_assumed`, citing each request and listing the statuses the test compares a response's `status()` with in `assert_eq!`, `assert_ne!` or `assert!(… == …)` (an integer, `.as_u16()` or `StatusCode::X`). A computed method or target, a path and method no Endpoint serves, or two equally specific matches is a Gap naming the request. SQL statements (AC-1015) in test code (a test function, a `#[cfg(test)]` module inline or declared out of line with `#[cfg(test)] mod x;` and the modules under it, a module named `tests` or `*_tests`, or a crate's `tests/` directory) are not facts about the system (#2018).
- **AC-0965** Given Java test sources, when the repository is ingested, then each method annotated `@Test`, `@RepeatedTest` (`org.junit.jupiter.api`), `@ParameterizedTest` (`org.junit.jupiter.params`) or JUnit 4 `@Test` (`org.junit`), proven by a named or wildcard import or its qualified spelling (one proven by a wildcard import alone is no test when the test's package declares a type of that name), is a Confirmed `TestCase` (`framework: "junit"`) `DEFINED_IN` its file and `IMPLEMENTED_BY` its method Symbol; each `MockMvcRequestBuilders` call in its own body (`get`/`post`/`put`/`patch`/`delete`/`head`/`options`, or `request(HttpMethod.X, …)`), proven by a static import or its class name, whose URL is a literal, a `+` concatenation or a `static final String` constant the repository declares once (constants of constants settled), is joined to the Spring endpoint whose route matches its path (origin, query and fragment dropped; a route `{var}` matches one segment, including the test's own URI-template `{var}`, a literal route segment only a literal) and serves its method, the most specific route winning, as one InferredStrong `REQUESTS` edge per test and endpoint marked `handler_assumed` with the statuses the test expects (`status().isXxx()`, `status().is(n)`); a computed method or URL, a path no Spring endpoint serves, or a tie is a Gap naming the request (#2020).
- **AC-1102** Given Kotlin test sources, when the repository is ingested, then each function annotated `@Test`, `@RepeatedTest` (`org.junit.jupiter.api`), `@ParameterizedTest` (`org.junit.jupiter.params`), JUnit 4's `@Test` (`org.junit`) or `kotlin.test`'s `@Test` (proven by the qualified spelling, a named or aliased import, or a star import that no product Kotlin or Java declaration of that name in the test's package obscures) becomes a Confirmed `TestCase` (framework `junit`, language `kotlin`) `DEFINED_IN` its file and `IMPLEMENTED_BY` its function Symbol; and each request in its body (Spring's Kotlin MockMvc DSL `mockMvc.get("…")` and the other verbs, proven by an import from `org.springframework.test.web.servlet`; a `MockMvcRequestBuilders` builder; or a Ktor test client verb proven by an import from `io.ktor.client.request`; a DSL or client verb counts only on a receiver declared as a `MockMvc` or Ktor `HttpClient`, built by `HttpClient(…)` or `createClient`, or `client` inside `testApplication { … }`) whose URL is a string literal, with `$x`/`${…}` template parts standing for one path segment, is joined to the Spring or Ktor endpoint whose route matches its path and method (the most literal route wins) as one InferredStrong `REQUESTS` edge per test and endpoint (`link: handler_assumed`) carrying the statuses the test expects (`status { isOk() }`, `status().isNotFound()`). A computed method or URL, a path no recovered endpoint serves, and two equally specific matches are Gaps naming the request; calls inside MockK `every`/`verify` blocks are not read.
- **AC-1112** Given Kotlin test sources, when the repository is ingested, then each class extending a Kotest spec style (`FunSpec`, `StringSpec`, `ShouldSpec`, `DescribeSpec`, `BehaviorSpec`, `WordSpec`, `FreeSpec`, `FeatureSpec`, `ExpectSpec`) proven by import from `io.kotest.core.spec.style` has each test its constructor lambda or `init` blocks declare (the style's leaf call, such as `test`, `it`, `then`, `should`, `scenario`, `expect`, or a string invoked with a lambda in `StringSpec`, `FreeSpec` and `WordSpec`, inside the style's containers such as `context`, `describe`, `given`/`when`, `feature`, `"x" - { }` and `"x" should { }`, and through loops and other statements around them) as a Confirmed `TestCase` (framework `kotest`, its style, `disabled` for an `x`-prefixed call) named by its class and container chain joined by ` > `, `DEFINED_IN` its file and with no `IMPLEMENTED_BY` (a style imported under an alias proves it too; a test under an `x`-prefixed container is `disabled`; calls inside a local function, class or anonymous function register none); the requests its lambda performs are read and linked as AC-1102's are; a test or container whose name is not a plain string literal yields a Gap instead of a test; and a `FunSpec` the repository declares itself, or a star-imported style that a same-package product type obscures, is no Kotest spec (#2021).
- **Trace:** R2 · `adapters-lang-go`, `adapters-lang-java`, `adapters-lang-kotlin` · — · T-0960,T-0965,T-1102,T-1112

### US-0045 — Recover the journeys users take through a client
- **Actor:** Reviewer or developer
- **As a** reviewer **I want** each client's screens, the navigations between them and the server calls made on the way recovered from source **so that** I can follow what a user does to reach a goal and which server behavior each step reaches, without trusting a model's account of it.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0349** Given a `new VueRouter({ routes })` or `createRouter({ routes })` call whose callee is import-proven from `vue-router`, when the repository is ingested, then its route table is read when `routes` is an array literal, a `const` bound to one, or a call of a relatively imported function or class static method whose body returns one array literal. Each route object with a `path` becomes one Confirmed `Screen` (`screen:{repo}@{path}`, `router: "vue-router"`) citing the route and the registration. A path is a string literal or a member of a `const` object of literals, directly or through a class static field naming or holding one (`Navigation.PATH.MAIN`); a child path joins its parent's. The Screen `RENDERS` its component's file when the component is a relatively imported local, or a key of a `const` object of such imports passed as the table function's argument (`getRoutes(components)` with `components.Main`). The edge is Confirmed when the import names the file it resolves to; an extensionless import found only as an existing `.vue` file assumes the bundler's extension list, and its edge is InferredStrong with `resolution: "vue_extension_assumed"`. An import with an explicit extension names only that file or, for `.js`/`.jsx`, its NodeNext TypeScript source; it never falls back to `.vue`. A redirect without a component renders nothing; the same route registered twice is one Screen, and a rendering registration wins. A computed path, a table that does not resolve, or a component that does not resolve is a Gap (kind `route-unresolved`) naming the site, the last linked from its Screen by `RENDERS`; nothing is guessed. A local `VueRouter` not imported from `vue-router` registers nothing. The coverage matrix claims `vue-router` client routes for JavaScript and TypeScript, and partially for Vue, whose templates (and so `<router-link>` targets) are not parsed.
- **AC-0350** Given recovered Vue Router screens, when the repository is ingested, then a navigation becomes one InferredStrong `NAVIGATES_TO` edge from the Symbol whose body performs it (or the file, at module level) to the target `Screen`, with `router: "vue-router"`, `router_basis: "this.$router"` and `conditional`, citing each call. A navigation is either a direct `this.$router.push(target)` or `.replace(target)`, or a call of a relatively imported function or class static method that passes one of its parameters, unshadowed, to `router.push` or `router.replace`. In the second case, `router` must be a module `let`/`var` assigned only by setters of that file (`router = $router`), and some file must call such a setter with `this.$router`. The target is a literal, a constant-table member of the caller's file, or `Owner.FIELD.KEY` of a relatively imported class's static table. Calls from one Symbol to one Screen are one edge, since the store keeps one edge per source and target; it lists every call under `sites` in source order, each with its span, `via` (`direct` or `wrapper`) and its own `conditional`, and the edge is conditional only when every call is under an `if` branch, `switch` case, `catch`, conditional expression or the right of `&&`, `||` or `??`. A computed target, or one that names no recovered Screen, is a Gap (kind `navigation-unresolved`) linked by `NAVIGATES_TO`. Back navigations (`router.go(-1)`, `router.back()`) are not read.
- **AC-0351** Given an `entryProvider { … }` call whose callee is import-proven from `androidx.navigation3.runtime`, when the repository is ingested, then each `entry<X>(…) { … }` call in its trailing lambda with one type argument `X` that resolves to a uniquely declared repository type becomes one Confirmed `Screen` (`screen:{repo}@navigation3:{fqn}`, `router: "navigation3"`, `key: fqn`) citing the entry, or a `route-unresolved` Gap when `X` is not a declared repository type. Each top-level call statement in the entry's trailing lambda whose callee resolves to a repository function Symbol emits a Confirmed `RENDERS` edge from the Screen to that Symbol; callback arguments passed into entries emit no edges, and a local `entryProvider` registers nothing. In a class with a back-stack property initialized to `mutableStateListOf<NavKey>(…)`, `MutableStateFlow(mutableStateListOf<NavKey>(…))` or `rememberNavBackStack(…)` (with `NavKey` and `rememberNavBackStack` import-proven from `androidx.navigation3.runtime` and `MutableStateFlow` from `kotlinx.coroutines.flow`), each `add` on that property (`p.add`, `p.value.add`, or `add` inside `p.apply` / `p.value.apply`) — directly, through a same-class `NavKey` parameter helper that adds its unmutated parameter, or through a same-class `(…) -> NavKey` builder helper that adds the result of calling its parameter directly or via a local `val` — emits one InferredStrong `NAVIGATES_TO` edge from the enclosing function Symbol (or the enclosing class Symbol in an `init` block or property initializer) to the target `Screen`, with `router: "navigation3"`, `router_basis: "back_stack"`, `conditional` and `sites` (`via`: `direct`, `param_helper` or `builder_helper`), or a `navigation-unresolved` Gap when the target is computed or names a declared type with no recovered Screen. A back-stack initializer's single key argument marks its recovered `Screen` with `initial: true` and `back_stack_owner`, citing each initializer in the Screen's provenance (plus sorted `initial_owners` when named across classes), navigation sites in `init` blocks or property initializers carry `phase: "init"`, and callback arguments (`::fn`, non-overloaded `recv::fn`, or a single-call lambda; an unqualified name inside `with(recv) { … }` resolves to `recv`'s member first) passed to a rendered composable are recorded on the `Screen` under `entry_callbacks` with `read` (false only when the composable never references the parameter) and up to four direct parameter-forwarding hops. `removeAt`, `removeIf` and `clear` are ignored. The coverage matrix claims `navigation3` client routes partially for Kotlin.
- **AC-0352** Given recovered path-router `Screen` nodes with entry screens (`route == "/"`), `RENDERS` edges to component files or symbols, `NAVIGATES_TO` edges between screens, stitched `FETCHES` edges to server `Endpoint` nodes, and traced server flows, when the spec bundle is compiled, then rule `journey-candidate@1` recovers bounded T2 `InferredWeak` journey candidates (`ProducingMethod::Rule { rule: "journey-candidate", version: "1" }`) and renders them in `journeys.md`. Each screen's rendered-code call closure walks `RENDERS` targets (`Symbol` directly, or `Symbol` nodes with `DEFINED_IN` to a rendered `File`) and same-repo `CALLS` edges up to `max_calls_per_screen = 64`. Simple paths from each entry screen up to `max_screens = 6`, `max_expanded_states = 256` and `max_candidates = 32` per entry stop at the first step whose closure calls a goal `Endpoint`: a server flow reaching a `WRITES` edge to a `DataEntity` (`goal_basis: "observed_write"`) wins over an HTTP `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` call with no observed write (`goal_basis: "method_assumed"`, `write_coverage: "unknown"`), and earlier assumed-write calls before an observed write are listed under `earlier_assumed_goals`. A post-goal `NAVIGATES_TO` from the same `Symbol` and file path whose unconditional site starts at or after the `byte_end` of a goal call recorded as straight-line in that Symbol (`straight_line: true` on its `FETCHES` relation) appends one ordered result step (`result: "ordered"`); a navigation before the call, in another function, or only at a conditional site leaves `result: "unordered"`, and so does any navigation after a call under a branch, loop, `try`, short-circuit, optional chain or callback of that Symbol, or after a call whose relation does not record this (#1012). The TypeScript adapter records `straight_line` on each fetch site, and the relation keeps it through a system join; `"ordered"` says the navigation follows the call being made, never that the call completed first. Cycles are not re-entered, shortest paths win per `(entry, goal)` with extra simple paths counted in `alternatives` (`alternatives_complete: false` when any budget bound truncates), and unresolved `FETCHES` or `NAVIGATES_TO` `Gap` edges on a step are preserved without stopping traversal. Candidate `id` hashes `(version, budget, step_screen_ids, navigations_taken, goal.endpoint)` while `support_fingerprint` hashes the supporting steps, goal, flows, static test references and truncation state. Initial destinations (`initial: true`) are also entry screens; startup (`phase: "init"` on the back-stack owner) and read `entry_callbacks` navigations are crossed only as recorded `startup` / `entry_callback` assumptions with their limitation (every read registration reaching a crossed callback is listed and its registration span cited) while unread callbacks are listed on the step, and `journeys.md` shows a per-entry search summary. In `verified-only` export, T2 `InferredWeak` journey candidates are excluded and `journeys.md` states why; in `best-effort` export, each candidate renders with inline provenance and states that it is a static path candidate and not a recorded session or runtime trace.
- **AC-0353** Given a Kotlin call `x.m(…)`, `x?.m(…)` or `this.x.m(…)`, when the repository is ingested, then it resolves to a T0 `Confirmed` `CALLS` edge to `T.m` (`resolution: "declared-type"`, evidence the call span) when the nearest lexical binding of `x` states its type `T`: a function or lambda parameter, a primary-constructor `val`/`var` property or class-body property of the enclosing class (or of an outer class from an `inner` class), a plain constructor parameter outside member functions, or a local or top-level property with a type annotation or a `T(…)` initializer. Nullable and generic forms (`T?`, `T<X>`) denote `T`. `T` names, in Kotlin's precedence, an explicit import, a same-file type through the enclosing type chain, the same package, or a star-imported package; the first FQN this repository declares exactly once decides. A nearer binding with no stated type (an inferred or delegated local, a loop variable, a destructuring entry, an implicit `it`) blocks outer bindings. A member `T` does not declare, a duplicate FQN, or a `T(…)` initializer whose `T` is not a constructible class (or is also a top-level function) asserts nothing and produces no `Gap`.
- **AC-0354** Given a Kotlin `hiltViewModel` call whose callee is import-proven from `androidx.hilt.navigation.compose` or `androidx.hilt.lifecycle.viewmodel.compose`, when the repository is ingested, then a binding initialized by it has a declared type for AC-0353: `hiltViewModel<VM>(…)` denotes `VM`, and `hiltViewModel(…) { f: F -> f.m(…) }`, whose last expression calls a method on its single typed parameter, denotes the declared return type of `F.m` (resolved in `F.m`'s own file, as for a view model `@AssistedFactory`). A same-named function that is not import-proven, a callback of any other shape, and a `with` receiver with no declared type type nothing.
- **AC-0355** Given a Kotlin `@Binds` function in an abstract class or interface that carries import-proven `@dagger.Module` and `@dagger.hilt.InstallIn(SingletonComponent::class)` in a main source set, whose only other annotation is `@Singleton` and whose single parameter type `Impl` and return type `Iface` are plain repository types without type arguments, where `Impl` is a concrete class that directly declares `Iface` as a supertype and no other such binding names `Iface`, when the repository is ingested, then each member `m` declared on both gets an `InferredStrong` `CALLS` edge `Iface.m -> Impl.m` (`resolution: "hilt-binds"`, `assumption: "hilt_dispatch"`, `binding` the bind function, evidence the `@Binds` declaration), and the declared-interface edge stays `Confirmed`; any other component, qualifier, multibinding, generic key, duplicate key or non-main source set yields no edge.
