- **AC-0356** Given a journey candidate whose goal endpoint is fetched inside an anchor's flow (a Screen, extension context or command), so the endpoint has no flow of its own, when its goal basis is decided, then the writes observed for that endpoint are those of the Symbols the flow reaches downstream of the endpoint (its handler and their callees). A write upstream of the endpoint (a client-side Symbol) is never the endpoint's, and the candidate's flow and test listings are unchanged.
- **AC-0357** Given Python code using a session proven as SQLAlchemy's (a module-level `scoped_session(…)` or `sessionmaker(…)()` with the factory import-proven from `sqlalchemy.orm`, or `<db>.session` of a proven Flask-SQLAlchemy instance), when the repository is ingested, then `S.add(M(…))`, `S.add(x)` or `S.delete(x)` with `x = M(…)` earlier in the same body, `S.add_all([M(…), …])` and a `S.query(M)….delete()` / `.update(…)` chain give a `Confirmed` `WRITES` edge from the enclosing function to `M`'s `DataEntity` (`resolution: "session"`, `operation`, sorted `operations`, evidence the confirming call). A call `X.m(…)` on a recovered model class gives an `InferredStrong` `WRITES` edge (`resolution: "model-helper"`, `via: "<Class>.m"`) when `m`, resolved on `X` then its repository bases depth-first, is a `@classmethod` that writes its own `cls` that way. An override that does not write, an unresolvable base before the definition, a helper writing another model, a local or unimported `Session`, a non-model class, `getattr`, an instance call and a write in another function give no edge.
- **AC-0854** Given a Flask application, when the repository is ingested, then each Flask `GET` route whose recorded responses include `html` or `redirect`, or whose handler calls Flask's `render_template` with a literal name, becomes one InferredStrong `Screen` (`screen:{repo}@flask:{path}`, `route`, `name` = endpoint name, `router: "flask"`, `server_rendered: true`) citing the route, with a `RENDERS` edge to its handler. With Flask's `redirect` and `url_for` imported where the call is (at module level or in the function itself, and not rebound by a parameter, assignment, loop, `with` target, local import or nested definition of the same name), `redirect(url_for("name"))` with a literal name in a function (outside nested functions, lambdas and classes) is a `NAVIGATES_TO` from that function to each page of that endpoint name; `.name` resolves to the Blueprint of the function's routes when they name exactly one. `render_template("x")` with a literal name is a `RENDERS_TEMPLATE` from the function to the one file whose path after its last `templates/` directory is `x` (`.html`, `.jinja` or `.j2`); none or several record nothing. Each reached template is a `Template` node (`template:{repo}@{path}`) and `INCLUDES` what it `{% extends %}` or `{% include %}`s by a literal name. Outside Jinja comments, `<a href="{{ url_for('name') }}">` and a `GET` form whose `action` is `url_for('name')` (a call named exactly `url_for`, not one such as `safe_url_for`, with any literal endpoint name a route declares) are `NAVIGATES_TO` from the template to each page of that name; a `POST` form with such an action `SUBMITS` (`method: "POST"`) to that name's `POST` routes; a `POST` form with no `action` in a template a page's handler renders directly `SUBMITS` from the handler to the `POST` route at the page's own path. Every `NAVIGATES_TO` and `SUBMITS` edge is InferredStrong, lists its `sites` (span and `conditional`: under a Python branch, loop, `try` or `with`, or inside a template `{% if %}` / `{% for %}`) and is `conditional` only when every site is; a name no route answers records nothing (#1977).
- **AC-0855** Given server-rendered `Screen` nodes (AC-0854), when `journey-candidate@1` runs, then a screen's closure also walks `RENDERS_TEMPLATE` and `INCLUDES` edges to `Template` nodes and reads their `NAVIGATES_TO` and `SUBMITS` edges, a `SUBMITS` to an `Endpoint` counting as a client call with its recorded method; a server-rendered screen is labelled by its endpoint name; and flowtracer does not anchor a flow at a server-rendered screen, whose `GET` route already traces the same handler, so traced flows are unchanged (#1977).
- **AC-0857** Given journey candidates (AC-0352), when `journeys.md` is compiled, then each candidate opens with a **Path:** line naming every screen in order by its route (and its view name when that differs), how each was reached (`via` the handler, or `via a link in` the template or file, marked conditional when a taken site is), the goal operation as `METHOD path` and the tables it writes; the steps, entry, search table, goal, writes, result, flows, tests (the first five, then "and N more"), earlier assumed goals, unresolved items, unread callbacks and the assumptions table print the same names, a callable or test by the qualified name its id carries, and navigation sites print as `path (byte N)`; graph ids appear only on the `Id` line, in the assertions, the fact index and `bundle.json`, and an id that resolves to no node prints as itself (#1981).

- **AC-0955** Given derived Capabilities in D1 and `SUBMITS`, `NAVIGATES_TO` (from a template or a screen) and `FETCHES` edges, when §1.3 is compiled, then under `#### User actions: <capability>` each edge whose target is one of the capability's Endpoints, or a Screen that `RENDERS` one of its handlers, is one row with the page it is on (the routes of the Screens whose handler renders the template, else the template or component), what the user does (`Submit "Log in"`, `Follow "Sign in with GitHub"`, or the bare kind and method when no label was recorded), what it reaches and the edge's citation, rows on one page together, at most 24 per capability with the rest counted; a redirect a handler returns is not listed; a capability nothing reaches has no block; the workspace is charged in the knowledge-story compile bound (#2005).

Back navigations remain planned in W11.
- **AC-0975** Given Flask templates whose links and forms name a route with `url_for`, when they are ingested, then a link or `GET` form naming an endpoint no recovered route answers with `GET`, a form naming one no recovered route answers with its method, and a link or form whose name is relative (`.x`) are each a `Gap` (`gap:user-action:{repo}@{path}@{start}`, `kind: "user-action-unresolved"`) with the endpoint name, the method, the reason and the words the site shows, citing the tag; the site's `NAVIGATES_TO` or `SUBMITS` edge points at the Gap with confidence `Gap`; `static` and `<blueprint>.static` names and a link to a route that is not a page are not Gaps (#2005).
- **AC-0976** Given `SUBMITS` or `NAVIGATES_TO` edges from a template to a Gap, when D1 §1.3 is compiled, then after the capability blocks `#### User actions with no recovered target` states how many there are and lists each with its page, what the user does, the name and method it gives and why nothing answers it, citing the edge, at most 24 rows with the rest counted; with no such edge there is no block, and the workspace is charged in the knowledge-story compile bound (#2005).
- **AC-1309** Given a goal call bound by `const name = call(…)` or `const name = await call(…)` and a later navigation in the same Symbol under an `if` or ternary whose condition reads `name` (SimpleLogin's `const res = await callAPI(…)` then `if (res.status === 201) Navigation.navigateTo(PATH.NEW_ALIAS_RESULT)`, inside `try`), when navigations are recovered and journey candidates are compiled, then the TypeScript adapter lists in that site's `result_of` the byte range of every bound call read by a branch around it, and `journey-candidate@1` appends the navigation's screen as the ordered result step (`result: "ordered"`, ADR-0050 decision 4, "in a branch on that call's result") even when the call is not straight-line. A `let` binding, a condition on another name, a name rebound by a `catch` parameter or loop header in between, a branch inside a nested callback, or a `result_of` naming only other calls records or proves nothing, and the result stays `"unordered"` (#2261).
- **AC-1310** Given a path from an entry screen that reaches an observed-write goal and continues to another goal (SimpleLogin: `/` → `/login`, which writes on `POST /api/auth/mfa`, → `/api-key-setting` → `/main` → `POST /api/v2/alias/custom/new`), when journey candidates are compiled, then rule `journey-candidate@2` (ADR-0084, amending ADR-0050 decision 4) yields the write step's own candidate and keeps expanding past it, after every path that crosses no observed write has been searched as under `journey-candidate@1`; per (entry, goal) the primary is the shortest path among those that cross no earlier observed write (the `journey-candidate@1` path), with paths that cross one ranked after it; in rank order, a further path through a screen no better-ranked kept path of that goal passes through is kept as its own candidate with `alternative_of` naming the primary's id, a `journeys.md` heading `{label} (via {added screens})` and an `Alternative of` line; at most `max_alternatives = 64` alternatives per entry are kept, best-ranked first, and a cut records `truncated: "alternatives"` and `alternatives_complete: false` on every candidate the entry keeps; the default budget raises `max_expanded_states` to 4096 and `max_candidates` to 64; every other path is only counted in `alternatives` (#2261).

### US-0046 — Augment the deterministic base with a derived knowledge layer
- **Actor:** Engineer or agent
- **As a** engineer **I want** knowledge-layer facts (embeddings, retrieval, knowledge nodes) kept in a derived overlay that cites the base and never writes into it **so that** the deterministic base stays the system of record while the knowledge layer augments it.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Done
- **AC-0498** Given a base graph snapshot and derived facts, when they are published to the knowledge overlay, then every fact must cite existing base facts and carry valid semantic- or agentic-tier provenance within its confidence ceiling, no overlay node may reuse a base node id and every overlay edge endpoint must be a base or overlay node, or the whole publication is rejected and the previous overlay kept; the overlay reads back only at the base revision it was derived from (stale otherwise) under a content identity independent of input order; the base graph and its generation are unchanged; tampered rows, rows past the publication bounds (measured before loading), facts without valid provenance, foreign schema and other graph instances are refused; and graph clear, schema reset and retained-source forget discard the overlay through the graph reset hook (#1440).
- **Security:** Local disposable store only; linked or special files are refused; no provider call.
- **Performance:** Bounded to 200,000 facts and 256 MiB, with exact rejection rather than truncation.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445) · `core-graph`, `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0498
- **AC-0499** Given a base graph snapshot, when the knowledge embedding phase runs, then units are chosen and rendered by the versioned `knowledge-units@4` recipe (AC-1505 added documentation sections, AC-1516 commits) in a fixed label-priority and id order, the traced flows the knowledge producers derived from that base are units after its rules and before its modules and symbols, each rendered by its trigger, status and hops and at most 2,048 of them admitted, each unit's text is redacted before it is truncated to 2 KiB so no secret reaches the provider whole or cut, units past 16,384 and flows past their share are counted as a named omission, vectors are embedded through the ADR-0037 cache so an unchanged unit makes no provider call, and with no embedding provider the phase is skipped as the named omission `embedding-provider-unavailable` with the base untouched (#1441).
- **AC-0506** Given an ingest that has committed the deterministic base, when the knowledge phase runs in the background afterwards, then a single worker (later ingests coalesced into one follow-up pass) embeds that base's units, and those of the flows traced from it, through the local provider only while T2 is enabled and the pinned embedding model is installed, and republishes the knowledge overlay for the same base revision with a recipe recording whether the provider phase ran and its embedding namespace; T2 switched off (AC-0588), no installed provider, a failing provider and a base past the bounded snapshot read are each a named omission that never fails the ingest; an overlay is never published for a base that moved during the phase; and the base graph is unchanged (#1441).
- **AC-0734** Given unit vectors embedded for retrieval, when they are embedded or embedded again, then a usearch cosine index is rebuilt from exactly those vectors, one entry per unit in unit order; a query's vector leg asks the index for at most 800 candidate units and scores and orders them from the stored vectors with ties by node id, so the index decides no score; a base of at most 800 units, and any base whose index could not be built or queried, has every vector scored and gives the answer it gave without an index (#1441).
- **AC-1516** Given a base whose `File` facts all cite one full commit id per repository, when the knowledge phase runs with an embedding provider, then the host reads that repository's first-parent history from that commit under one read-only source operation released before anything is embedded, at most 2,048 commits newest first, each with its message and at most 64 paths its tree changed from its first parent with the base's files first, and never its author or committer name or email; each commit that touched a file the base holds is a `Commit` unit `commit:{repo}@{sha}` after the documentation sections and before modules and symbols (`knowledge-units@4`), rendered by its message (whitespace collapsed), sha and one `path:` line per touched path, redacted to a fixed point before the 2 KiB cut, and a retrieval hit on it is ranked, never an anchor, and cites the base `File` nodes it touched. A repository ingested from a working tree or citing several commits, a source that is busy, unregistered, not itself a repository root or missing the commit gives no commits; remote clones fetch 2,049 commits deep so that history is present, and a shallow clone's history ends before its boundary commit; commits past their share count under the unit-cap omission; the report counts `commitsRead`, and names `issue-source-unavailable` under `sourceOmissions` whenever the provider phase runs, since no issue tracker is read. Nothing is read without a provider, and commit text never enters the graph (#1441).
- **AC-1505** Given a base whose documentation reader recorded `DocSection` facts (ADR-0065), when the knowledge phase runs with an embedding provider, then the first 2,048 sections by id are units after the traced flows and before modules and symbols (`knowledge-units@3`), each rendered by its scalar properties and, when the host could read it, `text:` with the section's bytes from its registered source, whitespace collapsed and redacted to a fixed point before the unit is cut to 2 KiB. The host reads the spans under one read-only source operation that it releases before anything is embedded, through the confined evidence read (at most 256 KiB per section), and keeps a section's text only when those bytes still hash to the section's provenance `content_hash` (`spec::section_content_hash`). A section whose source is busy, unregistered, moved since ingest or unreadable is embedded by its heading alone. The phase report counts `sectionsRead` and `sectionsUnread`. Sections past their share are counted under the unit-cap omission, and no text is read when no provider runs. Document text never enters the graph (#1441).
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445) · `semantic` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0499
- **AC-0501** Given a base graph snapshot and a query, when hybrid retrieval runs, then nodes whose id, name, path or `METHOD path` equals the query come first as anchors, followed by their one-hop neighbours, and only then knowledge units ranked by SQLite FTS5 and, when unit vectors and a local provider are present, fused by reciprocal rank with vector similarity (the query is embedded only by the model that embedded the units; any other model is refused); ties break by node id, before any per-leg row limit, so pinned vectors give identical results; with no provider the lexical leg alone answers; only a local provider receives unit or query text; graph hits that fill the limit return without ranking or a provider call; every hit cites its base node (and anchoring edge) and carries the T2 tier; a traced flow the knowledge producers derived from the snapshot is a ranked unit, never an anchor or neighbour, whose hit cites the base facts the flow cites (#1441); nothing is written to the base (#1442).
- **Security:** Indexes only the redacted unit text; the query is redacted before it reaches the provider; query terms are quoted, never FTS5 syntax.
- **Performance:** In-memory index over at most the 16,384 knowledge units; queries above 512 bytes are refused; at most 200 hits and 200 candidates per ranked leg.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1445) · `semantic` · ADR-0051 · T-0501

- **AC-0870** Given a base graph, when the subsystem rule (`knowledge.subsystems`) runs, then the source files that define a Symbol or Component, outside test, fixture, vendored, versioned third-party, migration, minified and declaration files, are grouped by concept across directories and each group of three or more files is one Semantic/InferredWeak `Subsystem` candidate with one `MEMBER_OF` edge per member file, a versioned Rule method and every member file cited. The signals are read from base facts alone, with no provider: the words of a file's name and directories, of the symbols it defines, of the routes and templates it serves and of the data entities it writes (that weight shared among the entities, so a file touching many is about none), the facts it calls, imports, writes or renders, and direct calls and imports between two files. A file called or imported from at least eight files and six percent of the unit files is set aside in one `shared` group (`Shared code`) instead of joining a concept, unless it is named for a subject (AC-0914). Groups are the modularity communities of the nearest-neighbour similarity graph (best of sixteen fixed visiting orders), after which each file moves to the group it is most similar to; the groups are then made to follow their names (AC-0914), and names are unique. The candidates are the same under any input order and under any local registry key, the input snapshot is unchanged, and the overlay admits them. A repository with more than 65,536 unit files derives nothing under the named omission `subsystem-file-cap` (AC-1042); the knowledge phase publishes the candidates with the other producers, reports that omission with the producer omissions, and names the rule and its version in the overlay recipe. The showcase run writes each group's name, terms and files (#260).

Below 8,192 unit files a signal that many files carry is already dropped, so the candidates are byte-identical to the earlier rule and the rule version is unchanged (#260).

- **AC-0914** Given the groups the subsystem rule found, when they are named, then each group's terms are ranked by the share of its files that carry the term, the term's weight in them and how few other groups carry it, and a name is chosen among at most five terms that score at least six tenths of the first: the subject is the one most of the group's files carry, so a rare word a few files share does not name the many (`Metric`, not `Backend and metric`). The next such term, when it stands beside the subject in the members' file or directory names in at least three tenths of the files that carry either, reads with it as one phrase in the order found more often (`Custom domain`, `Event bus`). The groups are then made to follow their names, in at most six rounds: a group in which no such term is carried by three tenths of the files is leftovers that share no subject and is not published, its files being in no group; a group whose first five terms hold a second subject (a term in the file names of files that do not carry the first subject, where those files are at least three, half of the group's files the first subject leaves out (AC-1003) and half of the files so named) becomes one group per subject when each side keeps at least three files, every other file going with the side it resembles more; and two groups with one name become one group. A file then left in no group joins the most similar group whose every subject word it carries with at least a directory name's weight, and otherwise stays ungrouped. A file called or imported from enough files to be shared (AC-0870) is set aside only when its own name carries no subject word (`config`, `utils`); one named for a subject (`alias_utils`) is that subject's core and stays in the grouping, so a concept does not scatter when its hub file becomes widely used. The rule version is 2. Measured on SimpleLogin against the expected set written before the first run, on the graph the current extractors produce (which holds more calls than the one AC-0870 was measured on, and on which rule version 1 scores 0.60 over 153 labelled files and loses the alias group): pairwise F1 0.75 (precision 0.78, recall 0.72) over the 145 labelled files it groups, with alias, authentication, email, custom domain, subscription, OAuth, event, metric, phone, onboarding and abuse groups under those one-word or one-phrase names; mailbox files still sit in the email group (#260).

- **AC-0521** Given admitted graph rows and JSON values, when the official selected compiler's logical workspace is bounded, then security findings count as typed generated rows with a proven value bound: one fixed per-finding schema, measured through the real projection, plus the admitted source values. This replaces one value per serialized output byte. Every action, scope and evidence entry costs exactly as many output values as its smallest accepted source form, so concentrated single-row, escaped and many-minimal inputs stay within the bound. Byte charges, escaping and the remaining compiler roles are unchanged (#1509).
- **Security:** Accounting only. Projection output, identity and the configured policy limits are unchanged. The derived reservation floor is intentionally lower, so a reservation offer that was refused before can now be admitted.
- **Performance:** At 4 MiB / 5,000 rows / 100k values the selected-compiler logical peak falls from about 18.98 GB to about 9.51 GB, and the Capability compiler peak from about 20.04 GB to about 10.57 GB. These are logical accounting currency, not RSS. Policy limits are unchanged.

- **AC-0522** Given admitted graph bytes, rows and JSON values, when the official selected compiler's logical workspace is bounded, then typed `from_value` decodes of provenance, omissions and source-rule payloads are charged by an explicit ownership recurrence instead of one value role per input byte. Each string payload is credited to the one JSON string token it comes from, and each collection slot to the one array item it holds. Enums and nested structs stay inside the parent slot that is already charged. Fixed fallback strings and omission classification (its token slots, lowercase expansion and prefix) carry their own explicit charges, including cached and per-candidate omission `Arc` counters. For provenance and omissions, a changed field, variant or field type fails compilation next to the element lists. The source-rule closure (GuardedExitEvidence and its nested types) remains source-audited against the reviewed core-graph element list. Serde attribute changes are a manual-review residual in both (#1509).
- **Security:** Accounting only. Decoding, classification, projection output and the configured policy limits are unchanged. The derived reservation floor is intentionally lower, so a reservation offer that was refused before can now be admitted.
- **Performance:** At 4 MiB / 5,000 rows / 100k values the selected-compiler logical peak falls from about 9.51 GB to about 3.95 GB: source rules about 5.77 → 0.72 GB, registers about 5.00 → 2.31 GB. These are logical accounting currency, not RSS. Policy limits are unchanged.

- **AC-0523** Given admitted graph bytes, when the official selected compiler's registers workspace is bounded, then the `supports_listed` token scratch and its lookup clone are charged by the most subject-id parts (five), not one fragment slot per input byte. The token is cleared at every space, so between clears it keeps at most the last fragment of each part. Node, edge and hop subjects are built only through `node_subject` and `link_subject`, whose fixed array shares that constant. The listed-subject probe keeps the historical flattened space split, including empty, leading, trailing, consecutive-space and Unicode names (#1509).
- **Security:** Accounting only. Subject ids, listed-subject matching and the configured policy limits are unchanged. Direct construction of a subject id outside the two helpers still needs reviewer scrutiny. The derived reservation floor is intentionally lower.
- **Performance:** At 4 MiB / 5,000 rows / 100k values the registers workspace falls from about 2.31 GB to about 0.63 GB, and the selected-compiler logical peak from about 3.95 GB to about 2.36 GB. These are logical accounting currency, not RSS. Policy limits are unchanged.

- **AC-0731** Given a stop reason, when an omission is classified, then its plain words are built in one buffer: each token is appended as it is read and cut off again when it is identifier-like or shouty, and the buffer is lowercased once. The selected words are byte-identical to the historical unquote, split, filter, join and lowercase form, including quote toggles, Unicode whitespace and final sigma. The classifier workspace is therefore charged as the reason plus one and a half times the reason, not an unquoted copy, a token slot per two bytes and six times the reason. The lowercase factor is checked for every Unicode scalar value (#1509).
- **Security:** Classification results, omission details and the configured policy limits are unchanged. The derived reservation floor is intentionally lower.
- **Performance:** One allocation fewer per classified reason and no token list. The current-omission charge falls from about 18 to about 4.5 bytes per admitted graph byte. These are logical accounting currency, not RSS.

- **AC-0732** Given trace output limits, when the tracer's retained output and the compiler's registers workspace are bounded, then evidence and attempted-tier element slots copied from flow output are charged by the output bytes divided by the smallest serialized form of one element (the five-field evidence object with empty strings and one-digit offsets; an empty string and its separator), measured from the real serializer, and still by admitted hops times input values. This replaces one element slot per output byte. The densest arrays meet the bound exactly and one byte less admits one element fewer (#1509).
- **Security:** Accounting only. Tracing, flow output, projection and the configured policy limits are unchanged. The derived reservation floor is intentionally lower, so a reservation offer that was refused before can now be admitted.
- **Performance:** With AC-0731, at 4 MiB / 5,000 rows / 100k values (trace 64 / 4,096 / 1 MiB) the selected-compiler logical peak falls from about 2.36 GB to about 1.44 GB, and at 1 MiB / 2,000 rows / 25k values from about 1.31 GB to about 0.62 GB: original and projected flows about 498 MB → 49 MB. These are logical accounting currency, not RSS. Policy limits are unchanged.

- **AC-0524** Given ordinary (unbounded) spec compilation, when the bundle builds the US-TM, gap register and rule-evidence artifacts, then each Markdown artifact and its JSON index come from one producer pass with both outputs selected, and every one of the six artifacts is byte-identical to its single-artifact selected compilation, including grouped rule Markdown past the flat limit. On the REA graph (310k nodes) this halves the gap-register pair and cuts the rule-evidence pair by about 40% in a release build (#1478).
- **Security:** Output-equivalent. Artifact bytes, assertions and the selected/bounded path are unchanged; the index artifact keeps no assertions, as single-index production already did.
- **Performance:** On the REA graph (310k nodes, release) the gap-register pair falls from about 15 s to 7 s and the rule-evidence pair from about 7 s to 4 s; whole export about 20 s.

- **AC-0525** Given recovered Capability facts, when the base user-stories artifact is rendered, then each story's name and id and every inline-provenance cell (summary, method, evidence, extractor, content hash) are written as literal Markdown text: HTML is entity-escaped, Markdown punctuation, backticks and GFM strikethrough `~` are backslash-escaped, and newlines are shown as ` ⏎ `, so fences, headings, code spans, emphasis, strikethrough and HTML stay inert. Bare-URL autolinks are not suppressed. The built-in artifact viewer decodes every CommonMark backslash escape and these three entities, so it shows the exact recovered text. Structured assertions keep the exact stored text and provenance, output-byte admission counts the escaped bytes at the exact boundary and one byte short, and this intentionally changes base user-stories bytes and content ids (#1453).
- **Security:** Presentation only, not a redaction guarantee; stored text is shown, not removed. Derived-story sidecar text stays inert JSON as before.
- **Performance:** Per-character escaping on story text only.

- **AC-0515** Given a unique GraphQL Endpoint with structurally valid AC-0517 import-bound permission syntax, matching legacy permissions and consistent deterministic source provenance, the pure Actor producer emits one endpoint-scoped Semantic/InferredWeak caller-set candidate. It preserves the exact ordered permission alternatives, including duplicates, under any_of and records the named versioned assumption “Allow(...) admits callers holding any listed permission”. Identity binds the rule, assumption, endpoint and exact site; its evidence is the original site evidence and its BaseRef cites the Endpoint. Absent, Public, malformed, mismatched, ambiguous or unproved observations abstain; oversized candidates are counted under the existing overlay per-fact cap.
- **Security:** The exported Allow name selects an explicit weak convention, without repository/path/commit/module whitelists or effective runtime authorization, authenticated user, role, principal or anonymous claims; Confirmed syntax is never promoted. Owner remains a token. Compiler-visible assumption rendering is required in a separate slice before user-facing adoption.
- **Performance:** Deterministic node-key lookup and existing per-fact JSON admission only; callers admit the input and aggregate publication separately. No providers, ingestion, new numeric policy or RSS guarantee.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443) · `spec`, `core-graph` · ADR-0051 · T-0515

- **AC-0518** Given valid Capability and canonical Actor sidecars, the combined official user-stories compiler renders one artifact preserving Capability-only bytes when Actor input is empty. Each Actor shows exact ordered alternatives, the complete named/versioned weak assumption, Endpoint citation and site evidence together. Full canonical producer validation rejects malformed, duplicate, shadowed or missing/ambiguous support atomically; each valid Capability or Actor filtered by its own provenance or supporting facts is counted once, with a content-free omission explanation rather than a claim that nothing was recovered (#1540). Hostile data stays escaped and content plus assertions share existing output admission. Validation returns borrowed stories before rendering, dropping lookup/projection indexes while retaining every support vector; the combined logical peak composes these disjoint stages without changing the existing input/output limits (#1555). Renderer reference slots remain additive while current per-item Capability/Actor scratch is composed by maximum across the sequential assertion loops; legacy public bounds retain their existing totals (#1557). The combined fixed user-stories route retains common projection, ADR/security, flow, output and base-row roles while reserving only its selected renderer indexes; generic compiler bounds remain unchanged.
- **Security:** Assumed caller sets are Semantic/InferredWeak conventions, not identified users, roles, principals or effective runtime authorization. No producer semantics, providers, host hookup or graph writes change.
- **Performance:** Checked Actor-only workspace accounts canonical verification and retained/render roles separately; callers compose it with Capability/compiler admission and retain responsibility for input and currency. No numeric policy or RSS guarantee.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1488, #1540, #1555, #1557) · `spec` · ADR-0051 · T-0518

- **AC-0532** Given the knowledge phase's bounded base snapshot, when the phase publishes the overlay, then the AC-0515 permission Actor rule runs after the Capability, BusinessFlow and DomainEntity producers over that same snapshot, and its weak caller-set candidates, each citing exactly its base `Endpoint`, are published with the phase, with or without a provider. Its per-fact oversized omissions count in the report; the recipe names the rule and version within the name bound; if the overlay refuses the combined set, none of the derived facts are published and the recipe says `knowledge-derived-rejected`; a rerun over the same base republishes the identical set; the base is unchanged (#1530). A test-only published-category census counts Capability, BusinessFlow, DomainEntity and Actor nodes after successful full-set publication, excludes edges, and reports zero when derived publication is refused.
- **Security:** The rule is pure and deterministic over the local snapshot and makes no provider call; the candidates keep their weak provenance and named assumption, which do not attest effective runtime authorization. Derived facts are not embedded.
- **Performance:** One node-key pass over the already bounded snapshot; the combined derived set is still admitted, or refused whole, by the existing overlay caps. No new numeric limit.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1530) · `app` · ADR-0051 · T-0532

- **AC-0534** Given canonical Actor and Capability sidecars plus caller-admitted flows and explicit security limits, the spec-only combined user-stories entrypoint uses the existing shared dispatcher: empty Actor input preserves the Capability flow/security artifact bytes and identity, combined output retains flow-sensitive ADR admission, restrictive security admission returns Limited without an artifact, and an invalid Actor refuses the whole compilation.
- **Security:** Caller input, currency and compiler/Actor workspace admission remain required; no host route, provider, overlay selection or graph write is connected by this slice.
- **Performance:** Thin wrapper over existing validation and rendering; no budget, numeric policy or workspace formula changes.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1532) · `spec` · ADR-0051 · T-0534

- **AC-0535** Given a current knowledge overlay holding canonical Actor and Capability facts, when an MCP read session captures the overlay and compiles the official `user-stories` artifact, then the session reads Capabilities and Actors together through the bounded combined overlay read under one shared count and byte charge, and compiles them through the admitted combined compiler with the session's flows and security limits. Each Actor story keeps its ordered assumption alternatives and Endpoint citation. Under VerifiedOnly the weak Actor is omitted and counted, never claimed absent. A canonically invalid captured Actor returns a storage error without an artifact. The capture's byte bound admits exactly the serialized base graph, and the logical Actor workspace is added to the session's capability compile reservation (#1539, #1443).
- **Security:** Assumed caller sets stay Semantic/InferredWeak conventions; no provider call, graph write or new route is added.
- **Performance:** The existing capture and compile reservations are extended by the retained Actor slots and the logical Actor workspace bound; no new numeric policy or RSS guarantee.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1443, #1539) · `app`, `core-graph`, `spec` · ADR-0051, ADR-0037 · T-0535
- **AC-0583** Given a recovered base larger than the interactive read default (50,000 rows or 32 MiB), when the knowledge phase runs after ingest, then it reads the base under the desktop investigation ceiling (10,000,000 rows, 4 GiB) and publishes its overlay, so the deterministic knowledge nodes exist for a repository of real size; a base past that ceiling is still the named `knowledge-base-bounds` omission. Reads that reserve memory in advance (MCP sessions and MCP-started tasks) keep the interactive default (ADR-0052 amendment, #1771, #1602).
- **Security:** Local read only; no provider call is added and the overlay caps are unchanged.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1771) · `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0052 · T-0583
- **AC-0588** Given the knowledge phase has run after an ingest, when it skipped or left out work, then the app shows a notice naming each reason with its code: the base past the read ceiling (`knowledge-base-bounds`), T2 off (`semantic-tier-disabled`), no installed or a failing embedding provider, derived nodes refused as a set, a producer that yielded nothing, units past the embedding cap and oversized candidates; a phase that failed outright shows its error; the outcome of the last phase is kept so a window opened afterwards still shows it; and a phase that did everything shows nothing. With T2 off the phase still runs: no provider is probed and nothing is embedded, and the deterministic producers (Capability, BusinessFlow, DomainEntity, Actor) publish as before. A desktop context query on a base larger than the interactive read default (50,000 rows or 32 MiB) reads under the desktop investigation ceiling and answers; a base past that ceiling returns the bounds error. MCP reads keep the interactive default (ADR-0052 amendment, #1782).
- **AC-0851** Given a derived Capability from the GraphQL resolver rule (`knowledge.capability.graphql-resolver-group`) or the controller rule (`knowledge.capability.http-controller`), when its title is derived, then it reads as what a reader does with the group: a subject that starts with an action word (`reset password`, `delete account`) stands alone, capitalized; otherwise `Manage` when any grouped operation is a GraphQL mutation or an HTTP method other than GET, HEAD and OPTIONS, `View` otherwise, followed by the subject the class, Go package or Rust module name speaks of (for a Flask Blueprint, its handler file's name, unless that is generic such as `views.py`, served under the Blueprint), with framework words (`Resolver`, `Controller`, `Resource`, `views`, …) and audience words (`Shop`, `Admin`) removed, known abbreviations spelled out and, for a class, the last word plural unless it is uncountable or the subject holds a preposition, and, for GraphQL, the API it is served on (`Manage draft orders (admin API)` for `DraftOrderResolver`); a Blueprint names the audience of its files (`Manage custom domain (dashboard)`), less any trailing words the file name repeats (`View domain check (admin)`); a Go root package or Rust crate root reads `operations`; a name made only of framework words keeps them; candidates that would share a title name the URL route prefix their routes share, up to the first parameter segment (`View orders (/admin/orders)`), when that tells each of them apart, and otherwise keep AC-0801's handler-file disambiguation; the title stays Semantic/InferredWeak and every cited support is unchanged (#1443).
- **AC-0849** Given derived Capabilities in a `best-effort` D1 (`product-specification.md`), when §1.5 Business rules is compiled, then under each Capability it lists the admitted BusinessRule observations that `GOVERNS` a callable reached from the handler Symbols of its cited `HANDLES` edges along admitted Symbol-to-Symbol `CALLS` edges within 3 hops and whose local exit rejects (a `throw`, or a `return` whose whole value is one construction of an `…Error` or `…Exception`, parenthesized or not), each row with the owner callable, its stored same-callable branch conditions (a falsy branch as `not …`, a compound one negated whole as `not (…)`, `not` over a negation dropped, and a compound condition parenthesized when joined by `and`), the outcome and the rule's citation id, each cited fact asserted once; a callable more than 3 Capabilities reach is listed once under shared rejections; other exits, rules no Capability reaches and dependencies stay in `rule-evidence.md` alone; only stored sanitized displays are printed and the complete execution predicate and consumer effect are stated as not established; with no derived Capability (an Actor-only sidecar included) §1.5 still names `rule-evidence.md` alone; the rendering workspace is charged in the knowledge-story compile bound (#1443).
- **AC-1004** Given a rule row whose conditions say a value differs from a literal and the same conditions pin that value to another literal (an `elif` chain: `form name is not “cancel”, and form name is “change-yearly”`), when its Rule cell is stated (AC-0849), then the difference is not said and only the pinned value is: `When the form name is “change-yearly”, …`. Values are matched by their parsed source expression, not their wording, so a form field and a query parameter that read alike are never one value. A difference on a value nothing pins, on another source, or beside a pin that is not an equality to a literal (`is set`, `is one of …`), is kept. Likewise an earlier `elif` test that a plain local name and something else do not both hold (`not (alias and alias.user_id == user.id)`) is not said beside a condition that the name is not set (`no alias is given`) (#1998). The When cell is unchanged. Both local drafting models misread the longer cell as no option matching (#2083).
- **AC-0950** Given derived Capabilities arranged by area (#260) in a `best-effort` D1, when §1.5 is compiled, then after each area's Capabilities a `Rules in the <area> area's own code` subsection lists the rejection and change rows (rendered as the capability rows are) of every callable defined in one of the area's member files that none of the area's Capabilities reach and that is not shared (more than 3 capabilities reach it), at most 32 rejections per area with the rest counted, including an area no Capability reaches at all; a callable one of the area's Capabilities reaches is not repeated there; without areas nothing is added, so an area's briefing states the rules its own code holds even when only another area, a command, a job or injected configuration reaches them (#2066).
- **AC-0951** Given TypeScript source with a `transitions` property whose value is an object of at least two entries, each a state whose value is an object with a `to` array of plain string literals (the Vendure order, payment, fulfillment and refund processes), when it is ingested, then it is one `StateMachine` fact citing the table, named by the outermost declaration holding it, with the type its nearest declaration states, every state in order, each state's allowed targets and the `onTransition…` hooks beside it; any other shape (a computed target, a spread, one entry) is not read; and D1 §1.5 states each machine under the area of its file (or under `State machines` without one) as a table of each state and the states it may move to (`nothing (final)` for none), followed by the guarded exits of its hooks, which refuse a move (#2067).
- **AC-0956** Given TypeScript source with an unguarded `return` that states how a value is chosen (the answer of a predicate named `isX`/`hasX`/`canX`/`shouldX`/`mustX` or declared `: boolean`, unless a constant; a pick with a fallback `found || fallback` or `??` whose left side is, or is declared as, a `.find(…)`; or the answer of a public method of a class that `implements` an interface named for a configured strategy, `…Strategy`/`…Handler`/`…Calculator`/`…Process`), when the TypeScript adapter recovers it, then the return is a guarded-exit rule with no conditions, a pick's returned name keeps its local definition, and an expression-bodied arrow over plain names passed as an argument (`find(r => r.test(zone, taxCategory))`) is captured; a plain unguarded return, a constant, a private helper, and a block-bodied, typed, async or parameterless arrow are not; and D1 lists such an unguarded answer beside the rejections in §1.5, saying a pick as "It picks the first of the active tax rates whose test with the zone and the tax category holds; when none does, the default tax rate." and a boolean answer of comparisons as "A tax rate's test holds when …".
- **AC-0850** Given derived Capabilities in a `best-effort` D1 (`product-specification.md`), when §1.4 Acceptance criteria per capability is compiled, then under each Capability it lists every recovered TestCase with an admitted `REQUESTS` edge to an Endpoint that is the source of one of the Capability's cited `HANDLES` edges, one row per test with its qualified name, or its name when none is recorded (a `test_snake_case` segment shown as words), its file, every such operation it requests and the citation id of each `REQUESTS` edge, each cited fact asserted once; a test of no Capability's operation is not listed; the lead states that a reference is static and not evidence that the test runs or passes, and points to `test_arrangements.md` and `rule-evidence.md`; with no derived Capability or no requested operation §1.4 still names those artifacts alone; the rendering workspace is charged in the knowledge-story compile bound (#1443).
- **AC-0859** Given derived Capabilities and traced flows in D1 (`product-specification.md`), when §1.6 Business flows is compiled, then under each Capability every operation whose flow reaches past its handler gets one line naming its status, its handlers, the further callables in trace order (first 8, then "and N more") and the DataEntities the flow's participants write and read with the operations each relation records, plus its unresolved hop count, citing the handler and data edges; operations handled with nothing further are listed together, flows outside every Capability keep the trigger, kind, status and hop table, and no raw node id is printed (#1985).
- **AC-0852** Given Python source, when the Python adapter recovers it, then every `return` and every `raise` with a value under same-function `if` / `elif` / `else` branches is a BusinessRule carrying schema-v2 `GuardedExitEvidence` that `GOVERNS` its enclosing function Symbol (a nested function owns its own exits; a lambda or class body owns none; an unguarded exit or a bare `raise` is none), with the taken branch's condition truthy and each earlier condition of the same `if` falsy, outermost first; a call to a function the file defines at top level is a Target dependency, a parameter the body never rebinds a Binding, anything else Unresolved; the execution predicate and consumer effect are not established and each reason (including loop, exception, context-manager, match and mutation control in the function) is a Gap the rule `DEPENDS_ON`; stored displays remove comments and withhold credential-shaped or sensitively named literals and byte strings through the shared `core-redact` recognizers; a file with recovered syntax yields one omission Gap and no rule; cached files cite the ingested commit; and D1 §1.5 lists a Python `raise` as a rejection and a `return` of a tuple ending in a 4xx or 5xx status literal, not a success tuple or a plain value (#1973).
- **AC-0922** Given a repository with test code (a `*.test.*`/`*.spec.*` file or a file under a `test`/`tests`/`__tests__`/`e2e` directory for TypeScript/JavaScript; a `test_*.py`/`*_test.py`, `conftest.py` or a file under `tests`/`test` for Python) and source in a language with no rule-evidence producer (`File` facts whose `language` is not Python, TypeScript/JavaScript, Go (AC-0923), Java (AC-0967) or Rust (AC-1017), e.g. Kotlin), when it is recovered and exported, then the test code yields no `BusinessRule`, and `rule-evidence.md` and D1 §1.5 state the files per language that no rule evidence was read from, so the rules are not taken to cover them (#2009).
- **AC-0923** Given a Go function, method or route-handler `func` literal (not in a `_test.go` file), when it is ingested, then each `return` with a value under same-function `if`/`else` branches is a `BusinessRule` of kind `guarded_exit`, and each call statement that writes an error status (a `net/http` 4xx/5xx constant or such a number among its arguments) followed by a bare `return` is one of kind `guarded_effect` holding that call, owned by its Symbol through `GOVERNS`, with sanitized branch conditions and the same unestablished interpretation Gaps as the Python and TypeScript producers; a return that only passes an error on (`if err != nil { return …, err }` or `fmt.Errorf("…%w", err)`) and returns inside other literals are not rules. In D1 §1.5 such a refusal is a rejection, not a change ("When the request's method is not “POST”, the request is refused with status 405 (“method not allowed”)"), a returned `errors.New("…")` or `ErrX` value is "it fails with the error “…”", and Go conditions read by rule (`err != nil` as "the “store save” call fails" when `err` is bound from `s.Store.Save(…)` in the `if` initializer or the statement just before it (a local receiver is left out, an imported package or exported field kept), else "an error occurred", `s.Store == nil` in a `Service` method as "the service's store is not set", `errors.Is(err, ErrX)`, `r.FormValue("k") == ""`, `http.MethodPost`, a receiver's predicate of one value); a row whose callable is a route-handler `func` literal names it by the route it handles (`handler of POST /sign-in`), not by its offset (#2009).
- **AC-0967** Given a Java method or constructor (not in a Maven/Gradle test source set), when it is ingested, then each `return` with a value and each `throw` under same-method `if`/`else` branches is a `BusinessRule` of kind `guarded_exit` (`LocalExit::Return` or `LocalExit::Throw`), owned by its Symbol through `GOVERNS`, with sanitized branch conditions (the `if` parentheses dropped, secrets compared by `==`, `!=` or `.equals` withheld) and the same unestablished interpretation Gaps as the Python, TypeScript and Go producers (an earlier exit, loops, `switch`, `try`/`synchronized` and field or compound writes as control Gaps); exits inside a lambda, an anonymous or local class body or an initializer are not rules; a reused file's rules carry the new commit; Java files are no longer named as source no rule evidence was read from; and in D1 §1.5 a thrown exception reads as the error it raises: its literal message when its first argument is one ("it fails with the error “Invalid id”"), else its name without the `Exception`/`Error` suffix ("it fails with a “login already used” error") (#2020).
- **AC-1017** Given a Rust `fn` (not in test code as AC-1016 defines it), when it is ingested, then each `return` with a value under same-function `if`/`else` branches (an `if let` condition captured as written) is a `BusinessRule` of kind `guarded_exit`, owned by the function's Symbol through `GOVERNS`, with sanitized branch conditions (secrets compared by `==`, `!=` or `.eq(…)` withheld) and the same unestablished interpretation Gaps as the Python, TypeScript, Go and Java producers (an earlier exit, loops, `match`, `?` and writes through a field, index or dereference or compound assignments as control Gaps; rebinding a local is not a mutation); returns inside a closure, an `async` block or a `const`/`static` initializer are not rules; a reused file's rules carry the new commit; Rust files are no longer named as source no rule evidence was read from; and in D1 §1.5 a returned `http::StatusCode` constant, bare, in `Err(…)` or in a tuple with a literal message, reads as a refusal ("When the ID is 0, the request is refused with status 404"), and another `Err(…)` as the error it fails with: its literal message (`anyhow!("…")`, `"…".into()`, `Error::new("…")`) as "it fails with the error “…”", an enum variant by its name in words ("it fails with a “password missing” error"), else "it fails with an error" (#2018).
- **AC-1106** Given a Kotlin function (top level, a class, object or companion member; not in a Maven/Gradle test source set), when it is ingested, then each `return` with a value and each `throw` under same-function `if`/`else` branches is a `BusinessRule` of kind `guarded_exit` (`LocalExit::Return` or `LocalExit::Throw`), owned by the function's Symbol through `GOVERNS`, with sanitized branch conditions (secrets compared by `==`, `!=` or `.equals` withheld, a string template withheld whole, narrowed by AC-1108) and the same unestablished interpretation Gaps as the Python, TypeScript, Go, Java and Rust producers (an earlier exit, loops, `when`, `try` and writes through a property or index, compound assignments and `++`/`--` as control Gaps; rebinding a local is not a mutation); the trailing lambda of an inline scope function (`let`, `run`, `also`, `apply`, `with`, `use`, as in `x?.let { … }`) belongs to the function unless the file declares that name or imports it by name or alias from outside `kotlin.`, while exits inside another lambda, an anonymous function, an object expression, a local function, an accessor, an `init` block or a secondary constructor, and a labelled `return@x`, are not rules; a reused file's rules carry the new commit; Kotlin files are no longer named as source no rule evidence was read from; and in D1 §1.5 a thrown construction of a capitalized class reads as the error it raises, as a Java one does (#2021).
- **AC-1108** Given a Kotlin function as AC-1106 reads it, when it is ingested, then the right side of an elvis (`x ?: throw …`, `x ?: return …`) is a guarded exit taking its left side falsy (absent); an exit in an entry of a subject-less `when` takes that entry's single condition truthy and every earlier entry's conditions falsy (an exit in a `when (subject)` entry or a several-condition entry is no rule, and only such a `when` is a `switch_control` Gap); a standard-library `require`, `check`, `requireNotNull` or `checkNotNull` call with one positional argument is a throw taking that argument falsy and carrying its message literal (else the call), and `error("…")` is a throw under the branches around it; a string template whose substitutions are plain names (`$id`, `${owner.id}`) keeps its text as written, while one substituting a sensitive name or holding a secret is withheld; a scope-function or precondition name is no proven standard-library call when another product file of the repository rebinds it with a non-private declaration (a same-package, default-package or star-imported top-level declaration unless the file imports that name from `kotlin` explicitly, a member of the enclosing type or a repository supertype for a bare call, any repository non-private member for a call on another receiver or inside a `run`/`apply`/`with` lambda), and the rules relying on it are dropped with their Gaps; and in D1 §1.5 a precondition's message and `error("…")` read as the error they fail with (#2021).

- **AC-0902** Given a Flask handler's returns, when its responses are recorded, then each top-level JSON key is also recorded under the literal or default status of the `return` that proves it (`keys_by_status`), including the keys of a dict a followed helper builds and the responses of a followed decorator or check; a key whose status is computed is in `keys` only. - **AC-0904** Given a D1 §1.2 guard row, a §1.5 rejection or a `↳` row whose condition or outcome names a configuration constant (an identifier token equal to a recorded `defaults` constant), when the rows are compiled, then once per table a `↳` row states the constant's default and the environment variable that replaces it, matched by name and inferred, citing the `ConfigRead`; a name more than one recorded read binds is stated as not stated with the count (#1998).
- **AC-0905** Given derived Capabilities from the controller or entry-point rule whose titles would collide, when a title names what tells them apart, then it names the file in words, never as a path: the file's stem, or its directory's name when the stem is generic (`index`, `views`, `__init__`), with a container or process manifest read as what it declares (`Dockerfile` as `container image`, a Procfile entry as `<key> process`), and every directory segment named when the short form still collides; a class or group that must also be named is in words (#1999).
- **AC-0906** Given a repository guard a counted Python guard reading names, when D1 §1.2 and the user stories name its caller, then the caller is the role the guard's name speaks of, the guard kept in the Guard column as evidence: `admin`, `superuser` or `staff` as "administrator"; `api`, `token` or `key` as "API client", "in sudo mode" added for `sudo`, `fresh` or `reauth`; `validate_<vendor>_request` and `verify_<vendor>_signature` (or `webhook`, `callback`, `hmac`) as "<Vendor> webhook"; `login`, `auth`, `user` or `session` as "signed-in user"; any other as "caller the “<guard in words>” check admits"; a Flask-Admin view's caller is "administrator" (#1999).
- **AC-0907** Given a statement Cartograph projects by rule rather than drafts (a §1.5 Rule cell, a §1.2 caller), when it is compiled, then it carries no code-shaped token outside a quotation (`“…”`): a token holding `_`, `::`, `.`, `#` or `(`, or a lower-case letter followed by an upper-case one, other than an HTTP path, a number or a sentence's own punctuation; a rendered Rule statement with such a token is not printed and the row states the Gap instead, the sibling of AC-0683 for projected statements (#1999).
- **AC-0908** Given a §1.5 rejection, a "What each guard checks" exit or a `↳` exit, when D1 is compiled, then its row reads `| Where | Rule | Source | Citation |`: the Rule restates the exit as a sentence in domain terms when its condition and outcome are simple enough to render by rule (a presence or flag test, a value tested as set, a comparison with a literal, a configured constant or the current time, a test for no value, membership of a literal set, an ownership test `x.user_id != user.id` as "does not belong to", and `and`/`or`/`not` of these), with the outcome as a refusal with its status and literal message, a redirect, an error, a predicate method's answer ("A user cannot create num aliases when …") or the value a method gives ("the user's max alias is …", an accessor's `get_` dropped), keeping the fact's tier; otherwise it states "Not stated in domain terms (Gap)".
