- **AC-0910** Given a Python function with an assignment through an attribute (`x.field = value`, not in `__init__`) or a call statement whose result is unused, to a function name that is not a builtin, a method on `self` or `cls`, or a function of an imported lower-case module, when the file is ingested, then each is a `BusinessRule` of kind `guarded_effect` (observation `guarded_effect`) owned by its function through `GOVERNS`, with the same-function branch conditions around it (possibly none), the assigned place and value or the call as sanitized displays, and the same unestablished interpretation Gaps as a guarded exit, shared by the function's effects; logging calls (`LOG`, `logger`, `logging` receivers or log verbs), builtins, upper-case receivers (except `create` or `delete` on an imported class other than `Session`, including `x = Model.create(…)` kept in a local, or `filter`/`filter_by(…).delete()` on one: a record created or deleted; and `Session.add` of an imported class's constructor, directly or through a local assigned before it, whose value is the constructor call) and plain-name assignments are not effects, except, under an `if` branch, a local the function bound earlier set to `None` or to its own `.replace("…", "…")`; effects have their own per-file budget so they never crowd out exits; an effect under the exit kind, or an exit under the effect kind, is malformed (#1998, ADR-0020 amended).
- **AC-1012** Given a TypeScript callable with, under at least one same-callable branch condition, an assignment through a member not on `this` (`order.state = …`), a TypeORM repository write (`getRepository(ctx, Order).save(…)` or a `…Repository` field's `save`, `insert`, `update`, `upsert`, `delete`, `remove`, `softDelete`, `softRemove`, `increment` or `decrement`), `this.eventBus.publish(…)` or a call of a `this.…Service` method whose name is not a read (`get`, `find`, `is`, `has`, `can`, `count`, `list`, `check`, `validate`, `assert`), optionally awaited, when the file is ingested, then each is a `BusinessRule` of kind `guarded_effect` like AC-0910, with interpretation Gaps shared by the callable's effects; an unguarded statement, a constructor body and any other call are not effects; D1 states a repository write as "the “order line” record is saved" (created, updated, deleted), a published event as "the “order state transition” event is published" and a service call as "it runs “create history entry for order”" (#1998).
- **AC-0911** Given the guarded effects of the callables a capability's handlers reach, when D1 is compiled, then §1.5 lists them under the capability as a "Changes" table after its rejections (at most 16, nearest callable first; a callable more than three capabilities reach under "Shared changes"), and §1.2 "What each guard checks" lists a guard's effects beside its exits; each Rule cell restates the effect by rule when it is simple (an assignment to `g.user` or `request.user` as whom the request acts as, a boolean flag set or cleared, a time set to N units from now, a value set to a name or literal, line breaks removed from a Python value (`x.name = x.name.replace("\n", "")`; not JavaScript, whose `replace` changes only the first) as "line breaks are removed from the alias's name" (a test `"\n" in x` as "x contains a line break"), a local set to `None` as "the alias is discarded", an enum member as its words, a record created or deleted on a class as "a new “alias used on” record is created" or "the “contact” record is deleted", a Flask `flash(…)` message as "the user is shown “…”" (an f-string's replacement fields as "…"), a call as "it runs “name”"), otherwise a Gap, with the stored statement as Source and a configuration constant it names stated with its default (#1998).
- **AC-0912** Given a §1.2, §1.5 or `↳` row whose condition uses membership of a literal (`"k" in data` as "k is given" for request input, the session as "holds", any other value as "contains “…”"), membership of a named collection, a lookup `Model.get_by(field=…)` ("an alias with this email exists", joined by `or`), `isinstance(x, T)` for builtin types, `len(x)`, a time `arrow.now().shift(unit=±N)` compared as before/after, a header or key read through `.get(CONSTANT)` or `x["k"]`, a `validate…`/`check_…` method or function, `startswith`/`endswith` with a literal, an enum member, or a named function's result, when D1 is compiled, then the Rule cell restates it by rule, keeping the code-token check of AC-0907; a nested `or` group inside an `and` reads "either … or …" (and an `and` group inside an `or` "both … and …") so the grouping is kept, and only the request's own inputs (or a bare `data`/`form`/`json`/`payload` local) read as "given"; a member of a call's result, arithmetic and one-word calls stay Gaps (#2028).
- **AC-0915** Given a `↳` follow of a check that several repository functions share a name with, when its condition calls it on a receiver that names a class (`self`/`cls` the owner's class, `g.user` and `current_user` the `User` login model, any other receiver by its last segment in CamelCase), then the check is followed into that class's method only, the row saying it was matched on the receiver's class (inferred), and each function is followed once per table; with no such class, or not exactly one match, the row still names the check as not followed with the count. A predicate's unguarded `return <expr>` (a function named `is_`, `has_`, `can_`, `should_` or `must_`) is a guarded exit with no conditions (a constant `return True`/`False` only when an earlier exit makes it the "otherwise"), so its answer is stated as its rule ("A user is active when the user's delete on is not set") (#2029).
- **AC-0916** Given a non-HTTP capability (a mail listener, process start or scheduled job) whose entry point enters a file, when D1 is compiled, then §1.5 reaches the callables defined in that file and what they call; a guarded `return` naming a module string constant that holds an SMTP reply (`status.E502` = "550 …", matched by name and module stem, inferred) is a decision row stating the reply as accepted (2xx), deferred (4xx) or refused (5xx) with its text (a 3xx intermediate reply is no decision), and a returned local the function binds to reply constants before that return (`res_status = status.E502`) names each reply it may hold; a module's plain string constants are recorded on its File node, except a rebound name or a sensitive or secret-shaped value (#2027).
- **AC-0917** Given a rule condition that reads a request header through a module constant (`request.headers.get(constants.HEADER_X)`), when D1 is compiled and exactly one module binds that constant to a plain string, then the Rule cell names the header by that string (the “X-Sl-Allowcookies” header, matched by name, inferred), otherwise by the constant's words; a condition a nested guard repeats, alone or as the first part of a later `A and B`, is said once; a membership test against a literal list reads "is not one of “a”, “b”" (#1998).
- **AC-1030** Given a §1.5 or `↳` rule row whose condition calls a method on a receiver with no arguments (`self.lifetime_or_active_subscription()`), compares a sum (`a + b`) with a value, tests a flag bit (`FLAG == self.flags & FLAG`) or tests membership in a method's value (`x not in user.available_alias_domains(…)`), when D1 is compiled, then the Rule cell states it in domain terms ("the check “lifetime or active subscription” holds for the user", "the active alias count plus the num aliases is at most the user's max alias for free account", "the user's flags include the “free old alias limit” flag", "the alias domain is not one of the user's available alias domains for the alias options", a method's inputs kept), an accessor's `get_` is dropped, and a predicate given a number names it in brackets; and given a Python method that returns `True` or `False` on some path, its unguarded last answer after an earlier exit is a guarded-exit rule whatever the method's name, stated as holding otherwise ("Otherwise, the user's lifetime or active subscription is true when the user's active subscription is set"), while a constant last answer stays no rule (#1998).
- **AC-1034** Given a Python rule whose condition tests a local name the function binds once, by a plain `name = value` statement that runs before the condition in a block enclosing it, when it is recovered, then the condition's read of that name is a Binding and the rule carries the initializer as a local definition: calls, keyword arguments, attribute reads, parentheses, literals and names as structured nodes (each call's and attribute's value unresolved), any other shape as one unsupported node with a Gap, and a name the initializer reads followed the same way within the definition chain bound. A name that is a parameter, is bound twice or in another way, or is assigned after the condition stays unresolved. In D1 the rule's statement is followed by what each tested local was assigned, a header, query parameter or form field read by its name and a model lookup by its keys ("Here the API key is the API key whose code is the “Authentication” header."); an assignment outside the rendering rules adds nothing (#1998).
- **AC-1036** Given a Python `return` or `raise` inside `except T:` (or `except T as e:`), when it is recovered, then the rule carries the raised branch of `T`, and a `return` that is itself a statement of a `try` body carries the not-raised branch of each handler that names one type; a bare `except:`, an `except*` or a handler naming several types states no branch. In D1 the condition reads "a bad signature error is raised" / "no bad signature error is raised" (`Exception` is "an error") and the evidence `raised T` / `not raised T`; a rejection row whose condition tests a local assigned a plain function's result (`alias_suffix = check_suffix_signature(signed_suffix)`) follows that function as a check, as AC-0914 does for a called one (#1998). A signed value read back, `x.unsign(v)` or `x.unsign(v, max_age=N)` and `x.loads(v, max_age=N)`, reads "v, once its signature is checked" with "and it is at most N seconds old" when an age is given; a `loads` without `max_age` (`json.loads(v)`) is not said to check a signature.
- **AC-1040** Given a Python rule whose condition tests a local name the function binds more than once, when it is recovered, then the name is a Binding to the last plain `name = value` assignment that runs before the condition in a block enclosing it, provided no other binding of the name can run in between: a binding before that assignment is overwritten, one in an earlier clause of an `if` statement whose later `elif`/`else` clause holds the condition cannot run first, and `x = f(x)` reads the earlier value; a binding that could run in between, a loop that could carry a later binding back, or a `global`/`nonlocal` declaration leaves the name unresolved. A `+`-style arithmetic operator and a single subscript are recorded as written. In D1, when the textual rules cannot state a tested local, its recorded value is read node by node and each local name that value reads is stated in a sentence of its own (at most six per row): “Here the suggested alias is the prefix suggestion plus the suffix of the first of the suffixes.” A form field's value (`form.code.data`) reads as the “code” form field, a JSON body field (`data.get("name")` with `data = request.get_json()`) as the “name” field of the JSON body, `len(x)` as the number of x, `x.split("@")` as x split at “@”, and `int(x)`/`str(x)` as x; any other method's result is not said, two values of one name in one row are not said, and a plural subject takes “are”. Only a definition the condition itself reads is followed as a check (#1998, W13 R14).
- **AC-1046** Given a TypeScript rule whose condition tests a local const whose initializer is an awaited TypeORM query-builder chain (`await qb.getMany()`, with `qb = connection.getRepository(ctx, ProductVariant).createQueryBuilder('variant').leftJoin('variant.product', 'product').where('variant.deletedAt IS NULL').andWhere('variant.id IN (:...variantIds)', { variantIds })`), when it is recovered, then each query-builder step (`getRepository`, `createQueryBuilder`, joins, `where`/`andWhere`/`orWhere`, `orderBy`, `getMany`/`getOne`/`getCount`/`getManyAndCount`) is a recorded call and the `await` a recorded await, both with their values unresolved, while any other call, an optional call or a spread argument stays one unsupported node; expression depth is bounded at 16. D1 then states “Here the available variants are the product variants that are not deleted and whose product is not deleted, among the variant ids.”, and a step or condition outside the closed shapes (left joins only; one `where` first, then `andWhere`s; `x.deletedAt IS [NOT] NULL`, `x.col IS [NOT] NULL`, `x.col IN (:...p)`, `x.col = :p`, one predicate per string) says nothing: an inner join or a later `where` that replaces earlier conditions is not read (#590).
- **Security:** Local reads only. With T2 off no provider is contacted; the notice carries reason codes and counts, never source text.
- **Performance:** A desktop context query copies the whole base per page, so its cost grows with the graph; the knowledge phase now runs after every ingest, on the existing single coalesced worker.
- **Trace:** P1 (#1782) · `app`, `ui` · ADR-0051, ADR-0052 · T-0588
- **AC-1202** Given a committed ingest and the knowledge phase that follows it, when the phase reads its base, then it streams the graph under the same snapshot admission, in the same row order and revision as the materialized bounded read, and keeps every node and edge with its id and label, but holds `BusinessRule` props, which no producer, knowledge unit or overlay admission reads, as `{}`. The derived facts and the published overlay are identical. On REA the phase's read goes from +1.73 GB to +0.74 GB and its peak from 3.79 GB to 2.80 GB.
- **Trace:** P1 · `core-graph`, `app` · ADR-0051, ADR-0052 · T-1202
- **AC-1209** Given the knowledge phase after an ingest, when it reads its base, then each fact's own `prov` (when its text needs no escape) is held as marked compact JSON text as its row is streamed, so the whole graph is never decoded at once. Restored, every row is the stored row; every producer (capabilities, business flows, domain entities, actors, subsystems) and every knowledge unit reads both forms alike, a unit never renders the held text, and the derived facts are byte-identical (#2215).
- **Trace:** P1 · `app`, `spec`, `semantic` · ADR-0058 · T-1209

### US-0047 — Have an agent draft a section of a catalogue document
- **Actor:** Engineer or product owner
- **As a** reader of the recovered documentation **I want** an agent role to draft one section of a catalogue document from cited facts, for me to accept, reject or return **so that** the documents hold written content where no rule reaches, without any uncited or unreviewed statement appearing in them.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** In-Progress
- **AC-0663** Given document catalogue version 1.0 and an ingested system, when the export is compiled, then `product-specification.md` (D1) is emitted with every catalogue section in order, each P section and the frame of each P+A section taken from the existing projection or naming the exported artifact that holds it, each A section and each P+A section without an eligible draft marked as not drafted, each H section a labelled blank, and a header carrying the catalogue version, graph content hash, curated revision and export mode; a section not in the section table cannot be addressed, and every existing artifact is byte-identical except the fact index, which names the document among the artifacts citing each fact its frames print. When the knowledge overlay supplies derived Capabilities or Actors (#1443), the Actors section frames the caller roles (each permission set with the guarded operations it admits, labelled assumed caller sets, never effective authorization) and the Capabilities section the derived Capabilities (each with its operations and citation), whether D1 is exported or read alone; a refused overlay leaves D1 with its base frames.
- **AC-1315** Given a knowledge overlay that supplies derived permission Actors, when D1 is compiled, then each caller-role row of section 1.2 prints, in a Citation column, the citation ids of up to three of the Actors behind it (with a count of the rest), so a section 1.2 or 1.1 composition lists those Actors as citable facts and can cite who may act; before, the grouped role table printed no Actor id and an actor-only graph refused composition with `no_citable_fact` (#2326).
- **AC-0664** Given a drafting run with its admitted citation set, when the role finishes, then a draft is accepted only if it targets the requested address, holds 1 to 256 claims of at most 2,048 bytes each with 1 to 16 citation ids, and every cited id is in the admitted set; a claim with no citation, an id outside the set, an exceeded bound or a statement failing the existing replay and privacy checks refuses the whole finish with a closed reason code and stores nothing.
- **AC-0665** Given an admitted draft, when it is stored and reviewed, then its identity covers its address, claims, cited fact hashes, role definition, producing task and graph content hash; storing it again is idempotent; a review is an atomic compare-and-set recording accepted, rejected or returned; a returned draft admits exactly one successor for the same address, which cannot itself be returned; a stale review revision fails; and the draft, its review and its citations reopen unchanged after a restart.
- **AC-0666** Given accepted drafts, when the curated projection is computed, then each has exactly one state (superseded, unresolvable, stale, conflicted or eligible) with a reason, a draft is stale exactly when a fact it cites has a different content hash than the draft recorded, the curated revision covers drafts and proposals under one identity that any review changes, and the base graph content hash is unchanged.
- **AC-0667** Given one eligible accepted draft, when the export is compiled, then a `best-effort` export prints its claims in the addressed section, each with its citation ids, T3 / InferredWeak, the author role and the review status; a `verified-only` export of the same state prints no claim and reports the number omitted for that section; every cited id resolves in the fact index or the export fails; no projected statement is replaced; and an MCP read of the artifact returns the export's bytes for the same inputs and mode.
- **AC-1010** Given more than 256 accepted section drafts or curated findings, when the curated projection is read, then accepted drafts are bounded by their total encoded size (64 MiB, 256 drafts of the largest size the draft store admits) and accepted findings by theirs (16 MiB), not by their number, so every accepted one within the budget is read; past the budget the projection is not computed, with the reason `accepted_too_large`, and no part of them is shown; reviewed proposals keep their 256 accepted bound (#2040).
- **AC-1011** Given a knowledge overlay current for the export, when a section composer drafts a section that prints a derived Capability or Actor, then that fact is citable as itself beside the base facts it rests on; a draft citing it stages with its current hash, the curated projection resolves it against the overlay's Capabilities and Actors (eligible, not `unresolvable`), and `product-specification.md` prints the accepted draft and lists the derived fact in `fact-index.md`; with no current overlay the same draft is `unresolvable` and is not printed (#2040, #1785).
- **AC-1314** Given a section composition (`section-composer@1`, AC-0862, or the gated `draft-rules@1`, AC-0980), when its claims are staged, then a claim that spells out a route literal (a word starting with `/`) that none of its cited facts shows (a node's id, label or props without provenance, or an edge's ends, label or props) is left out and counted, matched as a whole route so that a longer route, a prefix or a segment of a provenance file path does not count; when every claim is left out, nothing is staged with `route_not_cited`. The composer is told that a route it states must be shown by a fact it cites, so it cites the endpoint rather than a capability whose title lacks the route (#2328).
- **AC-1316** Given a section composition, when D1 1.1 Purpose and scope is composed (AC-1313), then each section it summarises is read only up to its first `###` heading, and each table in it keeps at most 24 rows followed by a line counting the rows left out, so the request fits the input cap; and when claims are staged (AC-1314), a word of two or more `/`-joined segments that matches whole segments of a route some fact of the graph shows (such as `custom/new` for `/api/alias/custom/new`) counts as a route, and a claim whose cited facts do not show it is left out with `route_not_cited`, while prose such as `and/or` that matches no route is unaffected (#2335).
- **AC-1317** Given a section composition, when its claims are staged (AC-1314), then a claim is left out with `number_not_cited` when it states a numeral, count or version (`17`, `0.0.0.0:7777`, `15s`, `v2`) that none of its cited facts shows as a whole number or version, a section reference (`section 1.2`, `§1.3`) and a route (checked by AC-1314) aside, and with `endpoint_not_cited` when it asserts endpoints or routes (the words endpoint or route, or a route literal) without citing an Endpoint fact; the composer is told both rules. Every left-out claim is counted under the first bound or check it fails (`claim_kind_unknown`, `citation_not_citable`, `statement_not_admissible`, `statement_not_substantive`, `too_many_claims`, `statement_names_source`, `route_not_cited`, `number_not_cited`, `endpoint_not_cited`, or `gated_check` for the gated workflow), and the counts are returned with the outcome and written to the finished job's stage (#2349).
- **AC-1318** Given a section composition, when a claim's figures are checked (AC-1317), then they are matched against what each cited fact shows besides its ids (its label and props, without provenance), and only as a whole token with no letter or digit beside it, so the hex digits of an id (`a25f17b0`) or a longer number (`1.25`) do not show `25` or `17`, while a literal the fact shows whole (`15s`) still does; and the D1 1.3 composer guidance says never to state or enumerate routes, URL paths, methods or versions, since 1.3 cites capabilities, which show no route (#2357).
- **AC-1319** Given a section composition, when a staged claim is checked against the facts it cites, then it is left out as `claim_not_supported` when fewer than 15% of its content terms (stemmed words of three or more letters, without function words, frame words such as `capability` or `section`, citation ids or section references) appear in its cited facts' ids, labels or props and at least three do not, so a claim citing one `View admin` fact cannot assert a mobile screen and how every area groups server-side behaviour; a claim stating an HTTP method (`GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`) asserts an endpoint and must cite one; and the composer is told to name no screen, actor, channel or behaviour none of its cited facts names (#2371).
- **AC-1320** Given a system-scope composition of D1 1.3 or 1.1, when the composer's context is built, then it also reads an excerpt of 1.5: each capability's rules stated in domain terms with their citation (no Gap rows, no area-own-code, state-machine, shared or rate-limit rows, matched by their generated headings so a capability named like one keeps its rules), taken round-robin (every capability's first rule, then every second one) within a byte budget of 16 KiB for 1.3 and 6 KiB for 1.1, with a line counting the rules shown of those stated, so each rule's fact is citable; and the 1.3 guidance grounds each area in the Endpoint and rule facts behind its capabilities, stating an operation, route or method only by citing its Endpoint fact and a condition or limit only by citing its rule, replacing the AC-1318 instruction never to state routes (#2375).
- **AC-1321** Given a system-scope composition of D1 1.3 or 1.1 whose request with the AC-1320 rule excerpt would exceed the call's input ceiling less a 4 KiB margin, when its context is prepared, then it reads the longest excerpt that fits, cut to a half, a quarter or an eighth of its budget, or no excerpt when none fits, so the call is dispatched instead of refused as `InputTooLarge` (#2386).
- **AC-1322** Given a system-scope composition whose request with a rule excerpt (AC-1321) would exceed the input ceiling less its margin with the citation schema listing every citable id, when its context is prepared, then that excerpt is tried with a schema whose citation ids are any string before a shorter excerpt is tried, since the prompt already prints every citable id, and the reply's claims are still left out as `citation_not_citable` when they cite an id that is not citable; with no excerpt the schema lists the ids (#2399).
- **AC-1323** Given a D1 1.3 composition, when the composer is guided, then it is told to describe operations in domain terms, never spelling out a route, URL path, HTTP method or port and not using the words endpoint or route, to state a condition or limit only by citing its rule and a count only when a cited fact shows it, replacing the AC-1320 instruction to state an operation, route or method by citing its Endpoint fact, which the composer did not follow (#2408).
- **AC-1324** Given a system-scope composition that reads a rule excerpt (AC-1320), when its claims are staged, then a claim citing an excerpt rule without citing a Capability fact named like the capability the rule is listed under is left out as `rule_capability_not_cited` (a rule whose capability no citable fact names is not checked), and the excerpt tells the composer that a claim about what a capability enforces cites the rule and that capability and states the rule only for it (#2416).
- **AC-1325** Given a local composition whose claims passed the staging checks, when it is staged, then `claim-reviewer@1` reads each claim beside the text of the facts it cites (an excerpt rule as the rule of its capability, each fact once and cut to 400 characters) in one bounded call and gives one verdict per claim (shown, partly, overreach); each claim judged an overreach is left out as `claim_overreaches`, the outcome records `reviewed`, `review_unavailable` when the reply does not give one verdict per claim (no claim is left out), or `not_reviewed_cloud` for a cloud provider, and the draft is not staged when every claim is left out (#2419).
- **AC-0668** Given a request to draft one address, when `section-writer@1` runs, then it runs inside the existing investigation limits, consent and cancellation, its citation set is the facts of the section's frame and declared scope by citation id, a valid finish stages a draft and a refused or insufficient-evidence finish stages none, and no other role's definition, prompt fingerprint or saved history changes.
- **AC-0669** Given the Spec Workbench, when a catalogue document is opened, then each section shows its kind, its claims with their annotations and openable citations, the state and reason of any listed draft, and the draft and review actions; accepting, rejecting or returning updates the view from the next curated read.
- **AC-0670** Given an expected set for one SimpleLogin capability set written from the repository before any drafting run and never part of an ingested source, when the accepted drafts for those D1 sections are scored claim by claim by a reviewer who did not produce them, then each claim is recorded as supported, unsupported or missing against its cited sources, and the result is committed whatever it is.
- **AC-0682** Given an export, when it is compiled, then `bundle-manifest.md` is emitted and states the as-of of the bundle (the document catalogue version, the export mode, the graph content hash, the curated revision or the reason code for none, and each source registration with its revision) exactly as the host supplied them, stating a value that was not supplied as not supplied and inventing none; it lists every file of the full export exactly once under the SPEC-16 part that file belongs to, and a file no part names under its own heading; it reads no graph fact, carries no assertion and no citation id, prints stored values as inert text, is byte-identical when compiled alone or read over MCP, and no other artifact's bytes change.
- **AC-0683** Given a completed drafting run whose finish was admitted, when the draft is staged, then no draft is stored and the outcome is `statement_names_source` if any statement contains, as a whole token and case-sensitively, an identifier of a fact that statement cites: a path of the fact's evidence, the file name of such a path, or the name (or last qualified segment) of a cited `Symbol`, `Function`, `Module`, `Decorator`, `Declaration`, `Component` or `File` node when that name is shaped like code; a plain single word, an HTTP method or path, a field or message name, a path of the target's own documentation and an identifier of a fact the statement does not cite are not findings, and a draft without a finding is staged as before.
- **AC-0684** Given an MCP session that holds the task grant, when it starts `section-writer@1` with `cartograph_task_start`, then the start is admitted under the same address check as a start in the app and refused before any task when the question names no drafted section; when it reads `cartograph_task_result` for that task, then the reply adds `drafting` as `staged` with the draft id or `no_draft` with a closed reason code, a second read stores nothing more, the reply for a task of any other role is unchanged, and the session can neither submit draft text nor review a draft.
- **AC-0861** Given a `section-writer@1` drafting request, when its completion payload is prepared, then it carries a response schema that admits exactly the role's offered actions (`query_context`, `read_evidence`, `read_coverage_inventory` and the versioned supported or insufficient-evidence finish wrapper, with no `proposed_design` claim kind), the schema has a projection on the Anthropic, OpenAI and Ollama wires, the request seals with it unchanged, and other schema-4 roles still send none. The host decoder stays authoritative (#1989).
- **AC-0862** Given a drafted D1 section at the system scope, when it is composed (`section-composer@1`), then the host compiles D1 from one read of the graph, the decisions and the knowledge overlay, hands one bounded call that section's compiled text and one row per base fact it cites (a derived Capability or Actor is listed with the base facts it rests on and is citable itself, AC-1011; no row shows a node id or a source identifier), with a response schema whose citation ids are those facts alone. A cloud composition is sent only for the payload hash the user approved, and its egress is recorded. The reply is decoded strictly; a claim outside a per-claim bound, or naming a source identifier it cites (AC-0683), is left out and counted, and the rest are admitted against the section's citation set and stored as a draft of `section-composer@1` for review. A reply that is not the schema, a graph that changed, or a non-system scope stages nothing. The Workbench offers "Compose from the knowledge graph" on each drafted D1 section, shows the exact payload for consent, and states the outcome. The request carries the section's own writing guidance (capabilities by purpose, actor and outcome rather than a route list, following the capability areas the section lists when it lists them, with leave to merge or rename a weakly named area; rules translated into domain terms with exact messages and statuses), and the schema requires at least one claim and one citation per claim; a statement of fewer than five words is left out. D1 1.3 is read as an overview: the composer is handed it up to its first `###` heading, with a line counting the per-capability subsections left out, and cites only what that part prints (#1991).
- **AC-1313** Given D1 §1.1 Purpose and scope, which is agent-authored only and prints no fact of its own, when it is composed (`section-composer@1`, AC-0862), then the composer reads the §1.1 heading followed by the §1.2 actors text and the §1.3 overview (up to its per-capability detail), cites only the facts those print, and carries §1.1 guidance (purpose, users, capability areas and scope boundaries, not a capability-by-capability listing); a graph with capabilities therefore stages a §1.1 draft instead of refusing with `no_citable_fact` (#2319).
- **AC-0980** Given drafted D1 section 1.5 at the system scope and a configured local completion model, when the user chooses "Compose locally, checked", then the host runs the declarative workflow `draft-rules@1` (`crates/agents/workflows/draft-rules.workflow.json`, after the Universal Agentic Workflow contract, #2083) on that local model only, with no consent step because nothing leaves the machine: the compiled section is cut into chunks of at most 10 rule rows that repeat their headings and table header; each chunk is drafted with the composer's prompt for at most 8 claims (claims a reply holds past that are left out and fail its gate) and gated by deterministic checks of the reply against the rows the chunk cites (schema, citations resolve, every rule row covered, no source identifier it cites (AC-0683), no code identifier, quotes verbatim, every number in a cited row and no template placeholder, ordering words only where a cited row has them); a failing gate's prescriptions and the previous reply become the next attempt, at most twice, and after the last attempt only claims passing every check are kept. An auditor (`rules-auditor@1`) then judges each kept claim against its cited rows; claims it finds unsupported go back to the drafter once with its reason and are left out if still unsupported; an auditor reply outside its schema (malformed, a claim missing or judged twice) is asked for once more, and claims no valid reply judges are left out, never staged. Kept claims are assembled in chunk order, a statement drafted again word for word (only spacing differing) being one claim that cites the rows of both, up to the draft bound, and staged as a draft of `draft-rules@1` through the same admission rule as a composition. The outcome states the claims staged and left out, and the rule rows covered out of the section's rows in how many local calls. Another section, a cloud provider, or a run where no claim passes stages nothing with a closed reason.
- **AC-0981** Given D1 §1.5 compiled with areas (#260), when the user chooses "Compose locally, checked" at the system scope, then the host runs the AC-0980 workflow once for each area the section holds a block for, on that block alone (the section's heading, then the area's block), and stages each as a draft scoped to the citation id of the area's `Subsystem`, citing only facts that block prints; the rest of the section (its introduction and any block that is no area's, such as shared rejections) is drafted as the system-scope draft when it prints a fact. Each draft has its own bound, so a section with more rule rows than one draft holds is still drafted whole (#2083). Without areas, one system draft is staged as in AC-0980. A composition at an area's scope composes that area's block only; a scope that names no current area, or a non-system scope on a section not arranged by area, stages nothing with a closed reason. The outcome sums the claims staged over the drafts and the rule rows covered, and names the areas where nothing was staged. A run that stops (a cancel, a provider failure) after an area draft was staged reports the drafts staged so far under its job, names the part where it stopped as staging nothing (`run_stopped`) and drafts no further part; one that stops before any draft was staged fails as a whole.
- **AC-0925** Given recovered `Endpoint` facts, when D1 §1.3 is compiled, then after the Capability frame it states how many HTTP endpoints no included Capability cites, of how many, how many of those have no resolved handler (their `HANDLES` edge reaches no Symbol, so no rule can group them) and how many have a handler that no capability rule groups, counted by framework, and points to `endpoint-inventory.md` for the list; an endpoint recorded with Gap confidence is not counted (it is in the gap register); it states nothing when every endpoint is cited or there is none (#2007).
- **Security:** Agents propose only; acceptance never upgrades a tier or writes a fact (R-INT-1, R-INT-3). Egress consent and redaction per step are unchanged. No write to the ingested target.
- **Performance:** A drafting run is bounded by the existing call, byte and time limits; a draft is at most 256 claims and 256 KiB.
- **Trace:** W13 (#1748) · `spec`, `agents`, `context-hub`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0064, ADR-0061, ADR-0058 · T-0663..0670, T-0682..0684, T-0861, T-0862, T-0925, T-0980, T-0981

### US-0048 — Keep authored sections current as the code changes
- **Actor:** Engineer or product owner
- **As a** reader of the recovered documentation **I want** an accepted section to be marked stale, with the changed facts named, when a re-ingest changes what it rests on, and to request a reviewed re-draft **so that** the documents never silently describe code that no longer exists.
- **Priority:** Must · **Status:** Draft
- **AC-0671** Given an accepted draft and a graph in which facts it cites have changed or are gone, when the curated projection is computed, then the draft is reported with the sorted citation ids whose content hash differs from the recorded one and the sorted ids that resolve to no fact, in the curated Workbench read and in `curated.md`; the state rules are unchanged, and the same graph, drafts and reviews always give the same lists.
- **AC-0672** Given two snapshots of a fixture that differ in one handler and accepted drafts for several sections, when the document is compiled against the second snapshot, then exactly the drafts that cite a changed fact are marked stale and every other draft is unmarked, identically on a second run; a best-effort document prints a stale draft in place under a stale mark naming the changed ids and marks each claim that cites one, does not print an unresolvable draft and states how many of its cited ids are missing (`curated.md` names them, so every id the document prints resolves in the fact index), and lists the stale and unresolvable sections in its header; a verified-only document prints no drafted claim and states the same list.
- **AC-0673** Given the Spec Workbench with a stale or unresolvable accepted draft, when the document view is shown, then the draft shows the changed and missing citation ids under its state as the host reported them, each changed id openable as evidence and each missing id shown as text because no fact remains to open, marks each claim that cites a changed id, and offers a re-draft for the same address; a draft in any other state shows neither list nor the offer.
- **AC-0674** Given an accepted draft that is stale or unresolvable, when a re-draft is requested, then an ordinary drafting run is started for the same address and the draft it stages names the accepted draft as replaced; the replaced draft stays accepted and in place until the successor is accepted and is then superseded and kept; rejecting the successor changes nothing else and allows another re-draft; a draft has at most one successor that is not rejected, and a request for a draft in any other state is refused with a closed reason.
- **AC-0675** Given a draft that replaces another, when its change is read, then the host returns the claims added, removed and kept and the cited facts added, removed and changed, computed from the two stored drafts alone, and the Workbench shows them beside the successor before review.
- **Security:** Stale marking is a set comparison with no model call. A re-draft is an ordinary drafting run under the existing consent, redaction and limits; acceptance never upgrades a tier or writes a fact (R-INT-1, R-INT-3). No write to the ingested target and no edit of a target document (NG1).
- **Performance:** The comparison is linear in the cited facts of the accepted drafts, inside the existing curated read bounds.
- **Trace:** W15 (#1749) · `context-hub`, `spec`, `agents`, `app`, `ui` · ADR-0045, ADR-0064, ADR-0061 · T-0671..0675

### US-0049 — Read the target's existing documentation as evidence
- **Actor:** Engineer or product owner
- **As a** reader of the recovered documentation **I want** the READMEs, design notes and runbooks of a source read as documents and sections, linked to the facts they name exactly, with broken references listed **so that** I can see what the existing documentation refers to, where it no longer matches the code, and cite it as stated intent.
- **Priority:** Should · **Status:** Draft
- **AC-0676** Given a registered source with Markdown and plain-text files, when it is ingested, then each accepted file is one `Document` fact and each of its sections one `DocSection` fact with its heading, level and byte span, Deterministic and Confirmed, linked by `HAS_SECTION`; headings inside fenced code are not sections; the file, file-count and section bounds hold; and a second ingest of the same commit yields the same facts and the same graph content hash.
- **AC-0677** Given documentation files of which some are too large, not UTF-8 or past the file limit, when the coverage inventory is built, then it counts the files found, read and skipped by closed reason, the sections and the files read to the section bound; a skipped or partly read file makes the inventory limited; the coverage document prints the counts; no count is stated as completeness; and a repository with no documentation file keeps the inventory it had.
- **AC-0678** Given a section whose inline code spans and link targets name a fact id, a repository-relative file path, a route as method and path, or a name that exactly one fact of a named kind carries, when references are resolved, then each is linked by one `MENTIONS` edge citing the reference span and naming the rule; a reference that matches no rule or more than one fact is not linked and makes no finding; and text inside fenced code is never a reference.
- **AC-0679** Given a README that names one route that is served and one that is not, when the source is ingested, then the first is linked at T0 and the second is in the drift register citing the reference span and stating what it was compared with; a route served only with other methods cites those endpoints; a relative link to a file that does not exist is listed with the path looked for; and with no recovered endpoint in the system no route entry is made.
- **AC-0680** Given documents that contradict or restate recovered facts, when they are ingested, then no other fact's tier, confidence, content or content hash changes; the graph and every export hold a section's heading, span and hash and never its text; and a title or heading holding a secret is stored redacted.
- **AC-0681** Given ingested documents with exact references, when the export is compiled, then `documentation-evidence.md` lists each document, its sections and the facts each section mentions with rule and citation ids that resolve in the fact index, and counts the links by rule and the drift entries by kind, in both export modes; and the citation set of a section draft includes the `DocSection` facts that mention a fact in the set, so a documented-intent claim can cite one.
- **AC-0864** Given a published system and the pass that relinks found ADRs and reads documentation after publication, when it runs, then it reads the graph packed and decodes only the labels each step reads (Repo and ADR nodes, `DECIDES` edges, documentation facts and their edges, and Endpoint and named nodes for the reference index), a governed ADR target and a documentation reference are matched against every fact of the system by id and label, and documents are read only for the repositories the operation publishes. A reference to a recovered file in a published repository's README therefore yields its `MENTIONS` edge through the production pass, and relinking an unchanged tree changes nothing (#2003, #2004).
- **AC-1600** Given a source with documentation files and found decision records, when it is ingested, then one walk reads each file once under one size bound and yields the same `Document`, `DocSection`, `ADR` and `DECIDES` facts, ids and counts that the document reader and the found-decision reader yielded on their own; a decision record past the document file bound or under build output is still read as a decision; the `Document` of a decision record carries the `decision_record` role and a `RECORDS` edge to its `ADR`; a document without a role keeps the fact it had; and `documentation-evidence.md` prints the role.
- **AC-1601** Given declared API reference files, when the source is ingested, then each validated file is a `Document` with the `api_reference` role, also when it sits in a dot directory (read from the contents its loader validated) or is an OpenAPI `.json` file (format `openapi`, no sections); each is counted once in the coverage inventory, never twice when the walk already counted it, and is held to the same file bound; every declared file is read from its validated contents, so the document and the contract see the same bytes; a declared decision record under build output keeps both roles and its `RECORDS` edge; and the contract facts of those files are unchanged.
- **Security:** A document is evidence of stated intent only: it never promotes or overwrites a fact (R-INT-1). Section text is read through the redacting source read and is never stored in the graph; headings are redacted before storage. No write to the ingested target (NG1). No model call.
- **Performance:** One walk and one read per file for documents, found decisions and declared API references; at most 2,000 files of at most 1 MiB per repository and 512 sections per file; reference resolution is by indexed lookup.
- **Trace:** W16 (#1750) · `ingest`, `spec`, `core-graph`, `app` · ADR-0065, ADR-0036, ADR-0058 · T-0676..0681, T-0864, T-1600..1601

### Decision #1692 — authenticated analyst diagnostics (US-0032 / AC-0287)

Analyst @22 uses text-free replay locations and a separate
capacity-excluded obligation projection. The foundation provides typed field
paths and one-based sentence ranges, including matches spanning multiple fields.
It preserves the existing joined-prose detector, thresholds and metadata rules;
no rejected model/source text is included in diagnostic output. Capacity-excluded
IDs are collected at the exact hash filter before menu truncation, separately
from other blocked obligations. Exclusion never establishes an attempt,
acquisition or satisfaction.

Analyst @20/@21 prompts and canonical menu bytes remain unchanged. Analyst @22
has a distinct prompt and receipt family, invocation-bound authentication of saved
diagnostics, host workspace charges and missing-history/tamper rejection. The
correction allowance is unchanged. Capacity exclusions are recomputed at the
authenticated ledger cutoff and clear on a new ledger revision; they remain
unresolved, unattempted and unacquired. A clean development result and semantic
review remain separate from release acceptance.

For the @22 witness, admission establishes locator correctness from the
transient refused finish. Reopen can prove only integrity and binding after text
is discarded: an independent immutable response-metadata digest must bind the
response hash, finish hash, refusal ID and step. Missing or mismatched @22
witnesses fail closed; older roles omit the field entirely.
If diagnostic location disagrees with the replay detector, the finish stays
refused and uses generic schema-3 replay feedback, preserving the existing
correction allowance. Sensitive-output, size and invalid-action errors still
propagate; diagnostic fallback never admits a result.

### Decision #1697 — evidence-bound analyst claims (US-0032 / AC-0287)

Analyst @23 (prompt generation 13) checks literal enumeration claims against
its cited captured spans before admitting a finish. Unbound claims and spans
that are not literal enumerations are not applicable; bound false or
unverifiable claims receive text-free sentence locations through the existing
single correction turn and authenticated witness. Unsupported subset or
exception language is unverifiable, not interpreted semantically. Bound quantified
claims outside the grammar are refused with typed feedback, consuming the single
correction turn; this intentional over-refusal also applies to true claims whose
phrasing falls outside the supported grammar.

Default/configured/registered assertions require configuration evidence binding
the named identifier. Titles may resolve a subject only against identifiers in
the same finding's statement or citations: normalized words must uniquely match
a contiguous CamelCase prefix. No synonyms or stemming apply; unresolved or
ambiguous subjects are not applicable. Configuration subject binding is limited
to identifiers with multiple normalized words. Single-word identifiers (for
example, `Redis`) do not bind a subject, even when present in cited source; such
claims are not applicable. This is a known scope limit, not verified configuration.
Limitations are exempt. Default-prefixed
source paths and identifiers may establish the corresponding default identity,
but unrelated co-occurrence does not establish configuration.

Historical @20–@22 behavior and canonical bytes, privacy detectors, limits and
correction allowance remain unchanged. Refused prose is never stored. Golden
regressions bind the r3 findings to the actual captured source span; these local
checks do not establish real-provider or release acceptance.

### Decision #1701 — exact captured reservation and combined corrections (US-0032 / AC-0287)

Analyst @24 uses receipt-bound original and production-redacted serialized source
sizes when available, retaining the 6x fallback for historical captures. No source
read precedes the existing charge/access gate. After a charged digest-verified
read, both actual sizes must fit their reserved values before context installation.
Schema-6 feedback reports all applicable correction locations up to the existing
byte ceiling, with deterministic deduplication and an explicit omitted count.
The single correction allowance, detectors, privacy rules and numeric limits stay
unchanged. Historical @20–@23 bytes and reopen behavior remain unchanged.
Joint r3/r4 capacity measurements and real-model acceptance are separate gates.
See [implementation design](design/analyst24-reservation-feedback.md).

### Decision #1704 / issue #1705 — partial findings after correction (US-0032 / AC-0287)

Analyst @25 may withhold current-finish finding-local unverified enumeration or
configuration claims after the existing single correction. Every other refusal
class stays fatal. Re-derived unmet obligations or no surviving findings fail.
A text-free bound witness and explicit partial disposition survive sealing,
settlement, reopen and export; the result is never silently Supported. Historical
@20–@24 remain unchanged. See [implementation specification](design/1705-withheld-findings.md).

### Decision #1707 — bounded refusal diagnostic sidecars (US-0032 / AC-0287)

New refusal diagnostics use a separate immutable, independently witness-bound
sidecar capped at 4096 complete serialized bytes (ADR-0055). Closed reason codes,
source positions and token classes explain the existing verdict without raw
prose or identifier strings. Overflow records explicit omissions or a capacity
marker. Final withholding describes the current authored proposal. Historical
absence remains readable; deletion or rehashed substitution of a newly bound
sidecar is rejected. Diagnostics must preserve whole-run dispositions,
withholding, model bytes, later capacity and provider counts. Only new records of analyst roles 22–25 receive diagnostics. Earlier roles
remain unchanged; future roles adopt the witness at their own version bump.

### Issue #1729 — bounded enumeration predicates (US-0032 / AC-0287)

In progress; the new grammar is selected only by opt-in analyst @27. Historical
roles through @26 retain their existing verdict and diagnostic behavior. This
change is stacked on #1723 and must merge after it; adoption remains separate.
Given a cited literal transition table, when a finding uses the complete forms
`X is terminal` or `X is terminal and has no outgoing transitions`, the new
grammar accepts exactly an empty X row and rejects a nonempty row with a
text-free literal counterexample. Given `X is the only state that lists Y` or
`X is the sole state that lists Y`, X must contain every named target and no
other row may contain any named target. This does not constrain other targets
in X. Explicit and/or lists use the existing target-list grammar. Additional
prose and unknown subjects are not accepted as these predicates. Original
positions, historical false-universal results, privacy checks and diagnostic
schemas remain unchanged. T-0287 covers synthetic contrasts and diagnostics.
